## I / 第一章

===Original===

On the spot where the Hôtel Moderne now rears its more ambitious head, there stood at that time a cottage with sloping red-tiled roof and whitewashed walls. It was owned by one Baptiste Portal, an old peasant of the Dauphiné, who dispensed refreshments to travellers and passersby, as his father and grandfather had done before him, in the shape of somewhat thin *vin du pays* and an occasional glass of eau-de-vie, while he spent his slack time chiefly in grumbling at the fact that the new posting-inn on the high road had taken all his trade away. He did not see the necessity of the posting-inn, did not old Baptiste, nor for a matter of that of the high road or the post-chaise. Before all these new notions had come into the heads of the government people up in Paris, travellers had been content to come squelching through the mud on the back of a good horse, or come ploughing through inches of dust in the old *coche*. So why not now? And was not the old wine of Les Amandiers as good and better than the vinegar dispensed at the more pretentious posting-inn? The place was called Les Amandiers because at the back of the house there were two anaemic almond-trees with gaunt, twisted arms which covered themselves in the spring with sickly blooms, and in the summer with dust. In front of the house, up against the whitewashed wall, there was a wooden bench on which Baptiste’s privileged customers were wont to sit on fine evenings, to drink their vin du pays and join the old man in his wholesale condemnation of the government “up in Paris” and its newfangled ways. From this vantage-point a glorious view was obtained over the valley of the Buëche, and beyond Laragne as far as the peaks of Pelvoux: whilst to the right towered in the distance the grand old citadel of Sisteron with its turrets and fortifications dating from the fourteenth century, and the stately church of Notre Dame. But views and winding rivers, snowy peaks and medieval fortresses did not interest Baptiste Portal’s customers nearly as much as the price of almonds or the alarming increase in the cost of living.

===Chinese===

如今，在摩登旅馆（Hôtel Moderne）昂起它更气派的楼头的地方，当年立着一座村舍，红瓦坡顶，墙皮刷得雪白。店主人是巴普蒂斯特·波塔尔（Baptiste Portal），多菲内（Dauphiné）的一个老农，做的是祖上传下的款待行旅的营生——他父亲、他祖父当年怎么做，他也怎么做——卖些略嫌寡淡的本地土酒（*vin du pays*），偶尔再来一杯烧酒（eau-de-vie）；至于闲工夫，他多半用来嘟囔抱怨：大路上新开的那家客栈，把他家的生意全抢光了。老巴普蒂斯特就是看不出那客栈有什么必要——何止客栈，连那条大路、那种驿车（post-chaise），他通通看不出有什么必要。在巴黎当官的生出这些新花样之前，旅客们向来心满意足：要么骑一匹好马，一路踏着泥浆吧唧吧唧地来；要么坐着老式的乡村公共马车（*coche*），在几寸厚的尘土里犁地一般拱过来。如今凭什么就不行了？再说，杏树园（Les Amandiers）的老酒，难道不比那家更神气的客栈里卖的酸醋一样地好、还好上十分吗？这地方所以叫作“杏树园”，是因为屋后长着两棵面黄肌瘦的杏树，枝干枯瘦虬曲，春天给自己披一身病恹恹的花，夏天蒙一层灰扑扑的土。屋前贴着白灰墙摆着一条木凳，天气晴好的傍晚，巴普蒂斯特那些有脸面的老主顾惯于坐在那里，喝着本地土酒，同老头儿一道，把“巴黎城里”的政府和它那些新派做派骂个体无完肤。从这个居高临下的好位置望出去，景致极佳：比埃什河（Buëche）河谷尽收眼底，越过拉拉涅（Laragne），一直望到佩尔武（Pelvoux）的群峰；右侧远处，锡斯特龙（Sisteron）那座苍老雄伟的大城堡巍然耸立，塔楼与壁垒还是十四世纪传下来的，旁边立着庄严的圣母教堂（Notre Dame）。只是，蜿蜒的河流也好，雪峰古堡也好，在巴普蒂斯特·波塔尔的主顾们看来，都远不及杏仁的行情、生活费的高涨来得要紧。

===Original===

Now on this particular afternoon in May the mistral was blowing mercilessly across the valley from over the snows of Pelvoux, and the cold and the dust had driven all the good Portal’s customers indoors. The low-raftered room, decorated with strings of onions which hung from the ceiling together with a bunch or two of garlic, of basil and other potherbs, and perfumed also with the aroma of the pot-au-feu simmering in the kitchen, had acquired just that right atmosphere, cosy, warm and odorous, beloved of every true man born in Dauphiné. It was a memorable afternoon, remembered long afterwards and retold by the gossips of Sisteron and Laragne in all its dramatic details. But at this hour, nothing more dramatic had occurred than the arrival of a detachment of soldiers, under the command of an under-officer, who had come up from Orange, so they said, in order to fetch away the young men who were wanted for the army. They had demanded supper and shelter for the night.

===Chinese===

且说五月里的这个下午，密史脱拉风（mistral）正越过佩尔武的积雪，无情地刮过河谷来；天又冷，尘又大，把好心的波塔尔的主顾们全都赶进了屋。这间屋梁低矮的屋子，天花板上挂着一串串洋葱，还悬着一两把大蒜、罗勒和别样香草；厨房里煨着家常炖菜（pot-au-feu），香气一阵一阵透出来——恰好酿成了那种恰到好处的气氛：暖和，安逸，气味厚实，是每一个多菲内土生土长的真正汉子都心爱的。这是一个值得纪念的下午；事过多年，锡斯特龙和拉拉涅的爱嚼舌的人们还会把它讲给人听，一五一十，连那些富有戏剧性的细节也不放过。可是在眼下这个时辰，还谈不上什么戏剧性：无非是一小队士兵开到，领队的是个下级军官——据说是从奥朗日（Orange）开上来的，要把那些该去当兵的年轻人带走。他们要了晚饭，还要过夜的地方。

===Original===

Of course soldiers, as soldiers, were very much disapproved of by those worthies of Sisteron who frequented Les Amandiers, more especially now when what they did was to fetch away the young men for cannon-fodder, to fight the English and prolong this awful war which caused food to be so dear and hands for harvesting so scarce. But on the other hand, soldiers as company were welcome. They brought news of the outside world, most of it bad, it is true﻿—nothing good did happen anywhere these days﻿—but news nevertheless. And though at the recital of what went on in Paris, in Lyons or even as near as Orange, the guillotine, the tumbrils, the wholesale slaughter of tyrants and aristos, one shuddered with horror and apprehension, there were always the lively tales of barrack-life to follow, the laughter, the ribald song, and something of life seemed to filtrate into this sleepy half-dead corner of old Dauphiné.

===Chinese===

当然，在常来杏树园的那些锡斯特龙体面人眼里，兵——单说“兵”这个身份——本来就十分要不得，如今更是如此：这伙兵办的差事，就是把年轻人抓去当炮灰，去打英国人，去把这场可恶的战争拖长——仗打得粮价这样贵，收庄稼的人手这样缺。可是话又说回来，兵当作伴儿，倒受欢迎。他们带来外面世界的消息，多半是坏消息，不假——如今这年头，哪儿也出不来好事——可终归是消息。虽说一听人讲起巴黎、里昂（Lyons）、乃至近在眼前的奥朗日的情形——断头台呀，死囚车呀，对暴君和贵族佬们的大开杀戒呀——人人都要吓得心里发毛、脊背发凉，可接下来总还有营房生活的新鲜故事，有哄笑，有荤曲儿；于是一星半点的生气，似乎就这样渗进了老多菲内这个昏昏欲睡、半死不活的角落。

===Original===

The soldiers﻿—there were a score of them﻿—occupied the best place in the room, as was only fitting; they sat squeezed tightly against one another like dried figs in a box, on the two benches on either side of the centre trestle table. Old Baptiste Portal sat with them, beside the officer. Some kind of lieutenant this man appeared to be, or other subaltern; but oh dear me! these days one could hardly tell an officer from the ragtag and bobtail of the army, save for the fact that he wore epaulettes. Now this man﻿—but there! what was the use of comparing these ruffians with the splendid officers of the King’s armies in the past?

===Chinese===

士兵们——总有二十来个——占了屋里最好的位子，这也合该如此；他们分坐在当中那张支架长桌两侧的两条板凳上，一个紧挤着一个，活像盒子里码紧的干无花果。老巴普蒂斯特·波塔尔也同他们坐在一起，挨着军官。这人看模样是个中尉，再不就是别的什么下级军官；可是天可怜见！如今这年月，若不是肩上多一副肩章，你简直分不出军官同队伍里的残兵游勇来。至于这个人嘛——唉，罢了！拿这路痞棍去比当年王家军队里那些器宇轩昂的军官，又有什么用呢？

===Original===

This one certainly was not proud. He sat with his men, joked, drank with them, and presently he convened friend Portal to a glass of wine: “*A la santé*,” he added, “*de la République*, and of Citizen Robespierre, the great and incorruptible master of France!”

===Chinese===

这一位倒当真不摆架子。他同手下的兵坐在一起，说说笑笑，跟他们一块儿喝酒，不多会儿便邀老朋友波塔尔也来上一杯：“祝健康（*A la santé*），”他又添上一句，“祝共和国（*de la République*），也祝罗伯斯庇尔公民——法兰西伟大而不可腐蚀的主宰！”

===Original===

Baptiste, wagging his old head, had not liked to refuse, because soldiers were soldiers and these had been at great pains to explain to him that the reason why the guillotine was kept so busy was because Frenchmen had not yet learned to be good Republicans.

“We’ve cut off the head of Louis Capet and of the widow Capet too,” the officer had added with grim significance, “but there are still Frenchmen who are bad patriots and hanker after the return of the tyrants.”

===Chinese===

巴普蒂斯特摇着他苍老的脑袋，却不敢推辞：兵到底是兵；何况这伙人已经煞费苦心向他讲明白了，断头台之所以这样忙个不停，就是因为法国人还没学会做规矩的共和党人。

“路易·卡佩的脑袋，咱们砍了；卡佩寡妇的脑袋，咱们也砍了，”军官接着说，话里带着冷森森的深意，“可还有那么些法国人是坏爱国者，还眼巴巴地盼着暴君们卷土重来呢。”

===Original===

Now Baptiste, like all his like in the Dauphiné, had learned in childhood to worship God and honour the King. The crime of regicide appeared to him unforgivable, like that mysterious sin against the Holy Ghost, which M. le Curé used vaguely to hint at, and which no one understood. In addition to that, Baptiste greatly resented His late Majesty King Louis XVI and his august Queen being irreverently referred to as Louis Capet and the widow Capet. But he kept his own counsel and silently drank his wine. What his thoughts were at the moment was nobody’s business.

===Chinese===

巴普蒂斯特呢，同多菲内所有同他一路的人一样，打小儿受的就是敬上帝、尊君王的教育。弑君这桩罪，在他看来无可饶恕，就像亵渎圣灵那桩玄妙的罪过——本堂神父（M. le Curé）从前影影绰绰地点过那么一两回，可谁也没听懂过。不但如此，先王路易十六陛下和他尊贵的王后，如今竟被人没上没下地叫作路易·卡佩、卡佩寡妇，这也叫巴普蒂斯特心里老大不痛快。但他把话揣在肚里，闷声喝干了自己的酒。他这会儿在想些什么，与旁人无干。

===Original===

After that, talk drifted to the neighbourhood: the aristos who still clung to the land which by right belonged to the people. Neither Baptiste nor his customers﻿—old peasants from the district﻿—were a match for the lieutenant and his corporals in such discussions. They did not dare argue, only shook their heads and sighed at the coarse jests which the soldiers uttered against people and families whom everyone in the Dauphiné knew and esteemed.

The Frontenacs for instance.

The talk and the jests had turned on the Frontenacs: people who had owned the land for as long as the oldest inhabitant could remember and God only knows how long before that. Well! it appeared that in the eyes of these soldiers of the Republic the Frontenacs were bad patriots, tyrants and traitors. Didn’t Citizen Portal know that?

===Chinese===

打那以后，话头就转到了本乡本土：谈那些贵族佬——至今还死攥着照理该归人民的土地。在这种题目上，巴普蒂斯特同他的主顾——本区的老农们——哪里是那位中尉和他那几个下士的对手。他们不敢争辩，只是摇头，叹气，听凭士兵们把粗野的笑话往那几户人家身上泼——那几户人家，多菲内里里外外谁不认得、谁不敬重。

比如弗朗特纳克（Frontenac）一家。

话头和笑谑果然都落到弗朗特纳克一家头上：这片土地归他们家，年头久到本区最老的寿星也只能记得“打记事起就是”，再往前，只有天晓得了。嘿！原来在这些共和国士兵眼里，弗朗特纳克一家是坏爱国者、是暴君、是叛徒。波塔尔公民难道不知道么？

===Original===

No! Portal did not﻿—he had never been called “citizen” before, and didn’t like it: he was just Baptiste to those who knew him, *quoi*?﻿—Nor would he admit that the Frontenacs were traitors. There was Monsieur, who knew more about cattle and almonds than any man for leagues around. How could he be a bad patriot? And Madame, who was very good and pious, and Mademoiselle who was so ill and delicate. But on this there followed an altercation﻿—stern rebuke of Baptiste from the officer for talking of “Monsieur,” of “Madame” and “Mademoiselle.” Bah! there were no aristos left these days. “Aren’t we all citizens of France?” the lieutenant concluded grandiloquently.

===Chinese===

不知道！波塔尔不知道——他活到这把年纪，头一回被人称作“公民”，还老大不爱听：在熟人嘴里，他就是巴普蒂斯特，可不是么？——他也不肯承认弗朗特纳克一家是叛徒。且不说那位先生（Monsieur），论牛马、论杏仁，方圆多少里没有比他更在行的，他怎么会是坏爱国者？还有那位夫人（Madame），心肠那样好，那样虔诚；还有那位小姐（Mademoiselle），身子又那样娇，那样弱。可这句话惹出了一场口角——军官疾言厉色地训斥巴普蒂斯特，不许他“先生”“夫人”“小姐”地乱叫。呸！如今这年头，哪还剩什么贵族佬。“咱们不全都是法兰西的公民么？”中尉拿腔拿调地下了断语。

===Original===

Silence and submission on the part of all the groundlings which followed on the lieutenant’s rebuke, somewhat mollified the latter’s aggressive patriotism. He condescended to relate how he had been deputed to make a perquisition in the house of the Frontenacs, and if anything was found the least compromising, then the devil help the whole brood: their lives would not be worth an hour’s purchase. In fact, in the lieutenant’s opinion﻿—and who better qualified to hold one?﻿—the Frontenacs were already judged, condemned, and as good as guillotined. He held with the “law of the suspect,” lately enacted by the National Assembly, did Lieutenant Godet.

===Chinese===

中尉一顿训斥之后，满屋的乡巴佬鸦雀无声、俯首帖耳，这倒把他那咄咄逼人的爱国气焰抚平了几分。他屈尊讲起自己的差事来：原来他奉了委派，要去弗朗特纳克家宅里搜查；但凡搜出一星半点连累人的东西，那就让魔鬼去保佑他们一窝子吧——他们的性命连一个钟头也换不来。说实在的，照中尉的高见——这高见，谁比他更有资格持有呢？——弗朗特纳克一家早就是审过了、判定了、同送上断头台只差一刀的了。嫌疑犯法令是国民议会（National Assembly）新近颁布的，戈岱中尉（Lieutenant Godet）举双手拥护。

===Original===

Again much wagging of heads! “The Committees in all Sections,” Godet now goes on airily, and proceeds to pick his teeth after that excellently stewed scrag-end of mutton, “the Committees in all Sections are ordered in future to arrest all persons who are suspect.”

No one knows what is a Committee, nor yet a Section: but they are evidently fearsome things. But no matter about them: the thing is who are the “suspect” who are thus arrestable?

===Chinese===

跟着又是一片摇头！“各区（Section）的委员会，”戈岱这会儿轻飘飘地接着讲，一面把那顿美味的炖羊颈肉吃罢，剔起牙来，“各区的委员会奉了命令，今后凡有形迹可疑的人，一概逮捕。”

谁也不知道委员会是个什么，区又是个什么：可它们分明是些叫人害怕的物事。不过这些且不去管它：要紧的是，那些说抓就能抓的“嫌疑犯”，究竟是些什么人？

===Original===

“The Frontenacs are suspect,” the lieutenant explains whilst sucking his toothpick, “and so are all persons who by their actions﻿—or﻿—their writings have become﻿—er﻿—suspect.”

===Chinese===

“弗朗特纳克一家就是嫌疑犯，”中尉一面嘬着牙签，一面讲解，“凡是凭行动——或者——凭文字，变成了——呃——嫌疑犯的人，也都是。”

===Original===

Not very illuminating perhaps, but distinctly productive of awe. The worthies of Sisteron, those who are privileged to sit close to the centre table and actually to put in a word with the soldiers, sip their wine in silence. Just below the tiny window at the end of the room two charcoal-burners, or woodcutters﻿—I know not what they are﻿—are lending an attentive ear. They dare not join in the conversation because they are comparative strangers, vagabonds really, come to pick up a few sous by doing menial work too lowering for a local peasant to do. One of them is small and slender, but looks vigorous; the other, much older, with stooping shoulders, and grey, lank hair that falls over a wrinkled forehead. He is harassed by a constant, tearing cough which he strives in vain to suppress out of respect for the company.

===Chinese===

这话讲得也许不甚明白，威势却是一等一的。锡斯特龙的那些体面人——有幸挨着中间长桌坐、真能同士兵们搭上一句话的——都一声不响地小口呷着酒。就在屋子尽头那扇小窗底下，坐着两个烧炭夫，要不就是砍柴的——我也道不明他们究竟是干什么的——正竖起耳朵听着。他们不敢搭腔，因为他们是十足的外乡人，说穿了就是流浪汉，专靠干些本地庄稼汉嫌掉价的贱活儿，挣几个苏（sou）。两人里一个身材瘦小，瞧着却精悍；另一个上了年纪，肩膀佝偻，灰白稀疏的头发耷拉在起皱的额前。一阵阵撕心的咳嗽折磨着他；出于对在场诸位的敬意，他拼命想忍住，却总是忍不住。

===Original===

“But,” the worthy Portal puts in tentatively, “how does one know Monsieur le﻿—I mean citizen officer, that a person is in verity suspect?”

===Chinese===

“不过，”可敬的波塔尔试探着插了一句嘴，“长官先生——呃，我是说公民长官——一个人到底凭什么知道，什么人当真是个嫌疑犯呢？”

===Original===

The lieutenant explains with a sweeping gesture of the toothpick: “If you are a good patriot, Citizen Portal, you are able to recognize a Suspect in the street, you can seize him by the collar then and there, and you may drag him off before the Committee, who will promptly clap him in prison. And remember,” he added significantly, “that there are forty-four thousand Committees in France today.”

===Chinese===

中尉把牙签大手一挥，讲解道：“你要是个好爱国者，波塔尔公民，你就能在街面上认出嫌疑犯来，当场揪住他的领子，把他拖到委员会去，委员会立刻把他下进牢里。还有，记住，”他意味深长地补上一句，“如今法兰西总共有四万四千个委员会。”

===Original===

“Forty-four thousand?” somebody exclaims.

“And twenty-three,” Godet replies, gloating over his knowledge of this trifling detail.

“Forty-four thousand and twenty-three,” he reiterates and claps the table with the palm of his hand.

===Chinese===

“四万四千？”有人失声叫起来。

“还带二十三个零头，”戈岱答道，为自家连这点零碎都一清二楚而扬扬得意。

“四万四千零二十三，”他又重申一遍，一巴掌拍在桌面上。

===Original===

“One in Sisteron?” someone murmurs.

“Three!” the lieutenant replies.

“And the Frontenacs are suspect, you say?”

“I shall know that tomorrow,” rejoins the other, “and so will you.”

===Chinese===

“锡斯特龙有一个？”有人低低地问。

“三个！”中尉答道。

“照你这么说，弗朗特纳克一家是嫌疑犯了？”

“这个嘛，我明天就知道了，”对方回敬道，“你们大家也都会知道的。”

===Original===

The way he said those three last words caused everyone to shudder. Over at the far end of the room, the charcoal burner, or whatever he was, had a tearing fit of coughing.

===Chinese===

他说最后那三个字时的那副腔调，叫满屋子的人都打了个寒噤。屋子远远的那一头，那个烧炭夫——管他到底是干什么的——猛地发作起一阵撕肺的咳嗽。

===Original===

“ ’Tis little Fleurette who will weep her eyes out,” good old Baptiste said with a doleful shake of the head, “if anything happens to Mad﻿—to the citizens up at the château.”

“Fleurette?” the lieutenant asked.

“She is Armand’s daughter﻿—Citizen Armand you know﻿—why﻿—?”

He might well stare, for the officer, for some unaccountable reason, had burst into a loud guffaw.

===Chinese===

“把眼睛都要哭坏的，是小弗勒蕾特（Fleurette）啊，”好心的老巴普蒂斯特凄然摇着头说，“万一那位夫——我是说，万一山上庄园（château）里那几位公民有个三长两短。”

“弗勒蕾特？”中尉问道。

“她是阿尔芒（Armand）的女儿——你知道的吧，公民阿尔芒——怎么？”

他满可以发怔，因为那位军官不知中了什么邪，竟放声大笑起来。

===Original===

“Citizen Armand’s daughter did you say?” he queried at last, his eyes still streaming with the effort of laughing.

“Yes, of course. As pretty a wench as you can see in Dauphiné. Why shouldn’t Armand have a daughter, I’d like to know.”

“Do tigers have daughters?” the lieutenant retorted significantly.

===Chinese===

“你说什么，公民阿尔芒的女儿？”他好半天才问出话来，笑出来的眼泪还挂在腮上。

“是啊，这还用问。多菲内地面上打着灯笼也难找的俊姑娘。我倒要请教，阿尔芒凭什么就不该有个女儿。”

“老虎也生女儿么？”中尉意味深长地顶了一句。

===Original===

Somehow the conversation languished after that. The fate which so obviously awaited the Frontenacs who were known and loved, cast a gloom over the most buoyant spirits. Not even the salacious stories of barrack-life, on which the men now embarked with much gusto, found responsive laughter.

===Chinese===

不知怎么，话头从此就蔫了下去。弗朗特纳克一家，人人识得，人人爱敬，而横在他们前头的命运又是那样明摆着，连最乐天的性子也给罩上了一层阴翳。就连士兵们随即兴头十足讲起来的营房荤话，也逗不起一声应景的笑。

===Original===

It was getting late too. Past eight o’clock, and tallow was dear these days. There was a cart-shed at the back of the house, with plenty of clean straw: some of the soldiers declared themselves ready for a stretch there: even the voluble officer was yawning. The regular customers of Les Amandiers took the hint. They emptied their mugs, paid over their sous, and trooped out one by one.

===Chinese===

时候也不早了。八点都过了，如今油脂蜡烛金贵着呢。屋后有间停车棚，干净的麦草多的是：有几个士兵声言乐意去那儿伸开手脚睡一觉；连那位健谈的军官也打起哈欠来。杏树园的老主顾们得了这个暗示，把杯中残酒喝干，付过各自的苏，一个跟着一个出去了。

===Original===

The wind had gone down. There was not a cloud in the sky, which was a deep, an intense sapphire blue, studded with stars. The waning moon was not yet up, and the atmosphere was redolent of the perfume of almond blossom. Altogether a lovely night. Nature in her kindest, most gentle mood. Spring in the air and life stirring in the entrails of the earth in travail. Some of the soldiers made their way to the shed, whilst others stretched out on the floor, or the benches of the room, there to dream perhaps of the perquisition to be made tomorrow and of the tragedy which would enter like a sudden devastating gust of wind into the peaceful home of the Frontenacs.

===Chinese===

风息了。天上没有一丝云，是深深的、浓得化不开的宝石蓝，缀满天星。残月还没有升起来，空气里浮动着杏花的芬芳。好一个可爱的夜晚。大自然拿出她最仁慈、最温柔的性子。春意在空中，生命在临盆大地的腹中蠢动。有几个士兵往车棚去了，其余的摊开手脚躺在屋里的地板上、板凳上，在那儿做起梦来——梦里没准儿就是明天的搜查，就是那场悲剧：它将像一阵骤起的狂风，横扫进弗朗特纳克家安宁的宅院。

===Original===

Nature was kind and gentle: and men were cruel and evil and vengeful. The Law of the Suspect! No more cruel, more tyrannical law was ever enacted within the memory of civilization. Forty-four thousand and twenty-three Committees to mow down the flower of the children of France. A harvest of innocents! And lest the harvesters prove slack, the National Convention has just decreed that a perambulating army shall march up and down the country, to ferret out the Suspect and to feed the guillotine. Lest the harvesters prove slack, men like Lieutenant Godet with a score of out-at-elbows, down-at-heels brigands, are ordered to scour the country, to seize and strike. To feed the guillotine in fact, and to purge the Soil of Liberty.

Is this not the most glorious revolution the world has ever known? Is it not the era of Liberty and of the Brotherhood of Man?

===Chinese===

大自然是仁慈温柔的：人却是残忍、邪恶、睚眦必报的。嫌疑犯法令！自有文明的记忆以来，还不曾颁布过比这更残忍、更暴虐的法令。四万四千零二十三个委员会，来刈割法兰西儿女中的花朵。一场无辜者的收割！唯恐收割的人手不尽力，国民公会（National Convention）刚刚颁令：要有一支巡行的大军，在全国版图上来回开拔，把嫌疑犯们搜出来，喂给断头台。唯恐收割的人手不尽力，像戈岱中尉这样的人——手下带着二十来个衣肘磨穿、鞋跟塌败的匪兵——奉命扫荡乡野，去抓人，去砍杀。说到底，就是喂饱断头台，涤净那自由的土壤。

这难道不是开天辟地以来世间所见最光荣的一场革命么？这难道不是自由的时代、人类博爱的时代么？

## II / 第二章

===Original===
The perambulating army had now gone to rest: some in the cart-shed, some along the benches and tables or floor of the inn. The lieutenant in a bed. Is he not the officer commanding this score of ardent patriots? Therefore must he lie in a bed﻿—old Portal’s bed﻿—whilst old Portal himself and his wife, older and more decrepit than he, can lie on the floor, or in the dog’s kennel for aught Lieutenant Godet cares.

===Chinese===
这支流动大军如今歇下了：有的睡在车棚里，有的横在客栈的长凳上、餐桌上或地板上。中尉嘛，睡在床上。他岂不是统领着这二十名热血爱国者的军官？所以他就非躺在床上不可——老波塔尔的床——至于老波塔尔本人，还有他那位比他更老迈衰朽的老婆，睡地板去，或者睡狗窝去，戈岱中尉（Lieutenant Godet）才不管呢。

===Original===
The two woodcutters﻿—or shall we call them charcoal-burners?﻿—were among the last to leave. They had petitioned for work among the worthies here present: but money was very scarce these days and each man did what work he could for himself, and did not pay another to do it for him. But Papa Tronchet, who was a carpenter by trade and owned a little bit of woodland just by the bridge, close to Armand’s cottage, he promised one of the men﻿—not both﻿—a couple of hours’ work tomorrow: wood-cutting at the rate of two sous an hour, and then he thought it dear.

===Chinese===
那两个伐木工——还是叫他们烧炭工好？——属于最晚离开的那批人。他们曾向在场诸位体面人物讨过活计：无奈这些日子银钱短少，人人都是自己的活自己干，不肯花钱雇旁人代劳。唯独特龙谢老爹（Papa Tronchet）——他本行是木匠，在桥边、紧挨阿尔芒（Armand）小屋的地方有一小片林地——答应给两人中的一个——不是两个——明天几个钟头的活干：砍柴，工钱每小时两苏，就这样他还嫌贵呢。

===Original===
And so the company had dispersed: each man to his home. The two vagabonds﻿—woodcutters or charcoal-burners, they were anyhow vagabonds﻿—found their way into the town. Wearily they trudged, for one of them was very old and the other lame, till they reached a narrow lane at right angles to the riverbank. The lane was made up of stone houses that had overhanging eaves, between which the sun could never penetrate. It was invariably either as damp as the bottom of a well, or as dry and windswept as an iron stovepipe. Tonight it was dry and hot: broken-down shutters, innocent of paint, creaked upon rusty hinges. A smell of boiled cabbage, of stale water and garlic hung beneath the eaves; it came in great gusts down pitch-dark stairways, under narrow doors, oozing with sticky moisture.

===Chinese===
于是这伙人便散了：各自回家去。那两个流浪汉——管他伐木工还是烧炭工，反正就是个流浪汉——一路摸进了镇子。他们拖着疲惫的步子艰难前行，因为其中一个已经老态龙钟，另一个瘸着腿，一直走到一条与河岸成直角的窄巷跟前。巷子两旁全是石屋，屋檐前探，太阳从来照不进屋檐之间。这里向来不是潮得像井底，就是干得像铁烟囱管似的又干又灌风。今晚又干又热：破破烂烂的百叶窗上一星油漆也没有，在生锈的铰链上嘎吱作响。煮圆白菜、馊水和大蒜的气味悬在屋檐底下，一阵一阵地从漆黑的楼梯井里、从渗着黏腻潮气的窄门底下涌出来。

===Original===
The two vagabonds turned into one of these doors and by instinct seemingly, for it was pitch dark, they mounted the stone stairs that squelched with grease and dirt underneath their feet. They did not speak a word until they came to the top of the house, when one of them with a kick of his boot threw open a door; it groaned and creaked under the blow. It gave on an attic-room with sloping ceiling, black with the dirt of ages, and with dormer window masked by a tattered rag that had once been a curtain. There was a wooden table in the centre of the room, and three chairs, with broken backs and ragged rush-seats, dotted about. On the table a couple of tallow candles guttered in pewter sconces.

===Chinese===
两个流浪汉拐进了其中一扇门，似乎是全凭本能，因为里面漆黑一片；他们蹬上石楼梯，脚底下的油腻污垢被踩得咯吱作响。一路上他们一言不发，直到登上房子顶层，其中一个抬脚一靴子踢开一扇门；门在这一击之下呻吟着吱嘎大开。门内是一间阁楼房，天花板倾斜，被经年累月的污垢熏得乌黑，屋顶的老虎窗上一条破布遮掩着，那布从前想必是幅窗帘。房间中央摆着一张木桌，另有三把椅子散在各处，椅背断裂，灯芯草坐垫破烂不堪。桌上两支牛油蜡烛插在锡制烛台里，烛泪淋漓，摇曳欲熄。

===Original===
One of the chairs was drawn close up to the table and on it sat a young man dressed in a well-worn travelling-coat with heavy boots on his feet, and a shabby tricorne hat on the top of his head. His arms were stretched out over the table and his face was buried in them. He had obviously been asleep when the door was so unceremoniously thrown open. At the sound he raised his head and blinked drowsily in the dim light at the newcomers.

===Chinese===
其中一把椅子拉得紧挨着桌子，上面坐着个年轻人，身穿一件穿旧了的旅行外套，脚蹬沉重的靴子，头上扣着一顶寒酸的三角帽。他双臂摊在桌上，脸埋在臂弯里。门被这样不由分说地踹开时，他显然正睡得香。听到响动，他抬起头，就着昏暗的烛光，睡眼惺忪地朝来人眨了眨。

===Original===
Then he stretched out his arms, yawned and gave himself a shake like a sleepy dog, and finally exclaimed in English! “Ah! at last!”

===Chinese===
随后他伸了个懒腰，打个哈欠，像条瞌睡的狗似的抖了抖身子，终于用英语喊道：「啊！总算回来了！」

===Original===
One of the vagabonds﻿—the one namely who at Les Amandiers had appeared with bent shoulders and a hacking cough, now straightened out what proved to be a magnificent athletic figure, and gave a pleasant laugh.

===Chinese===
两个流浪汉中的一个——就是在杏树园（Les Amandiers）里驼背弯腰、咳嗽连连露面的那一位——此刻挺直了身躯，显出一副魁伟矫健的好身材，发出一阵愉快的笑声。

===Original===
“Tony, you lazy dog!” he said, “I’ve a mind to throw you downstairs. What say you, Ffoulkes? While you and I have been breaking our backs and poisoning our lungs with the scent of garlic, I verily believe that this villain Tony has been fast asleep.”

===Chinese===
「托尼（Tony），你这个懒骨头！」他说，「我真想把你扔下楼去。你说呢，福尔克斯（Ffoulkes）？你我在这儿累断了腰杆、让大蒜味熏毒了肺脏，我却深深疑心，这个恶棍托尼倒一直在呼呼大睡。」

===Original===
“By all means let’s throw him downstairs,” assented the second vagabond, now no longer lame, whom his friend had addressed as Ffoulkes.

===Chinese===
「扔吧，干脆把他扔下楼去。」第二个流浪汉附和道，他如今一点也不瘸了——他的伙伴方才称他为福尔克斯。

===Original===
“What would you have me do but sleep?” Tony broke in with a laugh. “I was told to wait, and so I waited. I’d far rather have been with you.”

===Chinese===
「不睡觉还能叫我干什么？」托尼笑着抢白，「人家吩咐我等着，我就等着呗。我宁可跟你们俩在一块儿。」

===Original===
“No, you wouldn’t,” Ffoulkes demurred, “for then you would have been dirtier than I, and almost as filthy as Blakeney. Look at him; did you ever see such a disgusting object?”

===Chinese===
「你才不会呢。」福尔克斯不服，「要是真跟我们在一起，你会比我更脏，脏得差不多赶上布莱克尼（Blakeney）了。瞧瞧他；你生平见过这么恶心的物件吗？」

===Original===
“By Gad!” rejoined Blakeney, surveying his own slender hands coated with coal-dust, grease and grime, “I don’t know when I have been quite so dirty. Soap and water!” he commanded with a lofty gesture, “or I perish.”

===Chinese===
「天哪！」布莱克尼应道，一面端详自己那双修长的手——上头糊满了煤灰、油垢和污泥，「我都记不起什么时候这么脏过了。肥皂加水！」他姿态高华地一挥手，发号施令道，「不然我就要完蛋了。」

===Original===
But Tony gave a rueful shrug.

===Chinese===
可托尼只是懊丧地耸了耸肩。

===Original===
“I have a bit of soap in my pocket,” he said, and diving into the capacious pocket of his coat he produced an infinitesimal remnant of soap which he threw upon the table. “As for water, I can’t offer you any. The only tap in the house is in the back kitchen which Madame, our worthy landlady, has locked up for the night. She won’t have anything wasted, she tells me, not even water.”

===Chinese===
「我口袋里倒是有一小块肥皂。」他说着，把手伸进外套那只宽大的口袋，掏出一小片可怜巴巴的肥皂头，扔在桌上。「至于水，我可没法奉上。全屋唯一的水龙头在后厨房里，咱们那位可敬的房东太太已经锁上过夜了。她告诉我，什么东西都不许浪费，连水也不例外。」

===Original===
“Fine, thrifty people, your Dauphinois,” commented Blakeney, wisely shaking his head. “But did you try bribery?”

===Chinese===
「好极了，真是节俭人家，你们多菲内（Dauphiné）人。」布莱克尼煞有介事地摇着头评点道，「不过，你试过贿赂没有？」

===Original===
“Yes! But Madame﻿—I beg your pardon, Citizeness Martot﻿—immediately called me a cursed aristo, and threatened me with some committee or other. I couldn’t argue with her, she reeked of garlic.”

===Chinese===
「试了！可那位夫人——恕我改口，女公民马尔托（Citizeness Martot）——当即骂我是什么可恶的贵族佬，还抬出这个那个委员会来威胁我。我没法跟她讲理，她满身大蒜味。」

===Original===
“And you, Tony, are an arrant coward,” Blakeney rejoined, “where garlic is concerned.”

===Chinese===
「而你呢，托尼，是个不折不扣的胆小鬼，」布莱克尼回敬道，「一碰到大蒜就露怯。」

===Original===
“I am,” Tony was willing to admit. “That’s why I am so terrified of you both at this moment.”

===Chinese===
「我正是啊。」托尼倒也爽快，「所以这会儿我对你们两位才怕成这样。」

===Original===
They all laughed, and since water was not obtainable, Sir Percy Blakeney, one of the most exquisite dandies of his time, and his friend Sir Andrew Ffoulkes, sat down on rickety chairs, in clothes sticky with dirt, their faces and hands masked by a thick coating of grime. Down the four walls of the small attic-room fillets of greasy moisture trickled and mingled with the filth that lay in cakes upon the floor.

===Chinese===
三个人都放声笑了。既然弄不到水，珀西·布莱克尼爵士——他那个年代最讲究入时的纨绔子弟之一——和他的朋友安德鲁·福尔克斯爵士便在摇摇欲坠的椅子上落了座，衣裳上黏满污垢，脸和手都罩着一层厚厚的油泥。小阁楼的四壁上，一道道油腻的水痕淌下来，同地板上结成块的秽物混作一处。

===Original===
“I can’t bear to look at Tony,” Blakeney said with a mock sigh, “he is too demned clean.”

===Chinese===
「我简直不忍心看托尼，」布莱克尼装模作样地叹了口气说，「他实在太——太干净了。」

===Original===
“We’ll soon remedy that,” was Ffoulkes’s dry comment.

===Chinese===
「这个毛病马上就能治过来。」福尔克斯干巴巴地评论道。

===Original===
And behold Sir Andrew Ffoulkes at close grips with Lord Anthony Dewhurst, and this in silence for fear of disturbing the rest of the house, and bringing attention on themselves. It was a sparring match in the best style, Blakeney acting as referee, its object﻿—to transfer some of the grime that coated the clothes and hands of Sir Andrew on to the immaculate Lord Tony. They were only boys after all, these men, who even now were risking their lives in order to rescue the innocent from the clutches of a bloody tyranny. They were boys in their love of adventure, and in their hero worship, and men in the lighthearted way in which they were prepared for the supreme sacrifice, should luck turn against them.

===Chinese===
且看安德鲁·福尔克斯爵士与安东尼·杜赫斯特勋爵（Lord Anthony Dewhurst）扭作一团，而且全程一声不吭，生怕惊动这屋里旁人的安歇、招人耳目。这是一场最上乘路数的拳斗练习，布莱克尼充任裁判，其宗旨——是把安德鲁爵士衣上手上的一些污垢，转移到一尘不染的托尼勋爵身上。说到底，这帮人都不过是群孩子，此时此刻正拿性命去冒险，好把无辜者从血腥暴政的魔爪底下救出来。在对冒险的酷爱上，在对英雄的崇拜上，他们是孩子；而一旦时运不济，他们又能以一身轻松豁达，随时准备最大的牺牲，在这上头，他们是男子汉。

===Original===
The sparring match ended in a call for mercy on the part of Lord Tony. His face was plastered with grime, his hands as dirty as those of his friends.

===Chinese===
这场拳斗练习以托尼勋爵讨饶告终。他的脸糊满了污垢，一双手也跟两位朋友的一样脏了。

===Original===
“Tony,” Blakeney said finally, when he called a halt, “if her ladyship were to see you now, she would divorce you.”

===Chinese===
「托尼，」布莱克尼终于喊停的时候说道，「尊夫人这会儿要是瞧见你这副模样，非跟你离婚不可。」

===Original===
Vent having been given to unconquerable animal spirits, there was a quick return to the serious business of the day.

===Chinese===
那股压不服的血气发泄过了，众人便迅速回到当天正事上来。

===Original===
“What is the latest?” Lord Tony asked.

===Chinese===
「有什么最新消息？」托尼勋爵问。

===Original===
“Just this,” Sir Percy replied: “That those hellhounds have sent out detachments of soldiers all over the country to ferret out what they are pleased to call treason. We all know what that means. Since their iniquitous ‘Law of the Suspect,’ no man, woman or child is safe from denunciation: now with this perambulating army, summary arrests occur by the thousand. It seems that at any moment any of those brigands can seize you by the coat-collar and drag you before one of their precious committees, who promptly sends you to the nearest guillotine.”

===Chinese===
「就是这样，」珀西爵士答道，「那些地狱恶犬已经往全国各地派出一支支士兵小队，去搜捕他们美其名曰『叛国』的勾当。我们都清楚那意味着什么。自从他们那条伤天害理的『嫌疑犯法令』出笼，无论男女老幼，谁也保不齐被人告发：如今再加上这支流动大军，不经审讯的逮捕动辄上千。看那光景，那帮匪徒里随便哪一个，随时都能揪住你的衣领，把你拖到他们那些宝贝委员会跟前，委员会一转身就把你送上最近的断头台。」

===Original===
“And you came across a detachment of those brigands, I suppose.”

===Chinese===
「这么说，你们是撞上那帮匪徒的一支分队了。」

===Original===
“We have; Ffoulkes and I spent a couple of hours in their company, in the midst of fumes of garlic that would have reduced you, Tony, to a drivelling coward. I vow the smell of it has even infested my hair.”

===Chinese===
「撞上了；我和福尔克斯在他们中间泡了两个来钟头，泡在能把托尼你熏成淌口水的懦夫那样的大蒜味里。我敢起誓，那股味儿连我的头发都钻进去了。」

===Original===
“Anything to be done?” Tony asked simply. He knew his chief well enough to perceive the vein of grim earnestness through all this flippancy.

===Chinese===
「有什么要办的吗？」托尼直截了当地问。他对自家首领知之甚深，听得出这份插科打诨底下那股冷峻的认真劲儿。

===Original===
“Yes!” Blakeney replied. “The squad of brigands who are scouring this part of France are principally after a family named Frontenac, which consists of father, mother and an invalid daughter. I had already found out something about them in the course of the day, whilst I carted some manure for a farmer close by. Beastly stuff, manure, by the way! I tried to get into touch with Monsieur, who is a stubborn optimist, and does not believe that any man could mean harm to him or to his family. I went to him in the guise of a royalist agent, supposed to have inside information of impending arrests. He simply refused to believe me. Well! we’ve met that type of man before. He will have a terrible awakening tomorrow.”

===Chinese===
「有！」布莱克尼答道。「正在法国这一带搜捕的那队匪徒，主要冲着一户姓弗朗特纳克（Frontenac）的人家去，一家三口：父亲、母亲，还有个缠绵病榻的女儿。白天里我给附近一个农夫运肥，已经摸到他们一些底细。顺带说一句，肥料这玩意儿，真够呛！我设法去接触那位先生，他是个顽固不化的乐天派，不信天下竟有人会存心害他、害他的家小。我乔装成一个保王党密使去见他，只说手握即将大逮捕的内线消息。他干脆就是不肯信我。唉！这种人咱们从前也领教过。明天，他就要挨一场骇人的大梦初醒了。」

===Original===
Sir Percy paused for a moment or two, a deep frown between his brows. His keen intellect, alive to all those swift tragedies which he had devoted his life to countermine, was already at work envisaging the immediate future, the personages of the coming drama, husband, wife, invalid daughter; then the perquisition, the arrest, summary condemnation and slaughter of three helpless innocents.

===Chinese===
珀西爵士顿了一两秒钟，眉心拧出深深的皱纹。那些倏忽而至的惨剧，他已将毕生心血用来与之周旋，此刻他敏锐的头脑已经在运作，预演着迫在眉睫的未来、这出即将开场的戏文里的各色人物：丈夫、妻子、病弱的女儿；接着便是搜查、逮捕、草草定罪，以及三个手无寸铁的无辜者惨遭屠戮。

===Original===
“I can’t help being sorry for the man,” he said after awhile, “though he is an obstinate fool! but it is the wife and daughter whom we cannot allow those savage beasts to capture and to kill. I caught sight of them. The girl is pathetic, frail and crippled. I couldn’t bear﻿—”

===Chinese===
「我不由得替那男人难过，」过了一会儿他说道，「虽说他是个顽固的蠢货！可那位太太和小姐，我们决计不能让那群野兽捉去杀了。我望见过她们母女一眼。那姑娘真叫人心疼，孱弱，又有残疾。我不忍——」

===Original===
He broke off abruptly. No need to say more, of course; they understood one another these men who had braved death so often together for love of humanity and for love of sport. Blakeney silent, one firm, slender hand clutched upon the table, was working out a problem of how to rescue three helpless people from that certain deathtrap which was already laid for them. The other two waited in equal silence for orders. The League of the Scarlet Pimpernel! pledged to help the innocent and to save the helpless! One to command, nineteen to obey: the two who were here in this filthy, dark attic-room were the chief’s most trusted officers; but the others were not far away!

===Chinese===
他蓦地收住了话头。自然，不必再多说一句；这几个人为了对人类的爱、也为了对这场游戏的爱，早已一次次共赴死地，彼此心照不宣。布莱克尼沉默着，一只坚定修长的手紧按在桌上，正在推演一道难题：如何把三个束手无策的人，从那个已经为他们布下的必死陷阱里救出来。另外两人也同样沉默地等着号令。猩红繁笺花同盟！誓扶无辜、誓救无助！一人发令，十九人听命：此刻在这间污秽昏暗阁楼里的两位，正是首领最信任的左膀右臂；而其余的人，也就在不远处！

===Original===
Seventeen others! scattered about the countryside, disguised, doing menial work in order to keep in touch with the population, spying, hiding in woods or huts; all of them under orders from their chiefs, and prepared for the call from him.

===Chinese===
还有十七个！散布在四乡，乔装改扮，干着下等杂役以便与百姓声气相通，刺探消息，藏身林间或棚屋；所有人一律听候诸位首领的调遣，随时准备应他一声召唤。

===Original===
“Tony,” Blakeney said at last, “you’d better find Hastings and Stowmaries at once and they must pass the word round to the others. I want three of them﻿—they can draw lots for that﻿—to go to the Four Oaks and there to remain until I can send Ffoulkes to them with full instructions. When you’ve done that, I want you and Ffoulkes to spend the night in and about Les Amandiers, and gather what you can of the projects of those brigands by keeping your ears open. I’ll keep in touch with you from time to time.”

===Chinese===
「托尼，」布莱克尼终于开口，「你最好立刻去找黑斯廷斯（Hastings）和斯托玛丽斯勋爵（Lord Stowmaries），让他们把话传给其余的人。我要他们中间出三个——这个可以抓阄来定——到『四橡树』（the Four Oaks）去，在那儿待命，直到我派福尔克斯给他们送去完整的指示。这件事办完之后，我要你和福尔克斯整夜守在杏树园里里外外，竖起耳朵，那帮匪徒的图谋能探多少是多少。我会随时跟你们联络。」

===Original===
“You think,” Ffoulkes put in, “that we’ll have trouble with the Frontenacs?”

===Chinese===
「你是认为，」福尔克斯插言道，「跟弗朗特纳克一家打交道会有麻烦？」

===Original===
“Not with the ladies, of course,” Blakeney replied. “We’ll get them safely out of the way before the perambulating army of jackals arrives. With God’s help we ought to have time enough to gather a few valuables together. The trouble will be with that obstinate, tiresome man. I feel sure he won’t move until the soldiers are hammering at his door. Anyway I shall know my way in and about the château by tomorrow morning, and will then get into touch with you both at Les Amandiers.”

===Chinese===
「跟两位女士自然不会，」布莱克尼答道，「在那支豺狼组成的流动大军开到之前，我们就能把她们安然转移出去。托上帝的福，时间总还够我们把几件细软收拾停当。麻烦会出在那个又顽固又恼人的男人身上。我敢断定，不到士兵捶响他的家门，他是决计不肯挪窝的。无论如何，到明天早上，我就能摸熟那座城堡里里外外的路径，然后就到杏树园跟你们两位碰头。」

===Original===
He rose: a tall, straight figure on whom the filthy clothes of a vagabond woodcutter sat with strange incongruity. But even in this strange garb which was grotesque as well as degrading, there was an extraordinary dignity in the carriage of the head, the broad shoulders, the firm, long Anglo-Saxon limbs, but above all in the flash of the eyes beneath their heavy lids and in the quiet, low-toned voice so obviously accustomed to be heard and obeyed. The two others were ready on the instant to act according to instructions; to act without argument or question. The fire of excitement was in their eyes: the spirit of adventure, of sport for sport’s sake had them in its grip.

===Chinese===
他站起身来：一个高大挺直的身影，那身流浪伐木工的肮脏衣衫穿在他身上，不称得离奇。可即便在这身既怪诞又有失体面的古怪行头里，他依然有一种异乎寻常的威仪——在那颗头颅的仪态里，在那副宽阔的肩膀上，在那结实颀长的盎格鲁-撒克逊人的四肢上，而尤其是，在那厚重眼睑之下目光的一闪中，在那沉静低沉、一听便知素来只消开口便人人听命的嗓音里。另外两人当即准备依令行事；不加争辩、不加盘问地行事。他们眼中烧着兴奋的火焰：冒险的精神、为游戏而游戏的那股劲头，牢牢攫住了他们。

===Original===
“Do I go with you now, Blakeney?” Ffoulkes asked, as his chief had remained for a moment standing, as if following a train of thought.

===Chinese===
「我现在就跟你一起走吗，布莱克尼？」福尔克斯问道，因为首领方才兀自站着出了一会儿神，像是循着一条思路想下去。

===Original===
“Yes,” Blakeney replied. “And by the way, Ffoulkes, and you too, Tony, while you are at Les Amandiers try and find out about this girl Fleurette the old innkeeper spoke about. He said that the girl would cry her eyes out if anything happened to the Frontenacs. You remember?”

===Chinese===
「是的。」布莱克尼答道。「对了，福尔克斯，还有你，托尼，你们在杏树园的时候，设法打听打听老客栈老板提起的那个叫弗勒蕾特（Fleurette）的姑娘。他说，弗朗特纳克一家若是出了什么事，那姑娘会把眼睛都哭坏的。你们还记得吧？」

===Original===
“I do. He also said that she was as pretty a wench as could be found in Dauphiné,” Ffoulkes put in with a smile.

===Chinese===
「记得。他还说，她是整个多菲内打着灯笼也难找的标致姑娘。」福尔克斯含笑插了一句。

===Original===
“Her father is named Armand,” Blakeney rejoined.

===Chinese===
「她父亲名叫阿尔芒。」布莱克尼接道。

===Original===
“And the lieutenant called him a tiger, rather enigmatically I thought.”

===Chinese===
「还有，那位中尉管他叫一头老虎，我倒觉得这话来得玄奥。」

===Original===
“This Fleurette sounds an engaging young person,” Lord Tony commented with a smile.

===Chinese===
「听上去，这位弗勒蕾特是个招人喜欢的年轻姑娘。」托尼勋爵含笑评道。

===Original===
“And should be a useful one in our adventure,” Blakeney concluded. “Find out what you can about her.”

===Chinese===
「而且在这场历险里，该是个用得着的人。」布莱克尼收了话，「关于她，能打听到什么就打听什么吧。」

===Original===
He was the last to leave the room. Ffoulkes and Lord Tony had already gone down the stone staircase, feeling their way through the darkness. But Sir Percy Blakeney stood for a minute or two longer, erect, silent, motionless. Not Sir Percy Blakeney, that is, the elegant courtier, the fastidious fop, the spoilt child of London society, but the daring adventurer, ready now as so often before, to throw his life in the balance to save three innocent people from death. Would he succeed? Nay! that he did not doubt. Not for a moment. He would save the Frontenacs as he had saved scores of helpless men, women and children before, or leave his bones to moulder in this fair land where his name had become anathema to the tigers that fed on the blood of their kindred. The true adventurer! Reckless of risks and dangers, with only the one goal in view: Success.

===Chinese===
他最后一个离开这间屋子。福尔克斯和托尼勋爵早已摸着黑走下石楼梯去了。可珀西·布莱克尼爵士又多站了一两分钟，挺立着，沉默着，纹丝不动。此刻的他，已不是那个珀西·布莱克尼爵士——优雅的廷臣、百般挑剔的纨绔、伦敦交际场上宠坏了的孩子——而是那个胆大包天的冒险家，一如以往无数次那样，随时准备把性命押上天平，救三个无辜者免于一死。他会成功吗？嗐！这他倒不怀疑。一刻也不怀疑。他会像从前救出一批又一批无助的男女孩童那样，救出弗朗特纳克一家；再不然，就把这把骨头留在这片美丽的土地上朽掉——在这里，那些以同胞之血饲喂自己的老虎，早已把他的名字当作不共戴天的诅咒。真正的冒险家！置一切风险危难于度外，心目中只有一个目标：成功。

===Original===
Sport? Of course it was sport! grand, glorious, maddening sport! Sport that made him forget every other joy in life, every comfort, every beatitude. Everything except the exquisite wife who in far-off England waited patiently, with deadly anxiety gnawing at her heart, for news of the man she worshipped. She, perhaps, the greatest heroine of them all.

===Chinese===
游戏吗？这当然是游戏！盛大的、辉煌的、令人如痴如狂的游戏！这场游戏让他忘却了人生中其余的一切欢愉、一切安逸、一切至福。忘掉了一切——只除了那位在遥远英格兰守候着的娇妻：致命的焦灼啃噬着她的心，她耐心等着她所崇拜的男人的音讯。而她，或许，才是他们所有人当中最伟大的女英雄。

===Original===
With a quick sigh, half of impatience, half of longing, Sir Percy Blakeney finally blew out the tallow-lights and made his way out into the open.

===Chinese===
珀西·布莱克尼爵士轻轻叹了口气——半是不耐，半是渴盼——终于吹熄了牛油烛，迈步走到外面去了。

## III / 第三章

===Original===
The house where Fleurette was born and where she spent the first eighteen years of her life, still stands about halfway down the road between Sisteron and Serres and close to Laragne, which was then only a village nestling in the valley of the Buëche. To get to it you must first go cautiously down the slope at the head of the old stone bridge, and then climb up another slope to the front door beside the turbulent little mill stream, the soft gurgle of which had lulled Fleurette to sleep ever since her tiny ears had wakened to earthly sounds.

The house is a tumbledown ruin now, only partly roofed in: doors and shutters are half off their hinges: the outside staircase is worm-eaten and unsafe, the whitewashed walls are cracked and denuded of plaster; the little shrine above the door has long been bereft of its quaint, rudely painted statue of St. Anthony of Padua with the Divine Child in his arms. But the wild vine still clings to the old walls, and in the gnarled branches of the old walnut tree, a venturesome pair of blackbirds will sometimes build their nest.

A certain atmosphere of mystery and romance still lingers in the tiny dell, and when we fly along the road in our twentieth-century motorcar, we are conscious of this romantic feeling, and we exclaim: “Oh! how picturesque!” and ask the chauffeur to halt upon the bridge, and then get our Kodaks to work.
===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特出生、并在那里度过人生最初十八年的那座房子，如今仍立在锡斯特龙与塞尔之间的道路中段，离拉拉涅不远；那时的拉拉涅还只是个依偎在比埃什河（Buëche）谷地里的小村庄。要到那座房子去，得先沿着古石桥桥头的坡道小心翼翼地走下去，再爬上另一道坡，才到得了大门口；门旁流着那条湍急的小小磨坊溪，溪水轻柔的淙淙声，自弗勒蕾特的小耳朵初次听见尘世的声响起，便一直哄着她入眠。

如今这房子已是一处坍塌的废墟，只剩半边还盖着屋顶：门扇窗板半脱了铰链，屋外的楼梯虫蛀斑斑、走上去摇摇晃晃，刷白的墙壁裂了缝，灰泥剥落净尽；门楣上方的小壁龛里，那尊画工朴拙、古趣盎然、怀抱圣婴的帕多瓦圣安东尼像，也早已不知去向。只有野葡萄藤还攀附在老墙上；老胡桃树虬曲的枝桠间，偶尔还会有一对胆大的乌鸫前来筑巢。

这小小的谷地里，至今还萦绕着一缕神秘而浪漫的气息。当我们乘着二十世纪的汽车沿路飞驰而过时，会觉出这份浪漫情怀，于是赞叹一声：「啊，多美啊！」吩咐司机在桥上停车，随即掏出柯达相机拍个不停。

===Original===
Perhaps when the plate is developed and we look upon the print, we fail to recapture that sense of a picturesque bygone age, and wonder why we wasted a precious film on what is nothing but a tumbledown old cottage, and why so many tumbledown old cottages are left to crumble away and disfigure the lovely face of France. But a century and a half ago, when Fleurette was born, there was an almond tree beside the front door, which in the early spring looked as if covered with pink snow. In those days the shutters and the doors and the outside staircase were painted a beautiful green, the walls were resplendent with fresh whitewash every year. In those days too the wild vine turned to a brilliant crimson in the autumn, and in June the climbing rose was just a mass of bloom. Then in May the nightingale often sang in the old walnut-tree, and later on, when Fleurette was tall enough, she always kept a bunch of forget-me-nots in a glass, in the recess above the front door, at the foot of the statue of St. Antoine de Padoue, because, as is well known, he is the saint to appeal to in case one has lost anything one values. One just made the sign of the cross and said fervently: “*St. Antoine de Padoue priez pour nous!*” and lo! the kindly saint would aid in the search and more often than not the lost treasure would be found.

All this was, of course, anterior to the horrible events which in a few days transformed the genial, kindly people of France into a herd of wild beasts thirsting for each other’s blood, and before legalized cruelty, murder and regicide had arraigned that fair land at the bar of history, and tarnished her fair fame forever. Fleurette was just eighteen when the terrible events came to pass that threatened to wreck her young life, and through which she learned not only how cruel and evil man could be, but also to what height of self-abnegation and heroism they could at times ascend.
===Chinese===
等到底片冲洗出来、相片拿到眼前，我们却再也捕捉不回那种如画往昔的意味了，只纳闷自己为何把一卷宝贵的胶卷浪费在这么一座破破烂烂的老屋上，纳闷为什么任凭这么多破烂老屋坍塌倾颓，糟蹋了法兰西美丽的容颜。可是一个半世纪以前，弗勒蕾特出生的时候，前门旁边立着一株杏树，早春时节满树繁花，恰似盖了一层粉红的雪。那时候，窗板、门扇和屋外的楼梯都漆着悦目的绿色，墙壁年年新刷石灰水，焕然生光。那时候，野葡萄藤一到秋天便转为一片艳丽的绯红，六月的攀墙蔷薇开成满满一团花海。到了五月，夜莺常在老胡桃树上歌唱；再往后，弗勒蕾特长高了些，她总在门上方的壁龛里、帕多瓦圣安东尼像的脚边，用一只玻璃瓶供上一束勿忘我——因为众所周知，谁要是丢了心爱的物件，求这位圣人最是灵验。只要画个十字，虔诚地念一句：「*St. Antoine de Padoue priez pour nous!*」（帕多瓦的圣安东尼，为我们祈祷！）瞧吧！好心的圣人便会出手相助，丢失的宝贝十有八九都能失而复得。

当然，这一切都在那场可怕的浩劫之前——不过几日光景，那浩劫便把法兰西性情温厚、心地善良的人们变成了一群彼此渴血的野兽；也早在合法化的残虐、谋杀与弑君，把那片美好的国土押上历史的审判席、永远玷污了她的美名之前。恐怖的变故临头时，弗勒蕾特刚满十八岁；这些变故险些葬送她的青春年华，却也叫她不仅认清了人心可以何等残忍、何等邪恶，更懂得了人有时竟能升华到何等崇高的自我牺牲与英雄主义的高度。

===Original===
Fleurette’s birthday was in May, and that day was always for her the gladdest day of the year. For one thing she could reckon on Bibi being home﻿—Bibi being the name by which she had called her father ever since she had learned to babble. Fleurette had no mother, and she and Bibi just worshipped each other. And of course Bibi had come home for her eighteenth birthday, and had stayed three whole days, and he had brought her a lovely shawl, one that was so soft and fleecy that when you rubbed your cheek against it, it felt just like a caress from a butterfly’s wing.

Old Louise﻿—who had looked after the house and watched over Fleurette ever since Fleurette’s mother had gone up to heaven to be with the *bon Dieu* and all the Saints﻿—old Louise had cooked a delicious dinner, which was a very difficult thing to do these days when food was scarce and dear, and eggs, butter and sugar only for the very rich who could bribe M’sieu’ Colombe, the *épicier* of the Rue Haute, to let them have what they wanted. But no matter! Old Louise was a veritable genius where a dinner was concerned, and M’sieu’ Colombe, the grocer, and M’sieu’ Duflos the butcher, had allowed her to have all she asked for: a luscious piece of meat, three eggs, a piece of butter, and this without any extra bribe. Then there were still half a dozen bottles of that excellent red wine which Bibi had bought in the happy olden days; and he had opened one of the bottles, and Fleurette had drunk some wine and felt very elated and altogether happy﻿—but for this there was another reason of which more anon.

Of course the latter part of the day had been tinged with sadness, again for that one reason which will appear presently: but not only because of that, but because of Bibi’s departure, which, it seems, could not be postponed, although Fleurette begged and begged that he should remain at least until tomorrow so as not to spoil this most perfect day. *Le bon Dieu* alone knew when Fleurette would see Bibi again, his absences from home had of late become more frequent and more prolonged.
===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特的生日在五月，这一天在她向来是一年中最快活的日子。一来，这一天比比准会在家——比比，是她打从咿呀学语时起对父亲的称呼。弗勒蕾特没有母亲，她和比比两个人，简直把彼此疼到了心尖上。当然喽，比比赶回来给她过了十八岁的生日，一住住了整整三天，还给她捎来一条漂亮的披肩——那披肩那样柔软、那样绒暖，拿脸颊贴上去蹭一蹭，感觉就像蝴蝶的翅膀轻轻拂过。

老路易丝——打从弗勒蕾特的母亲升天去陪伴好天主（*bon Dieu*）和众位圣人起，便一直照管着这个家、看护着弗勒蕾特——老路易丝做了一顿可口的晚餐。这在眼下可不是件容易事：如今吃食又缺又贵，鸡蛋、黄油和糖，只有阔人才弄得到——他们出得起钱，哄得上街（Rue Haute）的杂货商（*épicier*）科隆布先生把货卖给他们。不过这不要紧！说到置办一顿晚餐，老路易丝是个不折不扣的天才，杂货商科隆布先生和肉铺老板迪弗洛（Duflos）先生都依了她，要什么给什么：一块鲜美的肉、三只鸡蛋、一块黄油，而且没多收一文钱的贿赂。家里还剩着半打上好的红葡萄酒，是比比在往日的好时光里置下的；他开了一瓶，弗勒蕾特也喝了些酒，只觉得心花怒放、快活到了极处——不过这里头另有一层缘故，且容后文细表。

当然，这一天的后半天也蒙上了一层愁云，还是因为那层缘故——那缘故眼下就要说到了；但也不仅为此，还因为比比就要动身离去，而这似乎是无法挽回的事，尽管弗勒蕾特一再央求，求他至少住到明天，免得破坏这最圆满的一天。只有好天主才知道弗勒蕾特何时才能再见到比比；近来他离家越来越频繁，一去也越来越久。

===Original===
*Mais voilà!* on one’s eighteenth birthday one is not going to think of troubles until the very last minute when it is actually on the doorstep. And the day had been entirely glorious. Not a cloud: the sky of such a vivid blue that the forget-me-nots that grew in such profusion beside the stream looked pale and colourless beneath it. The crimson peonies behind the house were in full bloom, and the buds of the climbing rose on the point of bursting.

And now dinner was over. Louise was busy in the kitchen washing up the plates and dishes, and Fleurette was carefully putting away the beautiful silver forks and spoons which had been brought out for the occasion. She was putting them away in the fine leather case with the molleton lining, which set off the glistening silver to perfection, and little Fleurette felt happy and very contented. She worked away in silence because Bibi had leaned his darling old head against the back of his chair, and closed his eyes. Fleurette thought that he had dropped to sleep.

He looked thin and pale, the poor dear, and there were lines of anxiety and discontent around his thin lips: his hair too had of late been plentifully sprinkled with gray. Oh! how Fleurette longed to have him here at Lou Mas. Always and always. It was the only home she had ever known; dear, beautiful, fragrant Lou Mas. Here she would tend him and care for him until all those lines of care upon his face had vanished. And what more likely to bring a smile to his lips than dear old Lou Mas with its whitewashed walls and red-tiled roofs, with its green shutters and little mill stream beside which, for nine months in the year, flowers grew in such profusion; violets, forget-me-nots, and lilies of the valley in the spring, and meadowsweet throughout the summer until an early frost cut them down?
===Chinese===
*Mais voilà!*（可是有什么办法！）人到了十八岁生日这一天，是断不肯去想烦恼的，非等到烦恼当真找上门、堵在门口的最后一刻不可。而这一个日子，从头到尾都灿烂极了。天上没有一丝云；天空蓝得那样鲜明，以致溪畔开得那样繁茂的勿忘我，在它底下竟显得苍白失色。屋后的猩红芍药开得正盛，攀墙的蔷薇花苞涨得鼓鼓的，眼看就要绽开。

这时晚饭吃完了。路易丝在厨房里忙着洗刷盘碟，弗勒蕾特正小心地把专为这个日子取出来的漂亮银叉银匙一一收拾起来。她把它们收进那只内衬厚绒布的精致皮盒里，厚绒布把亮闪闪的银器衬托得恰到好处；小弗勒蕾特心里觉得又幸福又满足。她默默做着活计，因为比比已把他那可爱的老脑袋靠在椅背上，阖上了眼睛。弗勒蕾特以为他睡着了。

他显得又瘦又苍白，这可怜的亲爱的人，薄薄的嘴唇四周刻着忧虑与不满的细纹；他的头发近来也添了不少灰白。啊！弗勒蕾特多么渴望他能留在庐庄（Lou Mas）——永远，永远地留下来。这是她此生所知的唯一的家；亲爱的、美丽的、芬芳的庐庄。在这里，她会侍奉他、照料他，直到他脸上那些忧虑的皱纹全都消散。还有什么比亲爱的老庐庄更容易让他展颜一笑的呢——刷白的墙，红瓦的屋顶，绿色的窗板，还有那条小磨坊溪，溪旁一年有九个月繁花不断：春天是紫罗兰、勿忘我和铃兰，夏天一路开着绣线菊，直到早霜把它们摧折。

===Original===
As for this room, Fleurette knew that there could not be in the whole of France, anything more beautiful or more cosy. There was the beautiful walnut sideboard, polished until it shone like a mirror, there were the chairs covered in crimson rep, rather faded it is true, but none the worse for that, and there was Bibi’s special armchair adorned with that strip of tapestry which Fleurette had worked in cross-stitch, expressly for his birthday the year of her first communion. Never had there been such chairs anywhere. And that beautiful paper on the walls, the red and yellow roses that looked as if you could pick them off their lovely chocolate-coloured ground, and the chandelier with the crystal drops, and the blue vases with the gold handles that adorned the mantelpiece, not to mention the print curtains and the pink and blue check cloth upon the table. Oh, Fleurette loved all these things, they had been the playthings of her childhood and now they were her pride. If only Bibi would smile again, she felt that the whole world would be like heaven.

And then all at once everything went wrong. Fleurette had got her beautiful new shawl out of its wrappings and draped it round her shoulders and rubbed her cheek against it. Then she had said quite innocently: “It is so lovely, Bibi, and the wool is so soft and fine. I am sure that it came from England.” And it was from that moment that everything went wrong. To begin with, and quite by accident, of course, Bibi broke the stem of the glass out of which he had been drinking, and a quantity of very precious wine was spilt over the beautiful tablecloth.

Whereupon, unaccountably, because of course the table cloth could be washed, Bibi pushed his plate aside quite roughly and suddenly looked ten years older; so wan and pale and shrivelled and old. Fleurette longed to put her arms round him﻿—as she used to do in the happy olden days﻿—and ask him to tell her what was amiss. She was grown up now﻿—eighteen years old today﻿—quite old enough to understand. And if Bibi loved her as she thought he did, he would be comforted.

But there it was! There was something in the expression on Bibi’s face that checked Fleurette’s impulse. She went on quietly﻿—very quietly, like a little mouse﻿—with her work, and for a while there was silence in the cosy room with the beautiful roses on the wall that looked as if you could pick them off their chocolate ground: a silence that was unaccountably full of sadness.
===Chinese===
至于这间屋子，弗勒蕾特知道，整个法兰西再没有比它更漂亮、更温馨的了。瞧那口漂亮的胡桃木餐具柜，擦得像镜子一样亮；瞧那些蒙着猩红棱纹布的椅子，颜色虽说褪了些，却丝毫无损它的好处；还有比比专用的那把扶手椅，椅背上搭着一条挂毯，是弗勒蕾特用十字绣一针一线绣出来、专为他初领圣体那一年的生日绣的。天底下哪儿也没有这么好的椅子。还有墙上糊的那漂亮壁纸，红玫瑰黄玫瑰开在可爱的巧克力色底子上，鲜活得仿佛一伸手就能摘下来；还有那盏缀着水晶坠的吊灯，壁炉台上摆着的带金柄的蓝花瓶，更不必说印花布窗帘和桌上粉蓝格子的桌布了。啊，弗勒蕾特爱这一切；它们是她童年的玩物，如今则是她的骄傲。只要比比能再展笑颜，她觉得整个世界都会像天堂一样。

忽然间，一切都出了岔子。弗勒蕾特把那条漂亮的新披肩从包装里取出来，披在肩上，用脸颊贴着蹭了蹭。然后她天真无邪地说：「真漂亮，比比，这毛线又软又细。我敢说，这是英国来的。」恰恰就是从这一刻起，一切都出了岔子。先是比比一不小心——当然纯属无意——碰断了手中酒杯的杯脚，一些非常珍贵的酒洒了出来，弄脏了漂亮的桌布。

于是——莫名其妙地，因为桌布毕竟洗得干净——比比粗鲁地把盘子往旁边一推，人陡然间苍老了十岁，那样憔悴、灰白、干瘪、苍老。弗勒蕾特真想张开双臂搂住他——像幸福的往日里她常做的那样——求他告诉她到底出了什么事。她如今长大了——今天满十八岁——完全到了能懂的年纪。倘若比比爱她，像她所深信的那样爱她，他一定会得到安慰的。

可是不行！比比脸上的神情里有种东西，止住了弗勒蕾特的冲动。她安安静静地——安静得像只小老鼠——继续做她的活计；一时间，这间温馨的屋子里静悄悄的，墙上那些巧克力色底子的玫瑰鲜活得仿佛伸手可摘，而这寂静里，竟莫名地充满了悲伤。

## IV / 第四章

===Original===
Bibi was the first to hear the sound of footsteps coming up to the door. He gave a start, just as if he were waking from a dream.

“It’s M’sieu’ Colombe,” Fleurette said.

At once Bibi reproved her, a thing he hardly ever did: “Citizen Colombe,” he said sternly.

Fleurette shrugged her plump shoulders: “Ah well﻿—!” she exclaimed.

“You must learn, Fleurette,” Bibi insisted still with unwonted severity. “You are old enough to learn.”

She said nothing more; only kissed the top of his head, the smooth brown hair, of late so plentifully tinged with grey, and promised that she would learn. She stood by the sideboard intent on putting the silver away, with her back turned to Bibi so that he should not see the soft tone of pink that had crept into her cheeks, as soon as she had perceived that two pairs of feet were treading the path outside the door.
===Chinese===
比比头一个听见小径上传来走向门口的脚步声。他猛地一惊，就像从梦中醒来似的。

「是科隆布先生。」弗勒蕾特说。

比比立刻纠正她——这可是他几乎从来不做的事：「是公民科隆布。」他严厉地说。

弗勒蕾特耸了耸丰满的肩膀：「嗐，好吧——！」她叫道。

「你得学着点儿，弗勒蕾特，」比比仍旧带着少有的严厉坚持道，「你到了该学的年纪了。」

她没再说什么，只吻了吻他的头顶——那头柔滑的棕色头发，近来已添了不少灰白——答应说自己一定学。她站在餐具柜旁，一心一意地收拾银餐具，背朝着比比，好叫他瞧不见悄悄爬上她双颊的那抹淡红——打她一察觉门外小径上踏着两双脚，那红晕便不知不觉地浮了上来。

===Original===
Now there was a vigorous knock against the door, and a cheery, raucous voice called out loudly: “May one enter?”

Fleurette ran to the door and opened it.

“But certainly, certainly,” she said, and then added, seemingly very astonished: “Ah! and M’sieu’ Amédé too?” From which the casual observer would perhaps infer that the pink colour in her cheeks had been due to the arrival of M’sieu’ Colombe, the *épicier* of the Rue Haute, rather than to that of his son Amédé. It was no doubt also the worthy *épicier* with his round florid face, dark, twinkling eyes, and general air of ferocious kindliness that caused the pink colour to spread from Fleurette’s cheeks down to her neck and the little bit of throat that peeped out above her kerchief.

The good Colombe had already stalked into the room and with a familiar: “*Eh bien! Eh bien!* We did contrive to come and drink Fleurette’s health after all?” had slapped Bibi vigorously over the lean shoulders. But Amédé had come to a halt on the mat in which he was mechanically wiping his boots as if his very life depended on their cleanliness. Between his fingers he was twirling an immense posy of bright pink peonies, but his eyes were fixed on Fleurette, and on his broad, plain face, which shone with perspiration and good temper, there was a half-shy, wholly adoring look.
===Chinese===
这时，门上响起一阵用力的敲击，一个欢快而沙哑的大嗓门喊道：「可以进来吗？」

弗勒蕾特跑过去开了门。

「当然，当然可以。」她说，接着又添了一句，像是十分惊讶：「啊！阿梅代先生也一块儿来啦？」随便哪个旁观的闲人听了这话，多半会推断说，她颊上的红晕是为着上街（Rue Haute）的杂货商（*épicier*）科隆布先生的到来，而不是为着他的儿子阿梅代。毫无疑问，也正是这位可敬的杂货商——圆圆的红脸盘、乌黑闪亮的眼睛、一副凶巴巴却又和善可亲的气概——才叫那抹粉红从弗勒蕾特的双颊一路漫到脖颈，漫到围巾上缘露出的一小截喉咙。

好心的科隆布已经大步跨进屋来，照着老交情嚷了一句：「*Eh bien! Eh bien!*（好啊！好啊！）我们到底还是想法子来了，要喝上一杯，祝弗勒蕾特无病无灾，对不对？」说着便朝比比瘦削的肩膀重重拍了一记。阿梅代却在门口的蹭鞋垫上收住了脚，机械地蹭着靴子，仿佛他的性命全悬在靴子干净不干净上。他指间捻着一大束鲜粉色的芍药，眼睛却直望着弗勒蕾特；那张宽大憨厚的脸盘汗涔涔、乐呵呵地放着光，神情半是腼腆，满是痴恋。

===Original===
He gulped hard once or twice before he murmured, hoarse with emotion:

“Mam’zelle Fleurette!”

And Fleurette wiped her hot little hand against her apron before she whispered in shy response:

“M’sieu’ Amédé!”

Not for these two the new fangled “*citoyen*” and “*citoyenne*” decreed in far-off Paris. To their unsophisticated ears the clamour of a trumpet-tongued revolution only came as an unreal and distant echo.

Amédé appeared to have finished cleaning his boots, and Fleurette was able to close the door behind him before she held out her hand for the flowers which he was too bewildered to offer.

“Are these beautiful flowers for me, M’sieu’ Amédé?” she asked.

“If you will deign to accept them, Mam’zelle Fleurette,” he replied.
===Chinese===
他使劲咽了一两回口水，才用激动得发哑的嗓音喃喃说道：

「弗勒蕾特小姐！」

弗勒蕾特也先把发热的小手在围裙上抹了抹，才羞答答地低声应道：

「阿梅代先生！」

这两个人可不用巴黎远在天边新颁下来的那套时兴称呼——「公民」（*citoyen*）和「女公民」（*citoyenne*）。在他们质朴无华的耳朵里，那场号角高唱的革命喧腾，传到此处不过是一阵虚幻而遥远的回声。

阿梅代似乎总算把靴子蹭干净了，弗勒蕾特这才得以在他身后关上门，然后伸手去接那束花——他早慌得晕头转向，竟没想到要把花递过去。

「这束漂亮的花，是送给我的吗，阿梅代先生？」她问。

「只要您肯赏脸收下，弗勒蕾特小姐。」他答道。

===Original===
She was eighteen and he was just twenty. Neither of them had ever been away more than a few hours from their remote little village of Dauphiné where they were born﻿—she in the little house with the green shutters, and he in the Rue Haute above the shop where his father Hector Colombe had sold tallow-candles and sugar, flour and salt, and lard and eggs to the neighbours, ever since he had been old enough to help *his* father in the business. And when Amédé was four, and Fleurette two, they had made mud pies together in the village street with water from the fountain, and Amédé had warded Fleurette against the many powerful enemies that sometimes threatened her and caused her to scream with terror, such as M’sieu’ Duflos, the butcher’s, dog, or Achille the *garde-champêtre* with his ferocious scowl, or M’ame Amèlie’s geese.

They had sat together﻿—not side by side you understand, but the boys on the right of the room and the girls on the left﻿—in the little classroom in the presbytery, where M. le Curé taught them their alphabet and subsequently the catechism; and also that two and two make four. They had knelt side by side in the little primitive church at Laragne, their little souls overburdened with emotion and religious fervour, when they made their first communion: Fleurette in a beautiful white dress, with a wreath of white roses on her fair hair, and a long tulle veil that descended right down to her feet; and Amédé in an exquisite cloth coat with brass buttons, a silk waistcoat, buckled shoes and a white ribbon sash on his left arm.

And when Amédé had been old enough to be entrusted with his father’s errands over at Serres, a couple of leagues away, Fleurette had climbed behind him on the saddle, and with her arms round his waist, so as to keep herself steady, they had ridden together along the winding road white with dust, Ginette, the good old mare, ambling very leisurely as if she knew that her riders were in no hurry to get anywhere that day.

And now Fleurette was eighteen and Amédé twenty and her hair was like ripe corn, and her eyes as blue as the sky on a midsummer morn, whilst her mouth was dewy and fragrant as a rose in June. No wonder that poor Amédé felt as if his feet were of lead and his neck too big for his cravat, and when presently she asked him to fill a vase with water out of the carafe so that she could place the beautiful flowers in it, is it a wonder that he spilt the water all over the floor, seeing that his clumsy hands met her dainty fingers around the neck of the carafe?
===Chinese===
她十八岁，他刚满二十。两人都生在多菲内这处偏僻的小村子，一辈子不曾离开过几个钟头——她生在那座绿窗板的小房子里，他生在上街铺子的楼上。他父亲埃克托·科隆布在那间铺子里向街坊四邻卖油脂蜡烛和糖，卖面粉和盐，卖猪油和鸡蛋，打从他自己长到能帮*他*父亲做买卖的年纪起，就一直卖了下来。阿梅代四岁、弗勒蕾特两岁的时候，两个孩子在村街上用喷泉的水和泥捏过泥饼；每逢有什么来势汹汹的对头威胁弗勒蕾特、吓得她尖叫——比如肉铺迪弗洛先生家的狗，比如凶神恶煞瞪着眼刀的乡警（*garde-champêtre*）阿希尔（Achille），再比如阿梅莉大娘（M'ame Amèlie）家的鹅——出面护住她的，总是阿梅代。

他们也曾一同坐进本堂神父宅里的小课堂——您可别误会，不是并排坐：男孩子坐屋子右边，女孩子坐左边——本堂神父（M. le Curé）在那儿教他们认字母，后来教教理问答，还教二加二等于四。他们也曾在拉拉涅那座质朴的小教堂里并肩跪着，小小心灵满载着激动与宗教的虔敬，行了初领圣体：弗勒蕾特穿一身漂亮的白裙，淡金的头发上戴一顶白玫瑰花冠，长长的薄纱面纱一直垂到脚边；阿梅代穿一件精致的呢料上装，缀着黄铜纽扣，配丝绸马甲、带扣的鞋，左臂上系一条白缎绶带。

等阿梅代长到能放心替父亲跑腿、去几里格外的塞尔办事了，弗勒蕾特便爬上马鞍坐到他身后，双臂搂住他的腰好坐得稳当；两人就这样一同沿着落满白尘、弯弯曲曲的道路骑马走去，那匹好性子的老母马吉内特（Ginette）慢悠悠地遛着碎步，仿佛知道背上这两个人那天去哪儿都犯不着着急。

如今弗勒蕾特十八岁，阿梅代二十岁。她的头发像熟透的麦穗，眼睛蓝得像仲夏清晨的天空，嘴唇则像六月的玫瑰，带着露水，含着芬芳。可怜的阿梅代只觉得自己两脚灌了铅，脖子也粗得领巾都箍不住，这又何足为怪？待她开口请他从玻璃水瓶里倒些水把花瓶灌满，好安放那束漂亮的花，他那双笨手在瓶口边碰上她那双纤纤玉指，把水泼了满地——这难道还算稀奇吗？

===Original===
The good Hector pretended to be very angry with his son for his clumsiness.

“*Voyez-moi cet imbécile!*” he said with that gruff voice of his which had become a habit with him, because he had to use it all day in order to ward off the naughty village urchins who tried to steal the apples out of his shop.

“Mam’zelle Fleurette, why don’t you box his ears?”

Which, of course, was a very funny proposition that caused Fleurette and Amédé to laugh immoderately first and then to whisper and to chaff whilst they mopped the water off the tiled floor. And the good Hector turned once more to Bibi, and shaking his powerful fist at nothing in particular, he brought it down with a crash upon the table.

“And now those *gredins*, those limbs of Satan are taking him away for cannon-fodder. Ah! the devils! the pigs! the pig-devils!”
===Chinese===
好心的埃克托见儿子这般笨手笨脚，便装出十分恼火的样子。

「*Voyez-moi cet imbécile!*（瞧瞧这个大傻瓜！）」他说这话用的还是那副粗喉咙——这嗓门早已成了他的老习惯，因为他得整天用它，好吓退那些想从他铺子里偷苹果的顽皮村童。

「弗勒蕾特小姐，你怎么不刮他两个耳光？」

这个提议自然妙不可言，逗得弗勒蕾特和阿梅代先是笑了个前仰后合，随后又一面擦着瓷砖地上的水，一面交头接耳、打趣逗乐。好心的埃克托则又转回来同比比说话，挥起那只粗壮的拳头，冲着不知什么虚空晃了一晃，砰的一声砸在桌上。

「如今那些无赖（*gredins*）、那些撒旦的爪牙，要把我这儿子抓去当炮灰了。啊！这帮魔鬼！这帮猪猡！这帮猪猡加魔鬼！」

===Original===
Bibi looked up inquiringly.

“Taking him away, are they?” he asked dryly. Then he added with an indifferent shrug of the shoulders: “Amédé is twenty, isn’t he?”

“What’s that to do with their dragging him away from me, when I want him to help in the shop?” Hector retorted with what he felt was unanswerable logic.

“What would be the good of keeping shop, my good Hector,” Bibi rejoined simply, “if France was invaded by foreigners as she is already ruined by traitors?”

“Well! And isn’t she ruined now by all those devils up in Paris who can think of nothing better than war or murder?” growled Hector Colombe, heedless of the quick gesture of warning which Bibi had given him.

Adèle, the girl from the village who gave old Louise a hand about the house when Bibi was at home, had just come in from the kitchen with a pile of plates and dishes which she proceeded to range upon the dresser. Hector shrugged his big shoulders. Whoever would think of taking notice of Adèle? A wench who got five sous a day for scrubbing floors! An undersized, plain-faced creature with flat feet and red elbows. Bah!
===Chinese===
比比抬眼，探询地望过去。

「要抓他走，是吗？」他淡淡地问。随后他漠然耸了耸肩，又补了一句：「阿梅代满二十了，不是吗？」

「他满不满二十，同这件事有什么相干？我正要他帮着照看铺子，他们倒要把我儿子从我身边硬拖走！」埃克托反问道，自觉这番道理无可辩驳。

「我的好埃克托，」比比淡淡地答道，「法兰西既已让卖国贼毁成了这样，倘若再教外国人打了进来，那时候守着铺子还有什么用处呢？」

「哼！她如今难道不正是叫巴黎那帮魔鬼毁着的吗？那起子家伙除了打仗就是杀人，还想得出什么好主意！」埃克托·科隆布咆哮道，全没留意比比朝他飞快做出的警告手势。

阿黛尔——村里那个姑娘，比比在家时就过来帮老路易丝料理家务——刚从厨房进来，捧着一大摞盘碟，正往碗柜上摆。埃克托耸了耸他的宽肩膀。谁会想到提防阿黛尔呢？一个擦地板一天挣五个苏的丫头！矮墩墩的个子，扁塌塌的脸，扁平足，胳膊肘红通通。呸！

===Original===
But Bibi still put up a warning finger:

“Little pitchers have long ears,” he said in a whisper.

“Oh! I know, I know,” Hector rejoined gruffly. “It is the fashion these days for us all to spy on one another. A pretty pass they have brought us to,” he added, “your friends in Paris.”

To this Bibi made no reply. No doubt he knew that it was impossible to argue with Hector, once the worthy *épicier* was in one of his moods. Adèle had finished her task and glided out of the room, silent, noiseless, furtive as a little rat, which she vaguely resembled with small, keen eyes, and pointed nose and chin. In a corner of the room, by the window, still busy with those flowers which seemingly would not set primly in the vase, Fleurette and Amédé were talking under their breath.
===Chinese===
可是比比仍旧竖起一根指头，作出警告：

「小孩子家耳朵尖。」他悄声道。

「哦！我知道，我知道。」埃克托粗声粗气地应道。「如今兴的就是这个：人人互相窥探。他们把咱们作践到了这步田地，」他又添了一句，「你巴黎的那帮朋友。」

对这话比比没有作声。无疑他知道，这位可敬的杂货商一旦上了性子，同他是讲不进道理的。阿黛尔做完了活计，溜出屋去，无声无息、鬼鬼祟祟，活脱脱一只小老鼠——她那小小的锐眼、尖尖的鼻子和下巴，也确有几分像。屋角窗边，弗勒蕾特还在摆弄那束怎么也插不齐整的花，同阿梅代压着嗓子说话。

===Original===
“I’m going away, Mam’zelle Fleurette,” said he.

“Going away, M’sieu’ Amédé? Whither? When?”

“They want me in the army.”

“What for?” she asked naively.

“To fight against the English.”

“But you won’t go, will you, M’sieu’ Amédé?”

“I must, Mam’zelle Fleurette.”

“Oh, but what shall I﻿—I mean what will M’sieu’ Colombe do? You must remain here, to help him in the shop.” And fight against it as she would, there was an uncomfortable little lump in her throat when she pictured how terribly lost M’sieu’ Colombe would be without his son.
===Chinese===
「我要走了，弗勒蕾特小姐。」他说。

「要走，阿梅代先生？上哪儿？什么时候？」

「他们要我去当兵。」

「去做什么呀？」她天真地问。

「去同英国人打仗。」

「可你不会去的，对吧，阿梅代先生？」

「不去不行啊，弗勒蕾特小姐。」

「啊，可叫我怎么办——我是说，科隆布先生可怎么办？你该待在这儿，帮着他照看铺子呀。」她尽管拼命忍着，可一想到科隆布先生没了儿子会多么没了主意，喉咙里便哽上一团难受的小疙瘩。

===Original===
“Father is very angry,” Amédé said rather hoarsely, because he too had an uncomfortable lump in his throat now. “But it seems there’s nothing to be done. I have to go.”

“When?” Fleurette murmured, so softly, so softly, that only a lover’s ears could possibly have caught the whisper.

“I have to present myself tomorrow,” Amédé replied, “before M’sieu’ le Commissaire de police at Serres.”

“Tomorrow? And I have been so happy today!”

The cry came from an overburdened little heart, brought face to face with its first sorrow. Fleurette no longer attempted to keep back her tears, and Amédé, not quite sure whether he should cry because he was going away, or dance with joy because it was his going that was making Fleurette cry, put in time by wiping his face which was streaming with perspiration and tears.

“I wish I could at least have seen those children wedded,” the worthy *épicier* muttered in the interval of blowing his nose with a noise like a cloudburst. “At least,” he added with the good round oath which he reserved for occasions such as these, “before they take my Amédé away.”

Bibi on the other hand appeared to be more philosophical.

“We must wait for better times,” he said, “and anyhow Fleurette is too young to marry.”
===Chinese===
「父亲气得很。」阿梅代说话也有些发哑，因为此刻他喉咙里同样哽着一团疙瘩。「可是看样子没有办法，我非去不可。」

「什么时候？」弗勒蕾特轻声问，那声音轻啊、轻啊，怕只有情人的耳朵才捕捉得到这一声细语。

「我明天就得报到去，」阿梅代答道，「到塞尔的警察专员先生（M'sieu' le Commissaire de police）那里。」

「明天？可我今天一直这么快活！」

这一声喊，出自一颗不堪重负的小小心灵——它头一回面对面撞上了自己的哀伤。弗勒蕾特再不打算忍住眼泪；阿梅代呢，一时拿不准自己究竟该哭——因为自己就要走了——还是该欢喜得跳起舞来——因为恰恰是自己要走，才惹得弗勒蕾特落泪——便只管一面等着，一面抹脸，脸上汗水泪水早已淌成一片。

「我原想着，哪怕亲眼看着这两个孩子成了亲也好啊。」这位可敬的杂货商咕哝道，一边擤着鼻涕，那动静赛过一场瓢泼骤雨。「至少，」他又带着那句专为这种场合备下的响亮粗话补上一句，「也得赶在他们把我阿梅代抓走之前啊。」

比比呢，倒显得达观些。

「我们只好等时局好转，」他说，「再说弗勒蕾特年纪还小，嫁人还早。」

## V / 第五章

===Original===
Parting is not such sweet sorrow as the greatest of all poets would have us believe. At any rate Fleurette did not find it at all sweet, on this her eighteenth birthday, which should have been a very happy one.

===Chinese===
离别并不像所有诗人中最伟大的那位要我们相信的那样，是那般甜蜜的凄楚。至少，在这个本该快活非常的十八岁生日上，弗勒蕾特丝毫不觉得它有什么甜蜜。

===Original===
It was bad enough saying “adieu” to Bibi. But Fleurette was accustomed to that. Of late Bibi had been so often and so long absent from home; sometimes weeks﻿—nay! months﻿—would elapse and there would be no Bibi to fondle Fleurette and bring life and animation within those whitewashed walls that held all that was dearest to her in the world. It was undoubtedly heartrending to bid Bibi adieu: but in a way, one knew that the darling would come back to Lou Mas as soon as he was able, come for one of those surprise visits that made Fleurette as gay as a linnet all the while they lasted. But to say goodbye to Amédé was a different matter. He was going into the army. He was going to fight the English. *Le Bon Dieu* alone knew if Amédé would ever come back. Perhaps he would be killed. Perhaps﻿—oh! perhaps﻿—

===Chinese===
同比比道「别」已经够难受的了。可弗勒蕾特对这已经习惯。近来比比离家又勤、又久；有时一去几个星期——不！几个月——都不见比比回来疼爱弗勒蕾特，回来给那几面刷白的墙壁添几分生气与热闹——那墙里盛着的，是她在这世上最珍爱的一切。同比比道别无疑令人心碎：可总归知道，那亲爱的人一得空就会回到庐庄来，来上一趟出其不意的探望，让弗勒蕾特在那些日子里快活得像只红雀。可同阿梅代道别，就是另一回事了。他要去从军。他要去打英国人。只有好天主（*le Bon Dieu*）才知道阿梅代还能不能回来。也许他会阵亡。也许——啊！也许——

===Original===
Never in her life had Fleurette been so sad.

===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特生平从来没有这么伤心过。

===Original===
And now the last goodbyes had been said. Bibi, accompanied by M’sieu’ Colombe and Amédé, had walked away in the direction of the village, where he would pick up his horse, and start along the main road that led to Serres and thence to Paris.

===Chinese===
这时，最后的道别已经说完。比比由科隆布先生和阿梅代陪着，朝村子那边走去了；他要在村里牵回自己的马，然后沿大路先到塞尔，再从那里去巴黎。

===Original===
Fleurette remained on the bridge for some time, shading her eyes against the sun, because they ached so from all the tears which she had shed. The three men had become mere specks, ’way down the road: old Louise had gone back to her kitchen with Adèle, only Fleurette remained standing on the bridge alone. Tears were still running down her cheeks, whilst with aching eyes she strove to catch a last sight of Bibi as he and his two companions disappeared round the bend of the road. Or was it Amédé she was trying to see?

===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特还在桥上站了好一会儿，抬手遮着阳光——她哭了这半日，眼睛酸疼得很。三个人影已经缩成大路尽头的小点；老路易丝带着阿黛尔回厨房去了，只剩弗勒蕾特独自站在桥上。泪水还顺着她的脸颊往下淌；她忍着眼睛的酸疼，想再多看比比一眼，看他和两个同伴转过道路的弯处——或者说，她想再看的，是阿梅代？

===Original===
The afternoon sun had spread a mantle of gold over the snowy crests of Pelvoux: on the sapphire sky myriads of tiny clouds seemed to hold hearts of living flame in their fleecy bosoms. The wavy ribbon of the Buëche was like a giant mirror that reflected a whole gamut of glowing tints, blue and gold, and purple, whilst on the winding road, the infinitesimal atoms of dust seemed like low-lying clouds of powdered topaz. Suddenly in the direction of Sisteron those clouds rose, more dense: something more solid than powdered topaz, animated the distance: grew gradually more tangible and then became definite. Fleurette now could easily distinguish ten or a dozen men coming this way. They all wore red caps on their heads. Ahead of them came a man on horseback. He wore a tricorne hat, adorned with a tricolour cockade, and the sun drew sparks of flame from the steel bit in his horse’s mouth and from the brass bosses and buckles on the harness.

===Chinese===
午后的太阳给佩尔武（Pelvoux）积雪的峰顶披上了一袭金氅；宝蓝色的天上，千千万万朵小小的云，绒絮般的怀抱里仿佛都揣着一颗活火焰的心。比埃什河（Buëche）蜿蜒的缎带宛如一面巨镜，映出蓝、金、紫一整幅灼灼流彩；而曲折的大路上，细不可察的尘粒，宛如低低铺开的一层层黄玉粉雾。忽然，锡斯特龙那边的尘雾腾了起来，越来越浓：某种比黄玉粉更结实的物事在远处动了起来，渐渐显得真切，终于轮廓分明。弗勒蕾特这时轻而易举就能分辨出：有十来个人正朝这边走来，头上都戴着红帽子。他们前头是一个骑马的男人，头戴缀着三色帽徽的三角帽；阳光从马嘴上的钢衔铁、从辔具上的铜泡和铜扣上，擦出一簇簇火星。

===Original===
Now Fleurette could hear the dull stamping of hoofs on the dusty road, and the tramping of heavy, weary footsteps: and she watched, fascinated, these men coming along.

===Chinese===
这时弗勒蕾特听得见了：尘土路上马蹄沉闷的踏响，还有沉重疲惫的脚步声；她看得入了迷，望着这些人一路走来。

===Original===
All at once the rider put his horse to a trot, and the next moment he reined in on the bridge. He put out his hand and cried a sharp: “*Halte!*” whereupon the other men all came to a halt. Fleurette stood there wondering what all this meant. Vaguely she guessed that these men must be soldiers, though, of a truth, with the exception of the one on horseback, and who appeared to be their officer, there was very little that was soldierly about them. Their red caps were of worsted, and adorned with what had once been a tricolour cockade, but was now so covered with dust that the colours were well-nigh distinguishable. The men’s coats too, once blue in colour and fitted with brass buttons, were torn and faded, with several buttons missing: their breeches were stained with mud, they had no stockings inside their shoes, and it would have been impossible to say definitely whether their shirts had been of a drabby grey when they were new, or whether they had become so under stress of wear and dirt. Fleurette’s recollection flew back to the smart soldiers she used to see when she was a tiny tot and Bibi took her to Serres or Sisteron on fête days when the military band would march past in their beautiful clothes all glittering with brass buttons, and their boots polished up so that you could almost see yourself in them.

===Chinese===
骑马人忽然催马小跑起来，转眼便在桥上勒缰停住。他一抬手，厉声喝道：「*Halte!*」（立定！）其余的人便都站住了。弗勒蕾特站在那里，心里纳闷这到底是怎么回事。她隐隐约约地猜到，这些人多半是兵；虽说实在的，除了马上那个像是他们军官的，这些人身上实在没有多少兵的样子。他们的红帽子是绒线织的，缀着一朵从前想必是三色帽徽的东西，如今蒙满尘土，颜色几乎辨不出来了。他们的上衣原先是蓝色、钉着铜扣的，如今又破又褪，还缺了好几颗扣子；马裤沾满泥污，鞋里没穿袜子；他们的衬衫究竟是出厂时就是灰扑扑的，还是穿旧穿脏了才变成这副样子，已经无从说定。弗勒蕾特的记忆飞回从前：那时她还是个小不点儿，节庆日里比比带她去塞尔或锡斯特龙，军乐队列队走过，兵士们衣装漂亮，铜扣闪闪发光，靴子擦得简直能照出人影来。

===Original===
But there! everyone knew that these were terribly hard times and that new clothes were very, very dear: so Fleurette supposed that the poor soldiers had to wear out their old ones just like everybody else. And her sensitive little heart gave an extra throb or two, for she had suddenly remembered that M’sieu’ Amédé would also be a soldier very soon, wearing a shabby coat, and perhaps no stockings inside his shoes. Still thinking of M’sieu’ Amédé, she was very polite to the man on horseback, although he was unnecessarily abrupt with her, asking her gruffly whether Citizen Armand was within.

===Chinese===
不过罢了！谁都知道眼下是艰难透顶的世道，新衣裳贵了又贵：所以弗勒蕾特想，可怜的兵士们也只好同旁人一样，把旧衣裳穿到破为止。她那颗敏感的小小心儿多跳了一两下，因为她猛然想起，阿梅代先生很快也要当兵了，也要穿一身破旧上衣，鞋里说不定也没袜子。心里还惦着阿梅代先生，她对马上那人便客客气气；那人却对她粗暴得全无来由，粗声粗气地问她，公民阿尔芒在不在家。

===Original===
Fleurette said “No!” quite gently, and then, choosing to ignore the coarse manner in which the man uttered a very ugly oath, she went on: “Father has been gone a quarter of an hour and more, and if you﻿—”

===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特轻声答了句「不在」，又只当没听见那人粗鄙地吐出的一句难听咒骂，接着说道：「父亲走了一刻多钟了，要是您——」

===Original===
“Citizen Armand, I asked for,” the officer broke in roughly, “not your father.”

===Chinese===
「我要找的是公民阿尔芒，」军官粗暴地打断她，「不是你父亲。」

===Original===
“Father’s name is Armand,” Fleurette said, still speaking very politely. “I thought you were asking for him.”

===Chinese===
「父亲的名字就叫阿尔芒，」弗勒蕾特说，口气仍然十分客气，「我还当您问的是他。」

===Original===
The horseman, she thought, realizing his mistake, should have excused himself for speaking so rudely: but he did nothing of the sort. He just shrugged his shoulders and said in a very curious way, which sounded almost like a sneer:

===Chinese===
她心想，这位骑马人既然明白自己弄错了，总该为方才的出言无礼赔个不是：可他压根没有那意思，只是耸了耸肩，用一种很古怪的、几乎像是讥诮的腔调说道：

===Original===
“Oh! is that how it is? You are Citizen Armand’s daughter, are you?”

===Chinese===
「哦！原来如此？你就是公民阿尔芒的女儿，是吧？」

===Original===
“Yes! M’sieu’ l’officier.”

===Chinese===
「是的，长官先生。」

===Original===
“Call me citizen lieutenant,” the man retorted roughly. “Hasn’t your father taught you to speak like a good patriot?”

===Chinese===
「叫我公民中尉，」那人没好气地顶了回来，「难道你父亲没教过你，要像个好爱国者那样说话吗？」

===Original===
Fleurette would not have admitted for the world that she was half afraid of this unkempt, unshaved officer with the gruff voice, but she felt intimidated, shy, ill at ease. She would have given worlds to have someone friendly beside her, old Louise, for instance, or even Adèle.

===Chinese===
打死弗勒蕾特也不会承认，自己有些怕这个蓬头散发、没刮胡子、嗓音粗厉的军官；可她实实在在觉得胆怯、害臊、浑身不自在。她恨不得身边有个亲近友善的人陪着——老路易丝也好，哪怕是阿黛尔也好。

===Original===
“Shall I call M’ame Louise,” she suggested, “to speak with you?”

===Chinese===
「要不要我去把路易丝大娘叫来，」她提议道，「让她跟您说话？」

===Original===
“No,” the man replied curtly, “what’s the use if your father isn’t there? Which way did he go?”

===Chinese===
「不必，」那人简慢地答道，「你父亲不在，叫她来有什么用？他往哪边去了？」

===Original===
“To the village first, M’sieu’﻿—I mean citizen, to pick up his horse which he always leaves at M’sieu’ Colombe’s stables. He is going to Paris afterwards.”

===Chinese===
「先去村里，先生——我是说，公民，去牵他的马，那匹马他向来寄在科隆布先生的马厩里。之后他就去巴黎。」

===Original===
“How far is it to the village?”

===Chinese===
「到村里有多远？」

===Original===
“Less than a quarter of a league﻿—er﻿—citizen.”

===Chinese===
「不到四分之一里格——呃——公民。」

===Original===
“And the house,” the officer asked again, “where the ci-devant Frontenacs live, is that far?”

===Chinese===
「还有那座宅子，」军官又问，「旧贵族（ci-devant）弗朗特纳克一家住的，远吗？」

===Original===
“About half a league by the road from here,” Fleurette replied, “the other side of the village. There is a shortcut behind this house, past the mill, but﻿—”

===Chinese===
「从这儿走大路，约莫半里格，」弗勒蕾特答道，「在村子那一头。这房子后头有条近路，绕过磨坊就是，不过——」

===Original===
The man, however, was no longer listening to what she said. He muttered something that sounded very much like an oath, and then turned to the soldiers: “*Allons! Marche!*” he commanded sharply. The men appeared terribly dusty and tired and hardly made a movement to obey: at the first call of “*Halte*,” some of them had thrown themselves down by the edge of the road and stretched out full length on the heaps of hard stones piled up there; others had wandered down the slope by the bridge, and lying flat on the ground were slaking their thirst in the cool, clear water of the stream. Fleurette was very sorry for them.

===Chinese===
可是那人已经不在听她说了。他咕哝了一句什么，听上去很像是咒骂，随即转向士兵们，厉声下令：「*Allons! Marche!*」（走！开步走！）那些人看来尘土满身、疲惫不堪，几乎懒得动弹一下来听令：方才头一声「立定」传来，就有几个一屁股倒在路边，直挺挺躺在那里堆着的硬石堆上；还有几个溜下桥边的土坡，趴在地上，就着溪里清凉的活水解渴。弗勒蕾特很替他们难过。

===Original===
“May they wait a moment, M’sieu’ le﻿—I mean citizen lieutenant,” she pleaded, “I’ll get them something to drink. We haven’t much, but I know Louise won’t﻿—”

===Chinese===
「能不能请他们稍等片刻，先生——我是说，公民中尉，」她恳求道，「我去给他们弄点喝的来。家里东西不多，可我知道路易丝不会不肯——」

===Original===
But the officer took no further notice either of her or of the men. Having given his order to march, he had readjusted the reins in his hands, and struck his spurs somewhat viciously into his horse’s flanks. The horse reared and plunged for a moment, then started off at a sharp trot, clouds of dust flying out from under its hoofs.

===Chinese===
可那军官对她也好、对士兵也好，都再也不加理会。开拔的命令既已下达，他理了理手中的缰绳，把马刺相当狠地磕进马腹。那马人立起来、腾跃了片刻，随即迈开急促的小跑上路了，蹄下扬起一团团尘土。

===Original===
The men made an effort to rise. Fleurette put up a finger and smiled at them all.

===Chinese===
士兵们挣扎着往起爬。弗勒蕾特竖起一根手指，朝他们大家莞尔一笑。

===Original===
“Wait one minute,” she said, and ran quickly back into the house.

===Chinese===
「等一小会儿，」她说着，飞快跑回屋里去了。

===Original===
There was the best part of a bottle left of that good red wine: Bibi had not touched it again after he broke the stem of his glass. Fleurette had picked up the bottle and taken a tin mug from the dresser and was about to start out again before Louise thought of asking her what she was up to.

===Chinese===
那瓶上好的红葡萄酒还剩下大半瓶：自打碰断杯脚那回事之后，比比就没有再碰过它。弗勒蕾特抱起酒瓶，又从碗柜里取了一只锡杯，正要再往外跑，路易丝才想起问问她这是要干什么。

===Original===
“There are some poor, tired soldiers outside on the bridge,” Fleurette replied, “I want to take them something to drink. There’s not much of it, and twelve of them to share it, but it will be better than nothing, and perhaps *le bon Dieu* will make a miracle and make it be enough. They seemed so thirsty, poor dears.”

===Chinese===
「外头桥上有几个又累又乏的可怜士兵，」弗勒蕾特答道，「我想给他们送点喝的去。酒没有多少，却有十二个人分，可总比没有强，说不定好天主会显个奇迹，让它刚刚够呢。他们看样子渴极了，怪可怜的。」

===Original===
“Let Adèle go,” Louise said curtly, “I don’t like you speaking with those vagabonds.”

===Chinese===
「让阿黛尔去，」路易丝没多话，「我不喜欢你同那些流浪汉搭腔。」

===Original===
And while Adèle ran out, as she was bid, with mug and bottle, Louise continued to mutter half under her breath:

===Chinese===
阿黛尔依言拿着锡杯和酒瓶跑出去之后，路易丝兀自压着嗓子，半吞半吐地咕哝：

===Original===
“I can’t abide those sansculottes. Brigands the lot of them. What are they doing in the neighbourhood, I’d like to know. Up to no good you may depend. Let Adèle talk to them. It is not fit for a well-brought-up wench like you to be seen in such company.”

===Chinese===
「我见不得那些无套裤汉（sansculottes）。一个个全是强盗。他们跑到这地界来做什么，我倒要问问。管保没安好心。让阿黛尔去同他们说话。像你这样规规矩矩养大的姑娘家，让人瞧见同这路人混在一处，可不体面。」

===Original===
Fleurette did not pay much attention to old Louise’s mutterings. There was plenty to do in the house with washing up and tidying things away. And it was Louise’s habit to grumble at anything that was in any way unusual: a wet day in August, or a mild one in December, a calèche on the road, a horseman, a soldier, or a letter for Bibi. She was always called “old Louise,” although, in truth, she had scarce reached middle age; but her skin was dry and rough like the soil of her native Dauphiné, her face and hands were prematurely wrinkled and her voice had become harsh of late, probably for want of use, like a piece of mechanism that has stood still, and begun to grind for want of a lubricant. In Armand’s house, when he was absent, she ruled supreme. Fleurette never dreamed of disobeying, and Armand’s only peremptory orders to Louise were never to mention politics or current events to the child.

===Chinese===
