弗勒蕾特并不怎么在意老路易丝的咕哝。屋里洗洗刷刷、收拾归置，有的是活计。何况大凡稍稍出了寻常的事，路易丝都要抱怨几句：八月里下一天雨，或是十二月里暖和一天，路上过一辆篷车（calèche），来个骑马的，来个大兵，或是给比比来一封信，都逃不过她的怨言。她总被唤作「老路易丝」，其实论年纪还不到中年；只是她的皮肤又干又粗，像她多菲内老家的土地，脸和手都过早地起了皱，嗓音近来也发糙了——多半是因为缺少使用，就像一部久置不动的机器，缺了润滑油，一转起来便磨得吱嘎作响。阿尔芒不在家的时候，这个家里便是她当家作主。弗勒蕾特做梦也没想过不遵她的命；而阿尔芒对路易丝唯一斩钉截铁的叮嘱，就是永远不许在孩子面前提起政治和时局。

===Original===
Louise had nursed Fleurette at her breast when Fleurette’s mother died in childbed, and she had left her own baby in the care of her sister, already a widow and childless. Considerations of money had prompted her at the time, for Monsieur Armand, as he was then, had made her liberal offers: afterwards it was too late to regret. Her own daughter, Adèle, born of an unknown father who loved and rode away, had been brought up to a life of drudgery by her aunt, who sent the girl out to earn her own living as soon as she could toddle, whilst Fleurette was brought up to have everything she wanted; petted and idolized by a father plentifully supplied with money. Fleurette and Adèle were foster-sisters, but with destinies as wide apart as the peaks of Pelvoux.

===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特的母亲难产去世的时候，是路易丝用自己的乳汁把她奶大的；她把自己怀里的婴儿交给姐妹抚养——那姐妹当时已经守寡，膝下无儿无女。当初促使她这样做的是钱：他——那时还唤作阿尔芒先生——给她的报酬许得十分优厚；事后要悔，已经太迟。她自己的女儿阿黛尔，生父不详——那人恩爱一场，骑马走了——从小被姨妈当苦力使唤，刚会走路就被打发出去自谋生计；弗勒蕾特却是要什么有什么地娇养大的，被一个钱多得使不尽的父亲捧在手心里疼爱、供奉。弗勒蕾特和阿黛尔是奶姐妹，命途却像佩尔武的两座峰头，隔得老远。

===Original===
But Louise never spoke one bitter word when she saw Adèle with toil-worn hands scrubbing the kitchen floor on which Fleurette trod with dainty, high-heeled shoes. Perhaps she loved her foster-child more than she did her own: perhaps it was only the same considerations of money that already guided her conduct before, which prompted her later to indulgence toward the rich man’s daughter, whilst reserving her pent-up acrimony for the household drudge. No one knew what Louise’s feelings were towards Adèle﻿—Adèle herself least of all. The girl was silent, reserved, self-contained, very conscientious in her work, but not very responsive to the many kindnesses shown her by M’sieu’ Armand or Mam’zelle Fleurette. She still lived with her aunt who had brought her up, and she appeared to lay no claim on her mother’s affection: she had earned her own living ever since she was ten years of age, and now, at eighteen, she looked more like a woman than a girl: her little face was all pinched up, the lips thin, the eyes either sharp as needles or expressionless like those of a rodent. She hardly ever spoke and no one had ever seen her smile.

===Chinese===
可是，眼见阿黛尔一双操劳的手跪着刷洗厨房地板，而弗勒蕾特踏着秀气的高跟鞋从那地板上走过，路易丝从没有说过一句重话。也许她疼这个奶女儿，胜过疼亲生的；也许不过是那桩老念头——钱的算计——先前左右着她的行止，如今又促使她一味纵容有钱人的女儿，而把一腔压着的刻薄，尽数留给家里的苦力丫头。谁也不知道路易丝对阿黛尔究竟存着什么心思——最摸不透的，恰恰是阿黛尔自己。这姑娘沉默寡言、拘谨内敛、心事不露，干活一丝不苟，可是对阿尔芒先生和弗勒蕾特小姐的种种善待，却不大领情。她仍旧同抚养她的姨妈住在一起，似乎也从不指望母亲的疼爱：她打十岁起就自食其力，如今十八岁，看着不像个姑娘，倒像个妇人了：那张小脸缩作一团，嘴唇薄薄的，那双眼睛，时而尖得像针，时而毫无表情，像啮齿动物的眼睛。她几乎从不开口，也从没有人见她笑过。

===Original===
Old Louise’s mutterings presently turned to Adèle’s prolonged absence:

===Chinese===
过不多时，老路易丝的咕哝转到阿黛尔怎么迟迟不回来上头：

===Original===
“What is the girl about now, I should like to know? She is not a gossip as a rule.”

===Chinese===
「这丫头这会儿又干什么去了，我倒想知道？她平日可不是个爱闲磕牙的。」

===Original===
She went on with her washing up for a moment or two longer and then said sharply:

===Chinese===
她又接着洗刷了一两分钟，随后厉声说道：

===Original===
“Run along, Fleurette, and see what the wench is doing. Lazy baggage, with all the work there’s still to do.”

===Chinese===
「快去，弗勒蕾特，瞧瞧那丫头在做什么。懒骨头，家里还有一大堆活儿等着做呢。」

===Original===
Fleurette ran out at once. She too wondered why Adèle was such a long time. And there, sure enough, standing on the bridge was Adèle talking to the soldiers. The officer was already out of sight. Adèle talking! and Fleurette even thought that she heard her giggle. Incredible! The soldiers were all laughing and one of them was in the act of drinking the last drop cut of the tin mug.

===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特立刻跑了出去。她也纳闷阿黛尔怎么去了这么久。果然，阿黛尔正站在桥上同士兵们说话。那军官早已没了踪影。阿黛尔在说话！弗勒蕾特甚至觉得自己听见她在咯咯地笑。真是难以置信！士兵们全都在笑，其中一个正把锡杯里最后一点酒往嘴里倒。

===Original===
Fleurette stood for a moment on the doorstep, vaguely wondering what in the world had come over Adèle, when a rather curious incident occurred: the soldiers were all laughing, jesting apparently with the girl, and one of them, with head tilted back, was draining the last drop out of the tin mug. Fleurette was on the point of calling to Adèle when her attention was arrested by the appearance of an old man carrying what looked like a load of faggots tied up in a coarse sacking. He seemed to have climbed the slopes on the opposite side of the road; at any rate there he was, all of a sudden, immediately behind the group of soldiers.

===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特在门口台阶上站了片刻，隐隐纳闷阿黛尔究竟中了什么邪；恰在此时，发生了一件颇为古怪的事：士兵们全都在笑，明摆着是在同这姑娘打趣，其中一个仰着脖子，正把锡杯里的最后一滴酒倒干净。弗勒蕾特正要唤阿黛尔，注意力却被一个老头儿的出现攫住了：他背着个什么东西，瞧着像是一捆用粗麻袋扎好的柴。他多半是打大路对面的坡上爬上来的；总之，转眼之间，他就到了那群士兵的正背后。

===Original===
He appeared to be drunk, for he staggered as he walked and leaned heavily on a stout gnarled stick. Fleurette could not have told you exactly how it all happened, but all of a sudden Adèle’s giggling and the soldiers’ jests were interrupted by the old faggot-carrier tumbling down clumsily, right between them all.

===Chinese===
他看样子是喝醉了，走路直打晃，整个身子的分量都压在一根虬结的粗手杖上。弗勒蕾特说不清那究竟是怎么发生的，反正忽然之间，阿黛尔的咯咯笑声和士兵们的打趣，就被那背柴老人笨手笨脚的一跤打断了——他恰好跌在他们正中间。

===Original===
Adèle screamed. The soldiers swore, and one of them went to the length of giving the old man a savage kick, whilst two others incontinently picked him up between them and flung him over the parapet of the bridge. Fleurette gave a cry of dismay and ran to the poor man’s assistance. She felt hot with indignation at such wanton brutality. How right, she thought as she ran, had old Louise’s estimate been of these soldiers﻿—little better than brigands they were, and cruel to boot. The poor faggot-carrier, for such he seemed to be, was lying half in and half out of the stream: the grass and sloping ground had somewhat broken his fall, but nevertheless there he lay, motionless and groaning piteously. Fleurette called peremptorily to Adèle to come and help her hoist up the poor man on his legs again. He was very dirty, dressed in nothing but rags, his feet swathed in coarse bass matting; he was stockingless, shirtless and hatless; but he appeared to be powerfully built and Fleurette marvelled how he could have allowed himself to be thus maltreated without a struggle. No doubt he was drunk or crippled with rheumatism.

===Chinese===
阿黛尔尖叫起来。士兵们破口大骂，其中一个竟抬脚狠狠踹了老人一下，另有两个二话不说，把他往中间一架，扔过了桥栏。弗勒蕾特惊叫一声，跑过去救助那可怜的人。这等蛮横的暴虐，激得她满面发烧。她一边跑一边想：老路易丝对这些兵的评断多么在理——比强盗好不了多少，还外加凶狠。那可怜的背柴老人——看行头，他正是背柴的——半身在溪水里，半身在溪水外：草地和斜坡多少垫了垫他下坠的势头，可他还是躺在那里，一动不动，哼哼唧唧，怪可怜的。弗勒蕾特急急地招呼阿黛尔来帮她，两人一起把那可怜人架起来重新站稳。他脏极了，身上的衣裳只剩破布，两只脚裹着粗草席；没袜子，没衬衫，也没帽子；可他身架看着相当结实，弗勒蕾特暗暗称奇，他怎么能任人这般作践，竟不挣扎一下。想必不是醉了，就是风湿得直不起腰来。

===Original===
Up on the bridge the soldiers were preparing to start once more on their way. They took no more notice of their unfortunate victim nor of Adèle; but Fleurette looking up felt that their last glance was for her; some of them were regarding her with a leer, others with more pronounced malevolence. She distinctly saw one man nudge his neighbour and point a finger at her: whereat both of them gave a mocking laugh.

===Chinese===
桥上，士兵们正在准备重新开拔。他们不再理会那个倒霉的受害者，也不理会阿黛尔；可弗勒蕾特一抬眼，觉出他们临去的最后一瞥是冲她来的：有几个斜着眼色打量她，另几个眼里的恶意更加露骨。她清清楚楚看见一个人用胳膊肘捅了捅身旁的同伴，朝她指了一指：两人随即发出一阵讪笑。

===Original===
She felt hurt and indignant: in her sheltered life she had never met with malevolence before. However for the moment, her first care was for the poor faggot-carrier. Adèle had come to her assistance, and together the two girls succeeded in getting the old man on his legs again. He appeared more scared than hurt, and with his big, toil-stained hands, he felt himself all over to see, perhaps, if any bones were broken; and all the while he kept on murmuring rather pathetically: “*Nom de nom, de nom de Dieu!*” as if surprised that such a tragic adventure should have happened to him.

===Chinese===
她又伤心又气愤：在她这受人呵护、不识风浪的日子里，还从没有遇上过恶意。不过此时此刻，她头一桩挂心的还是那可怜的背柴老人。阿黛尔已过来帮了她，两个姑娘一齐动手，总算把老人架得重新站立起来。他看样子吓得不轻，伤倒不重；他用一双满是劳作污痕的大手，把自己周身上下摸了一遍，多半是想看看有没有摔断骨头；嘴里一直凄凄惨惨地念叨：「*Nom de nom, de nom de Dieu!*」（我的天，我的老天哪！）仿佛惊诧这样一桩惨祸竟会落到自己头上。

===Original===
Fleurette asked him if he were hurt, and he replied: “No, Mam’zelle﻿—that is citizeness,” and he added: “Ah, I shall never get used to these new ways. I am too old.”

===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特问他摔伤没有，他答道：「没伤着，小姐——该说女公民。」接着又道：「唉，这些新规矩，我是再也习惯不了啦。我太老喽。」

===Original===
“Can you get on your way now?” Fleurette asked.

===Chinese===
「您这会儿能上路了吗？」弗勒蕾特问。

===Original===
“Yes! yes, Mam’zelle, that is citizeness. But,” he went on piteously, “I am so hungry. I come from over Mison way and I have not had a bite since seven o’clock this morning.”

===Chinese===
「能，能，小姐——该说女公民。可是，」他可怜巴巴地接着说，「我饿得慌。我是打米松（Mison）那边过来的，今儿早上七点到现在，还没沾过一口吃的。」

===Original===
This naturally stirred Fleurette’s kind, compassionate heart. She told Adèle to run into the house and ask Louise for a hunk of bread. Adèle, silent and self-contained once more, obeyed without comment. The incident was closed as far as Fleurette was concerned. Her thoughts flew back to Amédé and to his last day and evening which he would be spending in his cosy home. She wished she had been bold enough to ask him to come and bid her a last adieu tomorrow morning before he went away to fight the English.

===Chinese===
这自然撩动了弗勒蕾特那副仁慈怜人的心肠。她打发阿黛尔回屋去，向路易丝讨一大块面包。阿黛尔又恢复了那副沉默内敛的模样，一声不吭地照办了。在弗勒蕾特这方面，这桩事就算过去了。她的心思又飞回阿梅代身上，想着他将在这温馨的家里度过的最后一天和最后一晚。她只悔自己胆子不够大，没敢开口约他明天早晨动身去打英国人之前，来同她道最后一声别。

===Original===
And while she stood there gazing out over the valley where the metal cross on the church steeple of Laragne glistened like gold in the sunlight, a strange voice﻿—soft yet firm﻿—suddenly struck her ear from somewhere close behind her.

===Chinese===
她正站在那里眺望山谷——拉拉涅教堂尖顶上那具金属十字架，在日光下亮得像黄金——忽然，一个陌生的嗓音，轻柔却坚定，冷不防从她身后近处传来，撞进她耳中。

===Original===
“Papers and valuables are behind the panel in Madame’s room.”

===Chinese===
「文件和值钱的东西，在夫人房里的壁板后头。」

===Original===
She swung round terrified, so terrified that the cry she was about to utter died away in her throat. She looked about her, scared, shivering with that nameless dread which assails every mortal in face of the supernatural. And yet everything seemed as peaceful as before: the little mill stream splashed and gurgled with its soft, persistent sound; in the old walnut tree a thrush was calling to its mate and the old faggot-carrier was busy tying up his faggots into the sacking again. Fleurette’s eyes rested for an instant anxiously upon him. She expected to see him raise his head, to look about him, to appear scared as she was herself; but he gave no sign of having heard anything of that mysterious voice, fresh and compelling like a command from heaven. Oh no! Fleurette could not have screamed. She was too panic-stricken just at first to utter a sound. And yet nothing had really happened to alarm the most timorous. Only those few words spoken by an unseen tongue. What did they mean? What could they mean? They were simple and commonplace enough: Fleurette repeated them to herself mechanically:

===Chinese===
她惊恐万分地猛然回身，惊恐得那一声已到喉咙口的尖叫，生生咽了回去。她环顾四周，心惊胆战，直打寒战——那是每个凡人直面灵异之事时都会袭来的、无以名状的恐惧。然而一切又似乎同先前一样安谧：小磨坊溪淙淙溅溅，水声轻柔，绵延不歇；老胡桃树上，一只画眉正在呼唤它的伴侣；背柴老人正忙着把他的柴捆重新扎回麻袋里去。弗勒蕾特的目光不安地在他身上停了一瞬。她本以为他会抬起头来，四下张望，露出同她自己一样的惊惶；可他毫无听见那神秘声音的样子——那嗓音清朗而不容违抗，宛如一道来自上天的号令。啊，不！弗勒蕾特叫不出来了。起初那一阵，她惊慌得太厉害，发不出一点声音。然而实际上，并没有发生什么足以惊动最胆小之人的事。不过是一段看不见的舌头说出的话罢了。那是什么意思？能是什么意思？这几句话再简单、再平常不过：弗勒蕾特机械地对自己默念了一遍：

===Original===
“Papers and valuables are behind the panel in Madame’s room.”

===Chinese===
「文件和值钱的东西，在夫人房里的壁板后头。」

===Original===
What did it mean? What papers? what valuables? and why should the mysterious speaker have wished her to know that they were behind the panel in Madame’s room? Madame was, of course, Madame de Frontenac over at the château, and all of a sudden Fleurette remembered that the mounted soldier had asked her the way to the château. Gradually she was feeling less scared. Less scared but more excited. She looked round at the statue of St. Antoine, at whose feet she had this morning placed a fresh bunch of forget-me-nots. Somehow she associated the mysterious voice with St. Antoine. Perhaps Madame had lost some valuable papers, and the kind saint had chosen this means of letting her know where her treasure was. Fleurette made the sign of the cross on her bosom; she remembered the story of Jeanne d’Arc which M. le Curé used to tell her, of how the humble shepherdess of Domremy had been compelled by heavenly voices to go forth and deliver France from her enemies and never rest until she had seen the King crowned in his cathedral of Rheims.

===Chinese===
这是什么意思？什么文件？什么值钱的东西？那位神秘的说话人，又为什么偏要让她知道，东西就在夫人房里的壁板后头？夫人，自然是庄园那头的弗朗特纳克夫人；弗勒蕾特猛然记起，方才那骑马的士兵问过她去庄园的路。渐渐地，她不那么怕了。不怕了，却越发兴奋。她回头望了望圣安东尼的塑像——今儿早上，她才在像脚下供过一束新采的勿忘我。不知怎么，她把那神秘的声音同圣安东尼联系在了一起。也许是夫人丢失了什么要紧的文件，好心的圣人便拣了这么个法子，让她知道宝贝的下落。弗勒蕾特在胸前画了个十字；她想起本堂神父从前常给她讲的贞德（Jeanne d'Arc）的故事：栋雷米（Domremy）那位谦卑的牧羊女，如何被上天的声音催迫着奋起，把法兰西从敌人手里解救出来，不亲眼见到国王在兰斯大教堂里加冕，便须臾不得安息。

===Original===
Fleurette felt something of that same fervour which had animated Jeanne d’Arc. She felt that she must go forth and tell Madame about the valuables and the papers. The evening was warm and she would not need her shawl. She could go just as she was as far as the château and be back before the twilight had faded into night. Adèle in the meanwhile appeared at the front door, she had her shawl over her head, and a hunk of bread in her hand. Then only did Fleurette remember the old faggot-carrier. She turned to him in order to bid him “Godspeed.” He stood there quite motionless, leaning upon his stick bending under the weight of his load of faggots which he had hoisted upon his back. His lank hair hung over his wrinkled forehead and half concealed his eyes. But, suddenly through the veil of lank grey hair Fleurette met the man’s glance fixed upon her; and her heart gave a queer jump. Those were not the eyes of a decrepit old man; they were young and clear and bright: of a luminous grey-blue, with heavy lids that could not wholly conceal the humorous twinkle in the eyes, nor yet the kindly, searching glance which was fixed on Fleurette.

===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特觉出，几分当年激励过贞德的那股热忱，此刻也在她胸中激荡。她觉得她必须去把值钱东西和文件的事告诉夫人。傍晚天气暖和，用不着披肩。她就这一身去庄园，趁暮色还没沉成黑夜，便能赶回来。这时阿黛尔出现在大门口，头上蒙着披肩，手里拿着一大块面包。直到这时，弗勒蕾特才又记起那背柴老人。她转向他，想道一声「一路平安」。他一动不动地站在那里，拄着手杖，脊背让他背上那担柴压得弯了下去。稀疏的头发披在皱纹纵横的额前，半遮着他的眼睛。可是忽然之间，透过那稀疏灰发的帘幕，弗勒蕾特迎上了那人定定落在她身上的目光；她的心古怪地一跳。那不是一双衰朽老人的眼睛；那双眼睛年轻、清亮、明澈：一种莹润的灰蓝色，厚厚的眼睑掩不住眸子里的谐谑闪光，也掩不住那落在弗勒蕾特身上的、善意而探询的注视。

===Original===
This was the moment when she really would have screamed. The sense of something ununderstandable and unreal was more than she could bear, she would have screamed, but those twinkling, searching eyes held her, and at the same time seemed to reassure her, to tell her not to be afraid. She felt as if she were in a dream: unable to do anything, only to stare and stare at the old faggot-carrier, while gradually all her terrors seemed to fall away from her, and she was filled with a sense of courage and of determination. The whole incident, the voice, the glance, her terror and reassurance had lasted less than five seconds. Already Adèle was close by. She was bringing the bread for the poor, half-starved man, and Fleurette now watched him, fascinated, as he took the bread with a humble: “Merci, Mam’zelle,” and started at once on it, like a man who has not tasted food all day. He was just a decrepit old man, bent with rheumatism, dirty, unkempt, insecure on his tottering limbs. He even raised his eyes once, and once more looked at her; but the glance was dim like that of an old man; there was no twinkle in the eyes, only the weariness of poverty and old age.

===Chinese===
这一下，她真要叫出声了。那股不可理解、不似真实的滋味，她实在承受不住；她本会尖叫的，可那双闪着光、探询着的眼睛摄住了她，同时又仿佛在宽慰她，叫她别害怕。她觉得自己像在梦里：什么也做不得，只能怔怔地、怔怔地望着那背柴老人；她的种种恐怖渐渐仿佛从身上褪了下去，满心涌起一股勇气和决意。这整个变故——那声音、那一瞥、她的惊恐与宽慰——前后不过五秒钟。阿黛尔已经到了近旁。她是给这可怜的、饿得半死的人送面包来的；弗勒蕾特这时看得入神，只见他谦卑地接过面包，道一声「*Merci, Mam'zelle*」（谢谢您，小姐），随即大嚼起来，活像个一整天没尝过吃食的人。他就只是一个风瘫伛偻、腌臜邋遢、两条腿颤颤巍巍站也站不稳的衰朽老头儿。他甚至还抬眼看了她一回，又望了她一望；可那目光已浑浊如老人，眼里再没有闪光，只剩下贫穷与衰老的疲惫。

===Original===
And Fleurette felt that she had dreamed it all: the voice, the glance, the message from St. Antoine, just as her terrors had faded from her, so now her excitement vanished too. It must all have been a dream. It *was* a dream! Perhaps old Louise, who was versed in all kinds of dreamlore, would know of an explanation for the whole mysterious occurrence. Feeling very tired all of a sudden﻿—for she felt the reaction after the tenseness of the last few moments, she went back into the house. In the doorway she turned to have a last look at the old faggot-carrier; leaning heavily upon his stick, he was making his way along the bank of the stream. The last she saw of him was his big bundle done up in sacking and his legs bending beneath the weight.

===Chinese===
于是弗勒蕾特觉得，这一切都是她梦出来的：那声音，那一瞥，圣安东尼捎来的口信；正像她的恐怖从她身上褪尽了一样，此刻她的兴奋也消散了。这一切想必只是一场梦。这*就是*一场梦！也许老路易丝——她精通种种圆梦的学问——能给这整桩神秘的变故说出个道理来。她忽然觉得非常疲乏——方才那阵紧张过后，松劲儿涌了上来——便回屋里去。在门口，她又回头望了背柴老人最后一眼：他重重拄着手杖，正沿着溪岸一路走远。她最后望见的，是他那用麻袋扎起的大柴捆，和柴捆重压下渐渐打弯的两条腿。

===Original===
Adèle wrapped in her shawl had gone the other way. She was already up on the bridge. With a little sigh of disappointment Fleurette went into the house. It had been such an exciting dream!

===Chinese===
裹着披肩的阿黛尔往另一头去了。她已经上了桥。弗勒蕾特轻轻叹了口失望的气，走进屋去。多叫人心跳的一场梦啊！

===Original===
But she did not speak about it to old Louise; she just went quietly about the house, doing one or two little bits of work that Adèle had left undone.

===Chinese===
不过她没有对老路易丝说起这事，只安安静静地在屋里各处转着，把阿黛尔撂下没做完的一两件零碎活计做完了。

===Original===
The slowly sinking sun had turned the gold on Pelvoux’ snowy crest to a brilliant rose, when Fleurette suddenly announced to Louise that she was going over to the château. She often went there, and at all hours of the day.

===Chinese===
徐徐西沉的太阳，把佩尔武雪峰上的金色染成一片艳丽的玫瑰色；正在这时，弗勒蕾特忽然向路易丝说，她要去一趟庄园。她常去那儿，一天里无论什么时辰都去过。

===Original===
“So long as you are home before dark,” was Louise’s only remark. “I don’t like those down-at-heel soldiers being about.”

===Chinese===
「天黑前回到家就行，」路易丝只说了这一句。「我不喜欢那些衣衫褴褛、鞋跟塌了的大兵在附近转悠。」

===Original===
Fleurette promised that she would not be late. She picked up her beautiful new shawl and wrapped it round her shoulders. The château was not far; over by the mountain track, it was not more than a quarter of a league at most. Swiftly Fleurette ran out of the house and then along the edge of the stream﻿—the same way that the old faggot-carrier had gone an hour or so ago.

===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特答应不晚归。她拿起那条漂亮的新披肩，裹在肩上。庄园不远；打山道过去，至多不过四分之一里格。弗勒蕾特飞快地跑出屋子，又沿着溪岸一路跑去——个把钟头之前，那背柴老人走的就是这条道。

===Original===
And now the mantle of twilight was falling over the valley; the jade-coloured sky held myriads of tiny, fleecy clouds of a brilliant glowing crimson, which one by one faded into grey, alone the snow of Pelvoux reflected the glory of the sinking sun, and in the old walnut-tree the thrush’s song was stilled.

===Chinese===
这时，暮色的大氅正落向山谷；玉色的天上，千千万万朵细小的绒云燃着灼灼的绯红，一朵一朵，渐次褪成灰色；唯有佩尔武的积雪，还映着西沉落日的余晖；老胡桃树上，画眉的歌声静了。

## VI / 第六章

===Original===
The place was always called le château for want of a more appropriate name. As a matter of fact it was just a large, rumbling, roomy farmhouse with stables and stable-yards and sheds and outbuildings, all built in a mass and at different times as necessity demanded, in the midst of a really fine park, shady with century old trees and fragrant with acacias and roses. Here for many generations the de Frontenacs, father and son, had lived, toiled and died, farming their land, honouring their King and otherwise not troubling their heads over much with politics or with art or literature. They were good, kindly, honest folk, all of them, and if the light of their intellect burned somewhat dim, that of their charity was always kept fed and bright.

They belonged to that sturdy stock which had given France one of her most valiant sons in Louis de Frontenac, the man who had made Eastern Canada a jewel worthy of the crown of France. The jewel had been lost since then, irretrievably lost to the English, and the crown of France been dragged in the mire of a bloody revolution, its glory forever overshadowed by the unforgivable crime of a purposeless regicide: but the present holder of the ancient name and owner of the lands had kept himself aloof from the awful dissensions that raged in the big cities; he had remained in his heart loyal to his martyred King, and though shorn of most of his wealth, deprived of a great deal of his inheritance, perpetually threatened with confiscation or attainder, he continued to lead the simple life, hoping for better things, detached as far as he was able from the turmoil that was ruining his country and shaming her in the eyes of the world.

===Chinese===
这地方向来被唤作「庄园」（le château），只因寻不出一个更贴切的名目。说实在的，它不过是一座散漫铺展、屋宇宽绰的大农舍，带着马厩、厩院、棚屋和各色附属杂房，全都攒作一团，是历朝历代视需要陆续添盖起来的，居中又坐落在一座真正精美的园林里——百年老树绿荫匝地，洋槐与玫瑰吐着芬芳。多少代弗朗特纳克人在这里父死子继，生息、劳作、终老，耕种自家的田地，敬奉自家的君王，除此之外，对政治也好，对艺术文学也好，都不肯过分劳神。他们个个是善良、和气、诚实的正派人；即便智识的灯火燃得略嫌昏暗，仁爱的心火却总有人添薪拨焰，长明不熄。

他们属于那一支坚毅强韧的族系——法兰西最英勇的儿郎之一、路易·德·弗朗特纳克（Louis de Frontenac）便出自这一族；正是此人，把东加拿大经营成了一颗明珠，堪为法兰西王冠上的珍饰。那颗明珠后来失落在英国人手里，无可挽回；法兰西的王冠也被拖进了血腥革命的泥沼，其荣光被一桩无可饶恕、又毫无意义可言的弑君之罪永远罩上了阴影。然而这个古老姓氏当今的承袭人、这片田产的主人，始终远离大城市里甚嚣尘上的可怕纷争；他在心底依旧忠于那位殉难的君王；纵然财富被削去大半，继承的家产被夺去许多，又无时无刻不受着产业充公、公权褫夺的威胁，他照旧过着简朴的日子，盼着时局好转，能离那场正在断送祖国、令她在全世界眼前蒙羞的动乱多远，便离多远。

===Original===
At all seasons of the year, and in all weathers, he could be seen out on his farm, directing the work in fields or stables, clad in rough boots and breeches, abrupt of speech, but kindly in deeds, beloved by some, envied by others, hated only by those few who see in every noble life a reproach to their own. His wife was the daughter of an Admiral in the late King’s navy, who had thought it prudent to serve the Republic, as he had served his King, with commendable detachment from his country’s politics. Though brought up in the midst of the gaieties and luxuries of Paris, Anne de Grandville had been quite content to follow the husband of her choice to the lonely farmhouse in Dauphiné, and to fall in with his bucolic ways: she donned a cotton kirtle and linen apron as readily as she had donned silken panniers in the past, and took as much pride in her cooking now as she had done once in her proficiency in the dance.

At one time Charles de Frontenac had sorely grieved because he had no son to whom he could bequeath his glorious name and fine inheritance, but now he was glad. With France handed over to the control of assassins, bandits, and regicides, the name of Frontenac might, he opined, just as well die out. What was the use of toiling to improve land which tomorrow might be wrested from its rightful owners: what was the use of saving money which would probably on the morrow fall into the hands of brigands? “Lay not up for yourselves treasures on this earth where rust and moth doth corrupt and where thieves break through and steal!” had never been so wise an admonition as it was today. All that Charles de Frontenac hoped to do was to put by a sufficient competence to keep his wife and invalid daughter in comfort once he was under the ground. That daughter was the apple of his eye. Bereft of position and most of his wealth, all his thoughts and hopes were centred on this delicate being who seemed like the one ray of sunshine amidst thunderclouds of disappointment and treachery.

===Chinese===
一年四季，无论什么天气，总能在田庄上望见他的身影，不是在田里，便是在马厩里督着活计；他脚蹬粗重的长靴，身穿马裤，说话直来直去，行事却仁厚可亲——有人爱戴他，有人嫉妒他，真正恨他的只有寥寥数人：唯有那种人，才会把每一种高洁的生命都看作对自己生命的责难。他的妻子是先王海军里一位海军上将的千金；这位上将觉得，改事共和国方为明哲，一如他从前效忠君王那样恪尽职守，对本国的政争始终保持着一副难能可贵的超然。安娜·德·格兰维尔（Anne de Grandville）虽是在巴黎的繁华热闹与锦衣玉食中长大的，却心甘情愿随她自己择定的夫婿，来到多菲内这幢冷清的农舍，入乡随俗过起了乡野的日子：从前她怎样欢欢喜喜地穿上丝绸裙撑，如今便怎样欢欢喜喜地换上布裙和亚麻围裙；往日她以舞艺娴熟自豪，如今则以一手好烹调自负。

夏尔·德·弗朗特纳克曾经痛心不已，只因膝下无子，无人可以承袭他光荣的姓氏与美好的家业；如今他反倒觉得庆幸。法兰西既已交到刺客、匪徒和弑君者手里，弗朗特纳克这个姓氏，依他之见，就此绝嗣也未尝不可。辛辛苦苦改良土地，明日说不定便要从正主手里被人夺走，何苦来哉？省吃俭用积攒银钱，后日多半要落进强盗的腰包，攒它作甚？「不要为自己积攒财宝在地上，地上有虫子咬，能锈坏，也有贼挖窟窿来偷」——这条古训，从没有像今天这样字字都是至理。夏尔·德·弗朗特纳克如今一心想做的，无非是攒下一份够用的家私，好教他身入黄土之后，妻子和多病的女儿还能过得安适。那女儿是他的掌上明珠。地位没了，家财去了大半，他全部的心思与指望，都倾注在这纤弱的小人儿身上；在失望与背叛堆叠成的满天雷云之中，她仿佛是唯一透进的一线阳光。

===Original===
Rose de Frontenac had been a cripple from birth, and it was her delicacy and her helplessness that had so endeared her to her father. He was a man resplendent with vigour and of herculean strength: one of those bull-necked men who could have taken his place in an ox-team and not proved a weakling. His hands were rough, his fist as hard as a hammer. His clothes smelt of damp earth and of manure; the descendant of a long line of aristocrats, Charles de Frontenac, was above all a son of the soil. To him his pale-faced, fragile daughter seemed like a being from another world; he hardly dared touch her cheek with his thick, clumsy fingers, nor dared he approach her save after copious ablutions and sprays of scent. His heart was as big as his body. He adored his daughter, he loved his wife, he beamed with fondness for Fleurette: Fleurette who was as gay as a linnet, who could always bring a smile to the pale lips of his wan, white Rose: Fleurette, who could sing like a lark, prattle like a young sparrow and whose corn-coloured hair smelt of wild thyme and of youth.

===Chinese===
罗丝·德·弗朗特纳克生来便有残疾，偏偏是这份娇弱与无助，教父亲把她疼到了心坎里。他本人精力焕发，有大力神般的膂力，是那种脖颈粗壮如牛的汉子，真把他套进犍牛队里一同拉犁，也决计显不出是个弱者。他一双手粗粝，拳头脑硬得赛过铁锤；他一身衣裳，透着湿土和粪肥的气味。这位数代簪缨的贵族后裔夏尔·德·弗朗特纳克，归根结底是个土地的儿子。在他眼里，他那面容苍白、身子单薄的女儿，恍如来自另一个世界的生灵；他几乎不敢用自己粗大的笨手指去碰她的脸颊，也不敢近她的身，除非先好生洗濯一番，再足足喷上几喷香水。他的心胸和他的身量一样阔大。他疼爱女儿，他怜爱妻子，见了弗勒蕾特更是满脸的慈爱：弗勒蕾特快活得像只红雀，总能逗得他那苍白羸弱的白玫瑰——罗丝——唇边浮起笑意；弗勒蕾特唱起歌来像只云雀，叽叽喳喳像只小麻雀，她那麦金色的头发里，透着野百里香和青春的气息。

## VII / 第七章

===Original===
Fleurette had walked very fast. She was still tremendously excited and would have run all the way, only that the road for the most part led sharply uphill and that her heart was beating and pumping wildly with agitation.

===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特一路走得飞快。她还处在极度的兴奋之中，本来真想一路跑着去的，只是这条路大半截陡峭上坡，而且她的心正激动得怦怦狂跳，不受使唤。

===Original===
Strangely enough the gates of the park were wide open, which was very unusual, as they were always kept closed for fear of the footpads and vagabonds. Old Pierre, who was in charge of the gate, was nowhere to be seen. Fleurette ran along the sanded avenue which, bordered by bosquets of acacia and elder, led in sharp curves up to the house. Twilight was slowly fading into evening, but even through the gathering darkness Fleurette noticed that the avenue, usually so beautifully raked and tidy, was all trampled and knocked about as if by the weight of many heavy feet. A minute later the main block of the château stood out before her, like a solid mass silhouetted against a jade-coloured sky. Just above the pointed roof of the turret at the furthest angle of the façade, a star shone with a cold, silvery radiance.

===Chinese===
说来奇怪，园子的大门竟四敞大开——这可是少有的事，平日里为了防备拦路劫匪和流浪汉，大门总是关得严严实实的。看门的老皮埃尔（Pierre）也不见踪影。弗勒蕾特沿着铺沙的林荫道一路跑上去，道旁夹着洋槐和接骨木的树丛，几记急弯，一路通到宅子跟前。暮色正缓缓沉入黑夜，可即便透过愈聚愈浓的昏暗，弗勒蕾特也看得出，这条平日搂耙得漂漂亮亮、整整洁洁的林荫道，如今满遭践踏、凌乱不堪，像被许多沉重的脚碾踏过一般。一分钟后，庄园的正宅已经耸立在她眼前，宛如一大团结实的黑影，衬在玉色的天上。就在宅子正面最远一角那座塔楼的尖顶上方，一颗星正吐着清冷的银辉。

===Original===
The entrance into the main building was under a broad archway which intersected the façade and led into the great farmyard and to the sheds and farm buildings. Fleurette felt vaguely conscious that something unusual had occurred at the château; though the place looked peaceful enough, it appeared strangely deserted, at this hour, when usually men and maids were still about their work. She slipped quickly under the archway, and turning sharply to the left, she came to the great paved hall where servants and farm hands sat at meals.

===Chinese===
正宅的入口开在一道宽阔的拱门底下，拱门横贯宅子正面，通向大场院和一应棚舍、农房。弗勒蕾特隐隐觉出庄园里出了什么不寻常的事；这地方看上去尽管够安静，此时却冷清得出奇——往常这个时辰，男女仆役们本该还在各忙各的活计。她飞快地溜进拱门，猛地向左一拐，来到那座铺石的大厅，仆人和雇工们平日就是在那里用饭的。

===Original===
She found the place in a strange state of confusion: the men﻿—they were all old men these days, as all the young ones had had to go and join the army and fight the English﻿—the men were standing about in groups, talking and gesticulating with their arms, after the manner of the people of Dauphiné who are glib of speech and free with their gestures; the maids were gathered together in the dark corner of the room, holding their aprons to their eyes. The oil lamp which hung from the whitewashed ceiling had not yet been lit: only one or two tallow-candles on the table guttered in their pewter sconces.

===Chinese===
她只见这地方乱作一团，透着古怪：男仆们——如今尽是老头儿了，年轻力壮的都不得不投军去打英国人——正三五成群地站着，挥着胳膊谈说比划，一派多菲内人的做派，那地方的人本来就口齿伶俐、手势豪放；女仆们则挤在屋子昏暗的角落里，围裙掩着眼睛。刷白的天花板上悬着的那盏油灯还没有点：只有桌上一两支牛油蜡烛，在锡镴烛台上淌着烛泪，摇曳欲熄。

===Original===
Old Mathieu, who was the acknowledged father of the staff and who was affectionately called Papa by the maids, was the first to spy Fleurette, who stood disconcerted in the doorway.

===Chinese===
老马蒂厄（Mathieu）是阖府仆役公认的老前辈，女仆们都亲亲热热唤他「老爹」；头一个望见怔在门口的弗勒蕾特的，正是他。

===Original===
“Ah! Mam’zelle Fleurette! Mam’zelle Fleurette!” he exclaimed and lifted his hands and cast up his eyes with an expression of woe: “*Quel malheur! Mon Dieu, quel malheur!*”

===Chinese===
「啊！弗勒蕾特小姐！弗勒蕾特小姐！」他失声叫道，双手一扬，两眼上翻，满脸悲苦：「*Quel malheur! Mon Dieu, quel malheur!*」（多大的祸事啊！我的天，多大的祸事啊！）

===Original===
He had on his bottle-green coat, his buckled shoes, and the white cotton gloves which he wore when he served the family at meals upstairs. They had just finished dinner, it seems, when the awful calamity occurred.

===Chinese===
他身上还穿着那件瓶绿色上衣、带扣的鞋子，戴着他上楼伺候主人用饭时才戴的白棉纱手套。看来那场可怕的灾祸降临时，主子们才刚用罢晚餐。

===Original===
“But what is it, papa?” Fleurette asked, feeling quite ready to cry in sympathy. “What has happened?”

===Chinese===
「可是到底怎么了，老爹？」弗勒蕾特问，一颗心已经酸酸的，直想陪着掉泪。「出了什么事？」

===Original===
“The soldiers, mam’zelle!” papa replied, and a fresh groan went the round of men and women alike, and one or two of the girls sobbed aloud.

===Chinese===
「来大兵了，小姐！」老爹答道。男女仆役间又是一阵新的哀叹，有一两个姑娘索性放声抽泣起来。

===Original===
Now as far as Fleurette was concerned, as recently as this very morning, the inner meaning of these words “the soldiers!” would not perhaps have had much significance. In her own little home, by Bibi’s strict orders, politics and social questions were never discussed. Fleurette was not supposed to know anything of the conflicts that were raging in the great cities, in the name of liberty and of fraternity. The horrors of summary arrests, of perquisitions, of sentences without trial, of wholesale executions, of hatred and revenge and lust were supposed to be beyond her ken; and knowing Bibi’s abhorrence of those subjects being broached, she kept her counsels and her knowledge to herself. But Fleurette was not brainless, and she had a large heart. With her brain she had noted many things which were wilfully kept away from her, and her kind heart had often been filled with pity at many of the tales which she had heard in the village, tales of suffering under this new kind of tyranny, wielded, it seems, in the name of liberty and of the brotherhood of man. She had heard many things and had forgotten nothing; but somehow until this morning these things had seemed remote, like the tales of ogres and demons which are told to frighten children. She had not disbelieved them, but vaguely she felt that nothing of the sort could possibly happen to people whom she knew and loved.

===Chinese===
在弗勒蕾特这方面，就在今天早上以前，「来大兵了！」这几个字的内里分量，她或许还体会不深。在她自己的小家里，按比比的严令，政治和社会问题是从不许谈论的。大城市里以自由和博爱之名闹得沸反盈天的那些纷争，原不该让弗勒蕾特知道分毫。肆意逮捕、抄家搜查、不经审讯的判罪、成批的处决，还有仇恨、报复与贪欲的种种恐怖，本该都在她的见闻之外；她也深知比比最忌讳这些话头被提起，便把自家的见识揣在心里，不出一声。可弗勒蕾特并非没有脑子，她还有一副宽厚的心肠。她用那颗脑袋，早把旁人存心瞒她的事记下了许多；她那副好心肠，也常常为村里听来的许多传闻而满怀怜悯——说的尽是这种新式暴虐之下的苦难，而那暴虐，据说还在以自由和人类博爱的名义行使着。她听过许多事，一件也不曾忘；只是不知怎么，直到今天早上，这些都还显得遥远，像讲来吓唬孩子的妖魔鬼怪的故事。她并非不信，只是隐隐觉得，这一类事万万不会落到她认识和亲爱的人身上。

===Original===
But since this morning many things had occurred which had widened her range of vision. Amédé, who did not want to go away, was being dragged from home in order to be made into a soldier and to fight the English. She had actually seen some of those soldiers, ragged, uncouth and unkempt, with their officer, like a great bully, speaking to her, as if she were a mere slut out of the streets. He had jeered when she told him that she was Citizen Armand’s daughter, and the soldiers had nudged one another and seemed to mock her when she met their glance. Then again she had heard mysterious voices and seen something in the person of a decrepit old faggot-carrier that had thrilled and puzzled her. All these things had worked a subtle change in Fleurette. The tales of ogres and demons no longer appeared quite so remote. The fact that there were evil and sorrow in the world had in a vague kind of way been brought home to her, and also that the spectre of death and misery of which she had only heard was actually lurking in this peaceful corner of Dauphiné and had already knocked at this very door.

===Chinese===
可打今天早上起，桩桩件件的事情接连发生，把她的眼界拓宽了。阿梅代本不愿离家，却硬生生要被拖去当兵，去打英国人。她还亲眼见过那样一些兵：褴褛、粗野、邋遢，那军官像个大恶霸似的对她说话，只当她是个街头拉来的贱货。她说自己是公民阿尔芒的女儿，他反倒嗤笑；士兵们彼此用胳膊肘你捅我、我捅你，待她迎上他们的目光，分明是在嘲弄她。再后来，她又听见了那神秘的嗓音，又在那衰朽的背柴老人身上瞧出了叫她心头一颤、百思不解的东西。这一切，在弗勒蕾特身上起了悄然的变化。妖魔鬼怪的故事，再不像先前那样遥远了。世上原有邪恶与苦难这回事，已隐隐约约落到了她心上；还有那她只听人说起过的死亡与凄惨的幽灵，原来当真潜伏在多菲内这个安谧的角落里，而且已经敲响了这一家的门。

===Original===
“The soldiers!” meant something to her now.

===Chinese===
「来大兵了！」这几个字，此刻对她有了实实在在的分量。

===Original===
“What happened?” she asked, and a dozen tongues were ready to embark on the telling of the tragic event. It was just after dinner. Madame and Mademoiselle had retired to the boudoir, as usual, and Monsieur was sipping his wine in the dining-room, when the great bell at the gate clanged loudly. Pierre, who was still at work in the stables, ran to open the gate: he was almost knocked down by two men on horseback who, without a word or question rode past him along the avenue up to the house followed by a dozen men or more in tattered uniforms and wearing dirty red caps on their heads. The sound of horses and of men stamping the ground brought some of the maids and farmhands out into the yard. The soldiers had come to a halt under the archway, the two riders then dismounted and ordered André to take their horses round to the stables. André, of course, did not dare disobey. Then, as the entrance door was closed, one of the soldiers knocked loudly against it with the butt-end of his musket, whilst one of those who had been on horseback and who appeared to be in authority called out summarily:

===Chinese===
「出了什么事？」她问。十几张嘴顿时都抢着要讲这桩惨祸。事情出在晚餐刚过。夫人和小姐照例退进闺房去了，先生还在饭厅里啜着他的酒，大门上那口大钟忽然当啷啷响得震天。还在马厩里干活的皮埃尔跑去开门：险些被两个骑马的人撞翻在地，那两人一言不发、一句不问，纵马从他身边掠过，径直沿林荫道奔向宅子，身后跟着十来个穿破烂军装、头上扣着脏红帽的兵。马蹄和人踏地的声响，把几个女仆和雇工引到了场院里。士兵们在拱门底下停住，两名骑手随即翻身下马，吩咐安德烈（André）把马牵去马厩。安德烈自然不敢不依。接着，见正门关着，一个士兵便抡起枪托，咚咚地把门砸得山响；骑马人里像是掌权的那个，不容分说地厉声喝道：

===Original===
“Open in the name of the Republic!”

===Chinese===
「以共和国的名义，开门！」

===Original===
Old Mathieu, who was upstairs clearing away the dinner things, terribly scared, ran down to open the door. Again without a word or question, the soldiers pushed past him until they came to the vestibule where they demanded to know where were the ci-devant Frontenacs. Old Mathieu here paused in his narrative and once more threw up his hands and cast up his eyes in horror.

===Chinese===
老马蒂厄正在楼上收拾杯盘，吓得魂不附体，跑下楼来开门。士兵们又是一言不发、一句不问，从他身边挤了过去，直来到前厅，在那里厉声盘问旧贵族（ci-devant）弗朗特纳克一家人的下落。讲到这里，老马蒂厄停了下来，再一次双手一摊、两眼上翻，满脸惊恐。

===Original===
“Ci-devant, Mam’zelle!” he exclaimed. “I ask you! Just as those devils up in Paris talked of our poor martyred King and Queen!”

===Chinese===
「旧贵族，小姐！」他嚷道，「您听听！这不正跟巴黎那帮魔鬼称呼我们可怜的殉难国王和王后一个样吗！」

===Original===
Of course he tried to stop the brigands from going up to see Madame like that, in their dusty shoes and dirty clothes. But what could he do alone among so many? Ah! if only Baptiste and Jean, Achille and Henri had been there, as in the good old days, fine sturdy fellows of the Dauphiné: they would soon have got the better of these down-at-heel bandits, and if it was a case of protecting Madame and Mademoiselle, why! there would have been some broken heads, and the soldiers of the Republic would have sung another song than they were singing now﻿—the muckworms! But there! Henri and André and the lot of the young ones had all been taken for cannon-fodder, to fight against the English, and there were only a few fogies left now like he﻿—Mathieu﻿—and the women.

===Chinese===
他当然想拦住那帮强盗，不让他们就那么蹬着满脚尘土的鞋、裹着一身脏衣裳上楼去见夫人。可他孤身一个，对方人多势众，他有什么办法？唉！要是巴普蒂斯特（Baptiste）和让（Jean）、阿希尔和亨利（Henri）都还在就好了，像从前的好年月那样，个个都是多菲内响当当的壮实汉子：这几个鞋跟磨塌的匪类，早叫他们收拾得服服帖帖；既是保护夫人和小姐的事，哼！保管要打破几颗脑袋，共和国的兵也唱不成眼下这支歌了——一群粪堆里的蛆！可是有什么法子！亨利、安德烈，还有那一拨年轻人，全都当了炮灰，拉去打英国人了；如今只剩下几个老朽——他，马蒂厄——和这帮娘儿们。

===Original===
Anyway, poor old papa was helpless. All he could do was to precede those hellhounds upstairs, so that he might at least warn Monsieur of what was coming. But even this they would not let him do; as soon as he had reached the upstairs landing, the same man who had ordered him to open the front door in the name of the Republic, and who wore a tricolour sash round his middle, this same man grabbed him by the shoulder and thrust him aside as if he were a bundle of faggots. And without more ado, he just walked into the dining-room where Monsieur was still quietly sipping his last glass of wine.

===Chinese===
总而言之，可怜的老爹是无能为力了。他能做的，只有抢在那群地狱恶狗前头上楼，好歹先给先生通个信。可连这个他们也不容他做；他刚上到楼梯口，就是先前喝令他以共和国名义开大门的那个、腰里缠着三色绶带的家伙，一把攥住他的肩膀，把他往旁边一搡，仿佛搡的是一捆柴火。那人二话不说，径直闯进饭厅——先生还在那里安安静静啜着他最后的一杯酒。

===Original===
From seeing Monsieur sitting there, the beautiful long-stemmed wineglass in his hand, his face quite serene, you would have thought that he had heard nothing of the turmoil on the stairs. But he had heard everything, the tramping of feet, the rough voices, the curt command to open in the name of the Republic. He knew what was coming. Perhaps he had expected it long ago. It was well to be prepared for anything these days. Anyway, there he sat, glass in hand, his elbow resting on the table, where Mathieu had but a few minutes ago been engaged in clearing away the dessert. At the rude entry made by all those ragamuffins into his beautifully ordered dining-room, he just turned his head and looked at the men.

===Chinese===
瞧先生坐在那里的模样——手中端着那只杯脚细长的漂亮酒杯，一脸安详——你会以为楼梯上那场喧嚷他一字也没听见。其实他什么都听见了：杂沓的脚步、粗厉的人声、那句以共和国名义开门的简短喝令。他知道来的是什么。兴许他早就料到有这一天了。如今这世道，无论什么都该防个备。总之，他就那么坐着，杯在手中，臂肘支在桌上——几分钟之前，马蒂厄还在那张桌上收拾餐后果点。眼见那群破衣烂衫的小无赖鲁鲁莽莽闯进他收拾得漂漂亮亮的饭厅，他只是回过头去，朝那些人望了望。

===Original===
“In the name of the Republic,” the man with the sash said curtly.

===Chinese===
「以共和国的名义。」缠绶带的那人简短地说。

===Original===
Monsieur put his glass down and rose slowly to his feet:

===Chinese===
先生放下酒杯，缓缓站起身来：

===Original===
“What is it you want?” he asked quietly.

===Chinese===
「你们要什么？」他平静地问。

===Original===
“The rest of the family, first of all,” the man with the sash replied. “I want you all here together.”

===Chinese===
「先把家里其余的人交出来，」缠绶带的人答道，「我要你们全家聚齐在这儿。」

===Original===
“Madame de Frontenac and my daughter Rose are not at home,” said Monsieur, still speaking very quietly.

===Chinese===
「弗朗特纳克夫人和我女儿罗丝不在家。」先生说，口气仍旧十分平静。

===Original===
“That’s a lie,” the other retorted. “They were at meal here with you.”

===Chinese===
「撒谎，」对方顶了回来，「她们方才还同你一道在这儿用饭。」

===Original===
And with careless finger he pointed to the serviettes and plates which still littered the table. Monsieur did not wince under the insult; nor was the saying of such a brigand an insult to so high-minded a gentleman as Monsieur. All he said was:

===Chinese===
说着他漫不经心地用手指了指还摊在桌上的餐巾和盘子。先生受这番侮辱，眉头也不皱一下；这样一个强盗嘴里的话，原也辱不着先生这般心气高洁的绅士。他只说了一句：

===Original===
“That is so. Madame and Mile de Frontenac were at dinner with me, until half an hour ago when they left the house together.”

===Chinese===
「不假。弗朗特纳克夫人和弗朗特纳克小姐方才确是同我共进的晚餐，直到半小时前，她们才一同离开了宅子。」

===Original===
“Whither did they go?”

===Chinese===
「她们去哪儿了？」

===Original===
“That I do not know.”

===Chinese===
「这我不知道。」

===Original===
“Which is another lie.”

===Chinese===
「又是一句谎话。」

===Original===
“If I did know,” Monsieur rejoined imperturbably, “I would not tell you.”

===Chinese===
「就算知道，」先生不动声色地答道，「也不会告诉你们。」

===Original===
“We’ll soon see about that,” the man with the sash said grimly. He then turned to the soldier who appeared to be in command over the others: “*Allons!* citizen lieutenant,” he said curtly, “the rest is your business. The two women have got to be found. That is the first thing, after that we shall see.”

===Chinese===
「咱们很快就见分晓。」缠绶带的人冷冷地说。随即他转向那个像是统带其余士兵的军官：「*Allons!*（来吧）公民中尉，」他简短地说，「底下就是你的事了。那两个女人必须找到。这是头一件，之后的事再说。」

===Original===
The officer then ordered two of his men to stand on guard over Monsieur, and since then the tramp, tramp of the soldiers’ feet had resounded throughout the château. Upstairs they went, and downstairs; in Madame’s room and in Mademoiselle’s, in the kitchen, the stables, the offices. They interrogated the men, they bullied the women; they turned everything topsy-turvy; they raked about in the hay and the straw of the stables, they scoured the park, they glued their ugly, dirty noses to the sanded paths, to try and find the imprint of footsteps. But neither of Madame or of Mademoiselle had they yet found a trace. They were still at it, raking and scouring and searching. In the intervals they tried to browbeat Monsieur, threatening him with summary shooting one moment, which only made him laugh and shrug his shoulders, and promising him immunity for his womenfolk if he would say where they could be found. But these promises only made Monsieur laugh and again shrug his shoulders.

===Chinese===
军官随即派了两名士兵看守先生；打那时起，士兵们踏踏的脚步声便响彻了整座庄园。他们楼上楼下地跑；夫人的房里、小姐的房里、厨房、马厩、各处下房，一处不落。他们盘问男仆，威吓女仆；把一切翻了个底朝天；在马厩的干草麦秸里又扒又搂；把园子整个篦了一遍；把丑陋的脏鼻子贴到铺沙的小径上，想寻出脚印来。可无论是夫人还是小姐，连一丝踪迹也没寻着。他们还在那儿搜，又扒又篦又找。空下来的间隙，他们就轮番吓唬先生：一会儿威胁要就地枪决，只惹得他一笑耸肩；一会儿又许诺保全他家的女眷，只要他说出人藏在哪儿。可这些许诺也只是惹得先生发笑，再耸一耸肩。

===Original===
“Immunity?” he said. “They have that already, thank God! for they are beyond your reach now. If they were not, do you think I would trust to your promises?”

===Chinese===
「保全？」他说，「这个她们已经有了，感谢上帝！因为你们如今够不着她们了。倘若够得着，你们以为我会信你们的许诺吗？」

===Original===
Old Mathieu paused. The story had neared its end:﻿—this tale of woe and anxiety and horror, such as the worthy old man had never thought to see. The others had not much to say; the maids were still crying, with excitement rather than with grief, and the old men had stared open-mouthed, or sagely nodded their heads.

===Chinese===
老马蒂厄住了口。故事临近了尾声：——这样一出忧惶骇人的惨剧，这位忠厚老人生平从没想到自己会亲眼见着。旁人没有什么可说的；女仆们还在哭，与其说是伤心，不如说是激动；老头儿们有的张着嘴发怔，有的煞有介事地点头。

===Original===
“Then,” Fleurette put in at last, “Madame and Mademoiselle have gone. Really﻿—really gone?”

===Chinese===
「那么，」弗勒蕾特终于插口道，「夫人和小姐是走了。真——真的走了？」

===Original===
Mathieu nodded with another sigh, half of perplexity, half of woe.

===Chinese===
马蒂厄又叹了口气，点了点头，一半是惶惑，一半是伤悲。

===Original===
“But whither?” Fleurette insisted. “How? Why?”

===Chinese===
「可是去了哪儿呢？」弗勒蕾特追问，「怎么走的？为什么走？」

===Original===
“God alone knows, Mam’zelle,” papa averred. “He has spirited Madame and Mademoiselle away to save them from these brigands.”

===Chinese===
「只有上帝知道，小姐，」老爹一口断言，「是上帝显灵，把夫人和小姐摄走了，好救她们躲开这帮强盗。」

===Original===
“Did anybody see them go?”

===Chinese===
「有人瞧见她们走吗？」

===Original===
Men and maids shook their heads. No one had seen Madame or Mademoiselle go. Old Mathieu was the last to have seen the ladies. He had just begun to clear the table, when they rose, and, as was their custom, went through to the boudoir. Mathieu had opened the door for them. And now he came to think of it, the ladies had each kissed Monsieur very tenderly before they went out of the room. Yes! the kiss had seemed like a farewell. Mathieu shook his head dolefully: he remembered it now, but hadn’t thought anything about it at the moment. Monsieur certainly appeared more thoughtful. Usually, while he drank his last glass of wine and Mathieu was engaged in washing the silver in the large copper bowl which he always brought into the room for that purpose, Monsieur would chat with him, talk over the gossip of the day. But tonight he had been unusually silent. Yes! Mathieu now remembered quite distinctly about the kiss, and about Monsieur being so silent. But he certainly had noticed nothing else unusual, until the moment when those brigands banged at the door and demanded admittance in the name of their godless Republic.

===Chinese===
男仆女仆都摇了头。夫人或小姐是怎么走的，谁也没有瞧见。最后见到两位女士的，是老马蒂厄。他刚动手收拾桌子，两位女士便起了身，照她们的习惯，穿过门进了闺房。马蒂厄替她们开的门。如今回想起来，两位女士在出屋之前，都各自同先生温存地吻别过。是啊！那一吻如今想来竟像是诀别。马蒂厄凄凄惶惶地摇头：他现在记起来了，当时却没往心里去。先生的确显得心事更重。往常，先生喝他最后一杯酒、马蒂厄在专为此搬进屋来的那只大铜盆里涮洗银餐具时，先生总要同他闲聊，讲讲当天的闲话新闻。可今晚他沉默得出奇。是啊！那一吻，还有先生那份沉默，马蒂厄如今都记得清清楚楚。可直到那帮强盗砸门、抬出他们那个目无神明的共和国名义强要进来的那一刻，他实在没有觉出旁的什么异样。

===Original===
Mathieu was on the stairs at that moment, so he did not know how Monsieur had looked when he heard all the tramping and the noise. But Madame and Mademoiselle were gone, of that there could be no doubt. The brigands had searched for them, like so many dogs digging for a bone, and not a trace was there of the two ladies, for the *bon Dieu*, no doubt, had made them invisible.

===Chinese===
那会儿马蒂厄正在楼梯上，所以先生听见那阵杂沓脚步与喧闹时是什么脸色，他没瞧见。可夫人和小姐走了，这是千真万确、毋庸置疑的。那帮强盗搜她们，活像一群刨骨头的狗，可两位女士踪影全无——不用问，是好天主（*bon Dieu*）把她们隐了形。

===Original===
Of old Mathieu and the staff, the officer in command took no notice, after he had summarily ordered them to muster up in the hall; he counted up the indoor-servants and the farmhands; those who had their homes outside the precincts of the château, he ordered roughly out of the place.

===Chinese===
带队的军官把老马蒂厄和阖府仆役简简单单喝令到大厅里集中之后，便再不把他们放在心上；他点了点室内仆役和雇工的数目；凡家安在庄园院墙之外的，都被他粗暴地轰出了这个地方。

===Original===
“Get back to your homes!” he had said to them, after he had inspected and questioned them; “and stay there quietly, if you value your lives.”

===Chinese===
「都回家去！」他查验盘问过他们之后，对他们说过这样一句，「安安分分待在家里，要是还惜命的话。」

===Original===
So there were only half a dozen old men, the four girls and the staff’s cook left in the château. All of them were scared, and as Mam’zelle Fleurette could see, they just stood about and talked and talked while the girls did nothing but cry. He﻿—Mathieu﻿—could do nothing with any of them. The work of the house ought to be carried on; none of them had had any supper yet. But there! young and old, they were, all of them, too much upset to work or to eat; and the tramp-tramp-tramp, upstairs and downstairs was nerve-shattering to everybody.

===Chinese===
于是庄园里只剩下六个老头儿、四个姑娘，外加宅里的厨娘。个个都吓坏了；弗勒蕾特小姐也看得出来，他们就那么四下里站着，说了又说，姑娘们则只顾哭。他——马蒂厄——对谁也没了办法。家里的活计本该照常做下去；晚饭他们也谁都还没吃上一口。可是有什么法子！老的少的，全都乱了方寸，做活也做不动，吃饭也吃不下；那楼上楼下踏踏踏踏的脚步声，震得人人神经发颤。

===Original===
Fleurette listened to the amazing story until the end. As Mathieu said, there was the ceaseless tramping of feet still going on. They﻿—those horrible soldiers of the Republic, unworthy to be called Frenchmen﻿—were still searching for Madame and Mademoiselle in order to drag them to Orange where the awful guillotine had been at work these months past; or perhaps even to Paris﻿—that den of horrors beside which the stories of demons and ogres were but trivial tales.

===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特把这桩骇人听闻的事一直听到结尾。正像马蒂厄说的，那没个停歇的脚步声还在响着。他们——共和国那帮可怕的兵，不配再称作法国人的——还在搜夫人和小姐，要把她们拖去奥朗日，那座可怕的断头台几个月来一直在那里开工；没准还要拖去巴黎——那座恐怖的渊薮，同它一比，妖魔鬼怪的故事都不过是无足轻重的小话。

===Original===
Madame and Mademoiselle! who never in their lives had done any harm to anyone: but rather spent every hour of the day planning and executing kind deeds! And Mademoiselle! so delicate and frail that even her father, who idolized her, hardly dared touch her. And now these men, these rough and uncouth soldiers, with their harsh voices and bullying ways, to think of their approaching Mademoiselle, pushing her, dragging her, it made Fleurette’s blood boil even to think of such a possibility. No wonder that the *bon Dieu* had made them invisible to the eyes of all those bandits.

===Chinese===
夫人和小姐！生平从没伤害过任何人，反倒把每天的时时刻刻都用在筹划善举、施行善事上！还有小姐！那样娇弱单薄，连把她奉若神明的父亲，都轻易不敢碰她一碰。如今竟是这些人，这些粗野无文的士兵，粗声恶气、横行霸道——一想到他们会凑到小姐跟前，推她拽她，单是想到有这种可能，弗勒蕾特就热血直往头上涌。难怪好天主要叫她们在那一群强盗眼里隐了形。

===Original===
Tramp! tramp! tramp! and now a loud banging as if pieces of furniture, chairs, tables were being overturned, and then a crash, as of broken china!

===Chinese===
踏！踏！踏！跟着又是一阵轰隆的砸响，像是家具、椅子、桌子被人掀翻，接着哗啦一声脆响，像是有瓷器摔碎了！

===Original===
“Holy Virgin!” Papa Mathieu exclaimed with a loud groan; “to think of Madame’s beautiful things! Those brigands are furious at not finding Madame and Mademoiselle, and are venting their wrath on inanimate things.”

===Chinese===
「圣母啊！」马蒂厄老爹大声悲叹道，「想想夫人那些漂亮的物事吧！那帮强盗找不着夫人和小姐，气急败坏，正拿这些没有生命的东西撒火呢。」

===Original===
It was these words of old Mathieu that sent Fleurette’s thoughts flying in another direction﻿—back to the early afternoon of this memorable day﻿—back to the first visit of these awful soldiers, and to the faggot-carrier with his bundle tied up in sacking. From thence to the voice! The mysterious voice that had told her where valuables and papers were to be found. It was such a flash of recollection that her whole face became transfigured; anxiety and superstitious awe gave place to that same fervour which had animated her when she met the eyes of the faggot-carrier: eyes that conveyed a message, which at last she was beginning to understand.

===Chinese===
正是老马蒂厄这几句话，让弗勒蕾特的思绪霍地飞向另一个方向——飞回这个难忘日子的午后——飞回这伙可怕的大兵头一回上门，飞回那背柴老人和他用麻袋扎起的柴捆。再从那里，飞向那个声音！那个告诉她值钱东西和文件下落的神秘声音。回忆电光石火般一闪，她整张脸都变了样；焦灼与迷信的敬畏，让位给了那股热忱——同她迎上背柴老人目光时胸中激荡的，正是同一股；那双眼睛里传递着一道讯息，她终于开始懂得了。

===Original===
“Papa!” she cried impulsively.

===Chinese===
「老爹！」她按捺不住地喊了出来。

===Original===
“Yes, Mam’zelle?” Mathieu asked with another sigh.

===Chinese===
「什么事，小姐？」马蒂厄又叹了口气，问道。

===Original===
“Did anything else happen?﻿—I mean anything unusual?﻿—Did Madame﻿—or Monsieur﻿—receive a letter? a message? or﻿—or did any other stranger come to the château this afternoon?”

===Chinese===
「还出过别的什么事吗？——我是说，不寻常的事？——夫人——或者先生——收没收到过什么信？什么口信？还是——还是今天下午有别的什么生人到庄园里来过？」

===Original===
“Oh think, Papa Mathieu, think,” she implored with tears of agitation choking her voice. “I cannot tell you how important it is. Try to remember﻿—was there anything?﻿—anybody?﻿—”

===Chinese===
「哦，想想吧，马蒂厄老爹，想想吧，」她恳求道，激动的泪水哽住了嗓音，「这有多要紧，我跟您说不清。好好想想——有没有什么事？——有没有什么人？——」

===Original===
Papa persistently shook his head, until Pierre, who was the gatekeeper, reminded him that Monsieur had gone down the avenue as far as the gate, just ten minutes before dinner was served.

===Chinese===
老爹只是一个劲儿摇头，直到看门的皮埃尔提醒他：开晚饭前十来分钟，先生曾沿着林荫道一直走到大门口去。

===Original===
“There’s nothing very unusual in that,” Mathieu retorted. “Monsieur is often out just before dinner is served.”

===Chinese===
「那有什么好稀奇的，」马蒂厄抢白道，「开晚饭前，先生常在外面走走。」

===Original===
“Yes!” Pierre insisted. “But what did he do this evening? He walked straight to the gate, which I had closed half an hour before. I saw him. He walked straight to the gate, he did, and you know the old acacia tree just the other side? Well! Monsieur put his foot on a bar of the gate and reached over to the forked branch of the old tree. I saw him quite plainly, I tell you. And when he walked back to the house he had a piece of paper in his hand with some writing on it, which he was reading. And I think, papa,” Pierre concluded triumphantly, “you’ll have to admit that there was something unusual in that.”

===Chinese===
「有！」皮埃尔不依不饶，「可今天傍晚他是去做什么了？他直奔大门口去了——那扇门，我提前半个钟头就关上了。我亲眼瞧见的。他直直地朝大门口走去，一点不假；您知道紧挨着大门外头那棵老洋槐树吧？嘿！先生一脚踩上门上的横档，探身够着了那棵老树的杈枝。我跟您说，我瞧得清清楚楚。等他往宅子走回来的时候，手里就捏着一张纸，纸上写着字，他一边走一边看。所以我说呀，老爹，」皮埃尔得意洋洋地下了结论，「您可得承认，这里头是有点不寻常了。」

===Original===
But Mathieu, with the obstinacy of old age and long service, would not admit it, even now.

===Chinese===
可是马蒂厄凭着年高资深养出的那股固执，到这会儿也不肯认账。

===Original===
“Monsieur,” he said, “met the mail-carrier at the gate, he often comes at this hour. He gave Monsieur a letter. Monsieur often gets letters﻿—”

===Chinese===
「先生，」他说，「是在大门口碰上了邮差，那人常在这个时辰来。他给了先生一封信。先生常收着信——」

===Original===
But here André interposed. Old André﻿—they were all of them old﻿—worked in the stables, and it was he who had taken the two horses from the soldiers when ordered to do so, and walked them round to the stables. It was then that he noticed two beggars hanging about in the yard: a man and a woman. He had peremptorily ordered them off the premises.

===Chinese===
可说到这里，安德烈插了话。老安德烈——他们全都是老头儿了——在马厩里干活；方才奉命接过士兵那两匹马、牵着送到马厩去的，正是他。也就是在那时候，他留意到场院里晃荡着两个叫花子：一男一女。他疾言厉色，把他们轰出了院子。

===Original===
“Beggars!” Fleurette exclaimed eagerly. “What were they like?”

===Chinese===
「叫花子！」弗勒蕾特急切地叫起来，「他们什么模样？」

===Original===
André said that as the sun was in his eyes he couldn’t see them very well. There was a man and a woman. He was busy with the horses and upset by the arrival of all these brigands. The woman he couldn’t see at all because of the shawl which covered her head, but he recollected that the man was a big fellow, bent nearly double under a huge bundle tied up in sacking.

===Chinese===
安德烈说，那会儿日头正晃着他的眼，两人他都没瞧真切。是一男一女。他当时忙着照应马匹，又叫这帮强盗的闯进来搅得心慌意乱。那女的他压根没看清，因为头上蒙着披肩；可他记得，那男的是个块头很大的汉子，让一大捆用麻袋扎起来的东西压得几乎折成了两截。

===Original===
“When I spoke to him,” André went on, “he mumbled something or other, but I just told him to clear out, he and his woman; we’d enough of vagabonds, I said, in the place with all these soldiers.”

===Chinese===
「我同他说话的时候，」安德烈接着讲，「他含含糊糊咕哝了几句什么，我就叫他滚蛋，带上他婆娘一块儿滚；我说，这地方光这些大兵就够乱的了，流浪汉一个也不缺了。」

===Original===
“And did he go?” Fleurette asked.

===Chinese===
「那他走了吗？」弗勒蕾特问。

===Original===
“Yes. I must admit that he went off quite quietly after that. I did not think he meant any evil, because when he first caught sight of me he did not attempt to hide or to run away.”

===Chinese===
「走了。我得承认，打那以后，他走得安安静静。我没当他不怀好意，因为他刚瞧见我那会儿，既没想躲，也没想跑。」

===Original===
“If he had,” André went on after a moment or two, “I would have been after him pretty quickly, and wanted to know what was in that big bundle.”

===Chinese===
「他要是躲了跑了，」安德烈过了一两分钟又接着说，「我立马就追上去了，还要问个明白，那大捆里头装的是什么。」

===Original===
He paused, a look of perplexity and of shamefacedness came over his wrinkled old face while he thoughtfully scratched his head: “Now I think of it,” he said, “I ought to have inspected that bundle. It looked mighty heavy for faggots or for rags. Perhaps he had been up to no good after all﻿—And directly after I lost sight of him and his woman I saw a whole lot of faggots lying in a heap close by the stable door.”

===Chinese===
他顿住了，皱纹纵横的老脸上浮起一层又惶惑又赧然的神色，一面沉吟着一面挠着头：「如今想起来，」他说，「我本该查一查那捆东西的。瞧那分量，装柴火也罢、装破布也罢，都沉得蹊跷。没准他到底不怀好意——而且，才一转眼不见了他和他婆娘的影儿，我就瞧见马厩门边堆着好大一堆柴。」

===Original===
The other old men and the maids had gathered closer round André and Fleurette. This was the first they had heard of the old vagabond and his woman, and the bundle which appeared so heavy.

===Chinese===
其余的老头儿和女仆们都围拢过来，把安德烈和弗勒蕾特围在当中。老流浪汉和他婆娘的事，还有那捆瞧着沉甸甸的东西，他们都是头一回听说。

===Original===
“You certainly ought to have inspected that bundle, André,” Mathieu said sententiously. He felt that there was a chance of recapturing his dignity which seemed to have been slightly impaired through his argument with Pierre. He could reassert his authority at any rate by rebuking André. “It looks,” he went on, “as if the old vagabond had brought a lot of faggots with him, then turned them out of the sacking and replaced them by God knows what valuables he may have stolen.”

===Chinese===
「你确实本该查一查那捆东西，安德烈。」马蒂厄一本正经地训诫道。他觉出这是挽回体面的一个机会——同皮埃尔那场争辩，似乎把他的体面折损了几分。至少，训一训安德烈，还能重树他的威信。「照这么看，」他接着说，「那老流浪汉是自己带了一堆柴来，把柴打麻袋里倒出来，再塞进天知道他从哪儿偷来的什么值钱物事。」

===Original===
“I was so upset, you understand, papa!” André murmured ruefully.

===Chinese===
「您不知道，我当时心里乱得很哪，老爹！」安德烈懊丧地低声道。

===Original===
“We were all of us upset, as you call it, André,” papa rebuked sternly, “but that is no excuse for neglect of duty.”

===Chinese===
「要说乱，咱们谁不乱，安德烈，」老爹严厉地训斥道，「可这不能当作玩忽职守的借口。」

===Original===
“Don’t scold André, papa,” Fleurette broke in excitedly. “My belief is that the old vagabond, as you call him, was a messenger from the Holy Virgin, sent on purpose to get Madame and Mademoiselle safely out of the way.”

===Chinese===
「别骂安德烈了，老爹，」弗勒蕾特激动地插进来说，「依我看，你们说的那个老流浪汉，是圣母差来的信使，特为把夫人和小姐平平安安救出是非之地来的。」

===Original===
“Oh, Mam’zelle!”

===Chinese===
「哦，小姐！」

===Original===
“From the Holy Virgin!”

===Chinese===
「圣母差来的！」

===Original===
“*Sainte Marie, mere de Dieu, priez pour nous!*” came in chorus from the maids. Even the cook, an elderly woman, jealous of her own dignity, was unable to conceal her excitement. The old men shook their heads, looked wise and sceptical.

===Chinese===
「*Sainte Marie, mere de Dieu, priez pour nous!*」（圣马利亚，天主之母，为我们祈求吧！）女仆们齐声念了起来。连那位上了年纪、一向顾着自己体面的厨娘，也掩不住心头的激动。老头儿们摇着头，一脸高深莫测而又将信将疑的神气。

===Original===
“What makes you say that, Mam’zelle Fleurette?” Mathieu asked in an awed whisper.

===Chinese===
「您怎么会这样想呢，弗勒蕾特小姐？」马蒂厄敬畏地压低嗓音问。

===Original===
But Fleurette was silent now. Already she had repented of having said so much. Discretion would have been so much wiser. That was the worst of her: she always allowed her tongue to run away with her. She looked eagerly from one anxious face to the other: well she knew that the little she had said would be talked over and commented on and be made the subject of gossip until it reached the village and possibly even Serres and Sisteron; and God only knew what harm this might do to Madame and Mademoiselle. She bit the tip of her tongue hard just to punish it for having wagged too freely, and seized with a sudden impulse, which she found irresistible, she snatched up a candle from the table, and incontinently turned and fled out of the hall, leaving the others to gape and stare after her, to scratch their heads, and to conjecture.

===Chinese===
可弗勒蕾特这会儿不作声了。话说了这么多，她已经后悔了。谨言慎行才叫聪明，偏偏她吃亏就吃亏在这上头：舌头总由不得她，一说就收不住。她急切地从一张焦灼的脸望到另一张：她心里明白得很，方才说的那几句，注定要被人翻来覆去地议论、评说，当成闲话的由头，一路传到村里，没准还传到塞尔和锡斯特龙；那会给夫人和小姐招来什么祸害，只有天知道。她狠狠咬了咬舌尖，权当惩罚它多嘴；跟着一阵冲动攫住了她，来势凶猛，由她不得——她从桌上抓起一支蜡烛，转身就逃出了大厅，撇下众人，叫他们目瞪口呆地望着她的背影，挠着头皮，胡猜乱想。

===Original===
Aye! and to gossip too.

===Chinese===
可不是嘛！还要嚼舌根呢。

## VIII / 第八章

===Original===
Perquisitions in these days of Liberty, Fraternity and Equality were perhaps among the minor horrors that befell innocent and guilty alike, at the behest of tyrants more implacable than the Inquisitors of Medieval Spain, more cruel than the Borgias: but they were terrible nevertheless. A perquisition meant, in most cases, the destruction of every household treasure, every family relic cherished through generations, it meant the wanton smashing of furniture and mirrors, the ripping up of valuable tapestries and of mattresses, the defacement of priceless pictures, it meant prying, hostile eyes thrust into receptacles, however secret, into private papers and even letters. Nothing was sacred to men deputed to insult and to offend, just as much as to search.

===Chinese===
在这个号称自由、博爱、平等的年头，搜查或许只是无辜者与有罪者一并遭逢的种种恐怖中较轻的一桩；发号施令的那些暴君，比中世纪西班牙的宗教裁判官更冷酷，比波吉亚家族（Borgias）更狠毒：然而搜查本身终究是可怕的。一场搜查，多半意味着家中每一件珍藏、每一件几代人宝爱的传家旧物尽遭毁灭；意味着家具和镜子被恣意砸烂，值钱的挂毯和床垫被豁开撕碎，无价的图画被涂损玷污；意味着一双双窥探的、满怀敌意的眼睛，捅进再隐秘不过的容器，捅进私人文件乃至书信。那些人奉命而来，既要搜查，也同样要侮辱人、冒犯人；对这样的人，天下没有任何东西是神圣的。

===Original===
When Fleurette reached that part of the house which was occupied by the family, she was confronted by the wildest, the most heart-stirring confusion. The carpets had been torn off the floors, the furniture for the most part lay in broken heaps about the rooms, mirrors and pictures had been dragged off the walls, broken crockery and glass was scattered everywhere, intermingled with horsehair and other stuffing out of chairs and mattresses, whilst all the walls, the doors, the window-frames bore traces of rude handling with bayonet or the heel of a boot. Fleurette, wide-eyed and appalled, ran from room to room; the guttering tallow-candle which she held threw flickering lights and grotesque shadows on the scattered objects about her, made them seem more weird, like the appurtenances of an abode of ghosts. Here in the pretty boudoir Mademoiselle’s embroidery frame lay smashed to tinder wood with threads of the work still hanging to it, bits of rags, pathetic in their look of abandonment and desolation. There in the withdrawing room, the beautiful satinwood spinet with its painted panels and exquisite marqueterie was lying on its side, its body gaping like a gigantic wound, the strings emitting a final vibration like the last song of a dying swan.

===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特走到宅子里主人一家居住的那一片，迎面撞见的是一片最狂乱、最教人心惊的狼藉。地毯从地板上扯了下来，家具大半碎成堆堆倒在各处房间里，镜子和画从墙上拽了下来，破碎的陶器玻璃撒得满地都是，同椅子和床垫里掏出来的马鬃及其他填料混作一处；而四面墙上、门上、窗框上，处处留着刺刀或靴跟粗暴敲凿的痕迹。弗勒蕾特睁大了眼睛，心惊胆战，一间屋一间屋地跑；她手里那支将熄的牛油蜡烛，把摇曳的光和怪诞的影投在四下散乱的物件上，使它们显得愈发诡异，宛如鬼宅里的种种陈设。这边漂亮的小闺房里，小姐的绣架碎成了引火柴，绣线的残缕还挂在上头，点点碎布，那份遭人弃置的荒凉模样好不凄惨。那边内客厅里，那架美丽的缎木斯皮内琴（spinet），画板雅致，细木镶嵌精绝，此刻侧翻在地，琴身豁开一道大口子，宛如一道巨大的创伤，琴弦发出最后一下颤音，恰似垂死天鹅的绝唱。

===Original===
From the direction of the dining-room came the incessant murmur of voices, but throughout the rest of the apartments, in the midst of all the wreckage, a silence reigned as of the grave. The place now was completely deserted. It seemed almost as if some terrible tornado had swept through these living-rooms: some implacable forces of nature rather than the hatred and cupidity of men. An earthquake could not have been more devastating, a fire more destructive.

===Chinese===
饭厅那边传来不住的人语嗡嗡，可其余各处居室，在这满目残破之间，却是一片坟茔般的死寂。这地方如今已空无一人。那情形几乎像是一场可怖的龙卷风横扫过这些起居之所：倒像是某种不容分说的自然伟力，而非人的仇恨与贪欲在作祟。地震也毁坏不到这步田地，大火也荼毒不到这般地步。

===Original===
And now in the midst of it all Fleurette came to a standstill, candle in hand: her breath came and went in quick short gasps, and her heart was beating furiously. The silence in this semidarkness with those long, ghostlike shadows seemed to oppress her; the broken bits of beautiful things which she had known and loved ever since she remembered anything, gave her an awful feeling of desolation and a kind of foreboding of things, still worse, to come. It was instinct which had brought her to a halt here in this one room amongst the others. It was always known as Madame’s room, for here Madame would always sit when she gave her orders to various members of the household, here that she would look through the household accounts whilst Fleurette and Rose, when they were still children, would sit in a corner of the sofa by the huge hearth, hand in hand, with a picture book on their knees, silent like a pair of tiny white mice, waiting until Madame had finished her accounts, because then they would all go into the garden to gather flowers for the rooms, and fruit for dessert, or perhaps go down into the kitchen and learn how to dress a chicken for the table, or how best to mix a salad.

===Chinese===
此刻，在这满目疮痍之间，弗勒蕾特停下了脚步，手里还擎着蜡烛：她的呼吸急促短浅，心口怦怦狂跳。这半明半暗里的寂静，连同那些长长的、鬼影似的暗影，仿佛沉沉压在她身上；这些她自打记事起就熟识并珍爱的美丽物件碎了一地，给她一种可怕的荒凉之感，还有一种大祸未了的预感——更糟的还在后头。是本能让她在这一间屋里、而不是别的哪间，收住了脚步。这间屋子一向叫作「夫人的房间」，因为夫人向宅子里各色人等吩咐事情时，总是坐在这里；她总在这里查阅家用账目，而弗勒蕾特和罗丝还小的时候，就会在大壁炉边沙发的角落里手拉手并排坐着，膝上摊一本图画书，安静得像一对小白鼠，等夫人把账看完——因为账一看完，大家就要到花园里去，给各屋剪花，采饭后果子；再不就下厨房去，学怎样收拾一只上桌的鸡，学怎样拌生菜才拌得最好。

===Original===
And Fleurette stood for a moment or two quite still, holding the candle high above her head, contemplating this wreckage. Then, having found a safe place on which to deposit the candle, she carefully closed the door which gave, like several others, on a long corridor that led to the main staircase at one end and to the service stairs at the other. The time had come to cease contemplation, to drive away superstitious fears and to act. Closing her eyes, Fleurette strove first of all to recapture pictures of long ago, to recreate the scenes enacted in this room, before this awful calamity had fallen on these people whom she loved so dearly. Memory was not rebellious. She could see the whole picture just as it had impressed itself on the tablets of her mind when she used to sit here as a child. There by the window Madame’s desk used to stand. It was lying on its side now, the drawers wrenched open, the handles broken, papers, pen and sand scattered about; the ink had run out of the glass container and stained the beautiful old Aubusson carpet. But there Madame used to sit. Fleurette could almost see her now, at the desk. Her big household books open before her. Writing, calculating, and putting her money by in a leather bag. And presently she would rise, pick up her bag and books and carry them across the room to a spot close to the wall, the other side of the hearth. Here she would come to a standstill, and putting her beautiful hand somewhere against the wall, she would turn to the two girls﻿—they were mere children then﻿—and smile at them in a mysterious way; and they would say solemnly: “Open Sesame!” just as they had heard in the tale of Ali Baba and the forty robbers, which Monsieur de Frontenac had often told them. As soon as they had said the magic words the wall would open like the entrance of the robber’s cave in the tale of Ali Baba, disclosing a recess into which Madame would put her books and her bag of money. Then she would once more turn and give a sign to the children and they would say: “Close Sesame!” and the mysterious door would swing to again and no trace be left of the recess which lay hidden somewhere behind the panelled wall.

===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特一动不动地站了片刻，把蜡烛高举过头，凝望着这片残骸。随后，她寻了个稳妥地方放下蜡烛，仔细掩上了一扇门——这扇门同另外几扇一样，开向一条长走廊，走廊一头通大楼梯，一头通仆人楼梯。时候到了，不能再呆看着了，要驱走迷信的恐惧，动手做事。弗勒蕾特先闭上眼睛，努力追回久远的图景，重现这场可怕的大祸——还没有临到这些她深爱的人们头上之前——在这间屋子里上演过的情景。记忆并不忤逆。整幅图景她看得清清楚楚，一如童年时她坐在这里、它印上她心灵石板的模样。那边窗子跟前，从前立着夫人的书桌。它如今侧翻在地，抽屉被撬开，拉手都拧断了，纸张、笔和吸墨沙撒了一片；墨水从玻璃瓶里泼出来，玷污了那匹美丽的旧奥布松地毯（Aubusson）。可夫人从前总坐在那里。弗勒蕾特此刻几乎能看见她：坐在书桌前，大大的家用账簿摊在面前，写着，算着，把银钱收进一只皮口袋。过一会儿她便起身，拿起皮袋和账簿，穿过房间，走到贴近墙壁的一处地方，在壁炉另一侧。她在那里站定，把一只秀美的手贴在墙上什么地方，转身朝两个小姑娘——那时她们还不过是小不点儿——神秘地一笑；两个小姑娘便一本正经地念道：「芝麻开门！」就像弗朗特纳克先生常讲给她们听的阿里巴巴和四十大盗的故事里那样。咒语一出，墙壁便应声而开，正像故事里强盗洞窟的洞门，露出一个壁龛，夫人便把账簿和银钱袋放进去。然后她再转过身来，朝孩子们示意，她们便念道：「芝麻关门！」那扇神秘的门便又阖上，包镶护板的墙壁里何处藏着壁龛，不留一丝痕迹。

===Original===
The whole of this picture stood out before Fleurette’s mental vision in every detail; the exact spot where Madame used to stand, the way she put out her hand and touched the panelled wall. Carefully picking her way through the maze of broken furniture, Fleurette came to a halt on the very spot where she had so often seen Madame standing, with her books and moneybag in her arms. She put out her hand and touched the panel as Madame had done: all over the carved panel she put her hand, touching and pressing each bit of carving in its turn. Her heart was still beating wildly, but not in any way with fear. In fact she was surprised at herself for not being afraid. It was just the excitement of this wonderful adventure! She, Fleurette, who had seen nothing of the world beyond her own village of Laragne and an occasional glimpse at Sisteron, suddenly found herself guiding the destinies of people whom she loved﻿—the messenger sent by the *bon Dieu* to help them in their need. There is no young human creature living who would not respond, heart and soul, to such a call, and Fleurette was of the South, a child of that romantic land of Dauphiné which had given so many of her heroic sons to strive and work for France.

===Chinese===
整幅图景连同每一处细节，都在弗勒蕾特的心眼中鲜明地立着：夫人惯常站立的确切位置，她伸手抚上护板墙壁的样子。弗勒蕾特小心翼翼地择路穿过破家具的迷宫，恰恰停在她多少回看见夫人抱着账簿和银钱袋站立的地方。她伸出手，像夫人从前那样抚上护板：她把手掌遍覆雕花护板，逐块逐块地又触又按。她的心仍在狂跳，却怎么也不是出于恐惧。说实话，她自己也纳罕竟不害怕。这全然是这场奇妙冒险带来的兴奋！她，弗勒蕾特，除了自家的拉拉涅村、除了偶尔望一眼锡斯特龙，世上什么也没见过，此刻却蓦然发觉，自己竟在指引她所爱的人们的前程命运——她成了好天主（*bon Dieu*）派来救助危难中之人的信使。世上没有一个年轻人不响应这样的召唤——全心全意地响应；何况弗勒蕾特是南方人，是多菲内（Dauphiné）那片浪漫土地的孩子，那片土地曾献出多少英雄儿郎，为法兰西奋斗效力。

===Original===
And suddenly, as Fleurette pressed her finger on every piece of carved relief, one by one, she felt the centre of a dog-rose yield to the pressure. Softly, noiselessly, the panel swung outwards, and there in the recess were the familiar household books and the moneybag. Beside them lay a leather wallet and a small casket fitted with a brass lock. Without any hesitation Fleurette took the bag, the wallet and the casket, leaving the books where they were. Never for a moment did the thought occur to her that she might be discovered in what would be a highly compromising position. She was too simple-minded, too innately honest to think that she might be suspected of theft.

===Chinese===
忽然，弗勒蕾特的手指逐块按压雕花浮雕时，她觉出一朵野蔷薇（dog-rose）的花心在指下松动了。护板轻轻地、无声无息地向外转开，壁龛里赫然是那几本熟识的家用账簿和银钱袋。它们旁边躺着一只钱夹和一只装着黄铜锁的小匣子。弗勒蕾特毫不迟疑地取了钱袋、钱夹和匣子，账簿留在原处。她一刻也不曾想到，若被人撞见，这将是个多么说不清的嫌疑处境。她心地太单纯，天性太诚实，压根想不到自己会被疑为偷窃。

===Original===
Having stowed the wallet and the bag in the wide pockets of her kirtle and hidden the casket beneath her shawl, Fleurette picked her way back across the room. She left the mysterious recess open because she did not know how to close it, and did not want to waste any time in trying to find out. She found her way to the door and opened it, then she blew out the candle and finally peeped out into the corridor.

===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特把钱夹和钱袋塞进裙子宽大的口袋，把匣子藏在披肩底下，便择路往回穿过房间。那神秘的壁龛她任它敞着，因为她不知道怎么关上，也不想浪费时间去找出法子。她摸到门口，开了门，吹熄蜡烛，最后朝走廊里窥探了一眼。

===Original===
It was deserted. The lingering evening light, pale and ghostlike, came creeping in through the row of tall arched windows facing her. As everywhere else in the château, the corridor bore the melancholy traces of the soldiers’ passage. It was the same devastation. The same wanton destruction was only too apparent in the torn carpet and the fragments of glass and broken sconces that littered the floor. Fleurette, turning her back on the direction of the main staircase, made her way to the back stairs which wound in a close spiral down to the service door.

===Chinese===
走廊里空无一人。迟迟不散的暮色苍白如魅，从她面前那一排高大的拱窗里悄悄爬进来。同庄园各处一样，走廊里也留着士兵过境的凄凉痕迹。还是那同一场浩劫。撕烂的地毯、撒了一地的玻璃碴和打碎的壁灯，把同样一场恣意的毁坏昭示得再明白不过。弗勒蕾特背对大楼梯的方向，朝后楼梯走去——那楼梯盘成一匝紧窄的螺旋，直通仆人出入的边门。

===Original===
Fleurette descended with quick, furtive steps, until, past the first curve of the spiral, the stairs were in total darkness. But she would have found her way all about the château blindfold, so well did she know its every nook and cranny. She came to the door and fumbled for the bolts. She had drawn one and taken off the chain, when she heard a measured tramp on the other side of the door. Steps were coming this way along the flagged path; a moment or two later they came to a halt close to the door. Fleurette hardly daring to breathe, listened. A voice said: “Did you go in there?”

===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特迈着疾速隐秘的脚步往下走，过了螺旋梯第一道弯，梯上便全然黑了。可纵然蒙上双眼，她也能走遍庄园的每个犄角旮旯——她熟悉它到了这般地步。她走到门前，摸索门闩。她已经拉开一根闩、退下门链，忽听得门那边传来一阵整整齐齐的脚步声。脚步正沿着石板路朝这边来；过了一两拍，在紧挨着门的地方停住了。弗勒蕾特大气也不敢出，侧耳听着。一个声音说：「你们进那里面去了吗？」

===Original===
“No, citizen,” replied another, “not by this door. The bolts are fastened on the inside.”

===Chinese===
「没有，公民，」另一个声音答道，「没走这扇门。门闩都是从里面闩死的。」

===Original===
Something else was said which Fleurette did not catch, and the steps receded in the direction of the front of the house. She waited a minute or two longer, breathless and motionless, until she heard what she thought was the tramp of feet in the corridor above her. The soldiers had apparently been ordered to come round again, perhaps they would be coming down those stairs. To hesitate now might prove fatal. Fumbling once more in the gloom, Fleurette found the last bolt and drew it, and the next moment was out in the open. The back door gave on the yard. On the right were the stables, and facing the door, the riding-school and one or two sheds; on the left the kitchens and servants’ quarters. In this direction too was the great archway and the main entrance into the house. Past the archway was the park and the avenue leading to the big gates.

===Chinese===
后面还说了些什么，弗勒蕾特没有听清，脚步声便朝宅子正面的方向远去了。她又屏息静气、一动不动地等了一两分钟，直到听见头顶走廊里传来她认作脚步踏地的声响。士兵们显然是奉命再兜一圈，说不定就要从那道楼梯下来。这时候再犹豫，只怕就要送命。弗勒蕾特又在黑暗里摸索一遭，找到最后一根门闩，拉开了；下一刻她已经到了屋外。后门通向院子。右手边是马厩，正对着门的是驯马场和一两间棚屋；左手边是厨房和仆役的下房。这个方向也有大拱门，是进宅子的正路。拱门外是园子和通向大门的林荫道。

===Original===
After a moment’s reflection Fleurette decided to avoid these main approaches: there was another way across the park, past the stable gate. Hugging the casket closely under her shawl, Fleurette set out in the direction of the stables. There was no one about and she felt comparatively safe. Night was now rapidly drawing in, and she fortunately had on a dark kirtle and dark worsted stockings. The air was very still and the waning moon not yet risen in the east. From far away came the sound of the bell of Laragne church. It struck eight. Fleurette felt a pang of anxiety. She had promised to be home before dark and Louise would be anxious and cross: and there was still something she wanted to do before she went home. Now she was past the stable door where, in a heap, just as old André had said, there lay a pile of faggots. The sight of them gave Fleurette a happy thrill. Was she not obeying the dictates of the mysterious voice which had spoken to her through the medium of the old faggot-carrier?

===Chinese===
略一思索，弗勒蕾特决定避开这几条正路：穿园子还有另一条道，打马厩门旁边过去。她把匣子紧紧拢在披肩底下，朝马厩的方向走去。四下无人，她自觉还算安全。夜色此刻正迅速四合，幸而她穿的是深色裙子和深色绒线袜。空气纹丝不动，残月尚未从东边升起。远处传来拉拉涅教堂的钟声。钟敲了八下。弗勒蕾特心里焦灼地一揪。她答应过天黑前回家的，路易丝这会儿该又急又恼了：何况回家之前，她还有一桩事要办。这时她已走过马厩门，门边果然堆着一堆柴捆，正如老安德烈（André）先前说的那样。一见那堆柴，弗勒蕾特心头一阵快活的悸动。她这不正是在遵从那借背柴老人之口对她说话的神秘声音的吩咐吗？

===Original===
The next moment, a firm step resounded on the flagstones of the stables, and a second later a man appeared under the lintel of the door.

===Chinese===
说时迟，那时快，马厩的石板地上响起一阵坚定的脚步声，一秒钟后，一个男人出现在了门楣底下。

===Original===
“Fleurette! what in God’s name are you doing here?”

===Chinese===
「弗勒蕾特！看在老天爷份上，你在这儿做什么？」

===Original===
Smothering a startled cry, Fleurette turned and found herself face to face with her father. He was standing at the stable door; his hands were clasped behind his back, and he had a tricolour sash round his waist. Now women, young girls, especially, those born and bred in outlying country districts, are credited with being stupid, silly in their fears, timorous like hens; and so no doubt would Fleurette have been in ordinary circumstances. She may not have been either clever or brave originally; she would perhaps have behaved in a silly, timorous fashion but for this one fact, that she knew that something terrible was happening to the Frontenacs whom she loved, and that she had been deputed by the *bon Dieu*, or merely by a human friend, to do something important for them. In order to do this she must keep her head; and trust any woman to keep her head if one she loves is in peril.

===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特压下一声惊呼，转过身，正同父亲打了个照面。他站在马厩门口，双手抄在背后，腰间缠着一条三色绶带。世人总道，女人——尤其是年轻姑娘，尤其是生养在偏僻乡野里的——又蠢又笨，遇事慌里慌张，胆小得像母鸡；寻常境况下的弗勒蕾特，无疑也会是这副样子。她原本未必聪明，也未必勇敢；若不是有这么一桩事实横在跟前——她知道一场可怕的大祸正临到她深爱的弗朗特纳克一家人头上，而她奉了好天主、或者不过是一位人间友人之命，要为他们办成一件要紧的事——她多半也会做出一副又蠢又怯的样子。要办成这件事，她必须保持头脑镇定；而只要心爱之人身陷险境，天下就没有哪个女人稳不住神。

===Original===
“What are you doing here, Fleurette?” Bibi reiterated rather sternly.

===Chinese===
「你在这儿做什么，弗勒蕾特？」比比相当严厉地又问了一遍。

===Original===
And Fleurette with a well-simulated nervous little laugh retorted lightly:

===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特装出一声紧张的轻笑，轻轻巧巧地顶了回去：

===Original===
“Why, Bibi *chéri*, I might retaliate. What are you doing here? I thought you were on your way to Paris.”

===Chinese===
「嗐，亲爱的比比（*chéri*），我倒要反唇相讥呢：您在这儿做什么？我还当您已经上了去巴黎的路。」

===Original===
“What are you doing here, Fleurette?” Bibi said once more, and Fleurette thought that his voice had never sounded so harsh before.

===Chinese===
「你在这儿做什么，弗勒蕾特？」比比又说了一遍。弗勒蕾特觉得，他的嗓音从来没有像此刻这样生硬过。

===Original===
“But Bibi,” Fleurette said simply, “I often come to see Madame and Mademoiselle. And after you left this afternoon I felt so lonely and sad, I thought I might seek Mademoiselle Rose for company.”

===Chinese===
「可是比比，」弗勒蕾特老老实实地说，「我常来看夫人和小姐呀。今天下午您走了以后，我又孤单又难过，就想来找罗丝小姐作个伴儿。」

===Original===
“And have you seen her?”

===Chinese===
「见到她了吗？」

===Original===
“No. They told me Madame and Mademoiselle had gone.”

===Chinese===
「没有。他们告诉我，夫人和小姐都走了。」

===Original===
“Who told you?”

===Chinese===
「谁告诉你的？」

===Original===
“Papa Mathieu.”

===Chinese===
「马蒂厄老爹（Papa Mathieu）。」

===Original===
“What else did he tell you?”

===Chinese===
「他还跟你说什么了？」

===Original===
“Only that there were soldiers come to the château; and that I’d better go home again﻿—and so I’m going.”

===Chinese===
「只说庄园里来了兵；说我顶好还是回家去——所以我就回去呀。」

===Original===
“He didn’t tell you anything else?”

===Chinese===
「他没再跟你说别的？」

===Original===
“No,” Fleurette replied innocently. “Was there anything else to say?”

===Chinese===
「没有了，」弗勒蕾特天真无邪地答道，「还有什么可说的吗？」

===Original===
“No﻿—er﻿—no,” Bibi rejoined. “Of course not. But Fleurette﻿—”

===Chinese===
「没——呃——没了，」比比应道，「当然没什么。可是，弗勒蕾特——」

===Original===
“Yes, Bibi darling?”

===Chinese===
「什么事，亲爱的比比？」

===Original===
“How often must I tell you that you must not talk of ‘Madame’ and ‘Mademoiselle’? There are no Madames and Mademoiselles now; we are, all of us equally, citizens of France.”

===Chinese===
「要我叮嘱你多少回，不许再口口声声『夫人』『小姐』？如今再没有什么夫人小姐了；我们大家一律平等，都是法兰西的公民。」

===Original===
“Yes, Bibi,” Fleurette rejoined demurely. “And I really, really am very careful when strangers are about. It doesn’t matter what I say before you, does it, chéri Bibi?”

===Chinese===
「是，比比，」弗勒蕾特端庄地应道，「有生人在跟前，我真的、真的很当心。在您面前说什么都不要紧，对不对，亲爱的比比？」

===Original===
“No, no,” Bibi muttered, seemingly without much conviction, and Fleurette then went on quickly:

===Chinese===
「不，不成，」比比喃喃道，听着不见得有多少底气；弗勒蕾特随即赶忙接了下去：

===Original===
“I must run home now, chéri Bibi, or Louise will be getting anxious. You are coming too, aren’t you? Louise will get you such a lovely supper and then﻿—”

===Chinese===
「我这就得跑回家了，亲爱的比比，不然路易丝要着急了。您也一起回来，好不好？路易丝会给您备一桌顶可口的晚饭，然后——」

===Original===
“No, my little one.” Bibi said. “I can’t. Not tonight. I must be in Orange tomorrow.”

===Chinese===
「不啦，我的小宝贝，」比比说，「不成。今夜不成。我明天得到奥朗日去。」

===Original===
“But Bibi﻿—”

===Chinese===
「可是比比——」

===Original===
“Run along, child,” Bibi broke in almost fiercely. “It’s a dark night, and there are always vagabonds about.”

===Chinese===
「快走吧，孩子，」比比几乎是疾言厉色地打断她，「夜黑，外头总有流浪汉游荡。」

===Original===
“Ah well then, good night, Bibi,” Fleurette murmured meekly.

===Chinese===
「那么好吧，晚安，比比，」弗勒蕾特温顺地低声说。

===Original===
“Good night, little one.”

===Chinese===
「晚安，小宝贝。」

===Original===
And suddenly Bibi put out his hand and grasped Fleurette by the wrist.

===Chinese===
忽然，比比伸出手来，一把攥住了弗勒蕾特的手腕。

===Original===
“Are you not going to kiss me, Fleurette?” he asked with oh! such a tone of sadness now in his voice.

===Chinese===
「你不打算亲亲我吗，弗勒蕾特？」他问道，哦！他的嗓音里此刻含着那样一种悲伤的调子。

===Original===
It was a terrible moment. What a mercy that the darling had seized her left wrist, rather than her right, because with her right hand Fleurette was hugging the small casket under her shawl. There were also the wallet and the moneybag in the pocket of her kirtle: oh! if Bibi should knock against them! Fortunately it was dark, and he could not see the bulge under her shawl. But, of course, she could not part from Bibi chéri without giving him a farewell kiss. He seemed sad and unhappy, and there was something about his whole manner that Fleurette did not understand.

===Chinese===
这是难熬的一刻。多亏这亲爱的人抓住的是她的左腕，而不是右腕，因为弗勒蕾特的右手正把小匣子拢在披肩底下呢。裙袋里还有钱夹和钱袋：哦！万一比比碰着了它们可怎么好！幸好天黑，他瞧不见她披肩底下鼓起的那一块。可是当然，总不能不吻别亲爱的比比就走。他显得又忧愁又不快活，他整个人的做派里有一种弗勒蕾特捉摸不透的东西。

===Original===
At first, when he startled her by suddenly appearing at the stable door, she had not even tried to conjecture what he was doing here; she was too deeply absorbed in her own adventure for the moment to do more than vaguely wonder what part Bibi was playing in the tragic events that had wrought such desolation at the château. Bibi chéri, who worshipped his little Fleurette, who was always so kind, so gentle, a slave to every one of her whims; he must have been dragged into this horrible affair, was perhaps an innocent tool of those cruel people in Paris, who monopolized his time and kept him away from his home.

===Chinese===
起初，他冷不丁出现在马厩门口、吓她一跳的时候，她连想也没想过猜他在这儿做什么；她全副心神都沉浸在自己的冒险里，一时只顾隐隐纳闷：把庄园糟蹋成这般荒凉的这场惨剧里，比比扮演的是什么角色。亲爱的比比，他那样疼他的小弗勒蕾特，一向那样和气、那样温柔，她随心起一个念头他便百依百顺；他一定是被拖进这桩可怕的事情里来的，说不定只是巴黎那些狠毒人物手下的无辜工具——那些人霸占着他的光阴，害得他远离家门。

===Original===
Indeed she had no mistrust in him whatever; but her trust in him did not go the length of telling him about the casket, or the mysterious voice of the faggot-carrier; those were her own secrets, secrets too which concerned the Frontenacs for whom Bibi had never evinced a very great affection, and had even tried to dissuade Fleurette from having too much intercourse with them.
