It was Adèle who brought the news. Young Colombe had been arrested by soldiers of the revolutionary army, she said, and he and M’sieu’ de Frontenac had been taken to Sisteron. A superior officer of the army had come in this morning and relieved the lieutenant of his command. There had been great excitement in Laragne owing to the arrival of this new detachment of soldiers who were as splendid as those of last night had been travel-stained and bedraggled. The whole of the squad, headed by that magnificent officer, had marched away in the direction of Sisteron, the two prisoners sitting in the wagon in the rear.

===Chinese===
报信的是阿黛尔。她说，小科隆布叫革命军的士兵抓了起来，他和德·弗朗特纳克先生一并被押去了锡斯特龙。今儿早上军中来了位上级军官，撤了中尉的指挥权。这队新开到的士兵那么齐整漂亮，恰似昨夜那批人马的风尘仆仆、拖泥带水，他们的到来叫拉拉涅着实轰动了一阵。整支小队由那位气派非凡的军官带领，取道锡斯特龙方向开拔而去，两名囚犯就坐在殿后的大车里。

===Original===
It was only bit by bit that old Louise succeeded in dragging all this news out of Adèle. The girl’s habitual reticence was put to a severe test by all the questions and cross-questions, whilst Fleurette stood by wide-eyed, distraught with the idea of these horrible complications in which her poor Amédé was being involved. But she would not show any emotion before Adèle, she felt vaguely that her foster-sister, never very expansive towards her, had suddenly become almost inimical. So she waited until Louise had extracted all the news she could out of the taciturn girl, and curtly ordered her back into the kitchen; then as the old woman was about to follow, Fleurette caught her by the hand.

===Chinese===
老路易丝一点一点地追问，才总算从阿黛尔嘴里把这些消息撬了出来。姑娘素来寡言，这一连串的盘问反问，把她那沉默的性子考较得够呛；弗勒蕾特立在一旁睁圆了眼睛，一想到她那可怜的阿梅代竟被卷进这些可怕的纠葛里，心里便乱作一团。可她不愿在阿黛尔面前露出半点激动——她隐隐觉得，这位义姐妹平日待她就谈不上亲热，此刻忽然几乎带上了敌意。于是她等着路易丝把能掏的话都从这寡言的姑娘嘴里掏尽了，便简慢地打发她回厨房去；随后老妇人正要跟出去，弗勒蕾特一把攥住了她的手。

===Original===
“Louise,” she said in a tone of almost desperate entreaty, “dear, kind Louise, I must go to Sisteron﻿—at once.”

===Chinese===
「路易丝，」她用近乎绝望的恳求声调说，「亲爱的、好心的路易丝，我一定得去锡斯特龙——马上就去。」

===Original===
“To Sisteron?” old Louise exclaimed frowning. “Heavens alive, what is the child thinking of now?”

===Chinese===
「去锡斯特龙？」老路易丝皱着眉头叫了起来。「老天爷在上，这孩子这会儿又在想什么呀？」

===Original===
“Of M’sieu’ Amédé, dear Louise,” Fleurette replied. “You heard what Adèle said. They have taken him to Sisteron.”

===Chinese===
「为了阿梅代先生呀，亲爱的路易丝，」弗勒蕾特答道。「你听见阿黛尔说的话了。他们把他带到锡斯特龙去了。」

===Original===
“And what of it?” Louise asked﻿—but she asked for form’s sake only, she knew quite well what was going on in Fleurette’s head.

===Chinese===
「那又怎么样呢？」路易丝问——不过她这一问也只是虚应故事，弗勒蕾特脑袋里转的什么念头，她清楚得很。

===Original===
“Only this, dear Louise,” the girl said with a little note of defiance piercing through her shyness. “We﻿—that is Amédé and I﻿—are tokened to one another.”

===Chinese===
「就是这个呀，亲爱的路易丝，」姑娘说，羞怯里透出一丝倔强。「我们——就是阿梅代和我——已经互订终身了。」

===Original===
“Tokened?” the old woman exclaimed with a gasp. “Since when?”

===Chinese===
「互订终身？」老妇人倒抽一口气，失声嚷道。「打什么时候起的？」

===Original===
“Since last night.”

===Chinese===
「打昨夜起的。」

===Original===
“And without your father’s consent? Well! of all the﻿—”

===Chinese===
「连你父亲的应允都还没有？好哇！真是天下少有——」

===Original===
“Chéri Bibi would approve,” Fleurette asserted, “if he knew.”

===Chinese===
「亲爱的比比会应允的，」弗勒蕾特一口咬定，「只要他知道。」

===Original===
Old Louise shrugged her shoulders. She would not trust herself to speak because the child looked so sweet and so innocent, and her pretty blue eyes were so full of tears, that Louise felt an almost unconquerable desire to take hold of her and hug her to her breast. Which act of weakness would have seriously impaired her authority at this critical juncture. She was wondering what to say next﻿—for in truth she more than suspected that the child was right, and that Citizen Armand would not object to those two young things being tokened to one another, when Fleurette broke in gently:

===Chinese===
老路易丝耸了耸肩膀。她不敢让自己开口，因为这孩子看上去那么甜美、那么天真，一双好看的蓝眼睛又噙满泪水，路易丝只觉得心头涌起一股几乎按捺不住的冲动，想把她一把搂进怀里。而这般心软的举动，在这要紧的节骨眼上，可是要大损她的威信的。她正琢磨着接下来说什么才好——说实在的，她何止是疑心这孩子说得对，公民阿尔芒压根儿就不会反对那对小儿女互订终身——弗勒蕾特却轻轻地开了口：

===Original===
“So you see, dear, kind Louise, that I must go to Sisteron﻿—now﻿—at once.”

===Chinese===
「所以你瞧，亲爱的、好心的路易丝，我一定得去锡斯特龙——这就去——马上就去。」

===Original===
“But Holy Virgin, what to do?”

===Chinese===
「可是我的圣母，去做什么呀？」

===Original===
“To see Amédé and comfort him.”

===Chinese===
「去看阿梅代，安慰安慰他呀。」

===Original===
“They won’t let you see him, child.”

===Chinese===
「他们不会让你见他的，孩子。」

===Original===
“Then I will find chéri Bibi,” Fleurette retorted calmly. “He has a great deal more authority than you and I credit him with, Louise. He can order whom he likes not only to let me see Amédé, but even to set him free.”

===Chinese===
「那我就去找亲爱的比比，」弗勒蕾特从容不迫地回道。「他手里的权柄，比你我想的还要大得多呢，路易丝。他爱吩咐谁便吩咐谁，不但能叫人家许我去见阿梅代，还能叫人家干脆把阿梅代放了。」

===Original===
“He would be very angry,” Louise argued, “to see you wandering about the high roads alone, while all those soldiers and riffraff are about.”

===Chinese===
「他会很生气的，」路易丝争辩道，「瞧你孤身一个人在大路上乱跑，何况眼下满路都是大兵和七七八八的闲杂人等。」

===Original===
Fleurette gave a quaint little smile.

===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特露出一丝古灵精怪的浅笑。

===Original===
“Bibi’s anger against me never lasts very long,” she said. “Anyway I will risk it. Louise dear, will you come with me?”

===Chinese===
「比比跟我生的气向来长不了，」她说。「横竖我豁出去了。亲爱的路易丝，你肯陪我一块儿去吗？」

===Original===
“I?”

===Chinese===
「我？」

===Original===
“Of course, you said that Bibi would be angry if I roamed about the high roads alone.”

===Chinese===
「当然啦，你方才不是说，我要是一个人在大路上乱逛，比比会生气的么。」

===Original===
Louise stood squarely in front of Fleurette, looked straight into those blue eyes, which never before had held such a determined glance. Fleurette could not help smiling at the old woman’s look of perplexity; she was the typical hen seeing her brood of ducklings take their first plunge in the pond.

===Chinese===
路易丝端端正正地站到弗勒蕾特面前，直直地望进那双蓝眼睛里——那双眼睛里，还从来没有过这般坚决的神色。弗勒蕾特瞧着老妇人一脸的困惑，忍不住笑了；她活脱脱是那只典型的老母鸡，眼睁睁看着自己孵的一窝小鸭子头一回跳进池塘里扑腾。

===Original===
“If you won’t come with me, Louise dear,” the girl said simply, “I shall have to go alone.”

===Chinese===
「要是你不肯陪我去，亲爱的路易丝，」姑娘说得简简单单，「我就只好一个人去了。」

===Original===
“Get along with ye, for an obstinate wench,” Louise retorted gruffly. But the next moment she had already changed her tone. “Get on your thick woollen stockings, child,” she said, “and your buckled shoes, and your brown cloak, while I put a few things in a basket for our dinner. If we don’t hurry, we shan’t be in Sisteron before nightfall.”

===Chinese===
「去你的，真是个犟丫头，」路易丝粗声粗气地回敬。可转眼间她已经换了口气。「穿上你的厚毛线袜，孩子，」她说，「再穿上带扣的鞋，披上棕色斗篷；我来往篮子里搁几样吃食，当咱们路上的午饭。咱们要是不赶紧些，天黑以前可到不了锡斯特龙。」

===Original===
“M’sieu’ Duflos will lend us his cart or a horse,” Fleurette rejoined gleefully, “but I won’t be long, dear, kind Louise.”

===Chinese===
「迪弗洛先生会借我们大车，要么就借匹马，」弗勒蕾特欢欢喜喜地应道，「我去去就来，亲爱的、好心的路易丝。」

===Original===
And swift as a young hare she ran out and then up the outside staircase to her room under the overhanging climbing rose.

===Chinese===
她像只小野兔般敏捷，一溜烟跑了出去，顺着屋外那道楼梯奔上楼，钻进垂覆的爬藤蔷薇掩映下的自己房里。

===Original===
A few minutes later the two women started on their way. Fleurette had on her dark kirtle, her thick stockings and buckled shoes; her fair hair was tucked away underneath her frilled mobcap. She carried her own cloak and Louise’s on her arm, whilst Louise tramped beside her, carrying a basket in which she had hastily packed a piece of bread, some cheese, and two hard-boiled eggs. If M’sieu’ Duflos, the butcher, would lend them his cart, they would be in Sisteron by midday; but in any case they would be there before dark.

===Chinese===
几分钟之后，娘儿俩便动身上路了。弗勒蕾特穿着深色的裙子、厚袜子和带扣的鞋，一头金发收在镶花边的小便帽（mobcap）底下。她臂弯里搭着自己的斗篷和路易丝的两件，路易丝迈着沉重的步子走在她身旁，手里提着篮子，里头匆匆装了一块面包、一点干酪，还有两枚煮硬的鸡蛋。要是肉铺老板迪弗洛先生肯把大车借给她们，晌午前就能赶到锡斯特龙；不管怎样，天黑以前总到得了。

## XIV / 第十四章

===Original===
But M’sieu’ Duflos had no cart to lend them﻿—that is he had no horse. Didn’t Mam’zelle Fleurette and Ma’ame Louise remember? Some of those brigands had been round the week before and requisitioned every horse they could lay their hands on all over the countryside; old nags, mares with foals, butchers’ cobs, nothing came amiss to them, nothing was sacred. Oh those soldiers! Were they not the curse of the country? And what difference there was between the so-called revolutionary army and a pillaging band of pirates, M’sieu’ Duflos, the butcher, really couldn’t say.

===Chinese===
可是迪弗洛先生没有大车可借给他们——确切说，是没有马。弗勒蕾特小姐和路易丝大娘不记得了吗？上礼拜就有一伙强盗到过这一带，把整个乡面上能弄到手的马全征了去；老驽马也罢，带驹的母马也罢，肉铺拉货的矮脚马也罢，没有一样他们嫌弃的，没有一样碰不得的。唉，那些大兵！他们岂不就是这地方的一害？要说这支所谓的革命军队同一伙打家劫舍的海盗还有什么两样，肉铺老板迪弗洛先生实在说不上来。

===Original===
All this he told the two women, to the accompaniment of wide gestures of his powerful arms and much shrugging of his broad shoulders. It was Fleurette who had put the question breathlessly to him, as soon as she had caught sight of him standing on the door of his shop, blocking it with his massive bulk.

===Chinese===
他把这一番话统统讲给两个女人听，一边挥动两条粗壮的胳膊大肆比画，一边不住耸着宽阔的肩膀。是弗勒蕾特先气喘吁吁地开口问他的——她一眼望见他站在自家铺子门口，那肥壮的身躯把门洞堵了个严严实实。

===Original===
“A horse? A cart? Alas! it was impossible! Ah! those brigands! those brigands!”

===Chinese===
「马？大车？唉！办不到哇！唉！这些强盗！这些强盗！」

===Original===
Fleurette could not conceal her disappointment at first; but she was so brave, so resolute; she was for making an immediate start so as to get to Sisteron before dark. Perhaps they would meet horse and cart belonging to some neighbour luckier than poor M’sieu’ Duflos. But Louise, more prudent, saw an opportunity for putting the mad adventure off until the next day. A start in the early morning could then be made, she argued, and horse or no horse, Sisteron might be reached before the sun was low. A good project forsooth. Let Fleurette return with her quietly now to Lou Mas and sleep on it. That was it! sleep on it! If only Fleurette would do that, she, Louise, felt quite sure, that counsels of prudence would prevail.

===Chinese===
起先，弗勒蕾特掩饰不住失望；可她那么勇敢，那么有决断；她主张立刻动身，好赶在天黑之前到锡斯特龙。说不定路上能遇上哪位比可怜的迪弗洛先生走运的邻居，借来马和大车。可路易丝更谨慎，她瞅准了一个机会，要把这桩疯疯癫癫的冒险推到明天再办。她据理力争：明儿一清早动身，不管有没有马，也能赶在日头偏西之前到达锡斯特龙。好一个万全的主意。这就让弗勒蕾特这会儿安安静静随她回庐庄去，睡上一觉再说。就是这话！睡上一觉再说！只要弗勒蕾特肯这么办，她路易丝满心相信，谨慎的道理终归会占上风。

===Original===
M. Duflos sagely nodded his head. Sisteron? He could not conjecture why Mam’zelle Fleurette should wish to go to Sisteron. Without an escort! And on foot! What would Citizen Armand say to it, if he knew?

===Chinese===
迪弗洛先生煞有介事地点了点头。锡斯特龙？他实在猜不透，弗勒蕾特小姐为什么要上锡斯特龙去。没人护送！还靠两条腿走！公民阿尔芒若是知道了，会说什么呢？

===Original===
Up to this point, you perceive, not a word about the exciting events that had convulsed Laragne a little over two hours ago. M’sieu’ Duflos watching Fleurette, marvelled how much the girl knew. She on the other hand was longing to ask questions, whilst dreading to lose time in unnecessary gossip. She looked about her at the familiar objects: the pump, the shop fronts, the *poste de gendarmerie*, on the other side of the square, and in the corner of the Rue Haute where the soldiers must have stood this morning with Amédé, a prisoner amongst them.

===Chinese===
你瞧，直到此刻，还没有人吐露半个字，提起那桩两个来钟头之前震动整个拉拉涅的惊人事件。迪弗洛先生一面瞧着弗勒蕾特，一面纳罕这姑娘到底知道多少。她呢，正憋着一肚子话要问，又怕在无谓的闲扯里耽搁工夫。她环顾四周那些熟悉的物事：压水井啦，一排排铺面啦，广场对过的宪兵队啦，还有上街的拐角——今天早晨，士兵们准是押着阿梅代站在那里，他夹在他们当中，成了个囚徒。

===Original===
Everything for the moment in Laragne appeared calm, not to say commonplace. The women had all gone home to cook the midday dinner; the men were at their work. Every moment she thought that she must see Amédé coming round the corner with his slow swinging step, looking for her! M’sieu’ Duflos and Louise were talking together, not exactly in whispers, but under their breath; the way people talk when the subject is exciting and perhaps awe-inspiring. And suddenly M. Duflos exclaimed with a great, big sigh of compassion:

===Chinese===
眼下的拉拉涅，一时处处显得平静，简直可以说是平平常常。女人们都回家烧晌午饭去了；男人们各自忙着活计。她时时刻刻都觉得，下一眼准会看见阿梅代迈着那慢悠悠、一摇一晃的步子，从街角转出来寻她！迪弗洛先生和路易丝正凑在一处说话，倒不算交头接耳，只是压着嗓子讲——人们谈的话题又刺激、也许还骇人的时候，就是这么个讲法。忽然，迪弗洛先生长长地、重重地叹出一声怜悯，嚷道：

===Original===
“If it is not a misery! *Mon Dieu! Mon Dieu!* those poor Colombes!”

===Chinese===
「这可不就是遭罪吗！我的天主（*Mon Dieu*）！我的天主！可怜的科隆布一家！」

===Original===
His kindly glance turned to Fleurette, and he saw her big blue eyes fixed on him. And as he was a very worthy fellow this M. Duflos, with a daughter of his own, he could not somehow return that glance; there was something in it that reminded him of a young animal in pain. He guessed that she had heard the news about young Colombe, and he knew, as everyone did in Laragne, that Fleurette, over at Lou Mas, and Amédé Colombe were fond of one another, and that they would be tokened as soon as the girl had turned eighteen. This love-romance had been part of the village life ever since the two children had made mud pies together in the market square with the dust of the road and the water from the fountain, and though Armand, over at Lou Mas had become very queer of late, and no one knew anything about the mysterious business which, of recent years, kept him away from home for months on end, everyone remembered the pretty Marseillaise whom nineteen years ago he had brought to Lou Mas as a blushing bride, and no one had forgotten the terrible tragedy of her death when she gave birth to Fleurette. With the kindliness, one might say the indifference, peculiar to the peasant, the neighbours put down Armand’s growing moroseness after that terrible event, and his secretive ways, to grief over the death of his young wife; and then after a while, they ceased to trouble about him at all, and almost forgot him as it were. But Fleurette had grown up among them all, a true child of sunny Dauphiné, in spite of her fair hair and blue eyes. They all loved her because she was so pretty, and though M’sieu’ Colombe, the prosperous grocer of the Rue Haute, might at one time have had more ambitious views for his son, he and Ma’ame Colombe soon fell victims to Fleurette’s charm, her dainty ways, her quaint little airs, as if she were a lady strayed into this remote village from some great city, and, above all, being natives of the South, and children of France, they succumbed to the fascination of her wealth; for there was no doubt that Armand was rich, and no doubt that he had made a declaration both privately to his friend Colombe and officially before the notary at Sisteron, that he would give his only child a dowry of ten thousand livres tournois, the day she married with his consent.

===Chinese===
他那和蔼的目光转向弗勒蕾特，只见她一双蓝色的大眼睛直勾勾地望着自己。这位迪弗洛先生是个心地极好的汉子，自己也有个女儿，不知怎么的，他竟不敢回望这道目光；那眼神里有一点什么，叫他想起一头疼痛难忍的小牲口。他猜她已经听说了小科隆布的事；而且，正像拉拉涅人人知道的那样，他也知道：住在庐庄的弗勒蕾特同阿梅代·科隆布两情相悦，只等姑娘满了十八岁，两人就要互订终身。这段儿女情缘，早就是村中生活的一部分了——打从两个孩子还在集市广场上、拿大路上的尘土和喷泉的水捏泥饼的时候起，就是如此。虽说阿尔芒近年来在庐庄变得十分古怪，那桩神秘的营生究竟是怎么回事，谁也无从知晓，近年害他一连几个月不着家门；可大家都还记得，十九年前他领回庐庄来的那位红着脸的新娘，是个漂亮的马赛女子；也没有人忘记那场可怕的悲剧——她生弗勒蕾特的时候，死了。左邻右舍怀着农民特有的厚道——也不妨说，特有的漠然——把阿尔芒自那场大变故之后日益加深的阴郁、把他那深藏不露的做派，一概归到他悼念亡妻的哀伤头上；过了一些时候，他们索性不再为他操心，简直就把他忘了个干净。可是弗勒蕾特是在他们大伙儿中间长大的，是多阳光的多菲内一个地道的孩子——虽说她生的是金头发、蓝眼睛。人人都爱她，因为她长得那么俊；上街那位殷实的杂货商科隆布先生，虽说一度对儿子怀过更高的指望，他和科隆布大娘不久也双双做了弗勒蕾特魅力的俘虏：她那娇俏的做派，那古灵精怪的小腔调，仿佛一位从大城里流落到这偏僻村庄来的贵妇；而顶要紧的是，他们既是南方人，又是法兰西的儿女，到底没能抵住她那份家财的迷力——因为阿尔芒有钱，这是毫无疑问的；他也毫不含糊地表过态，私下里对他的朋友科隆布说过，又当着锡斯特龙的公证人正式立过声明：只等他的独生女儿经他应允出嫁的那一天，他愿拿一万图尔里弗（livres tournois）给她作嫁妆。

===Original===
And here was this child now, whom everyone knew and whom everyone loved, turning great, pleading eyes on M’sieu’ Duflos until the worthy fellow felt so uncomfortable that he had to clear his throat very noisily and to expectorate on the sanded floor of his shop with a sound like the falling of a shower of hailstones on a tiled roof. He thought that Fleurette knew all the details of this morning’s dramatic story.

===Chinese===
而此刻，这个人人都识得、人人疼爱的孩子，正把一双大大的、恳求的眼睛定在迪弗洛先生脸上，看得这位好心的汉子浑身不自在，只得大声清了清嗓子，朝铺子里铺了沙的地面上啐了一口，那声响就像一阵冰雹噼里啪啦砸在瓦屋顶上。他还只当弗勒蕾特已经知道今早那出戏的全部底细了呢。

===Original===
“*Voyons*, Mam’zelle Fleurette,” he said with a rough attempt at consolation. “They won’t do anything to Amédé. Really. The boy meant no harm.”

===Chinese===
「嗳，弗勒蕾特小姐，」他开了口，粗声粗气里透着一番安慰的尝试。「他们不会把阿梅代怎么样的。真的。这孩子没有坏心。」

===Original===
All then would have been well if that fool Aristide Sicard, who was M’sieu’ Duflos errand man, had not put in a word.

===Chinese===
本来到这儿，事情也就都顺遂了，偏偏那个傻瓜阿里斯蒂德·西卡尔（Aristide Sicard）——迪弗洛先生跑腿打杂的伙计——多嘴插了一句。

===Original===
“No one,” he said, “is going to believe that Amédé Colombe is a thief.”

===Chinese===
「谁也不会相信，」他说，「阿梅代·科隆布会是个贼。」

===Original===
“A thief?” and Fleurette gave a funny little gasp. “Why should they think that Amédé is a thief?”

===Chinese===
「贼？」弗勒蕾特古怪地小小倒抽了一口气。「他们凭什么把阿梅代当成贼呢？」

===Original===
M’sieu’ Duflos, the butcher, had given his errand-man a vigorous kick, but the correction came too late. And now Fleurette wanted to know more.

===Chinese===
肉铺老板迪弗洛先生朝他那个跑腿的伙计狠狠踢了一脚，可惜这一番矫正来得太迟了。这下弗勒蕾特非要问个明白不可。

===Original===
“What is your meaning, M’sieu’ Aristide?” she insisted with that funny little air of determination of hers, whilst a frown appeared between her brows. As M’sieu’ Duflos explained to the neighbours afterwards, Fleurette looked as if she might be capable of anything at the moment. He was quite frightened at the expression in her blue eyes. It was too late to undo the mischief that that fool Aristide had done, so the butcher took the matter into his own hands. He had a sound knowledge of human nature, had M’sieu’ Duflos, and he prided himself on his tact.

===Chinese===
「你这话是什么意思，阿里斯蒂德先生？」她带着她那股古怪的小小坚决劲儿追问不休，两道眉毛中间蹙起了一个疙瘩。照迪弗洛先生事后对邻居们解释的话说，弗勒蕾特那一刻的模样，仿佛天大的事都做得出来。她蓝眼睛里那副神情，着实把他吓了一跳。那个傻瓜阿里斯蒂德闯下的祸，已经来不及收拾了，肉铺老板便索性把话头接过自己手里。迪弗洛先生于世道人心颇有一套健全的见识，也颇为自己的圆到机变自负。

===Original===
“You see, Mam’zelle Fleurette,” he began, “it’s this way. Those scurvy knaves﻿—I mean the soldiers of the Republic﻿—were full of choler because they had not found enough to steal at the château when they arrested poor M’sieu’ de Frontenac. At first, it seems, they thought that Madame and Mademoiselle had taken their valuables away with them when they ran away; but later on something must have aroused their suspicions, or else the same kind of fool as Aristide here must have got talking. Anyway, they seem to have got the idea that Amédé Colombe had hidden Madame’s valuables away somewhere and﻿—”

===Chinese===
「您瞧，弗勒蕾特小姐，」他开了腔，「是这么回事。那伙下三滥的恶棍——呃，我是说共和国的士兵们——满肚皮的火气，只因他们到庄园里抓可怜的德·弗朗特纳克先生的时候，没搜刮到足够的东西。起先呢，看样子他们还当是夫人和小姐逃走的时候，把值钱物件随身带走了；可后来，不知什么东西一定触起了他们的疑心，要不然，就是有哪一位同眼前这位阿里斯蒂德一路货色的傻瓜多嘴嚼了舌头。反正，他们不知怎么得了个主意，认定是阿梅代·科隆布把夫人的值钱物件藏到什么地方去了，于是——」

===Original===
“Madame’s valuables!” Fleurette exclaimed, trying to hide something of the excitement which was causing her heart to thump furiously. “They thought that Amédé﻿—﻿—?”

===Chinese===
「夫人的值钱物件！」弗勒蕾特喊出声来，一面竭力遮掩那阵叫她心口怦怦乱撞的激动。「他们以为阿梅代——？」

===Original===
“Why, yes!” M’sieu’ Duflos replied to her half-formulated query. “And unfortunately﻿—”

===Chinese===
「可不是吗！」迪弗洛先生答着她这半截问话。「而且，不幸的是——」

===Original===
“What?”

===Chinese===
「什么？」

===Original===
“Well! They found Madame’s valuables﻿—”

===Chinese===
「唉！他们找到了夫人的值钱物件——」

===Original===
But the worthy butcher got no further with his story. Without another word and swift as lightning, Fleurette had turned on her heel, and the next moment she was speeding across the marketplace in the direction of the Rue Haute, whilst M’sieu’ Duflos was left gazing in ludicrous perplexity at old Louise.

===Chinese===
可是这位好心的肉铺老板的故事就讲到这里为止了。弗勒蕾特一言不发，快如闪电般一转身，下一刻已经穿过集市，朝上街方向飞奔而去，撇下迪弗洛先生对着老路易丝干瞪眼，一脸滑稽的茫然。

===Original===
“What’s the matter with the child?” he queried, and thoughtfully passed his hand through his harsh, bristly hair. “I thought she knew.”

===Chinese===
「这孩子是怎么啦？」他问道，一面若有所思地把手插进自己那头又硬又扎的头发里撸了一把。「我还当她全知道了呢。」

===Original===
Old Louise shrugged her shoulders.

===Chinese===
老路易丝耸了耸肩膀。

===Original===
“She only knew that the lad had been arrested,” she said, “but she had not heard about Madame’s valuables being found in the Colombe’s cart-shed. I was just able to stop Adèle telling her. She is so fond of M’sieu’ Amédé.” Louise added with a sigh: “Oh! how I wish her father were here.”

===Chinese===
「她只知道那小伙子被抓走了，」她说，「可她还没听说，夫人的值钱物件在科隆布家的车棚里被搜了出来。我好不容易才拦住阿黛尔，没让她说漏了嘴。她那么喜欢阿梅代先生。」路易丝叹了口气，又添上一句：「唉！我真盼她父亲这会儿在家才好。」

===Original===
M’sieu’ Duflos was watching Fleurette’s trim little figure speeding across the square and then disappearing round the corner of the Rue Haute.

===Chinese===
迪弗洛先生望着弗勒蕾特那娇小利落的身影飞快穿过广场，消失在上街的拐角后面。

===Original===
“She’s run over to the *épicerie*,” he commented dryly. “The Colombes are fond of her. They’ll be able to comfort one another. Come in and have a *petit verre*, Louise. The child will be back soon.”

===Chinese===
「她跑到杂货铺（*épicerie*）去了，」他干巴巴地评论道。「科隆布一家人都疼她。他们正好可以彼此宽宽心。进来喝一小杯（*petit verre*）吧，路易丝。孩子一会儿就回来了。」

===Original===
But Louise would not come in, she did not want to lose sight of Fleurette, so after thanking the kind butcher for his hospitality, she too turned to go in the direction of the Rue Haute. But at the last M’sieu’ Duflos had one more word to say to her.

===Chinese===
可路易丝不肯进去，她不想让弗勒蕾特离开自己的视线，于是在谢过好心肉铺老板的殷勤之后，她也转身朝上街方向去了。不过临别，迪弗洛先生还有一句话要对她说。

===Original===
“There’s one thing more, Ma’ame Louise,” he said, with unwonted earnestness in his round, prominent eyes. “If I were you I would look after that wench of yours, Adèle, a bit sharper. No offence, you know, but people have been talking in the village. She was rather too familiar with all those draggled-tailed soldiers last night.”

===Chinese===
「还有一桩事，路易丝大娘，」他说，圆圆的凸眼睛里透着一股少见的认真。「我要是你，就对你家那个丫头阿黛尔看紧着点儿。您别见怪，村里人已经有闲话了。昨儿夜里，她同那些邋里邋遢的大兵厮混得也忒热络了些。」

===Original===
Old Louise, with all a peasant’s philosophy, shrugged her fat shoulders.

===Chinese===
老路易丝怀着一副农家人特有的达观，耸了耸她的胖肩膀。

===Original===
“You may be right, M’sieu’ Duflos,” she said dryly, “but the girl, you know, is no care of mine. My sister Amèlie looks after her.”

===Chinese===
「你兴许说得对，迪弗洛先生，」她干巴巴地说，「可这丫头，你知道，不归我操心。她由我姐姐阿梅莉照管着呢。」

===Original===
After which she gave a friendly nod to the amiable butcher and made her way up to the Rue Haute as fast as she could, though this was not really so fast as Fleurette’s nimble little feet had carried her.

===Chinese===
说完，她朝这位和气的肉铺老板友好地点了点头，便尽快朝上街赶去——虽说这「尽快」，到底比不得弗勒蕾特那双轻快的小脚板驮着她跑的那份快。

## XV / 第十五章

===Original===
There had been no need for words. As soon as Fleurette had entered the shop, Ma’ame Colombe had stretched out her arms, and Fleurette ran to her at once to be enfolded in a great maternal embrace. With her fair hair resting on Ma’ame Colombe’s ample bosom, the child began by having a good cry. She had had none since she heard the fatal news, for excitement had kept every other emotion in check. But now with those motherly arms round her, she felt free to let her sorrow and anxiety have free rein. But only for a moment or two. As soon as she felt Ma’ame Colombe’s ample bosom heaving against hers, and the older woman’s tears wetting the top of her fair head, Fleurette looked up, swiftly drying her eyes, and put on a reassuring smile.

===Chinese===
这会儿用不着说什么话。弗勒蕾特一走进铺子，科隆布大娘便张开了双臂，弗勒蕾特立刻扑上前去，被一个慈母般的阔怀抱搂了个满怀。孩子的金发偎在科隆布大娘丰满的胸脯上，先痛痛快快哭了一场。自打听了那个可怕的消息，她还一滴泪也不曾掉过，因为激动把其余一切情绪全压住了。可如今有这双母亲似的胳膊搂着，她觉得尽可以放开心怀，任悲伤和焦虑撒个够了。不过也只一会儿工夫。她一觉出科隆布大娘的胸脯正贴着自己起伏，老人家的泪珠打湿了她的金发头顶，弗勒蕾特便抬起头来，飞快拭干眼睛，摆出一副宽慰人心的笑容。

===Original===
It was difficult to speak at first with all those sobs choking one’s voice; nevertheless, whilst mopping her eyes with her pocket-handkerchief, Fleurette contrived to say:

===Chinese===
起初抽抽噎噎地堵着嗓子，连话都难说出口；不过弗勒蕾特一面用手绢儿揩着眼睛，一面还是设法说道：

===Original===
“You know, Ma’ame Colombe, that it is all right, don’t you? About Amédé, I mean.”

===Chinese===
「您是知道的，科隆布大娘，没事了，对不对？我是说阿梅代的事。」

===Original===
“All right, my dear? All right?” the poor woman reiterated, and shook her head with pathetic dubiousness. “How can it be all right, when my Amédé is accused of being a thief? And before the neighbours too!” she added, whilst a deeper tone of crimson than her kitchen-fire had lent to her kind old face, spread over her cheeks.

===Chinese===
「没事了，我的宝贝？没事了？」可怜的女人翻来覆去地说，满腹狐疑，凄凄惶惶地直摇头。「我家阿梅代被人指作贼，这怎么能算没事？还当着一众街坊的面！」她补了一句；厨房的炉火本已给她那张和善的老脸添了一抹红，此刻一层更深的绯红又漫上了她的双颊。

===Original===
“That’s just it, Ma’ame Colombe,” Fleurette continued eagerly. “Presently﻿—tonight I hope﻿—everyone will know that it was not Amédé who took those things.”

===Chinese===
「正是这话，科隆布大娘，」弗勒蕾特急切地接着说，「回头——我盼着就是今儿晚上——大家就都会知道，拿走那些东西的不是阿梅代。」

===Original===
“Of course he didn’t take them. But you know what village gossip is. If Amédé did not take Madame’s valuables, they keep on saying, how came they to be in our cart-shed? Oh, *mon Dieu*! *mon Dieu!*” she moaned, “to think that my Hector and I should live to see such disgrace.”

===Chinese===
「他当然是没拿。可你该知道村里人的闲话是什么样儿。他们一劲儿念叨：阿梅代若是没拿夫人的值钱物件，那这些东西怎么会上我们家的车棚里去？哎呀，我的天主（*mon Dieu*）！我的天主！」她呜咽道，「想想吧，我家埃克托和我，竟活到今天亲眼看这样的奇耻大辱。」

===Original===
“But, Ma’ame Colombe,” Fleurette put in, somewhat impatient with the older woman’s lamentations, “I am going to Sisteron tonight to tell the gendarme how Madame’s valuables came to be in your cart-shed, and who it was that stole them.”

===Chinese===
「可是，科隆布大娘，」弗勒蕾特插话道，对老人家这一通哀叹有些不耐烦了，「我今儿夜里就去锡斯特龙，告诉宪兵，夫人的值钱物件是怎么进的你们家的车棚，又是谁偷的。」

===Original===
“You, child? How should you know?”

===Chinese===
「你，孩子？你怎么会知道？」

===Original===
“Because it was I who took the valuables out of the secret place in Madame’s room,” Fleurette said glibly, “and I gave them to Amédé to take care of, and because it was I who gave them to him, he hid them in a corner of the cart-shed.”

===Chinese===
「因为把值钱物件从夫人房里的秘密地方取出来的，正是我呀，」弗勒蕾特口齿伶俐地说，「我把它们交给阿梅代保管；正因为是我交给他的，他才把它们藏在了车棚的一个角落里。」

===Original===
“Holy Virgin!” was all that Ma’ame Colombe was able to say in response to this amazing story, “the child has taken leave of her senses.”

===Chinese===
「我的圣母！」面对这个惊人的故事，科隆布大娘只说得出这么一句，「这孩子准是失心疯了。」

===Original===
“No, no, Ma’ame Colombe,” Fleurette insisted earnestly; “it is just as I have told you. I took the valuables out of Madame’s room while the soldiers were at the château, and I gave them to Amédé to take care of.”

===Chinese===
「不，不，科隆布大娘，」弗勒蕾特恳切地坚持道，「事情就同我告诉您的一模一样。大兵们在庄园里的那会儿，我把值钱物件从夫人的房里拿了出来，交给阿梅代保管。」

===Original===
“But why?” the poor mother exclaimed, in an agony of bewilderment. “In Heaven’s name why?”

===Chinese===
「可这是为什么？」可怜的母亲嚷了起来，惶惑得五内如焚。「看在老天爷的份上，到底为什么呀？」

===Original===
“Because﻿—”

===Chinese===
「因为——」

===Original===
And suddenly Fleurette hesitated. A hot flush rose to her cheeks and tears gathered in her eyes. She had felt Ma’ame Colombe’s perplexed glance on her, and for the time a stinging doubt gripped her heart and made her physically almost sick. What views would other people﻿—strangers or even friends﻿—take of her amazing story? of the heavenly voice and the mysterious faggot-carrier with the wonderful twinkling eyes? Would they believe her? or would they deride the whole tale? or again, like dear, kind Ma’ame Colombe, would they just feel anxious, perplexed, not wishing to condemn, and yet vaguely wondering what could have induced a girl like Fleurette to go rummaging about among Madame’s things, and inducing young Amédé to help her to conceal them.

===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特忽然迟疑了。一阵火辣辣的红晕涌上双颊，泪水在眼里聚拢。她觉出科隆布大娘那道困惑的目光正落定在自己身上，一时间，一阵刺人的疑惧攫住了她的心，叫她五脏六腑都几乎翻腾起来。旁人——生人也罢，甚至朋友也罢——会怎么看她这个惊人的故事呢？怎么看那个天上的声音，怎么看那个神秘的、一双奇妙的眼睛闪闪烁烁的背柴老人呢？他们会相信她吗？还是要把这整个故事嘲弄一番？再或者，像亲爱和善的科隆布大娘这样，只觉得担忧、困惑，无心责怪，却又隐隐纳闷：究竟是什么引得弗勒蕾特这样一个姑娘，去翻动夫人的东西，还拉了年轻的阿梅代帮她藏匿起来。

===Original===
An overpowering impulse prompted her to keep her beloved secret to herself. The sight of Ma’ame Colombe’s grief-stricken face almost shook her resolution, but in the end it was that first impulse which conquered. After all it was only a matter of a few days, hours perhaps, and everything would become crystal-clear. Fleurette’s little handkerchief was now like a wet ball in her hot hands; she breathed on it and dabbed her eyes; she straightened her cap and smoothed down her kirtle.

===Chinese===
一股不可抗拒的冲动促使她把心爱的秘密独自留在心底。眼见科隆布大娘那张愁苦的脸，她的决心几乎动摇；可到头来，还是起初那阵冲动占了上风。横竖不过几天工夫，兴许几个钟头，一切就会水落石出。弗勒蕾特的小手绢儿这会儿在她滚烫的手心里已经攥成了湿乎乎的一团；她朝它呵了口气，轻轻按了按眼睛；扶正帽子，抻平了裙子。

===Original===
“And so, dear Ma’ame Colombe,” she said calmly, “I am just going to Sisteron. Probably I shall find Bibi there; but even if I don’t, I shall go up to the Committee of Public Safety, and I shall tell them the whole truth, so that there’ll be no question of Amédé going to fight the English with the stain of theft upon his name.”

===Chinese===
「所以，亲爱的科隆布大娘，」她平静地说，「我这就动身去锡斯特龙。多半能在那儿找到比比；就算找不到，我也会上公安委员会去，把全部实情讲给他们听，好叫阿梅代去打英国人的时候，名字上不带偷窃的污点。」

===Original===
It was impossible to say anything more just then, because Louise had arrived at the *épicerie*, breathless, but happy to catch sight of Fleurette looking quite calm and reasonable.

===Chinese===
这当口再说什么也不成了，因为路易丝赶到了杂货铺（*épicerie*），气喘吁吁，不过一眼瞧见弗勒蕾特神色安然、通情达理，心里倒欢喜。

===Original===
“I hope you gave the child a good scolding, Ma’ame Colombe,” she said. “The idea of her wanting to trapeze the high road today when all these ruffianly soldiers are still about.”

===Chinese===
「您该把这孩子好好训了一顿吧，科隆布大娘，」她说。「瞧她想得好：这帮无法无天的大兵还没走干净呢，她倒想今儿个在大路上耍起空中飞人来。」

===Original===
But Fleurette only smiled. “Neither Ma’ame Colombe, nor anyone else,” she said, “could dissuade me from going to see Bibi now.”

===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特只是笑。「无论是科隆布大娘，还是随便哪一位，」她说，「如今都拦不住我去见比比了。」

===Original===
“Why!” Louise exclaimed pettishly, “this morning it was M’sieu’ Amédé you wanted to see.”

===Chinese===
「嘿！」路易丝气恼地嚷道，「今儿早上你要见的还是阿梅代先生呢。」

===Original===
“I do want to see Amédé,” Fleurette rejoined simply, “but I must see Bibi first.”

===Chinese===
「我是要见阿梅代，」弗勒蕾特直截了当地答道，「可我得先见比比。」

===Original===
And Louise saw her exchange an understanding glance with Ma’ame Colombe. It was all very bewildering and very terrible. Of course she was terribly sorry for the Colombes, but, just for the moment, she wished them all at the bottom of the sea. A little feeling of jealousy had crept into her heart when she saw Fleurette clinging to Ma’ame Colombe and whispering words into her ears which she, Louise, could not hear, and this uncomfortable feeling added to her discomfort. What could Ma’ame Colombe be thinking about to encourage Fleurette in her obstinacy? Louise could only suppose that all common sense had been drowned in an ocean of grief for the beloved only son.

===Chinese===
接着，路易丝看见她同科隆布大娘交换了一个会心的眼色。这一切真叫人糊涂，又真叫人害怕。她自然为科隆布一家难过得了不得；可就这会儿，她巴不得他们一家子统统沉到海底才好。她瞧着弗勒蕾特偎在科隆布大娘怀里，凑着她耳朵低语，说些她路易丝听不见的话，一丝妒意悄悄爬进了心里；这股说不清的滋味，更添了她心头的烦乱。科隆布大娘到底是怎么想的，竟一味由着弗勒蕾特犟下去？路易丝只能这么揣度：为着那心肝独子，一切常识怕都淹死在愁海里了。

===Original===
Ah! if only Monsieur Armand were here!

===Chinese===
唉！要是阿尔芒先生在这儿就好了！

===Original===
And with a last sigh, and a none too cordial farewell to Ma’ame Colombe, Louise, dolefully shaking her head, followed Fleurette out of the shop.

===Chinese===
路易丝末了又叹了口气，同科隆布大娘道了声不甚亲热的别，凄凄惶惶地摇着头，跟着弗勒蕾特走出了铺子。

## XVI / 第十六章

===Original===
It was long past sunset by the time the two women reached Sisteron. Louise was dog-tired, for the day had been hot and the roads heavy with dust. They had started from Lou Mas one hour before noon, and as they left the first outlying houses of the city behind them, the clock of the tower of Notre Dame was striking eight.

===Chinese===
两个女人赶到锡斯特龙的时候，日落早已过去多时。路易丝累得散了架——这一天天热，路上又积着厚厚的尘土。她们晌午前一个钟头从庐庄动身，待到把城郊最初几所房子甩在身后，圣母教堂钟楼上的大钟正敲响八点。

===Original===
The road between Laragne and Sisteron goes uphill most of the way, but withal, it is a beautiful road, winding through the wide valley of the Buëche, past orchards of grey-green olives and almond-trees laden with blossom. Once past the confluence of the Méouge with the Buëche, it rises in a gentle gradient and gradually reveals to the eye the magnificent panorama of the Basses Alpes with their rocky crests and wide flanks draped in the sombre cloaks of pinewoods: Mont de la Baume, St. Géniez, Signal de Lure; as beautiful a picture as Nature has to offer for the delectation of travellers, but possessing no powers of fascination over the two women, who tramped along in weariness and with anxious hearts.

===Chinese===
从拉拉涅到锡斯特龙的路，大半程都在上坡，不过究属是条美景之路：它在比埃什河开阔的河谷里蜿蜒穿行，掠过一片片灰绿色的橄榄树果园，掠过开满了花的杏树。一过梅乌热河（Méouge）同比埃什河的汇流处，路面便顺着舒缓的坡度攀升，渐渐把下阿尔卑斯群山（Basses Alpes）壮丽的全景展现在眼前：嵯峨的峰顶，宽阔的山坡，全都披着松林织就的暗沉沉的斗篷——博姆山（Mont de la Baume）、圣热尼耶（St. Géniez）、吕尔峰（Signal de Lure）；这是造化拿出来供旅人赏心悦目的一幅美景，对这两个女人却毫无摄人的魅力，她们只顾迈着疲惫的步子、揣着焦灼的心赶路。

===Original===
The road was lonely. Scarce anyone did they meet on the way; no one, at any rate, to inspire old Louise with alarm. Now and then, perhaps, a group of labourers toiling homewards would cast a bold glance on the pretty wench stepping it resolutely beside her old duenna. But after a ribald word or two, or at worst a coarse jest, they would pass on and the two women continued their way unmolested. But the events of the day, subsequently those of the evening, were but one long string of disappointments. As soon as the first outlying houses of the city came in sight, Fleurette began inquiring pluckily and determinedly.

===Chinese===
这条路冷冷清清。一路上几乎碰不见什么人；至少，没有一个叫老路易丝心惊肉跳的人。偶尔，或许会有一伙卖了一天力气往家赶的汉子，朝那个在年老的陪媪身边迈着坚决步子的俊俏姑娘，放肆地瞟上几眼。不过他们至多说上一两句轻薄话，或者讲句下作玩笑，也就走过去了，两个女人继续赶路，不曾受到什么滋扰。然而这一天里的事，接着又是这一晚上的事，接二连三，只是一长串的失望。城郊最初几所房子刚一映入眼帘，弗勒蕾特便鼓起勇气、拿定主意，开始打听了。

===Original===
“Citizen Armand,” she would ask, “from Lou Mas, over beyond Laragne?”

===Chinese===
「公民阿尔芒，」她逢人便问，「住在拉拉涅过去、庐庄的那位？」

===Original===
“What about him?”

===Chinese===
「他怎么啦？」

===Original===
“He is an important personage in Sisteron, how could I find him?”

===Chinese===
「他是锡斯特龙的一位要人，我该怎么找到他呢？」

===Original===
And because she was gentle and had pretty blue eyes, and because she looked weary and anxious, people would do their best to help her. Some suggested one place, some another; the posting-inn﻿—he might be known there, if he sometimes posted to Paris﻿—or else the commissariat. This latter place proved a danger spot. A ferocious-looking commissaire very nearly detained the two women on a charge of vagabondage. His ugly leers and unveiled threats nearly sent Louise off her head with terror; Fleurette, however, kept up her courage nobly. The thought of Amédé drove every other terror out of her heart. She had vaguely heard that her father had something to do with a certain Committee of Public Safety. When she told this to the Commissaire, his manner immediately underwent a complete change; he became almost obsequious, placed himself entirely at the disposal of the citizeness for any inquiries she might wish to make about her illustrious father. Unfortunately, he said, the hour was late; the offices of the Committee of Public Safety situated in the Town Hall were now closed for the night. Citizen Armand had probably found shelter under the roof of a friend. Until tomorrow morning nothing could be done.

===Chinese===
也因为她性情温柔，生着一双好看的蓝眼睛，又因为她满面倦容、神色焦急，人们都愿意尽力帮她。有的指点这一处，有的指点那一处；要么上驿站客栈去问问——他若有时乘驿车去巴黎，那儿兴许有人识得他——要不然，就上警察专员公署去。后一个地方，倒真是个凶险的去处。一个面目狰狞的警察专员，差一点儿以流浪罪把两个女人扣押下来。他那副丑陋的斜睨和毫不掩饰的威吓，几乎把路易丝吓得魂灵出窍；弗勒蕾特却把勇气撑得足足的，气度俨然。心里惦着阿梅代，别的恐惧便统统被赶出了心窍。她曾依稀听人说过，她父亲同某个公安委员会有些干系。她把这层关系对专员一说，专员的态度顿时来了个彻底的大转弯，变得几乎低声下气，把自己全然听候女公民差遣，任凭她去打听她那位显赫的父亲。只可惜，他说，时候已经晚了；设在市政厅里的公安委员会各办公室，如今已关门歇夜。公民阿尔芒多半已在哪位朋友家里安歇了。明天上午之前，什么事也办不成。

===Original===
One thing, however, appeared clear; the soldiers who had created so much stir in Laragne this morning had not come to Sisteron, nor was anything known of them. There was, the now servile commissary explained, a detachment of the 87th regiment of the line in garrison in the city, and two days ago a squad of the revolutionary army lately formed for the purpose of scouring the country for traitors and aristos had passed through Sisteron and gone on in the direction of Laragne. The commissary had heard something about a family named Frontenac against whom there was a black mark for treason against the Republic, but he did not know anything about the arrest of Monsieur or the escape of Madame and Mademoiselle﻿—whom he persistently referred to as the ci-devants﻿—nor did he know anything about the arrest of Amédé Colombe, citizen of Laragne.

===Chinese===
不过有一件事看来是清楚的：今早在拉拉涅闹出那么大动静的士兵们并没有来锡斯特龙，此地也没人知道他们的下落。这位此刻奴颜婢膝的专员解释说，城里驻有第八十七战列团（the 87th regiment of the line）的一支分遣队；两天前，新近为搜剿叛徒和贵族佬而组建的革命军队，有一小队人马路过锡斯特龙，往拉拉涅方向去了。专员听说过一户姓弗朗特纳克的人家，名下记着一条背叛共和国的黑账；可是先生被捕、夫人和小姐脱逃的事，他一概不知——那母女俩，他张口闭口只管她们叫「旧贵族」——同样，拉拉涅公民阿梅代·科隆布被捕的事，他也是一概不知。

===Original===
It was all very disappointing. Fleurette, trying to be brave, nevertheless felt at times an overwhelming inclination to cry. For one thing she was very tired, and being young and healthy she was also hungry. She and Louise had consumed the contents of their provision basket when the day was still young. Now it was getting near bedtime and the goal of her efforts not even within sight. The sullenness and mistrust that seemed to hang over the whole city had the effect of further damping her spirits. The echo of the terrible doings in Orange, in Toulon and Lyons had penetrated as far as this hitherto peaceful little town. Tales of summary arrests, of death-sentences without trial, of wholesale massacres were on everybody’s lips. Accusations of treason, it seems, were more frequent than daily bread. The women looked harassed, hugged their children to their sides, as they slunk down the ill-lit streets, whilst throwing furtive glances over their shoulders. The men stood about in groups of three or four in the dark angles of the streets or beneath the ill-lit doorways, until roughly ordered to go their way by men dressed in nothing but rags, who wore a tricolour sash round their waist and a cockade on their worsted cap.

===Chinese===
这一切实在叫人灰心。弗勒蕾特竭力装出勇敢，却仍时时感到一股压不住的哭意。一来她实在乏了，而且年纪轻、身子骨结实，也就格外饿。天色还早的时候，她和路易丝就把食篮里的东西吃了个精光。如今快到就寝的时辰，她苦苦奔走的目标却连影子也望不见。整座城仿佛笼着一片阴沉与猜疑，益发压得她意气消沉。奥朗日、土伦（Toulon）和里昂那些骇人暴行的回声，一路传到了这座迄今还算太平的小城。即决逮捕呀，不经审判的死刑呀，整批整批的屠杀呀，这些传闻挂在每个人的嘴上。看来，控告叛国的罪名，比每天的面包还要常见。女人们满面愁苦，顺着昏暗的街道贴边溜行，把孩子搂在身侧，一面不住回头偷觑。男人们三三两两，聚在街角的暗处或灯火昏暗的门洞底下，直到被一些汉子粗暴地喝令各自走开——那些汉子浑身上下只有一身破烂，腰间却束着三色绶带，呢帽上别着一枚帽徽。

===Original===
And so ultimately to Les Amandiers, a quiet little inn off the main streets of the town, that Louise knew of through the drovers from Laragne who frequented the place when they were in Sisteron on market-day. Baptiste Portal, the landlord, suspicious at first, not liking the look of the two unprotected women seeking for lodgings at this hour of the night, was mollified by seeing the colour of Louise’s money and the blue of Fleurette’s eyes. His temper, it seems, had not yet recovered from the assaults made upon it a couple of days ago by a set of ragamuffins who called themselves soldiers of the Republic, and by their loud-spoken and arrogant lieutenant; but he was willing enough to make the two women welcome, and to give them supper and a bed. Then only did they tell him who they were and what the purpose of their journey: to seek Citizen Armand of Lou Mas, whose daughter Fleurette had matters of the utmost importance to communicate to him.

===Chinese===
到头来，她们投宿到了杏树园——一家避开城中大街的清静小客栈，路易丝早知道这个地方：拉拉涅的赶牲口贩子逢集日到锡斯特龙来，惯常光顾这里。店主巴普蒂斯特·波塔尔起初心存疑忌，不喜欢这两个没有护持的女人深更半夜寻宿的模样，可等到见了路易丝银钱的成色、弗勒蕾特眼睛的湛蓝，火气也就软了下来。看样子，前两天有一伙自称共和国士兵的流氓痞子，加上他们那个大呼小叫、盛气凌人的中尉，把他的脾气糟践得不轻，至今还没有缓过来；不过他倒满愿意好好接待这两个女人，给她们晚饭和一张床。直到这时，她们才告诉他自己的来历、这趟行程的目的：来寻庐庄的公民阿尔芒，他的女儿弗勒蕾特有万分紧要的事要向他面陈。

===Original===
“*Qu’a ça ne tienne!*” Baptiste Portal exclaimed. “Armand was here but a couple of hours after noon. He was on his way to Orange.”

===Chinese===
「这算不得什么（*Qu'a ça ne tienne*）！」巴普蒂斯特·波塔尔嚷道。「阿尔芒午后两个钟头还在这里呢。他往奥朗日去了。」

===Original===
“To Orange!” A cry of terror from Louise; one of excitement from Fleurette. Orange, the tiger’s den! How could two unprotected women hope to enter it without being devoured? Orange where the guillotine was at work night and day! where men and women and even children were massacred in droves, where innocent people hardly dared speak or smile or pray, lest they be seized and thrown into prison, only to be dragged out again to a horrible death.

===Chinese===
「去奥朗日！」路易丝发出一声恐怖的喊叫；弗勒蕾特那一声里，却透着兴奋。奥朗日，那可是虎穴！两个没有护持的女人，还指望进得虎口而不被吞噬吗？奥朗日，断头台日夜开工的地方！在那里，男人、女人、连孩子都成群成群地遭屠戮；无辜的人连开口、微笑、祷告都不敢，惟恐被抓去投进监狱，回头又被拖出来，落一个死得凄惨。

===Original===
Orange!

===Chinese===
奥朗日！

===Original===
But Fleurette only smiled. What had they to fear seeing Bibi chéri would be there? Was not Bibi far, far more powerful than the whole of the revolutionary army? Fleurette had seen him at the château, with a great tricolour sash round his waist, giving orders, that the officer in command of the soldiers dared not disobey.

===Chinese===
可弗勒蕾特只是微笑。亲爱的比比就会在那儿，她们还有什么可怕的呢？难道比比不比整支革命军队远远、远远地更有威势吗？弗勒蕾特在庄园里亲眼见过他：腰间束着一条宽大的三色绶带，发号施令，连统领士兵的军官都不敢违抗。

===Original===
Orange! She was not afraid of Orange! Even if the great Robespierre was in Orange she would not be afraid to go.

===Chinese===
奥朗日！她才不怕奥朗日呢！就算伟大的罗伯斯庇尔本人坐镇奥朗日，她也照样敢去。

===Original===
After all, what did it mean? Two or three days’ journey in the old *coche* which, it seems, left the Place d’Armes two days of the week, at nine o’clock in the morning, and lumbering along through Peipin, and Saint Etienne-les-Orgues, gave one the chance of getting a good bed for the night at Sault, and again at Carpentras, if one was too tired to continue one’s journey then.

===Chinese===
说到底，这算一趟什么样的路程呢？不过是坐着那辆老旧的乡村公共马车走两三天：这车瞧着是每礼拜有两天，早晨九点从练兵场（Place d'Armes）发车，一路吱吱嘎嘎地碾过佩潘（Peipin）和圣艾蒂安-莱奥尔格（Saint Etienne-les-Orgues），让人有机会在索镇（Sault）宿上一夜好床；若是那时乏得赶不动了，到了卡庞特拉（Carpentras）还能再歇一夜。

===Original===
Orange indeed? Why should one fear Orange, when chance was all in one’s favour. As luck would have it, it was the very next day that the *coche* would be starting from the Place d’Armes. All one needed was a few things, a clean pair of stockings, a handkerchief or two, a bit of soap and a towel, which dear, kind Ma’ame Portal was only too ready to lend; these were tied in a bundle and formed the only indispensable luggage which Fleurette and Louise would take with them. Fortunately Louise had plenty of money in her pocket, being always well supplied by Bibi, and then, of course, in Orange, Bibi would be there and he would provide further as necessity arose.

===Chinese===
奥朗日又当如何呢？机缘全在自己这一边，凭什么要怕奥朗日？也是运气使然，恰恰就在第二天，乡村公共马车便要从练兵场发车。要带的不过是几样零星物事：一双干净袜子、一两条手绢、一小块肥皂、一条毛巾——这些，亲爱的好心的波塔尔大娘（Ma'ame Portal）都巴不得借给她们；物件打成一个小包袱，便是弗勒蕾特和路易丝随身的、必不可少的全部行李。幸而路易丝口袋里的钱很充足，比比向来把她供应得宽裕；再说，到了奥朗日，比比自然在那里，缺什么，他自会随时张罗。

===Original===
And thus it came to pass that among the passengers who took their places in the lumbering old vehicle that morning were two females, one of whom had corn-coloured hair and eyes bluer than forget-me-nots.

===Chinese===
于是事情就这样成了：那天早晨，在这辆吱嘎作响的老爷车里各就各位的乘客中间，坐着两个女人——其中一个，一头谷穗色的头发，一双比勿忘我还要蓝的眼睛。

## XVII / 第十七章

===Original===
The Hôtel de Ville at Orange still stands, as it did then, in the newly-named Place de la République; and if the tourist of today mounts its steps, enters the building through its central portal, crosses the wide vestibule and finally turns down a long corridor on his right, he will, almost at the end of this, come to a door which bears the legend: “*Travaux Publics.*”

===Chinese===
奥朗日的市政厅（Hôtel de Ville）至今还立在原地，一如当年，就在新命名的共和国广场（Place de la République）上。今天的游客若是拾级而上，从正中的大门走进楼里，穿过宽阔的门厅，再向右拐进一条长廊，差不多走到廊子尽头，就会来到一扇门前，门上钉着一块牌子，写着：「公共工程（*Travaux Publics*）」。

===Original===
Should he be bold enough to push open the door, he will find himself in a perfectly banal room, with whitewashed walls covered with maps and plans that are of no interest to him, a large desk at one end, and a few wooden chairs. There is a thin carpet in the middle of the red-tiled floor and faded green rep curtains temper the glaring light of the afternoon sun. But on this day of May 1794 there were no curtains to the window, and not even a strip of carpet on the floor. There was no desk either, only a long trestle table covered with a tattered green cloth, behind which, on wooden chairs, sat three men, dressed alike in dark blue coats tightly buttoned across the chest, drab breeches and high topped boots, and wearing tricolour sashes around their waist.

===Chinese===
他若有胆量推开这扇门，便会发现自己站在一间再平常不过的屋子里：四壁粉刷过白，墙上挂满他毫无兴趣的地图和图纸，屋子一头摆着一张大书桌，另有几把木椅。红砖地正中铺着一块薄地毯，褪了色的绿梭纹布窗帘，调和着午后烈日的强光。可是在 1794 年 5 月的这一天，窗口没有窗帘，地上连一条地毯也没有。也没有书桌，只有一张条凳架起的长桌，蒙着一块破破烂烂的绿桌布，桌后坐着三个人，坐在木椅上，装束一模一样：深蓝色上衣，胸前扣得严严实实，土黄色马裤，长统马靴，腰间都围着三色绶带。

===Original===
The one who sat in the centre and who appeared to be in supreme authority rested his elbow on the table, and his chin was supported in his hand. He was gazing intently on a man who stood before him, in the centre of the room, the other side of the table; a man who looked footsore and weary and who wore a military uniform all tattered and covered with slime and dust.

===Chinese===
坐在正中、看样子握有至高权威的那一个，肘支在桌上，用手托着下巴，正目不转睛地盯着一个人；那人站在他面前、屋子中央、桌子的另一头，一脸风尘困顿，身上的军装破破烂烂，糊满了泥浆和尘土。

===Original===
The two others also kept their eyes fixed on this man. They were listening with rapt interest to the story which he was relating. Early this morning he and a dozen others also attired in tattered uniforms had come into Orange in a state bordering on collapse. They had made their way to the barracks where the officer in command had mercifully given them food and drink. As soon as they had eaten and drunk, they tried to tell their story; but this was so amazing, not to say incredible, that the officer in command had thought it prudent to send for the superintendent of gendarmerie, who in turn had the men conveyed to the Hôtel de Ville, there to be brought before the Representative of the Convention on special mission who sat with the Committee of Public Safety. And now Lieutenant Godet stood alone to face the Committee; the others had been handed back to the gendarmerie to be dealt with later on. The representative on special mission who sat with the two other Members of the Committee at the table covered with the tattered green cloth, had questioned Godet, and he thereupon embarked upon the story of this amazing adventure. He began by relating the events which three days ago had set the quiet little commune of Laragne seething with excitement. He told of the arrival of the squad of soldiers in magnificent uniforms, under the command of an officer more superb than anything that had ever been seen in the countryside before. He told of the perquisition in the house of Citizen Colombe the grocer, by those magnificent soldiers, of the finding there by them of certain valuables belonging to the ci-devant Frontenacs, valuables which he himself had vainly searched for in the château, the evening before. He told of the arrest of young Colombe: of the high-handed manner in which the superb officer had relieved him, Godet, of his command, and ordered him and his men, together with the ci-devant Frontenac, to join his squad, and to march with him out of Laragne. He had told it all with a wealth of detail, and the members of the Committee had listened in silence and with rapt interest.

===Chinese===
另外两个人也把目光钉在这人身上，凝神屏息地听他讲述自己的遭遇。今天清晨，他同另外十来个人——同样一个个衣衫褴褛——走进了奥朗日，几乎到了垮掉的地步。他们摸到兵营，营里管带的军官发了善心，给了他们吃喝。刚一吃饱喝足，他们便要讲自己的经历；可这经历离奇得吓人，简直可以说是叫人没法信，管带的军官觉得稳妥起见，派人去请宪兵队总监，宪兵队总监又把这一行人押到市政厅，送到同公安委员会一同办公的国民公会特派代表面前。如今单剩戈岱中尉一个人面对委员会；其余的人已经交还宪兵队，听候发落。同另外两位委员坐在那张蒙着破绿布的桌后的特派代表审问了戈岱，戈岱随即讲起这段惊人冒险的始末。他先从三天前那桩把安安静静的拉拉涅小公社搅得群情鼎沸的事件讲起。他讲到那队军装华丽的士兵开进城来，带队军官的气派，乡下人见所未见。讲到那些神气的士兵如何搜查杂货商公民科隆布的家宅，如何在他家搜出旧贵族弗朗特纳克家的几件值钱物件——头天晚上，他本人还曾在庄园里为这些东西白白搜了一场。讲到小科隆布被逮捕；讲到那位神气十足的军官如何蛮横地解除了他戈岱的指挥权，命令他和他的人，连同旧贵族弗朗特纳克，一并编入他的队伍，随他开出拉拉涅。他讲得巨细靡遗，几位委员默默听着，听得入了神。

===Original===
But now the man at the table who was the representative on special mission, and who appeared chief in authority, broke in with an exclamation that was almost one of rage.

===Chinese===
可这时，桌旁那位特派代表——看样子是掌总的一个——猛地插进来一声断喝，几乎怒不可遏。

===Original===
“And do you mean to tell me, citizen lieutenant,” he said in a harsh, rasping voice, “that you could mistake a lot of English spies﻿—for that is what they were, you may take it from me﻿—that you could mistake them, I say, for soldiers of our army. Where were your eyes?”

===Chinese===
「那么你是想告诉我，公民中尉，」他声音又粗又刺耳地说道，「你竟会把一伙英国奸细——他们就是奸细，你只管信我的话——你竟会把他们，我说，当成了我们军队的士兵。你的眼睛长到哪儿去了？」

===Original===
Lieutenant Godet gave a shrug which he hoped would pass for unconcern. In reality he felt physically sick; a prey to overwhelming terror. At first, when he and his men had come in sight of the city, they had felt nothing but relief to see the end of what had been almost martyrdom. It was only afterwards, when he found himself in this narrow room, with its whitewashed walls and its silence, and face to face with those three men, that fear had entered his heart. He felt like an animal in a cage﻿—a mouse looking into the pale, piercing eyes of a cat. He passed his tongue once or twice over his parched lips before he gave reply.

===Chinese===
戈岱中尉耸了耸肩，指望这一下能装出满不在乎的样子。实际上他只觉得浑身发虚，整个人被一阵压倒一切的恐怖攥住了。起初，他和他的人望见这座城的时候，心里只有如释重负——那场几乎算得上受难的苦差总算到了头。只是后来，等他发觉自己置身于这间四壁刷白的窄屋里，四下死寂，同那三个人面对面，恐惧这才钻进了他的心。他觉得自己像关在笼子里的畜生——一只正望着猫那苍白刺人的眼睛的老鼠。他舔了一两下干得冒烟的嘴唇，才开口回答。

===Original===
“I was not the only one, citizen,” he said sullenly, “who was deceived. The whole commune of Laragne was at the heels of those soldiers. My own men were mustered before the pseudo-captain and heard him give words of command.”

===Chinese===
「上当的又不是我一个，公民，」他闷闷地说，「受骗的是整个拉拉涅公社，全都追在那伙士兵屁股后头跑。我手下的人就在那位假上尉面前列队，听他发号施令。」

===Original===
“But Englishmen, citizen lieutenant,” the man at the table argued; “Englishmen! Their appearance! Their speech!”

===Chinese===
「可他们是英国人哪，公民中尉，」桌旁那人反驳道，「英国人！瞧他们那副模样！听他们那口谈吐！」

===Original===
“They spoke as you and I would, Citizen Chauvelin,” Godet retorted, still sullenly, “As for appearance, one man is like another. I could not be expected to know every officer of our army by sight.”

===Chinese===
「他们说话，同您同我一个腔调，公民肖维兰，」戈岱反唇相讥，依旧闷声闷气，「至于模样嘛，人跟人还能差到哪里去。总不能指望我把我们军队里的军官一个个都认得脸熟。」

===Original===
“But you said they were splendidly dressed!”

===Chinese===
「可你说过，他们穿得漂漂亮亮！」

===Original===
“They were. I knew the uniform well enough. Had there been a doubtful button or a galloon wanting I should have spotted it.

===Chinese===
「他们是穿得漂亮。那种军装我熟得很。哪怕有一颗纽扣可疑、少一根饰绦，我也瞧得出来。

===Original===
“But so clean!” one of the others at the table remarked with a sigh, that might have been of envy, “so magnificent!”

===Chinese===
「可他们那么齐整！」桌旁另一个叹了口气说，那口气里没准儿还带着艳羡，「那么神气！」

===Original===
“I knew that there were some *compagnies d’élite*,” the lieutenant rejoined, “attached to certain regiments. How could I guess?”

===Chinese===
「我知道有几个团队附设着精锐连（*compagnies d’élite*），」中尉接道，「我哪里猜得到呢？」

===Original===
“It might have been better for you, if you had,” the man in the centre remarked dryly.

===Chinese===
「你若是猜得到，」正中那一位冷冷地说，「兴许倒好了。」

===Original===
Godet’s wan face took on a more ashen hue; again he passed his tongue over his parched lips.

===Chinese===
戈岱那张蜡黄的脸愈发灰败了；他又舔了舔干裂的嘴唇。

===Original===
“Haven’t we had enough of this?” one of the others at the table now put in impatiently. “We are satisfied that those English spies, or whatever they were, acted with amazing effrontery, which makes me think that perhaps they are a part of that gang that we all know of, and of which Citizen Chauvelin spoke just now. We are also satisfied that Citizen Lieutenant Godet did not show that acumen which an officer in his responsible position should have done. What we want to know now is, what happened after the pseudo-captain of the so-called 33rd division had arrested that young Colombe and marched out of Laragne?”

===Chinese===
「这一套我们还没听够吗？」桌旁另一人不耐烦地插了进来。「那伙英国奸细——不管他们究竟是什么人——干起事来脸皮厚得骇人，这一点我们已经信了；这倒叫我想起，他们兴许就是那一个人人心里有数的匪帮里的角色，正是公民肖维兰方才提到的那一伙。公民中尉戈岱，没能拿出一名身居其职的军官本该有的敏锐来，这一点我们也信了。现在我们要知道的是：那位自称第三十三分队的假上尉，抓了小科隆布、开出拉拉涅之后，又出了什么事？」

===Original===
“And in your interest, citizen lieutenant,” the man in the centre rejoined sternly, “I advise you to make a statement that is truthful in every detail.”

===Chinese===
「而且我劝你，公民中尉，」正中那人严厉地接道，「供词要字字属实，这于你自身有利。」

===Original===
“Had I wished to tell lies,” the soldier retorted sullenly, “I shouldn’t be here now. I should have﻿—”

===Chinese===
「我若是有心扯谎，」那士兵倔头倔脑地顶了回去，「这会儿就不会在这儿了。我本来可以——」

===Original===
“No matter,” the other broke in curtly, “what you would have done. The State desires to know what you did.”

===Chinese===
「不必说，」对方干脆打断了他，「你本来可以怎么怎么，无关紧要。国家要知道的是你干了什么。」

===Original===
“Well!” Lieutenant Godet began after a moment or two during which he appeared to collect his thoughts. “We marched out of Laragne in the direction of Serres. The captain﻿—I still, of course, looked upon him as a captain﻿—had so disposed us that I and my own men were between two squads of his. We were footsore, all of us, because we had had three days’ tramping in the dust, one day battling against hard wind, another with long hours spent in scouring the château of those traitors Frontenacs; we were also very hungry. Remember that we had been dragged out of our beds in the early morning, and not given a chance of getting a bite or drink before starting on the march. But they, the others, were fresh as if they had just come out of barracks with their bellies full.﻿ ﻿… They marched along at a swinging pace, and it was as much as we could do to keep step with them.”

===Chinese===
「好吧！」戈岱中尉沉吟了一两刻，像是先把思路收拢了，这才开了腔。「我们开出拉拉涅，朝塞尔方向走。那位上尉——我自然还把他当作上尉——把队伍布得很妙：我同我的人，正夹在他两拨队伍中间。我们个个脚底都走烂了，因为已经在尘土里连踩了三天：一天顶着硬邦邦的大风赶路，又花一整天的工夫去搜弗朗特纳克那伙叛徒的庄园；我们还饿得慌。别忘了，我们是一清早被人从被窝里拖出来的，上路前连一口吃的、一口喝的都没捞着。可他们，那一伙人，却个个精神抖擞，仿佛刚吃饱喝足从兵营里开出来。……他们迈开大步，一摇一摆地往前赶，我们拼了命，才跟得上他们的步点。」

===Original===
The man’s voice became somewhat more steady as he talked. The note of terror which had been so conspicuous in it at first had given place to one of dull resentment. Encouraged by the obvious interest which his story had evoked in his hearers, he resumed more glibly:

===Chinese===
讲着讲着，这人的嗓音渐渐稳了些。起初那股扎眼的惶恐腔调，已经换成了一腔沉甸甸的怨气。眼见自己的故事分明勾起了听者的兴味，他胆子一壮，口齿也顺溜起来：

===Original===
“About half a league north of Laragne, a bridle-path branches off the high road; into this the captain ordered his company to turn, and we continued to plod along through the dust and in the midday heat, till we came to a tumbledown cottage by the roadside; a cottage flanked by a dilapidated shed, and a bit of garden all overgrown with weeds. Here a halt was called, and the prisoners were ordered out of the wagon. A moment or two later a woman appeared at the cottage door, some words were exchanged between her and the captain, and subsequently, when order to march was given, the prisoners marched along with us; the wagon and horses having been left behind at the cottage.”

===Chinese===
「拉拉涅以北约莫半里格，有一条马道从大路上岔出去；上尉命令队伍转上这条道，我们便继续在尘土里、在正午的暑气里一步步往前蹭，直蹭到路边一座东倒西歪的小屋跟前；屋子一侧搭着一间破棚子，还有一小片荒草没膝的园子。在这儿传令停下，犯人被押下大车。过了片刻，屋门口出现一个女人，她同上尉交谈了几句；随后开拔令一下，犯人便同我们一道步行；大车和马都撂在那座小屋了。」

===Original===
“Didn’t you think this very strange, citizen lieutenant?” one of the men at the table asked; “a wagon and horses which you would naturally presume belonged to the State, being thus left at a tumbledown roadside cottage?”

===Chinese===
「你不觉得这事蹊跷吗，公民中尉？」桌旁一人问道，「一辆大车、几匹马，你顺理成章该认定是国家的财物，就这么撂在路边一座破屋跟前？」

===Original===
“Whatever I may have thought,” the lieutenant replied, “it was not my place to make observations to my superior officer.”

===Chinese===
「我心里想什么姑且不论，」中尉答道，「对上级军官评头论足，可不归我管。」

===Original===
“Superior officer!” the man in the centre remarked, with a gesture of contemptuous wrath.

===Chinese===
「上级军官！」正中那人应了一声，愤然做一个鄙夷的手势。

===Original===
“I think, Citizen Chauvelin,” the accused now put in a little more firmly, “that you are unnecessarily hard on me. There was really nothing to indicate﻿—”

===Chinese===
「我看哪，公民肖维兰，」受审的这一位这时插了话，口气硬了些许，「您对我未免太苛了。实在是什么迹象也没有——」

===Original===
But the other broke in with a vicious snarl:

===Chinese===
可对方一声恶狠狠的咆哮，打断了他：

===Original===
“Nothing to indicate﻿—? Nothing? The eyes of a patriot should be sharp enough to detect a spy or a traitor through any disguise﻿—”

===Chinese===
「什么迹象也没有——？没有？爱国者的眼睛就该尖利，任凭什么伪装，也该一眼看穿奸细和叛徒——」

===Original===
He paused abruptly, and cast a quick, inquisitorial glance at his two colleagues first, then at the soldier before him. Had he detected a trace, a sign, a flicker of the eyelid that betrayed knowledge of his own past? of the times﻿—numberless now﻿—that he too had been hoodwinked by those bold adventurers who called themselves the League of the Scarlet Pimpernel, and by their chief whose prowess in the art of disguise had marked some of the most humiliating hours in Chauvelin’s career? Calais, Boulogne, Nantes, Paris; each of those great cities had a record of the Terrorist’s discomfiture when brought face to face with that mysterious and elusive Scarlet Pimpernel. Even, now crushed in the hot palm of his hand, he held a scrap of paper which had revealed the author of the plot to which that fool Lieutenant Godet had fallen a victim﻿—just as he, himself, Chauvelin, had done﻿—just like that﻿—and so many times﻿—. The penalty for him had always been more humiliation, a further fall from the original high place which he had once occupied in Paris: and with it the knowledge that one day the masters of France would tire of his failures. Ah! he knew that well enough, he knew that they would tire, and then they would crush him as they had crushed others, whose only crime, like his, had been failure.

===Chinese===
他猛地顿住，先朝两位同僚、又朝面前的士兵飞快扫了一眼，那目光像审讯一般探人。他可曾捕捉到一丝痕迹、一点征兆、一下泄露天机的眨眼皮，表明对方知道他的老底？知道那一回回——如今已数不清了——他自己也叫那伙胆大包天、自号猩红繁笺花同盟的冒险家们蒙蔽过？也叫他们的首领蒙蔽过——那人一手易容改扮的绝技，在肖维兰的生涯里烙下了几段最见不得人的时辰？加来、布洛涅、南特、巴黎；这几座大城，每一座都留着记录：这位恐怖党人一跟那位神秘莫测、来去无踪的猩红繁笺花打了照面，便栽了跟头。甚至此刻，他的手心里还滚烫地攥着一小张纸——就是这张纸，泄露了那桩骗局的始作俑者，害得蠢货戈岱中尉做了冤大头——正像他肖维兰自己栽过的那样——正是那样——还不止多少回——。落到他头上的惩罚，向来是又添一层羞辱，从他在巴黎原先占着的高位上再朝下跌一截；随之而来的，还有那份心知肚明：终有一日，法兰西的主子们要腻烦他的连番败绩。啊！这个他再清楚不过了，他清楚他们终有腻烦的一天，到那时他们就会像碾死旁人一样把他碾死——那些人同他一样，唯一的罪名，就是败绩。

===Original===
His only claim to immunity, so far, had been the fact that he alone, of all the members of the National Assembly, of all the members of Committees, or of the Executive, knew who the Scarlet Pimpernel really was; he had seen him without disguise; he knew him by name, not only him but some of his more important followers; and when some of the ferocious tyrants, who for the time being were the masters of France, did at times loudly demand the suppression of Citizen Chauvelin, for incompetence that amounted to treason, there were always others who pleaded for him because of that knowledge. Many felt that with the death of Chauvelin, the last hope of capturing that band of English spies would have to be abandoned; and so they pleaded for his retention and their fellow-tyrants allowed him another few months’ grace so that he might accomplish that which they knew was the great purpose of his life. And whenever in the opinion of those bloodthirsty tigers, who held France under their domination, some outlying provincial districts had need of what they called “purging from the pestilence of traitors,” whenever wholesale arrests, perquisitions, wholesale death-sentences or brutal massacres were the order of the day, Citizen Chauvelin was sent down with special powers, always in the vain hope that the Scarlet Pimpernel, emboldened by success, would fall into the trap perpetually set for him. The English spy’s predilection for aristos, his sympathies so quickly aroused when traitors happened to be women or young children, was sure to draw his activities to any region where prisons were full and the guillotine kept busy.

===Chinese===
他至今还能免遭清算，全凭一件事实：国民议会全体议员之中、各委员会诸公之中、行政当局大员之中，唯独他一人知道猩红繁笺花究竟是谁；他见过那人不加伪装的真容；他知道那人的姓名——不光是他，还有他手下几员更要紧的干将。因此，每逢那班一时称霸法兰西的凶残僭主高声鼓噪，要拿办事不力、几近叛国的罪名革掉公民肖维兰，总有另一些人替他求情——求的正是他肚里这点见识。许多人觉得，肖维兰一死，缉拿那帮英国奸细的最后一线指望便只好撒手；于是他们力主留他任上，那些同僚僭主便容他再宽限几个月，好让他去成就那桩他们都知道是他此生宏图的大业。而在那班把法兰西踩在铁蹄之下的嗜血猛虎看来，凡是哪处偏远的外省地面需要来一番他们所谓的「肃清叛徒瘟疫」，凡是大搜捕、大抄检、成批的死判或野蛮的屠戮成了当务之急，派下去执掌特权的，总是公民肖维兰——永远抱着那一线痴想：猩红繁笺花连胜之后气焰一高，自会落入那张为他常设不撤的天罗地网。那英国奸细素来偏疼贵族佬，叛徒一旦恰巧是妇人孩童，他的恻隐之心便最易触动；哪里的牢狱塞得满满、断头台忙个不歇，他的手脚准会伸到哪里去。

===Original===
Thus it was that Citizen Chauvelin had been sent to Orange. The Southern provinces of France had been left far too long to welter in a morass of treason; there were veritable nests of traitors in the châteaux and farms of Provence and Dauphiné. The country had to be purged: the traitors extirpated; the magnificent Law of the Suspect be set in motion to do this cleansing process. Any man who ventured to criticize the government, who complained of taxation or restrictions was a traitor; any man or woman who owned more than they needed for bare subsistence, who refused to pour of their surplus into the lap of patriots, was a traitor; and the country must be purged of them, until the dictatorship of the proletariat was firmly established, until every man, woman and child in the whole of France had been dragged down to the same level of mental and physical wreckage.

===Chinese===
公民肖维兰此番派到奥朗日，根由便在这里。法兰西南方各省，听任它们在叛乱的泥沼里泡得实在太久了；普罗旺斯和多菲内的庄园里、农庄里，处处是名副其实的叛徒窝。这国家非清洗不可：叛徒非斩草除根不可；还得叫那部堂堂的嫌疑犯法令开动起来，包揽这桩荡垢涤污的工程。谁敢非议政府，谁抱怨捐税禁令，谁就是叛徒；谁家的产业超出了糊口之需、又不肯把盈余倒进爱国者的腰包，谁就是叛徒；必须把这号人从国中荡涤干净，直到无产阶级专政稳稳立定，直到全法兰西的男女老幼，人人被拖到身心俱残的同一水平线上，方才罢休。

===Original===
There had been a dramatic pause after Chauvelin’s outburst of contemptuous wrath; for a minute or two, while the old clock up on the wall ticked away with slow monotony a strange silence remained hanging over the scene. Whatever the other three men may have known or remembered of the noted Terrorist’s past history, they thought it wiser to say nothing. In these days of universal brotherhood and Liberty, every man in France was frightened of his neighbour. The time had come when the lustful tigers, satiated with the blood of those whom they deemed their enemies, had turned, thirsting, for that of their whilom friends. The makers of the Terror had started digging their fangs into each other’s throats. The victory now was to the most ferocious. After the Girondins, Danton, he, who had ordered the September massacres, two and a half years ago, had had to yield to a more vengeful, more merciless power than his own.

===Chinese===
肖维兰那通鄙夷的雷霆发过之后，场上戏剧性地静了片刻；有那么一两分钟，只有墙上的老挂钟慢吞吞、单调地嘀嗒作响，一种古怪的沉默悬在头顶。另外三个人，无论知晓还是记得这位赫赫有名的恐怖党人多少旧事，都觉得一声不吭才是上策。在这普天之下讲博爱、个个嘴里喊自由的年月，法兰西人人都怕自己的近邻。世道已经到了这一步：那班欲壑难填的猛虎，喝饱了他们眼中的仇敌之血，又渴起昔日朋友的血来。制造恐怖的人们，已经开始把毒牙扎进彼此的喉咙。如今的胜利，归最凶残者。继吉伦特派之后，两年半前下令制造九月屠杀的丹东，也不得不向一个比他更记仇、更无情的权势低头了。

===Original===
Chauvelin knew that. The victory today was to the most ferocious. He who would sacrifice friends, brother, sister, child, was the true patriot; the man who stayed his hand in face of a revolting crime that would put a wild beast to the blush, was unworthy the name of citizen of France. Therefore death to him. Death to the weakling. To the Moderate. This was the era of the Universal Brotherhood of Death.

===Chinese===
这个，肖维兰心里有数。今日的胜利，归最凶残者。肯牺牲朋友、兄弟、姐妹、亲儿的，才是真正的爱国者；面对一桩连野兽见了都要脸红的兽行还肯缩一缩手的，就不配法兰西公民这个名号。所以，处死他。处死那软骨头。处死那温和派。这便是死亡普世博爱的时代。

===Original===
What chance then had this unfortunate Lieutenant Godet now? brought to justice﻿—save the mark!﻿—before a man who knew that to show weakness was to court death. No wonder that all the swagger and the arrogance which made him but a day or two ago the terror of a lot of peasants at Sisteron or Laragne, was knocked out of him, by a mere glance from those pale, piercing eyes. And he﻿—a mere notary’s clerk born and bred in the depth of Dauphiné, and thrust into the army, as a mule is thrust into harness﻿—knew nothing of Paris save from hearsay, and nothing of the men whose word had even sent a king and a queen to the scaffold. He knew nothing of Citizen Chauvelin, save that he was a man of power, before whose piercing glance and tricolour sash, every man instinctively cringed and trembled. He knew nothing of Chauvelin’s tussles with those same English spies who had so effectually led him, Godet, by the nose; nothing of Citizen Chauvelin’s past life, very little of the present. He was just a mouse in the power of a cat; allowed a little freedom just now, while he told his tale of failure.

===Chinese===
那么，眼前这个倒霉的戈岱中尉，还有什么生路可指望呢？他被押到「公道」跟前——好一个公道！——面对的判官深知：示弱便是自寻死路。也难怪：就在一两天前，他还凭着一身的威风骄横，做锡斯特龙、拉拉涅一干乡下人的凶神；如今只消那双苍白刺人的眼睛一扫，这一身威风骄横便被打得无影无踪。再说他——不过是多菲内腹地生、腹地长的一个小小公证人录事，被人硬塞进军队，好比骡子被人硬套上轭具——对巴黎，他只在风闻里识得一点皮毛；对那班一句话就能把国王和王后送上断头台的人物，他更是一概不知。对公民肖维兰，他只知道此人有权有势，在那刺人的目光和三色绶带面前，人人都会不由自主地畏缩发抖。肖维兰同那班英国奸细有过怎样的交手——正是那班人牵着他戈岱的鼻子，牵得那样牢——他一无所知；公民肖维兰的过去，他一无所知，眼下的种种，他也所知寥寥。他不过是猫爪之下的一只老鼠；此刻容他稍得自由，无非是让他讲讲自己那段败绩罢了。

===Original===
“Continue, citizen lieutenant,” Chauvelin now said more calmly. “We are listening.”

===Chinese===
「接着讲，公民中尉，」肖维兰这时口气平和了些，说道，「我们听着呢。」

===Original===
“Let me see,” Godet rejoined vaguely, “where was I?”

===Chinese===
「让我想想，」戈岱恍恍惚惚地接道，「我讲到哪儿了？」

===Original===
“On the bridle-path off the main road,” Chauvelin responded with a sneer; “half a league north of Laragne. The wagon and horses presumably belonging to the State left in a tumbledown cottage by the roadside. A thrilling situation forsooth. An ordinary situation you would have us believe. Pray continue. What happened after that?”

===Chinese===
「在大路旁岔出去的那条马道上，」肖维兰冷笑着应道，「拉拉涅以北约半里格。一辆照理该归国家所有的大车和几匹马，撂在路边一座破屋里。好一桩惊心动魄的事。你倒要我们信，这不过是一桩平平常常的事。请往下讲。后来又怎么样了？」

===Original===
“We marched and we marched and we marched,” the lieutenant resumed sullenly. “We marched until we were ready to drop. We had had three days of marching, and had started in the morning without a bite, hungry! *Nom d’un nom!* how hungry we were! and weak and faint! The hours sped on; we could see the sun mounting the heavens and then start on its descent. The heat was intense, the dust terrible. It filled our eyes, our nostrils, our mouths. The soles of our feet were bleeding, sweat poured down our faces and obscured our vision. We marched and we marched, through two villages, the names of which I do not know; then over mountain passes, across rocky gorges, stepping over streams, climbing the sides of hills, the banks of rivers. I am a stranger in these parts. And I was tired. Tired! I knew not where we were, whither we were going. March! March! March! Ceaselessly. Even had I dared, I would no longer have had the strength to ask questions or to beg for mercy.”

===Chinese===
「我们走啊，走啊，走啊，」中尉又闷声闷气地讲了下去。「一直走到人都要瘫倒。我们整整走了三天，一早开拔，粒米未进，饿呀！见鬼（*Nom d'un nom*），我们那个饿法哟！人又虚又晕！时辰飞快地过去；我们眼看着太阳升上天顶，又眼看它转着往回落。热得厉害，尘土吓人。尘土灌满了我们的眼睛、鼻孔、嘴巴。脚底板走出了血，汗水顺着脸往下淌，糊住了视线。我们走啊，走啊，穿过两个村子，村名我叫不上来；接着翻山口，过石峡，跨溪涧，爬山坡，沿河岸。这一带我人生地不熟。我又乏了。乏呀！我不知道我们到了哪儿，往哪里去。走！走！走！没个停歇。就算我敢开口，我也早没有力气问一句话、讨一声饶了。」

===Original===
And at the recollection of those hours of agony, Lieutenant Godet wiped the perspiration from his streaming brow.

===Chinese===
回想起那几个钟头的苦楚，戈岱中尉抹了一把额上涔涔而下的汗水。

===Original===
“Well?” Chauvelin queried dryly, “and the others, the Englishmen?”

===Chinese===
「后来呢？」肖维兰干巴巴地问，「那伙人呢，那些英国人？」

===Original===
“They marched along at a swinging pace,” Godet replied, smothering a savage oath. “Without turning a hair. They kicked up no dust. They did not sweat. They just marched. No doubt their bellies were well filled.”

===Chinese===
「他们照样大摇大摆地走，」戈岱答道，咽下一句毒咒。「眉毛都不动一根。他们脚下不起尘土。汗也不出一滴。他们只管走。不用说，肚皮都是填得满满当当的。」

===Original===
“And the prisoners?”

===Chinese===
「犯人呢？」

===Original===
“They set to with a will. And I make no doubt but they had fed and drunk while they sat in the wagon. At any rate they showed no fatigue.”

===Chinese===
「他们走得劲头十足。而且我毫不怀疑，他们坐在大车里那阵，早就吃饱喝足了。至少他们看不出一丝倦意。」

===Original===
“How long did you continue on the march?”

===Chinese===
「你们这样走了多久？」

===Original===
“Till one by one we﻿—my comrades and I﻿—fell out by the roadside.”

===Chinese===
「走到我们——我和我的弟兄们——一个接一个倒卧在路旁为止。」

===Original===
“And those who fell out were left, while the others went on?”

===Chinese===
「倒下的就撂下不管，其余的接着往前走？」

===Original===
“Yes! We had gone through the second village, and were marching along the edge of a stream, when the first lot of us dropped out. Three of my men. They just rolled down the bank of the stream; and there lay on their stomachs trying to drink. The captain﻿—or whatever he was, curse him!﻿—called ‘Halt!’ and one of his men ran down the bank and had a look at those three poor fellows who lay there striving to slake their hunger as well as their thirst in the cool mountain stream. But, *nom de nom*! They﻿—the miscreants!﻿—had no bowels of compassion. I believe﻿—for in truth I was too tired to see anything clearly﻿—that one of them did leave a hunk of bread by the side of the stream: perhaps he was afraid that those poor fellows would die of inanition and then their death would be upon his conscience.”

===Chinese===
「是啊！过了第二个村子，我们正沿着一条溪走，头一批人就掉了队。是我手下三个人。他们就顺着溪岸滚下去，趴在那儿想法子喝水。那上尉——管他到底是什么东西，叫他遭瘟！——喊了声『立定！』，他手下一个兵跑下溪岸，看了看那三个可怜虫——他们趴在那儿，指着在清凉的山溪里又解渴、又解饿。可是，见鬼（*nom de nom*）！他们——那伙恶棍！——没有一副热心肠。我相信——说实话，我那时乏得看什么都看不真了——他们里头倒有一个人，真在溪边撂了一大块面包：也许是怕那几个可怜虫活活饿死，到时候这条人命记在他良心上。」

===Original===
“Well! And did all the men fall out that way?”

===Chinese===
「好！那么弟兄们全都是这么掉队的？」

===Original===
“Yes! We were marching three abreast: and three by three we all fell out. Always beside the stream, for we suffered from thirst as much as from hunger. The stream seemed to draw us, and three of us, as if by common understanding, would just roll down the bank and lie on our stomachs and try to drink. The captain no longer called a halt when that happened. One of his own men would just throw pieces of bread down to the edge of the stream, just as they would to a dog.”

===Chinese===
「是的！我们三人一排往前走：便三人三人地全都掉了队。总在溪边，因为我们渴得同饿得一样厉害。那溪水像是在招引我们，我们三个人，像约好了似的，顺着溪岸就滚下去，趴在地上想法子喝水。再遇上这种事，那上尉索性不喊停了。他自己手下便会有个兵，把一块块面包扔到溪边去，就像扔给一条狗似的。」

===Original===
“And you were the last to fall out?”

===Chinese===
「你是最后一个掉队的？」

===Original===
“The very last. I verily believe, when I rolled down the bank and felt the cool stream against my face, that I had died and reached the Elysian fields. A piece of bread was thrown to me, and I fell on it like a starved beast.”

===Chinese===
「正是最后一个。我顺着溪岸滚下去、觉出那清凉的溪水贴上脸的时候，我当真信自己已经死了，到了极乐世界（Elysian fields）。一块面包扔到我身边，我扑上去，活像一头饿疯了的畜生。」

===Original===
“And then what happened?”

===Chinese===
「后来出了什么事？」

===Original===
“Nothing.”

===Chinese===
「没出什么事。」

===Original===
“What do you mean? Nothing?”

===Chinese===
「你这是什么意思？没出什么事？」

===Original===
“Nothing as far as we were concerned. The bank of the stream, for a length of two kilometres or more, was strewn with our dead﻿—that is not dead, you understand, but fatigued, and only half-conscious with hunger: while those miscreants, those limbs of Satan, marched off without as much as a last look at us! Gaily they marched away singing. Yes, singing, some awful gibberish, in a tongue I did not understand. That is,” poor Godet went on ruefully, “when first I had an inkling of the awful truth. That strange tongue gave it away. You understand?”

===Chinese===
「就我们而论，什么也没出。长达两公里开外的一段溪岸上，横七竖八躺满了我们这些倒毙的——并不是真死了，您明白，是乏垮了，饿得只剩半昏迷：那伙恶棍，那班撒旦的爪牙，却开步走了，连末了一眼都不曾朝我们瞧！他们唱着歌，欢天喜地开拔而去。是的，唱着歌，唱些乌七八糟的调调，那语言我听不懂。就是说，」可怜的戈岱苦着脸接着说，「打那时候起，我才头一回落下一点影子，咂摸出那可怕的真相来。正是那口外来腔把它戳穿的。您明白吗？」

===Original===
The others nodded.

===Chinese===
桌旁几个人点了点头。

===Original===
“And then, by chance, I put my hand in the back pocket of my tunic, and felt that piece of paper.”

===Chinese===
「后来，碰巧我把手伸进上衣后袋，摸到了那张纸。」

===Original===
With finger that quivered slightly, he pointed to Chauvelin’s hand; between the clenched claw-like fingers there protruded the corner of a scrap of paper. Chauvelin failed to suppress the exclamation of rage which rose to his lips.

===Chinese===
他伸出一根微微打颤的手指，指向肖维兰的手；那只手攥得死紧，指头钩曲如爪，指缝间露出小小一角纸片。一声怒骂涌到肖维兰嘴边，他没能压住。

===Original===
“*Nom de nom!*” he muttered savagely through his teeth, and with his handkerchief he wiped the beads of moisture that had risen to the roots of his hair.

===Chinese===
「见鬼（*Nom de nom*）！」他从牙缝里狠狠挤出一句，掏出手帕，揩了揩发根上沁出来的汗珠。

===Original===
“And so they marched away,” one of the others remarked dryly. “In which direction?”

===Chinese===
「他们就那么走掉了，」桌旁另一人干巴巴地说，「朝哪个方向？」

===Original===
“Straight on,” the soldier replied laconically.

===Chinese===
「一直往前，」那士兵惜字如金地答道。

===Original===
“On the way to Nyons, I suppose, and Walreas?”

===Chinese===
「我猜，是奔尼翁斯（Nyons）和瓦尔雷亚（Walreas）去的吧？」

===Original===
“I suppose so. I don’t know the neighbourhood.”

===Chinese===
「我猜是吧。这一带我不认得。」

===Original===
“You do not seem to have known much, Lieutenant Godet,” Chauvelin put in with a sneer.

===Chinese===
「你像是知道得不多啊，戈岱中尉，」肖维兰冷笑着插嘴。

===Original===
“I come from the other side of the Drac,” Godet retorted. “I could not﻿—”

===Chinese===
「我是德拉克河（Drac）那一边的人，」戈岱顶了回去，「我没法——」

===Original===
But Chauvelin broke in with an oath:

===Chinese===
可肖维兰一声咒骂，打断了他：

===Original===
“Wherever you come from, citizen,” he said sternly, “it was your duty to become acquainted with the country through which you were ordered to march your men.”

===Chinese===
「且不论你是哪里人，公民，」他声色俱厉地说，「奉命带兵穿行的地方，把那一带的地面摸熟，原是你的本分。」

===Original===
“I had no orders to take them through mountain passes,” Godet remarked sullenly. “We came through here a month ago and have kept to the high road. At Sisteron I had my orders to arrest the ci-devant Frontenacs. You, Citizen Chauvelin, must know how conscientiously I did my duty. All the orders you gave me I fulfilled. After Sisteron you ordered me to go to Laragne, and thence to Serres. It was you ordered me to halt at Laragne for the night.”

===Chinese===
「没人命令我带他们翻山口，」戈岱闷闷地说。「一个月前我们打这儿过，走的一直是大路。到了锡斯特龙，我奉命去逮捕旧贵族弗朗特纳克一家。您，公民肖维兰，想必知道我办差办得多么尽心尽意。您下的命令，我一条条都照办了。出了锡斯特龙，您命令我去拉拉涅，再从那儿去塞尔。命令我在拉拉涅歇夜的不是别人，正是您。」

===Original===
“All this is beside the point,” one of the others broke in roughly. “All we can gather from this confused tale is that all traces of the English spies have completely vanished.”

===Chinese===
「这些都不相干，」桌旁另一人粗暴地插进来。「这段乱糟糟的故事，我们听来只听出一桩事：那伙英国奸细的踪迹，已经断得干干净净。」

===Original===
“For the moment,” Chauvelin assented dryly. “It is for Lieutenant Godet to find those traces again.”

===Chinese===
「眼下是断了，」肖维兰干巴巴地附和道，「再把踪迹找回来，就得看戈岱中尉的了。」

===Original===
He spoke now with extreme bitterness, and the glances which he levelled at Godet were both hostile and threatening. It would be curious to try and follow the mental processes which had given rise to this hostility. Godet, after all said and done, had only failed in the same manner as he himself, Chauvelin, had so often done. He had been hoodwinked by a particularly astute and daring adventurer who was an avowed enemy of France: and if being thus hoodwinked was a crime against the State, then the powerful member of the Committee of Public Safety and the humble lieutenant of infantry were fellow-criminals. This, of course, Godet did not know. Not yet: or he would not have been in such dread of this man with the pale eyes and the talon-like hands. The others he did not fear nearly so much. No doubt they too were cruel and vengeful these days. Strike or the blow will fall on you, was the rule of every man’s conduct. Pochart and Danou took their cue from Chauvelin; his was the mastermind, his the more ruthless nature, all they did was to try and show their zeal by saying Amen to every suggestion, every sarcasm, every accusation put forth by their colleague.

===Chinese===
他此刻说起话来苦涩已极，射向戈岱的目光里既怀敌意，又含威吓。这股敌意究竟循着怎样一条心思生出来的，倒叫人好奇，值得细究一番。归根结底，戈岱栽的跟头，同他肖维兰自己不知栽过多少回的跟头，原是同一路数。他是叫一个格外狡黠、格外胆大包天的冒险家给蒙了——那人还是个公然与法兰西为敌的仇人：倘若这样叫人蒙了也算一桩危害国家的罪，那么公安委员会里那位大权在握的委员，同这个不起眼的步兵中尉，便是一对同案罪犯。这一层，戈岱自然不知道。眼下还不知道：不然的话，他也不会这样怕这个长着苍白眼睛、鹰爪般双手的人了。另外那两位，他倒不怕得这么厉害。毫无疑问，这些日子他们也一样心狠手辣、睚眦必报。不先出手，必遭毒手——这是人人行事的信条。波夏尔（Pochart）和达努（Danou）都看肖维兰的眼色行事；主心骨是他，更狠毒的性子也是他的，那两位所做的，无非是对这位同僚提出的每一项建议、每一句讥诮、每一桩指控，都应一声「阿门」，以显自己的热忱。

===Original===
In fact the proceedings by now had developed into a kind of duel between the accused and the principal judge; it was a duel made up of acrimonious accusations on the one hand, and of defence that weakened perceptibly as the accused became more and more confused through ever increasing terror. The other two only put in a word here and there. They wished to know how the adventure had finally come to an end.

===Chinese===
事实上，这场审问到了这会儿，已经演成一场被告同主审官之间的决斗：一方是恶狠狠的指控，一方是辩白——被告越叫与日俱增的恐怖搅得神魂颠倒，这辩白便越是眼见得软弱下来。另外两位委员只在这里那里插上一言半语。他们只想知道，这场冒险到头来是怎么收的场。

===Original===
“In a long, weary tramp to Orange,” Godet replied; “weary beyond what words can describe, footsore, hungry and thirsty we tramped.”

===Chinese===
「靠一趟又长又苦的跋涉到了奥朗日，」戈岱答道，「那份苦，言语都形容不出；脚板磨烂，肚里饥，喉咙干，我们一路捱了过来。」

===Original===
They had to cover three leagues. How they lived through it, they none of them knew. At one or two villages which they encountered, they obtained a little food, and some drink. For the space of a league and a half, he, Godet, and two others got a lift in a farmer’s wagon. On the way they asked news of the English spies. They had been seen marching merrily; but soon all traces of them had vanished.

===Chinese===
他们得走完三里格的路。这一程怎么活下来的，他们谁也说不清。路上碰见的一两个村子里，他们讨到一点吃的，一点喝的。有一里格半的路程，他戈岱同另外两个人搭上了一辆农夫的大车。一路上他们打听那伙英国奸细的消息。有人见过他们欢欢喜喜地行军；可不多时，一切踪迹就都断了。

===Original===
“Had I been the traitor you say I am, Citizen Chauvelin,” Godet said in the end, “would I have come into Orange with my tale? I would have tried to run away and to hide. Made my way to Toulons, what? and joined the army there. You would not have found me then; months would have gone by before you heard of my adventure.”

===Chinese===
「我若真是您说的那种叛徒，公民肖维兰，」戈岱末了说，「我还会带着我这段经历进奥朗日来吗？我早就想法子逃跑、藏起来了。干脆投土伦去，怎么样？到那边投军。那时您就找不着我了；等您听到我这桩遭遇，怕已过去好几个月了。」

===Original===
“You underestimate the power which is in my hands, citizen lieutenant,” was Chauvelin’s curt comment. “Only one thing could save you from the consequences of your treachery, and that was to speak the truth and to redeem your crime.”

===Chinese===
「你把攥在我手里的权柄估摸得太低了，公民中尉，」肖维兰的答话简短干脆。「只有一条路能救你逃脱叛国之罪的下场，那就是讲出实情，赎你的罪。」

===Original===
He paused a moment, and then addressing his two colleagues he said with slow deliberation:

===Chinese===
他略停了停，随即转向两位同僚，慢条斯理、字斟句酌地说道：

===Original===
“We all agree, I think, that Citizen Lieutenant Godet has been guilty of gross negligence, which today, when France is threatened by traitors within as well as by her enemies on her frontier, amounts to treason against the State. Silence!” he went on, throwing a stern glance on Godet who had uttered a violent word of protest. “Listen to what hope of indulgence it is in my power to give you. The State against whom you have sinned will grant you the chance of retrieving your crime. We will grant you full powers under the new Law of the Suspect. You shall go into the highways and the byways with full power to seize any man, woman or child, whom you as much as vaguely suspect of complicity in this affair. Do you understand?”

===Chinese===
「我想，我们一致认为：公民中尉戈岱犯下了重大的玩忽之罪；而今，法兰西内受叛徒滋扰，边境之外又有大敌环伺，这罪过就无异于叛国。住口！」他继续说道，朝厉声抗议的戈岱投去严厉的一瞥，「听听吧，我有多大余地给你开一条宽宥的路。被你冒犯的国家，将给你一个将功折罪的机会。我们将依据新颁的嫌疑犯法令，授你全权。你且到通衢大道、僻巷小路上去，大权在握，凡是你哪怕隐隐约约疑心与此案有牵连的男人、女人、孩子，一律可以逮捕。你可明白？」

===Original===
“I think I do,” Godet replied dully.

===Chinese===
「我想我明白，」戈岱木然答道。

===Original===
“The State,” Pochart put in sagely, “would rather have the English spies’ than your head, citizen lieutenant.”

===Chinese===
「国家呀，」波夏尔煞有介事地插言，「宁要英国奸细的脑袋，也不要你的脑袋，公民中尉。」

===Original===
“The State will have Citizen Godet’s head,” Chauvelin rejoined dryly, “or the English spies. The choice rests with Citizen Godet himself.”

===Chinese===
「国家要定公民戈岱的脑袋，」肖维兰干巴巴地应道，「不然，就要英国奸细的。何去何从，全凭公民戈岱自己。」

===Original===
There was a moment’s pause. The eyes of the soldier were fixed upon the pale, determined face of his ruthless judge. He knew that his life hung upon the decision uttered by those thin, bloodless lips. He was in the grip of a white terror; his teeth were clenched and his tongue clove, hard and dry, against the roof of his mouth. He was terrified, and in his wildly beating heart there was an immense hatred for the man who thus terrorized him. He longed to get at him, to grip him by the throat, to scream out insults into that pale, stern, colourless face. He longed to see that same fear of death which was paralysing him, dim the light of those pale eyes. His own impotence made that hatred more intense. It shone out of his eyes, and Chauvelin meeting them caught the glance like that of an enraged cur, ready to spring. Indifferent, he shrugged his shoulders and the ghost of a sneer curled round his thin lips. He was accustomed to hatred and desire for revenge.

===Chinese===
场上静了片刻。那士兵的双眼死死盯住这位冷酷判官那张苍白而决绝的脸。他明白，自己的性命就悬在那两片薄而无血的嘴唇即将吐出的裁断上。他被一种死白的恐怖攫住了；牙关紧咬，舌头又干又硬，死死贴在上膛。他怕到了极处，而在这颗狂跳的心里，对这个如此恐吓他的人，也蓄起了一腔巨大的仇恨。他恨不得扑上前去，掐住他的喉咙，冲那张苍白、冷峻、毫无血色的脸嘶声痛骂。他恨不得看见那正叫他瘫软的死亡恐怖，也把那双苍白眼睛里的光罩暗下去。自己的无能为力，倒把这仇恨烧得更旺。仇恨从他眼里直冒出来；肖维兰迎着那目光接了个正着——那眼色，活像一条被激怒的恶狗，弓身欲扑。他无动于衷，耸了耸肩，一丝若有若无的讥诮在他薄薄的唇边一挑。仇恨也罢，报复的欲望也罢，他早就司空见惯了。

===Original===
