It seemed almost the worst moment of this awful day to see Adèle﻿—Adèle!﻿—standing there, like some sly and furtive rodent, snapping at the hand that had fed and tended it. The lessons taught by all these makers of a revolution which was going to be a millennium for the people, and inaugurate an era of brotherly love, had been well learnt by all those who had nothing to lose and everything to gain by venality, by treachery and the blackest of ingratitude. And Chauvelin himself had been head master in that school, where this wretched little bastard had learned how to hate; she was the personification of that proletariat which he had striven to exalt, of the low, mean mind that never tries to rise, and only strives to drag down others to the level of its own crass ignorance. Adèle was only a product of that levelling process which was going to make of mankind one great family, full of love for one another, of pity for the weak and contempt of the strong, and which had only succeeded in arousing a universal hatred in every breast and envy of everything that was lofty and pure. The levelling process according to its early protagonists﻿—idealists for the most part﻿—was destined to eliminate all tyranny and to protect those who were too weak to protect themselves; but all it had succeeded in doing was to substitute one tyranny for another; it had not levelled the classes, made one man as good as another; what it had done was to hurl down from his self-imposed altitude of nobility or of virtue every man who was unwilling to step down of his own accord. It had set every beggar on horseback who was a beggar by nature, and kept him there by virtue of ruthlessness and of cruelty. None but a fellow-beggar, more ruthless perhaps, and more cruel, could unseat him. Death was the only real leveller, and this glorious revolution had become a fraternity of death. The Republic of France must march to Liberty over corpses, one of its makers had said, and another added sententiously that no traitor failed to return, except the dead. Terror reigned now everywhere, marching hand in hand with its handmaiden the guillotine.

===Chinese===
在这可怕的一天里，这几乎是最难堪的一刻：眼看着阿黛尔——阿黛尔！——站在那里，活像一只阴祟狡猾的鼠辈，反咬那只喂养过它、看护过它的手。这场革命，据称将为人民带来千年盛世，开创一个手足情爱的纪元；它的这班缔造者教下的课业，另一班人学了个透熟——那班人凭着卖身求荣、背信弃义和最黑心的忘恩负义，失无可失，样样有可得。而那所学校的校长，正是他肖维兰本人；就在那所学校里，这个可悲的小杂种学会了怎样去恨。她正是他一手着力抬举的那个无产阶级的化身，也是那种卑贱褊狭心地的化身——这样的心地从不思向上，只一味把旁人往下拖，拖到自己那冥顽愚昧的水平为止。阿黛尔不过是那场均平运动的产物。据称那运动要使人类结成一个大家庭，人人彼此相爱，怜惜弱者，鄙夷强者；而它真正成就的，只是在每个人胸中激起一种无所不在的仇恨，激起对一切高尚纯洁之物的嫉妒。在早期的倡导者——多半是些理想主义者——看来，这场均平运动命定要扫除一切暴政，庇护那些弱得无法自卫的人；可它办成的一切，不过是以一种暴政顶替另一种暴政。它并没有拉平各个阶级，没有做到人人等量齐观；它做到的，是把每一个不肯自行走下高台的人，从他凭高贵或德行自居的高度上，一把掀翻下来。它让每一个生就的乞丐都骑上了马背，又靠狠辣与凶残把他扶稳在马上。除了另一个乞丐——兴许更狠辣、更凶残的乞丐——谁也休想把他掀下马来。死亡才是唯一货真价实的均平者，而这场光荣的革命，已经变成了一个死亡的兄弟会。它的一位缔造者说过，法兰西共和国必须踏着尸体走向自由；另一位则煞有介事地补上一句：叛徒没有不回来的——除非是死人。如今恐怖君临四方，与它的侍婢断头台携手同行。

===Original===
The time was no longer far distant when this titanic battle between all these beggars on horseback would reach its fiercest struggle ere it ended in a gigantic cataclysm, and when the gorge of all these tigers would rise at last in face of the daily hecatombs which had made a graveyard of the fair lands of France. But that time was not yet. Men like Chauvelin had seen visions of Retribution like fiery Fata Morgana pointing to the inevitable hour, but the Godets, the Danous, the Pocharts and the Adèles knew not the signs of the times. They had learnt their lesson and were applying it for their own advancement and above all for their own safety, destroying all that was destructible, taking Earth and Heaven to witness that they whose lives had been nought but misery and hunger would henceforth sweep off the face of the earth all those who had only known ease and comfort, who had practised virtue, and never known despair.

===Chinese===
那一天已经不远了：这班骑在马背上的乞丐之间巨灵般的争斗，将厮杀到最凶残处，才以一场天崩地裂的大灾变收场；到那时，这班猛虎面对把法兰西锦绣河山变成大坟场的每日大屠戮，也终于要掩鼻作呕。可那时辰还没有到。像肖维兰这样的人，早已望见过「报应」的幻象，如同烈焰灼灼的海市蜃楼（Fata Morgana），直指那无可回避的时辰；而戈岱之流、达努之流、波夏尔之流和阿黛尔之流，是读不懂这时代的种种征兆的。他们学好了课业，正拿它谋自己的高升，首先图的是自家的性命，荡毁一切毁得掉的东西，还请天地作证：他们这一生受的尽是苦、挨的尽是饿，从今往后，定要把那些只识安逸舒服、奉行美德、从不识绝望的人，从这地面上扫荡净尽。

===Original===
And Adèle, whose hatred of Fleurette had thriven all these years as in a forcing-house, had learnt her lesson well. Fleurette to her meant tyranny, the tyranny of riches over her poverty, of good food over her empty stomach, of neat kirtle over her rags. Poverty and Hunger had enchained her to Fleurette’s wheel, had forced her to wash dishes, to scrub floors, to sleep on a straw pallet. But now her turn had come. Her very misery had put it in her power to drag Fleurette down to her own level. She had imbibed the principles of this glorious revolution until she felt herself to be one of its prophets. She had spied on Fleurette and denounced her because she had seen at last a way to satisfy her hatred and to lull her envy to rest.

===Chinese===
而阿黛尔——她对弗勒蕾特的恨，这些年来一直像在温室里一般疯长——这一课也学得好。在她心里，弗勒蕾特就意味着暴虐：富足之于她的贫穷，美食之于她的空肚子，整洁的裙子之于她的破衣烂衫，桩桩都是暴虐。贫穷与饥饿把她拴在弗勒蕾特的苦役轮上，逼她洗盘子、刷地板、睡草垫。可如今轮到她了。正是她的苦命，给了她权柄，把弗勒蕾特拖到她那一层去。她把这场光荣革命的原则灌了一肚子，直灌得自命为它的先知之一。她窥伺弗勒蕾特、告发弗勒蕾特，只因她终于看见了一条路子，既泄得了恨，又哄得住嫉妒。

===Original===
She had plenty to say when questioned by Pochart and Danou; proud of the fact that for over two years now she had supplied the Sisteron section of the Committee of Public Safety with information about the district. She had known the ci-devant Frontenacs and it was﻿—she was proud to state﻿—chiefly owing to her that they came to be suspected of treason. They used to turn one of their rooms into a chapel on Sundays and a ci-devant priest, who was not Constitutional, performed there rites and ceremonies with wafer and cup which had long since been decreed treasonable against the State. Adèle had been forced by the ci-devant Frontenac women to be present at these treasonable practices; she had even been made to scrub the floor of that temple of superstition and to remove the dust from the so-called altar. Her patriotic soul had risen in revolt and she had journeyed to Sisteron one day when she was free and placed the matter before the Committee of Public Safety who had commended her for her zeal.

===Chinese===
波夏尔（Pochart）和达努（Danou）问她话时，她有的是可说的；她还颇为自豪：两年多来，她一直向公安委员会锡斯特龙区供给本地区的情报。她早就识得旧贵族（ci-devant）弗朗特纳克一家，而且——她很自豪地声称——这一家子会落下叛国的嫌疑，主要就得归功于她。他们每逢礼拜天就把一间屋子改成小教堂，一位不肯归顺宪政的旧日神父，便在那里用圣体饼和圣爵行那套仪节——这等仪节，久已明令定为叛国之举。旧贵族弗朗特纳克家的女眷们，还强逼阿黛尔到场亲历这些叛国行径；甚至逼她去擦洗那座迷信神庙的地板，掸除那所谓祭坛上的灰尘。她那颗爱国的灵魂奋起反抗了；一天趁得闲，她专程去了锡斯特龙，把这件事呈到公安委员会面前，委员会还嘉奖了她的热忱。

===Original===
“Adèle!” Fleurette exclaimed involuntarily. “How could you? Indeed *le bon Dieu* will punish you for this.”

===Chinese===
「阿黛尔！」弗勒蕾特不由得失声喊道。「你怎么能这样？好天主（*le bon Dieu*）一定会为这个惩罚你的。」

===Original===
At which remark everybody laughed﻿—except Chauvelin, who smothered a groan. Oh! the child! the senseless, foolish, adorable child! She seemed wilfully to run her darling head into the noose. Adèle turned a sneering glance on Fleurette.

===Chinese===
这一句话惹得众人都笑了——只有肖维兰例外，他压下了一声呻吟。啊！这孩子！这糊涂的、痴傻的、招人疼爱的孩子！她简直是存心让自己那可爱的脑袋往绞索里钻。阿黛尔朝弗勒蕾特投来讥诮的一瞥。

===Original===
“I’ll chance a punishment from your *bon Dieu*,” she said flippantly, “for the joy of seeing you punished by the Revolutionary Tribunal.”

===Chinese===
「你的好天主（*bon Dieu*）要罚就罚，我碰这个运气，」她轻佻地说，「图的就是那眼福：看你受革命法庭（Revolutionary Tribunal）的惩办。」

===Original===
And strange to say Chauvelin did not strike her, though she stood quite near him, with only the width of the table between her and his avenging hand. But he did not strike her, even though his muscles ached with the desire to strike her on the mouth. It was pride that held him back. How those men would have laughed to see him lose his self-control with this wench who was only emitting principles that he indirectly had taught her. Retribution! Nemesis at every turn.

===Chinese===
说也奇怪，肖维兰竟没有动手打她，虽然她站得离他很近，她同他那只报复的手之间，只隔着一张桌子的宽度。可他没有打，尽管他浑身筋肉都因想扇她那张嘴而隐隐发胀作痛。拦住他的是骄傲。那几个人要是见他同这么个丫头失了自持，还不知要笑成什么样——她满口吐出来的，无非是他间接教给她的那套原则。报应！复仇女神（Nemesis）如影随形。

===Original===
And now Adèle embarked upon her main story. Her spying on Fleurette. Long, long had she suspected her, with her airs of virtue and bunches of forget-me-nots in front of a statue representing a ci-devant saint. “*Saint Antoine de Padoue, priez pour nous!*” every time she placed a fresh bunch of flowers before that statue. Bah! such superstition made a patriot’s gorge rise with disgust. But Adèle had said nothing. Not for a long time. She knew that citizen Chauvelin﻿—he was known as Armand over at Laragne﻿—was a great patriot and an intimate friend of Citizen Robespierre over in Paris. So Adèle decided to bide her time, and she did. Until that evening when at last the Frontenacs were arrested and the château ransacked. That night Adèle had had her suspicions aroused by Fleurette’s strange airs of mystery, her desire to meet Citizen Colombe alone on a dark night. Fleurette had always been such a *Sainte Nitouche* that Adèle guessed that something serious was in the wind.

===Chinese===
于是阿黛尔开始讲她的正题：她如何窥伺弗勒蕾特。她疑心她，由来已久——瞧她那副贤惠做派，还老在一尊旧日圣徒的像前供一束束的勿忘我。每回在圣像前供上新的一束花，她总要念一句：「*Saint Antoine de Padoue, priez pour nous!*」（帕多瓦的圣安东尼，为我们祈祷！）呸！这等迷信，直叫爱国者恶心反胃。可阿黛尔什么也没说。好长时间都没说。她知道公民肖维兰——在拉拉涅那边，他化名阿尔芒——是位大爱国者，还是巴黎那头罗伯斯庇尔公民的心腹之交。于是阿黛尔打定主意静候时机，她也真等到了。一直等到那个傍晚，弗朗特纳克一家终于被捕，庄园也被抄了个底朝天。就在那天夜里，弗勒蕾特那副神秘兮兮的古怪做派，还有她要在黑夜里单独见公民科隆布的打算，惹起了阿黛尔的疑心。弗勒蕾特向来是那么一位*Sainte Nitouche*（装模作样的小圣女），阿黛尔便料定这里头必有大文章。

===Original===
Like a zealous patriot she had watched, and she had seen Fleurette hand over a casket and a wallet to young Colombe. She had heard the two talk over the question of hiding these things in a shed behind Citizen Colombe’s shop, and finally seen them locked in each other’s arms, which confirmed her in the idea that Fleurette, with all her appearances of virtue, was a woman guilty of moral turpitude.

===Chinese===
她像个热心尽责的爱国者那样暗中盯梢，亲眼看见弗勒蕾特把一只匣子和一只钱夹交到小科隆布手上。她听见两人商议，要把这些东西藏进公民科隆布铺子后头的工具棚；末了还看见他们两个紧紧搂作一处——这就叫她认定：弗勒蕾特空有一副贤淑的外表，骨子里是个道德败坏的女人。

===Original===
And still Chauvelin did not strike her on the mouth. He fell to wondering what crime he had committed that was heinous enough to deserve this punishment of impotence.

===Chinese===
可肖维兰仍旧没有扇她的嘴。他不免自问：自己究竟造下了什么孽，竟恶劣到该受这般束手无策的惩罚。

===Original===
The others listened for the most part in silence. Only occasionally did one or the other break into a chuckle. *Nom de nom*, what an event! Representative Chauvelin! the man of almost arrogant integrity, sent to Orange to spy and report on the workings of the Committee of Public Safety, one of the makers of the Terror, a man whose every glance was a menace, and every word a threat of death! When Adèle had finished speaking, Pochart winked across at Danou. Here was a find that would exalt them both, bring their names to the notice of the great men over in Paris. All sorts of possibilities of reward and advancement loomed largely before them. And Pochart rubbed his large, coarse hands contentedly together and Danou poured himself out a glass of water and drank it down. All these possibilities had made him thirsty.

===Chinese===
其余的人多半默默听着，只偶尔这一个或那一个嗤地笑出声来。见鬼（*Nom de nom*），这可是桩了不得的大事！肖维兰代表！这位持身近乎倨傲的正直之士，奉派到奥朗日来窥察、密报公安委员会的运作，恐怖时期的缔造者之一，一个目光即是威吓、开口即是死亡威胁的人物！阿黛尔说完的时候，波夏尔朝达努那边挤了挤眼。这可是桩天大的发现，够把他们两个都抬举起来，把他们的名字呈到巴黎那班大人物的眼前。种种奖赏与升迁的前景，赫然铺展在他们眼前。波夏尔心满意足地搓着他那双粗大的手；达努给自己斟了一杯水，一饮而尽——这满眼的前景，让他口渴了。

===Original===
Fleurette too was silent. For the first time in her life she had come in contact with human passions of which hitherto she had not even dreamed. Adèle, the little maid of all work, with the coarse hands, the red elbows and narrow ratlike face, who wore Fleurette’s cast-off clothes and worn-out shoes, had suddenly become an ununderstandable and terrifying enigma. Fleurette felt as if she could not utter a sound, that any word of protest which she might raise would choke her. The girl’s words, her bitter accusations, spoken in an even monotone, gave her a feeling as of an icy-cold grip upon her heart. Surrounded from her cradle onwards with love and care this first glimpse of spite and hatred paralysed her. Only when Adèle spoke of M’sieu’ Amédé and of that kiss which had tokened him to Fleurette, that delicious kiss under the almond-trees, only then did a hot blush rise to her cheeks, and tears of shame gather in her eyes.

===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特也沉默着。有生以来头一遭，她撞见了连梦也不曾梦见过的人心欲念。阿黛尔，那个打杂的小女仆，一双手粗粝，两肘发红，一张窄脸活像老鼠，穿的是弗勒蕾特淘汰下来的旧衣裳和穿破的鞋——霎时间竟变成了一道无从索解、骇人的谜。弗勒蕾特觉得自己发不出半点声音，任何一句想要申辩的话，都会把她噎住。那姑娘的话，那些用平稳单调的口吻吐出的刻毒指控，给了她一种冷冰冰攥住心脏的感觉。从摇篮里起就被爱与呵护团团围住，这头一回窥见的怨毒与仇恨，把她整个惊瘫了。只有当阿黛尔说到阿梅代先生，说到那个叫他和她互订终身的吻——杏树底下那个甜津津的吻——只有到那时，一股火辣辣的红晕才涌上她的双颊，羞愧的泪水才在她眼里聚拢。

===Original===
Beyond that she felt like an automaton, while these four creatures who hated her and who hated Bibi were discussing her fate. Bibi was strangely silent and motionless although from time to time the others referred a question or two to him in which case he replied in curt monosyllables. There was much talk of “detention” and of “revolutionary tribunal.” Of course Fleurette did not understand what these meant. Since Bibi appeared so indifferent, she supposed that nothing very serious was going to happen to her.

===Chinese===
除此之外，她只觉得自己像一具自动木偶，听凭这四个恨她、也恨比比的家伙议论她的命运。比比出奇地沉默、一动不动，虽然旁人不时也把一两句问话递到他跟前，那时他也只用简短的单字作答。他们口口声声，说的尽是「拘押」啦、「革命法庭」啦。弗勒蕾特自然不懂这些都是什么意思。既然比比显得这样满不在乎，她便料想不会有什么大不了的事落到自己头上。

===Original===
Presently Adèle and Godet were dismissed. Adèle swept past her with her shawl once more over her head hiding the expression of her face. Her eyes did not meet Fleurette’s as she glided past like a little rat seeking its burrow. Perhaps she was ashamed. Godet was ordered to send two men along﻿—they would be wanted to take the *citoyenne* to the house of detention. Godet gave the salute and followed Adèle out of the room.

===Chinese===
不多时，阿黛尔和戈岱便被打发出去了。阿黛尔从她身边一掠而过，披巾重新裹上头，遮住了脸上的神情。她像一只寻洞的小老鼠般悄悄溜过，眼睛并不与弗勒蕾特相接。兴许她是害臊了吧。戈岱奉命派两个弟兄过来——押这位女公民（*citoyenne*）去拘押所，用得着他们。戈岱行了个军礼，跟着阿黛尔出了屋。

===Original===
Fleurette’s feet were aching. She had been standing quite still for over half an hour, and was longing to sit down. Bibi’s eyes were upon her now, and his long thin hands were fidgeting nervously with a paper-knife. At one time he clutched it so tightly, and half raised it, as if he meant to strike one or the other of his colleagues. Fleurette, tired and a little dizzy, only caught snatches of their conversation. At one time Bibi said very quietly:

===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特的两只脚站得生疼。她一动不动地站了半个多钟头，巴不得能坐下来。这会儿比比的眼睛望着她，那双细长的手正神经质地摆弄着一把裁纸刀。有一阵子，他把刀攥得死紧，还半举起来，仿佛当真要朝他哪一位同僚戳过去。弗勒蕾特又累又有些头晕，他们的话只断断续续听进几句。有一回，比比非常平静地说：

===Original===
“You are very bold, Citizen Danou, to measure your influence against mine.”

===Chinese===
「您胆子不小，公民达努，竟拿您的影响力来同我的较量。」

===Original===
And the man on Bibi’s left retorted very suavely:

===Chinese===
坐在比比左手边的那人温文尔雅地回敬道：

===Original===
“If I have transgressed, citizen representative, I’ll answer for it.”

===Chinese===
「我若是有僭越之处，公民代表，我自会担待。」

===Original===
“You will,” Bibi rejoined, and his words came through his thin, compressed lips, harsh and dry like blows from a wooden mallet against a metal plate. “And with your head, probably.”

===Chinese===
「你担待得起，」比比应道，话从他抿紧的薄唇间迸出来，又硬又干，像木槌敲在金属板上，「八成是拿你的脑袋来担。」

===Original===
“Is that a threat, Citizen Chauvelin?” the other asked with a sneer.

===Chinese===
「这话算是威胁吗，公民肖维兰？」对方讥诮地问。

===Original===
“You may take it so if you wish.”

===Chinese===
「您愿意这么看，悉听尊便。」

===Original===
The man on Chauvelin’s right, Citizen Pochart, had in the meanwhile been writing assiduously on a large piece of paper. Now he pushed the paper in front of Chauvelin and said curtly:

===Chinese===
这工夫，坐在肖维兰右手边的那位——公民波夏尔——一直在一张大纸上奋笔疾书。这时他把纸推到肖维兰面前，简慢地说：

===Original===
“Will you sign this, citizen representative?”

===Chinese===
「您把这个签了吧，公民代表？」

===Original===
“What is it?” Chauvelin asked.

===Chinese===
「这是什么？」肖维兰问。

===Original===
“Order for the provisional arrest of one Fleur Chauvelin, suspect of treasonable connections with the enemies of France, pending her appearance before the Revolutionary Tribunal.”

===Chinese===
「兹令：弗勒尔·肖维兰（Fleur Chauvelin）一名，嫌涉与法兰西敌人勾连叛国，暂予逮捕，候革命法庭传审。」

===Original===
Chauvelin raised the paper and read it through carefully. His hand that held the paper was perfectly steady.

===Chinese===
肖维兰拿起那张纸，从头至尾细细读了一遍。执纸的那只手，稳得没有一丝颤动。

===Original===
“Your signature,” Pochart went on, and held out the quill pen invitingly toward Chauvelin, “as Representative of the National Convention on special mission is necessary on this order.”

===Chinese===
「您的签名，」波夏尔接着说，一面把鹅毛笔殷勤地朝肖维兰递去，「这道命令，非有国民公会特派代表的签名不可。」

===Original===
“You may take that as a threat too, Citizen Chauvelin,” Danou added with a sly wink directed at his colleague Pochart, “for if you do not sign, there’s others that will, and sign one too that will be even more unpleasant for you.”

===Chinese===
「您也不妨把这个当作威胁，公民肖维兰，」达努朝同僚波夏尔狡黠地眨了眨眼，补充道，「因为您若不签，自有别人来签，还要签出一道对您更不痛快的手令来。」

===Original===
Chauvelin took the pen, and the two men, Pochart and Danou, sprawling over the table, had the satisfaction of seeing him sign the order for the arrest of his own child﻿—her death probably. Not the first time either that something of the sort had occurred, that a man put his seal on the death-warrant of his kith or kin. Had not Philippe d’Orléans voted for the death of his cousin the King? Chauvelin signed with a steady hand, his lips tightly pressed one against the other. They should not see, these fiends, what torture he was enduring; they should not see that at this moment he felt just like a brute beast writhing in agony. Not that he had abandoned hope with regard to Fleurette. He felt confident that he could turn the order into a mere scrap of paper presently, and see those two snarling dogs fawning at his feet once more, kicked with the toe of his boot and howling in vain for mercy.

===Chinese===
肖维兰接过笔来。波夏尔和达努两人趴在桌上，称心如意地看着他签下逮捕自己亲骨肉的命令——多半也是她的死刑令。这一类事原也算不得头一遭：一个人在自己骨肉至亲的死刑文书上钤印画押。菲利普·德·奥尔良（Philippe d'Orléans）不也曾投票赞成处死他的堂兄国王吗？肖维兰下笔稳稳地签了，两片嘴唇紧紧压在一处。这些魔鬼休想看见他正受着怎样的酷刑；休想看见他此刻只觉得像一头痛苦翻滚的畜生。这并不是说他已对弗勒蕾特撒手绝望。他深信，转眼之间他就能把这道命令化作一张废纸，再看着那两条狺狺狂吠的狗重新爬到他脚边献媚，被他靴尖踢开，哀嚎着讨饶而不可得。

===Original===
It was only from humiliation that, conscious of his power, he had decided that silence and outward acquiescence were his best policy. He had certain cards up his sleeve which the others wot not of, but he could only play them if he succeeded in lulling them into a sense of security by his obvious indifference. Fortunately his reputation stood him in good stead. He was known by his enemies to be so ruthless and so unscrupulous﻿—such an ardent patriot, declared his friends﻿—that his indifference now where his own daughter was concerned, did not even astonish Pochart and Danou. It was just like Citizen Chauvelin to send his own daughter to the guillotine. And this estimate of his character helped him to play the role that would mean life to Fleurette.

===Chinese===
只是这场奇耻大辱之下，他深知自家权柄在握，才拿定了主意：沉默与表面的顺从，是眼下的上上之策。他袖子里还藏着几张牌，旁人无从知晓；可要打出它们，先得凭这副明摆着的无动于衷，哄得他们心安理得才行。所幸他的名声帮了他的大忙。仇家们都知道他狠辣无情、无所顾忌——朋友们则称道，他是那样一位热忱的爱国者——以致此刻他对亲生女儿的漠然，竟连波夏尔和达努也不觉得惊讶。把亲生女儿送上断头台，这正是公民肖维兰素来的做派。而世人对他性情的这番估量，恰好帮他演好这个角色——那角色，关乎弗勒蕾特的性命。

===Original===
So there he sat for a few minutes, perfectly impassive, his face a mask, his hand perfectly steady, perusing the paper, and then deliberately drawing his pen through one of the words and substituting another.

===Chinese===
于是他又坐了几分钟，全然无动于衷，脸像一副面具，手稳稳当当，把那纸细细审读，然后从容地提笔划去其中一个词，换上了另一个。

===Original===
“We’ll say the house of Caristie,” he said dryly, “the other is already full.”

===Chinese===
「就写卡里斯蒂（Caristie）的宅子吧，」他干巴巴地说，「另一处已经关满了。」

===Original===
Pochart shrugged his shoulders. Why not concede this point? It was so fine to have the citizen representative under one’s thumb. What matter if his daughter was thrust into one prison rather than another?

===Chinese===
波夏尔耸了耸肩。这一点何必不让？能把公民代表捏在拇指底下，真是妙不可言。他的女儿是塞进这座监狱还是那座监狱，又有什么打紧？

===Original===
“Is the guard there?” Danou asked. “We have plenty of business to see to. This one has lasted quite long enough.”

===Chinese===
「卫兵到了吗？」达努问。「我们要办的公事还多着呢。这一桩也拖得够久了。」

===Original===
“There is still that report from Avignon to look through,” Pochart added. “It will need your attention, citizen representative.”

===Chinese===
「还有阿维尼翁（Avignon）来的那份呈报要过目呢，」波夏尔补了一句。「那上面少不了要劳您的神，公民代表。」

===Original===
“I’ll be with you in one moment,” Chauvelin replied calmly.

===Chinese===
「我这就来。」肖维兰平静地答道。

===Original===
He rose and went to the door. Opened it. Yes! there was the guard sent hither by Godet, two men to escort his Fleurette to the house of Caristie the architect, now transformed into a house of detention. Chauvelin did not even wince at sight of them. He closed the door quietly and then approached Fleurette. He took hold of her hand and drew her to the furthest corner of the room, out of earshot.

===Chinese===
他起身走到门边，开了门。不错！戈岱遣来的卫兵已经到了，两个人，来押他的弗勒蕾特去那位建筑师卡里斯蒂的宅第——如今改作了拘押所。见了他们，肖维兰连眉头都没有皱一下。他轻轻掩上门，然后走到弗勒蕾特跟前，拉起她的手，带她到屋里最远的角落里，避开了众人耳目。

===Original===
“You are not frightened, little one?” he whispered to her.

===Chinese===
「你不害怕吧，小家伙？」他悄声问她。

===Original===
“No, Bibi chéri,” she replied simply. “If you tell me not to be.”

===Chinese===
「不害怕，亲爱的比比，」她简单地答道，「只要您叫我别怕。」

===Original===
“There is nothing to be frightened at, Fleurette. These brutes wish you ill; but﻿—”

===Chinese===
「没有什么可怕的，弗勒蕾特。这班畜生想害你；可是——」

===Original===
“Why should they?”

===Chinese===
「他们为什么要害我？」

===Original===
“But I can protect you.”

===Chinese===
「可我能护住你。」

===Original===
“I know you can, chéri Bibi.”

===Chinese===
「我知道您护得住，亲爱的比比。」

===Original===
“And you won’t see that wretch Adèle again.”

===Chinese===
「你也再见不着阿黛尔那个贱丫头了。」

===Original===
“I wonder why she hates me! I thought we were friends.”

===Chinese===
「真不明白她为什么恨我！我还当咱们是朋友呢。」

===Original===
“There are no friends these days, little one,” he said almost involuntarily. “Only enemies or the indifferent﻿—They are the least dangerous.”

===Chinese===
「如今是没有朋友了，小家伙，」他几乎是脱口而出。「只有仇敌，或者漠不关心的人——数他们最不危险。」

===Original===
“There are those whom we love.”

===Chinese===
「还有我们心爱的人。」

===Original===
“You are thinking of Amédé?”

===Chinese===
「你说的是阿梅代吧？」

===Original===
“And of you, chéri Bibi.”

===Chinese===
「也说您，亲爱的比比。」

===Original===
“You believed me, didn’t you, little one, when I told you that young Colombe is safely out of harm’s way?”

===Chinese===
「我告诉你小科隆布已经安安稳稳脱了险境，你信了我的话，是不是，小家伙？」

===Original===
“Yes,” she said, “I believed you.”

===Chinese===
「嗯，」她说，「我信了您。」

===Original===
“Then you will hold your tongue about﻿—about what you know?”

===Chinese===
「那么，关于——关于你知道的那些事，你可要守口如瓶？」

===Original===
“I promise you, chéri Bibi. But I won’t allow Amédé to suffer for what I did,” she added with that determined little air of hers, which Chauvelin had learned to dread.

===Chinese===
「我答应您，亲爱的比比。可我不能让阿梅代替我做的事受罪。」她添上一句，带着她那股倔强的小神气——这股神气，肖维兰早已领教得心里发怵。

===Original===
“He won’t. He can’t,” he declared, whilst an exclamation of impatience at her obstinacy almost escaped him. “Have I not told you﻿—”

===Chinese===
「他不会的。他不可能。」他断然道，一声对她执拗的不耐烦呵斥，险些脱口而出。「我不是告诉过你——」

===Original===
“We are waiting, Citizen Chauvelin,” Danou’s unctuous voice broke in at this point. “As you are near the door, perhaps you will call the guard.”

===Chinese===
「我们候着呢，公民肖维兰。」达努那油润甜腻的嗓音这时插了进来。「您正好在门边，劳驾把卫兵唤进来吧。」

===Original===
He did. And stood silently by, while Fleurette was ordered to follow the men. She obeyed, after a last, smiling glance at Bibi. No! she was not frightened; she felt sure that he could protect her, and so long as M’sieu’ Amédé was safe﻿—

===Chinese===
他唤了。随后默默立在一旁，听着他们吩咐弗勒蕾特跟上那两个人去。她照办了，临行还含笑朝比比望了最后一眼。不！她不害怕；她笃定他护得住她，况且，只要阿梅代先生平安——

===Original===
The last words she said before she finally passed through the door were:

===Chinese===
她终于跨出门去之前，说的最后几句话是：

===Original===
“Poor old Louise! You’ll tell her, won’t you, Bibi, not to fret for me? and tell her to send me my crochet work if she can. I shall have plenty of time on my hands to get on with it.”

===Chinese===
「可怜的老路易丝！您会告诉她的，对不对，比比，叫她别为我发愁？还要告诉她，要是办得到，把我的钩针活计捎给我。我手上多的是闲工夫，正好接着往下做呢。」

## XXII / 第二十二章

===Original===
At four o’clock that afternoon the President of the Revolutionary Tribunal sitting at Orange received a summons to accompany Citizen Chauvelin, Representative of the National Convention on special mission, to Paris, there to present his last reports of the cases tried by him since the beginning of the year.

===Chinese===
当天下午四点钟，设在奥朗日的革命法庭（Revolutionary Tribunal）庭长接到一道传召，命他陪同国民公会特派代表公民肖维兰前往巴黎，到那边就其年初以来所审的各案呈递最后的报告。

===Original===
Public Prosecutor Isnard received the same summons; he hastened all in a flurry to the Hôtel de Ville to find Citizen Chauvelin.

===Chinese===
公诉人伊斯纳尔（Isnard）也接到了同样的传召；他心慌意乱，急忙赶到市政厅（Hôtel de Ville）去找公民肖维兰。

===Original===
“What does it all mean, citizen?” he asked.

===Chinese===
「这到底是怎么回事，公民？」他问。

===Original===
Chauvelin shrugged his shoulders.

===Chinese===
肖维兰耸了耸肩。

===Original===
“I know not,” he replied. “The summons came by courier an hour ago. I have my calèche here. We could start at daybreak tomorrow and be in Valence before dark. The next day should see us in Lyons, and the middle of next week in Paris.”

===Chinese===
「我不知道。」他答道。「传召是一小时前由信使送到的。我的篷车就在这里。咱们可以明天破晓动身，天黑之前赶到瓦朗斯（Valence）。第二天就该到里昂，下周过了一半，人就在巴黎了。」

===Original===
“Can you not conjecture﻿—?”

===Chinese===
「您就猜不出——？」

===Original===
Once more Chauvelin shrugged.

===Chinese===
肖维兰又耸了耸肩。

===Original===
“One never knows,” he said. “There must have come some denunciation. You and the President have your enemies, no doubt, as everyone else.”

===Chinese===
「谁也说不准。」他说。「准是有什么告发递上去了。您和庭长自也有你们的仇家，跟旁人一个样。」

===Original===
Public Prosecutor Isnard’s flabby cheeks were the colour of lead.

===Chinese===
公诉人伊斯纳尔两颊的松肉变成了铅灰色。

===Original===
“I have always done my duty,” he stammered.

===Chinese===
「我一向恪尽职守。」他结结巴巴地说。

===Original===
“No doubt, no doubt,” Chauvelin responded lightly. “You’ll be able to justify yourself, I feel sure, citizen. But you know what these summons are. Impossible to argue﻿—or to disobey.”

===Chinese===
「那是自然，那是自然。」肖维兰轻飘飘地应道。「您准能把话说清、为自己辩白的，公民，这我深信。可这种传召是个什么东西，您也知道。没法子争——也没法子抗。」

===Original===
“Yes, I know that. But the business here﻿—”

===Chinese===
「是，这我知道。可这儿的一摊子事务——」

===Original===
“What of it?”

===Chinese===
「那又怎么样？」

===Original===
“Our prisons are full. A batch of twenty at least should be tried every day. I have forty or fifty indictments ready now and we can keep the guillotine busy for at least a week. All that business will be at a standstill.”

===Chinese===
「咱们的牢房都满了。每天至少也该审掉一批二十个的。我手头眼下就备好了四五十份起诉书，够断头台足足忙上一个礼拜。这摊子事务全都要停顿下来了。」

===Original===
“You will have to work twice as hard on your return, citizen,” Chauvelin retorted dryly.

===Chinese===
「那您回来以后，只好加倍卖力气干活了，公民。」肖维兰干巴巴地回了一句。

===Original===
The arrival of the President of the Tribunal put a temporary stop to the colloquy. He too was flurried and not a little scared. He knew about these summonses that would come from time to time from Paris without any warning. They meant reprimands of a certainty. Perhaps worse. One never knows with leaders of the Government over there. One moment they would shout: “Strike! Strike!” at the top of their voices, “let not the guillotine be idle!” They would frame laws to expedite the extermination of all the traitors and suspected traitors. The next, they would draw back, accuse you of over-zeal, over-cruelty, what not? See how Carrier had suffered! He had been sent to Nantes to purge the city of aristos and bourgeois and *calotins*; he had done his best; invented a new way of disposing of ninety priests all at once by the mere unmooring of a flat-bottomed craft, laden with those traitors, and on a given signal opening all the hatches and sinking the whole craft with her cargo.

===Chinese===
法庭庭长的到来，暂时止住了这场交谈。他也是一副慌里慌张的模样，还着实有几分胆寒。这种传召他是有数的——时不时地，巴黎方面会毫无预兆地来上一道。那必定意味着训斥。兴许更糟。那边的政府首领们，谁摸得透呢。前一刻，他们还扯足了嗓子高喊：「杀！杀！」「别让断头台闲着！」还要定出法律，加速灭绝所有的叛国分子和嫌疑叛国分子。后一刻，他们又缩了回去，倒过来怪你过分热心、过分残酷，不一而足。瞧瞧卡里埃（Carrier）落了个什么下场！他被派到南特（Nantes）去清洗城里的贵族佬、资产阶级和虔诚信徒（*calotins*）；他尽了全力；还发明了一种新法子，把九十名教士一总儿处置掉——只消将一条载满那些叛国分子的平底船解缆开走，待约定信号一发，把所有舱盖一齐打开，整条船便连同它那批货载沉入水底。

===Original===
Well! Carrier had done that. He had effectually purged Nantes of traitors. Nevertheless he was summoned to Paris, and his head rolled into the basket on the Place de la République, just as if he had been an aristo. Look at Danton, and at﻿—but why recall it all? Anyhow, what a week of desperate anxiety this would be until Paris was reached. President Legrange had thoughts of flight, of taking refuge in the mountains as others had done. But Public Prosecutor Isnard dissuaded him. What was the good of running away? One always got caught, and then it would of a certainty be the guillotine. Chauvelin too was for immediate obedience.

===Chinese===
嘿！这一切卡里埃都干了。他把南特的叛国分子清除得干干净净。尽管如此，他还是被传到了巴黎，脑袋滚进共和国广场（Place de la République）上的篮子里，倒好像他自己就是个贵族佬。再看看丹东，再看看——可是，何必一一回想呢？横竖，在抵达巴黎之前，这一个礼拜真要焦灼煞人了。勒格朗热（Legrange）庭长动过逃走的念头，想学旁人的样子躲进山里去避一避。可公诉人伊斯纳尔劝住了他。逃有什么用？人总会被逮住，到那时板上钉钉就是断头台。肖维兰也主张即刻奉召。

===Original===
“I too am summoned,” he said. “We are all in the same boat. As for the business here, it will have to wait until our return.”

===Chinese===
「我也接到了传召。」他说。「咱们全在一条船上。至于这儿的事务，只好等咱们回来再办。」

===Original===
Public Prosecutor Isnard could not suppress a taunt.

===Chinese===
公诉人伊斯纳尔忍不住要讥刺一句。

===Original===
“There’s your daughter, Citizen Chauvelin,” he said. “We were going to make quick work of her. I had her indictment all ready. In fact the chief witness﻿—a wench who looks like an anaemic rat﻿—was in my study when your summons came.”

===Chinese===
「还有您那位千金呢，公民肖维兰。」他说。「我们本打算快快把她了结掉的。她的起诉书我都备齐了。说实在的，主要证人——一个模样活像贫血老鼠的臭丫头——您的传召送到那会儿，正待在我的书房里呢。」

===Original===
“I know, I know,” Chauvelin said with perfect indifference. “Well! all that can wait till our return.”

===Chinese===
「我知道，我知道。」肖维兰说，全然一副无动于衷的腔调。「嘿！那一切都等咱们回来再说吧。」

===Original===
After which he added lightly: “At daybreak, citizens, my calèche will be ready outside the Chat Noir. I await you then and advise you to eat a good breakfast. Our first stop will be Montélimar, where we can get relays. In the meanwhile I bid you adieu. I still have much work to see to before the close of day.”

===Chinese===
说罢他又轻飘飘添了一句：「明日破晓，二位公民，我的篷车会在黑猫客栈（Chat Noir）门外备好。我到时候等着你们，并劝你们吃一顿结实的早饭。头一程咱们停在蒙泰利马尔（Montélimar），到那儿可以换马。这会儿我就告辞了。天黑之前，我还有好些事务要料理。」

===Original===
For the first time this day Chauvelin heaved a genuine sigh of satisfaction when the two men had departed. His first manoeuvre had succeeded admirably. With the President of the Tribunal and the Public Prosecutor out of the way, the business of the State would be at a standstill in Orange and he would have at least three weeks of freedom before him in which to act. He had planned this summons, and intended to accompany the two men as far as Lyons. There he would find some pretext for sending them on their way without him, whilst he returned in secret to Orange. That was his plan, a risky one at best; but in less than three weeks he would either have found a way of getting Fleurette out of the clutches of these fiends, or he and she would both be dead. Strangely enough at this moment he fell to wondering what his arch-enemy, the Scarlet Pimpernel, would do under the circumstances and he longed for the possession of that same imaginative brain, that marvellous resource and unbounded pluck which had foiled him, Chauvelin, at every turn.

===Chinese===
两人走后，肖维兰这一天里头一回由衷舒了一口满意的气。他的第一步计谋成功得漂漂亮亮。庭长和公诉人一被打发开，奥朗日这边的国务就要陷于停顿，他面前便有至少三个星期的自由可供行事。这道传召是他一手谋划的，他打算把这两个人一路陪到里昂，到了那儿再寻个借口，打发他们自去上路，自己则悄悄折回奥朗日。这便是他的计划，往好里说也是一步险棋；可不出三个星期，他要么想出法子把弗勒蕾特从这帮恶魔的爪子底下救出来，要么他和她就双双没命。说来也怪，就在这当口，他忽然琢磨起他的宿敌猩红繁笺花处在这般境地会怎么做，并且渴望自己也能拥有那同一副富于想象的头脑、那份神妙的机变和无边的胆魄——正是凭着这些，那人才让他肖维兰回回受挫。

===Original===
The Scarlet Pimpernel! If that bold adventurer were to know that his bitterest foe was now probing the lowest depths of sorrow, that this cruel Nemesis had overtaken him at last, how he would exult, how jeer at his enemy. And of the many pinpricks which Chauvelin had had to endure today, he felt that none could hurt him so deeply as the thought that the Scarlet Pimpernel might hear of his trouble and hold jubilee over his soul agony.

===Chinese===
猩红繁笺花！那位大胆的冒险家，倘若知道他最切齿的仇人此刻正探入悲哀的最深处，知道这残忍的复仇女神终于赶上了他，他该多么欢欣，该怎样讥笑他的仇敌啊。而今天肖维兰不得不忍受的许许多多针扎似的刺痛之中，他觉得没有哪一下能像这个念头那样伤他入骨——猩红繁笺花或许会听闻他的祸事，为他的灵魂苦刑大大欢庆一场。

## XXIII / 第二十三章

===Original===
That first night the party slept at Valence in the Maison des Têtes, the quaint old house with its unique façade which stands to this day in the Grand’ Rue, and which in that year of grace 1794 had been requisitioned by the Drôme section of the Committee of Public Safety for its offices. A concierge with wife and family were in charge of the house and there were two or three additional rooms in it which were often placed at the disposal of any official personage who happened to be passing through Valence. Chauvelin had often stayed there on his way through to Paris and was a familiar figure to the concierge and his family; there was no difficulty whatever in finding accommodation for himself and his two friends in the Maison des Têtes for the night. Calèche and horses, together with driver and postilion, were put up in the stables at the back of the house.
===Chinese===
头一夜，一行人在瓦朗斯过夜，宿在人头馆（Maison des Têtes）——那座古雅的老宅，门面别具一格，至今仍屹立在大街（Grand’ Rue）上；而在蒙主恩宠的 1794 年，它已被公安委员会德龙区（Drôme）征作办公之所。宅子由一位门房带着妻子儿女看管，里头另有两三间空房，常备着供恰巧路过瓦朗斯的官员贵人随时取用。肖维兰在取道此地去巴黎的途中常在这儿落脚，是门房一家人眼熟面惯的人物；要给他自己和两位同伴在人头馆安顿一夜，自然毫无难处。篷车和马匹，连同车夫与驿童，一并安置在宅后的马厩里。

===Original===
Night had overtaken the party when some five kilometres outside Valence, and this last part of the way had to be done at walking pace. Thus it was nearly ten o’clock before the calèche drew up in the Grand’ Rue outside the Maison des Têtes, and the concierge, hurrying to greet the unexpected and important guest, had regretfully to inform him that neither the President nor any of the officials were here to welcome him as they had already gone to their respective homes. But the rooms were there, quite ready, at the disposal of Citizen Chauvelin and his friends, and supper would be got immediately for them. The three travellers stepping out of the calèche were more than thankful to find shelter and food at this hour. Already at sunset the sky had been threatening; great banks of cloud came rolling up from the southwest, driven by tearing gusts of wind; by nighttime a few heavy drops were falling, presaging the coming storm. No sooner were the travellers installed in the dining-hall in front of an excellent supper, than the storm broke in all its fury. It was accompanied by torrential rain and a tearing wind. Such wild weather during the month of May was almost unparalleled in the valley of the Rhône, so the concierge hastened to explain to the two strangers who accompanied Chauvelin. The night was very dark too, the very weather in fact for footpads and malefactors who, alas! infested the countryside more than ever now.
===Chinese===
一行人离瓦朗斯还有五公里上下，夜幕便追了上来，这最后一程只好让马缓步而行。因此篷车停到大街上人头馆门前时，已将近十点；门房急急赶来迎候这位不速而至的贵客，却只得遗憾地禀告他，本地的主席也好、诸位官员也好，都不在这儿迎驾——他们早已各自回家了。好在房间是现成的，收拾得妥妥帖帖，听凭肖维兰公民和他的同伴取用，晚饭也立刻便可置办。三位旅客跨下篷车，在这个时辰能有栖身之所和一顿饭吃，真是说不尽的感激。日落时分天色便已经不善；大片大片的乌云从西南方滚滚压来，被狂风一阵紧似一阵地驱赶撕扯着；到入夜时分，几颗沉甸甸的雨点已然落下，预示着暴雨将至。旅客们刚在饭厅里坐定，面对一顿丰盛的晚饭，风暴便挟着全部的狂怒发作起来。它裹挟着倾盆的暴雨和撕扯的狂风。五月里发这样的大脾气，在罗讷河谷（Rhône）几乎闻所未闻，门房忙不迭地向陪伴肖维兰的两位生客如此解释。夜色又极黑，这正是拦路贼和作恶之徒出没的天气——唉！如今这班人在乡间为祸，比以往任何时候都更猖獗。

===Original===
“What would you?” the man added with a shrug, “so many are starving these days; they get their existence as best they can. Honesty is no longer the best policy.”
===Chinese===
「那有什么法子？」那人耸耸肩，又添了一句，「如今这么多人在挨饿；他们只好各显神通，挣一条活路。诚实如今再也不是上策喽。」

===Original===
And then he caught Citizen Chauvelin’s eye, and nervously clearing his throat, began to talk of something else. It was not prudent to grumble at anything, or to make any remark that might be constructed into criticism of the present tyrants of France.
===Chinese===
话说到这儿，他撞上了肖维兰公民的眼光，便不安地清了清嗓子，扯起别的闲话来。不论对什么事发牢骚都不是稳妥的，何况说出什么话来，若被引申成对当今法兰西那班暴君的指摘，那更要命。

===Original===
Supper drew to an end, mostly in silence. Chauvelin was never of a loquacious turn of mind, and neither of the other two were in a mood to talk. After a curt good night the latter retired to the room which had been assigned to them. Chauvelin before doing the same gave orders to his driver and postilion to have the calèche at the door by seven o’clock on the following morning. Then he too went to bed, there to toss ceaselessly through the endless hours of wakefulness, his mind tortured with thoughts of his darling Fleurette, wondering how she would bear this first night in prison, the propinquity, the want of privacy, the lewd talk perhaps, or coarse jests of some of her roommates. It was only in the early dawn that, wearied at last in body and mind, he was able to close his eyes and snatch an hour or two’s sleep.
===Chinese===
晚饭将尽，席间大半是沉默。肖维兰从来不是个饶舌的人，另外那两位也没有谈兴。两人道过一句简慢的晚安，便回分派给他们的屋子里去了。肖维兰在上楼之前，先吩咐车夫和驿童，第二天早上七点把篷车备到门口。然后他也上了床，却在无眠的漫漫长夜里翻来覆去不得安生；心里备受煎熬，想的尽是他心爱的弗勒蕾特，不知道狱中这头一夜她怎么挨——那样人挤人的贴近，那样没有一丝独处的余地，说不定还有同屋女犯的淫词秽语、粗鄙玩笑。直到晨光初露，他身心俱疲，才算阖上眼，抓紧睡了一两个钟头。

===Original===
When the concierge brought him a steaming mug of wine in the early morning, his first inquiry was after the calèche. Was it being got ready?
===Chinese===
清晨，门房端来一大杯热气腾腾的酒，他开口第一句问的就是篷车。可曾备好？

===Original===
Yes! the concierge had seen the driver and postilion at work this hour past. Everything would doubtless be ready for a start by seven o’clock. It was now half-past six. Chauvelin drank the hot wine eagerly; his sleepless night and all his anxiety had produced a racking headache and a state of mental inertia difficult to combat. Slightly refreshed by the drink, he proceeded to dress. While he did so he heard a great clatter of horses’ hoofs striking the cobblestones, a good deal of shouting and rattling of wheels. His windows gave on the Grand’ Rue, and looking out he expected presently to see the calèche being driven round from the stable-yard at the back. But nothing came. He felt nervy and impatient, hoping that nothing would go wrong. Angered too with himself for feeling so flat on this very morning when he would need all his brainpower to carry his scheme successfully to the end.
===Chinese===
备好了！门房这一个钟头都瞧见车夫和驿童在忙活。到七点钟，一切想必齐备，随时可以动身。此时是六点半。肖维兰急切地喝下热酒；一夜无眠加满腹忧煎，弄得他头痛欲裂，又添了一种难以克服的心智怠滞。酒下肚，精神略振，他便动手穿衣。穿衣的当口，他听见马蹄叩着石子路一片脆响，还有许多喊叫之声和车轮辘辘。他的窗子正临着大街，探头张望，只道转眼便能望见篷车从宅后的马厩院子里转出来。可是毫无动静。他心浮气躁，只盼千万别出岔子。他又恼恨自己：偏偏在今天早上这样委顿——正是今天，他要使出全部心力，把自己的计策顺顺当当推到头。

===Original===
He intended journeying with the two men as far as Lyons, and there to invent a pretext for separating from them, sending them on to Paris by the stagecoach, and then returning quietly and secretly to Orange alone. Already he was fully dressed and ready to go downstairs. He heard the clock in the tower of St. Apollinaire striking seven. A minute or two later the concierge, wide-eyed and babbling incoherently, came bursting into the room.
===Chinese===
他打算同那两人同行到里昂，到那里再编个由头同他们分手，打发他们乘公共马车上巴黎去，然后自己悄悄地、隐秘地独自返回奥朗日。这时他已穿戴齐整，正要下楼，忽听得圣阿波利奈尔（St. Apollinaire）的钟楼敲了七下。一两分钟后，门房闯进屋来，瞪圆了眼睛，语无伦次地叽里咕噜。

===Original===
“Citizen! Citizen! *Nom de nom, quel malheur!*” These ejaculations were followed by a string of lamentations, and a confused narrative of some untoward event out of which the only intelligible words that struck clearly on Chauvelin’s ears were: “Calèche,” and “cursed malefactors!” His questions remained unanswered; the man continuing to lament and to curse alternately.
===Chinese===
「公民！公民！见鬼，天大的祸事（*Nom de nom, quel malheur!*）！」这几声喊叫之后是一连串哀叹，外加一段颠三倒四的祸事讲述；其中清清楚楚敲进肖维兰耳朵、听得真切的字眼，只有「篷车」和「该死的恶棍」！他连声发问，全无回音；那人只顾一会儿哀叹，一会儿咒骂。

===Original===
Finally bereft of all patience, Chauvelin seized him by the shoulder and shook him vigorously.
===Chinese===
肖维兰终于忍无可忍，一把揪住他的肩膀，狠狠摇晃。

===Original===
“If you don’t speak clearly, man,” he said roughly, “I’ll lay my stick across your shoulders.”
===Chinese===
「你要再说不明白，家伙，」他粗暴地说，「我这手杖就抽上你的肩膀了。」

===Original===
The man fell on his knees and swore it was not his fault.
===Chinese===
那人扑通跪倒，赌咒发誓说这不是他的错。

===Original===
“I could not be in two places at once, citizen,” he lamented. “I was looking after your two friends and my wife﻿—”
===Chinese===
「我总不能一分两半，同时待在两处呀，公民。」他哭诉道，「我那时正照应您那两位同伴，还有我的老婆——」

===Original===
Chauvelin raised his stick. “What is it that was not your fault?” he shouted at the top of his voice.
===Chinese===
肖维兰举起了手杖。「不是你的错，到底是什么事？」他扯开嗓子吼道。

===Original===
“That your calèche has been stolen, citizen!”
===Chinese===
「您的篷车叫人偷了，公民！」

===Original===
“What?”
===Chinese===
「什么？」

===Original===
“It is those cursed brigands! They have infested the town these past﻿—”
===Chinese===
「就是那伙该死的强盗！这些日子他们闹得满城都是——」

===Original===
The words died in his throat in a loud cry of pain. Chauvelin had brought his stick crashing upon his back.
===Chinese===
话音在他喉咙里断成一声响亮的痛叫。肖维兰的手杖已重重抽在他背上。

===Original===
“It was not my fault, citizen,” he reiterated protesting. “I could not be in two places at once﻿—”
===Chinese===
「不是我的错，公民。」他翻来覆去地分辩，「我总不能一分两半，同时待在两处——」

===Original===
But Chauvelin no longer stayed to listen. Picking up his hat and coat, he hastened downstairs, to be met in the corridor by the concierge’s wife and two sons all incoherent and lamenting. The whole house by now was astir. Public Prosecutor Isnard came clattering down the stairs followed by President Legrange, both in more or less hastily completed toilette. And thus the whole party with Chauvelin *en tête* proceeded at full speed to the stable yard, where the yawning coach-house and empty stalls told their mute tale. Of calèche, horses, driver or postilion not a sign. The stable man, an old fellow, and his aid, a very young lad, were busy at the moment telling the amazing story to a small crowd of gaffers and market-women who had pushed their way into the yard from the back and were listening, open-mouthed, to a tale of turpitude and effrontery, unparalleled in the annals of Valence.
===Chinese===
可肖维兰再也无心听他分辩。他抓起帽子和外衣，急步下楼，却在走廊里迎头撞上门房的老婆和两个儿子，一个个语无伦次、哀声叹气。这时全宅都已惊动。公诉人伊斯纳尔（Isnard）蹬蹬蹬奔下楼梯，身后跟着勒格朗热庭长（Legrange），两人的衣装都是七手八脚胡乱穿戴齐全的。于是这一行人便由肖维兰走在队首（*en tête*），全速直奔马厩院子——车棚大张着口，马栏空空如也，各自默默诉说着一切。篷车、马匹、车夫或驿童，踪影全无。马夫老头儿和他的帮手——一个毛头小子——这会儿正忙着向一小群看热闹的老汉和赶集的娘儿们讲这桩奇事；那班人是打后头挤进院子来的，正张着嘴，听这桩卑劣无耻、胆大包天的故事——这在瓦朗斯的史册上是前所未有的。

===Original===
At sight of Chauvelin and his tricolour sash, the crowd of gaffers and women respectfully made way for him, and he, seizing the old stableman by the shoulder, commanded him to tell him clearly and briefly just what had happened. Thus it was that at last he was put in possession of the facts that touched him so nearly. It seems that his own driver and postilion, up betimes, had got the calèche and horses quite ready and standing in the middle of the yard. They had in fact just put the horses to, and the postilion and driver were standing by the calèche door drinking a last mug of wine, when from the narrow lane which connects the yard with the rue Latour at the back, a band composed of four ruffians came rushing in. Before he, the stableman, could as much as wink an eyelid, three of these ruffians had seized the driver and postilion round their middle and thrust them into the calèche, followed them in, banged the coach door to, whilst the fourth climbed up to the box with the rapidity of a monkey, gathered up the reins and drove away.
===Chinese===
那群看热闹的老汉和娘儿们一见肖维兰和他腰间的三色绶带，都恭恭敬敬给他让路；他一把揪住老马夫的肩膀，喝令他把事情原委讲个明白、讲个简短。他这才总算弄清了这桩切身利害的事实。原来他自家的车夫和驿童一早起身，早已把篷车和马匹拾掇停当，停在院子正中。他们恰恰刚套好马，驿童和车夫正站在车门边喝临行前的最后一杯酒，冷不防从后头连接院子与拉图尔街（rue Latour）的那条窄巷里，冲进来四个恶棍。不等他马夫眨一眨眼皮，其中三个已拦腰抱住车夫和驿童，把他们塞进篷车，跟着钻了进去，砰地摔上车门；第四个则猴子般利索地蹿上车夫座，收拢缰绳，一溜烟赶跑了。

===Original===
In the meanwhile the lad who had been at work in the stables and heard the clatter came running out. Stableman and lad then ran to the lane and out into the rue Latour, only to see the calèche rattling away at breakneck speed. They shouted and strained their lungs to attract the notice of passersby, and they did attract their attention, but before they could explain what had happened, the calèche was well out of sight. The lad ran as fast as he could to the nearest *poste de gendarmerie*, but before the gendarmes could get to horse, no doubt those ruffians would have got well away with their booty.
===Chinese===
这当口，在马厩里干活、听见了响动的那个小子也奔了出来。马夫和小子随即奔到窄巷，一路追出拉图尔街，却只望见篷车正以不要命的速度辚辚远去。他们高声喊叫，喊得肺都要炸了，想引过路人注意；注意也确实引来了，可还没等他们讲明出了什么事，篷车早已没了踪影。小子撒腿飞奔，跑向最近的宪兵队（*poste de gendarmerie*），可是不等宪兵们翻身上马，那伙恶棍无疑早已带着赃物逃得远远的了。

===Original===
That was in substance the story to which Chauvelin had to listen, and through which he was forced to keep his temper in check. As soon as the stableman had finished speaking, the lad had put in his own comments, whilst the gaffers and gossips started arguing, talking, conjecturing, giving advice, suggesting, lamenting. Oh! above all lamenting! That the high roads were not safe, everyone knew that to his cost. Masked highway robbers held up coaches, attacked pedestrians, robbed and pillaged the countryside. That the streets of Valence were not safe was, alas! only too true. The gendarmerie was either incompetent or venal, and lucky the man who possessed nothing that could be taken from him. But this outrage today in broad daylight surpassed anything that had been seen or heard before. A calèche and pair, *pardieu*! was not like a purse that could be hidden in one’s waistcoat pocket. And so on, and so on, while Chauvelin, still silent and curbing his impatience, went back into the house, followed by his crestfallen friends and by the staff of the Maison des Têtes still lamenting and protesting their innocence and withal beginning to feel doubtful as to what the consequences might be to themselves of this untoward adventure.
===Chinese===
肖维兰不得不听完的，大意便是这样一个故事，而且还得强压着火气听。马夫话音才落，那小子又插进自己的一番议论，看热闹的老汉和长舌的婆娘们便开始争辩、议论、揣测、出主意、提建议、发哀叹。啊！尤其是发哀叹！大路不平安，这是人人吃过亏才领教的。蒙面的劫路强盗拦车打劫，袭击行人，抢掠乡里。至于瓦朗斯的街面也不平安，唉！那更是千真万确。宪兵队不是无能，便是贪赃，谁身上没什么可抢的，谁就算有福气的人。可今天这桩光天化日之下的暴行，实在盖过了以往一切所见所闻。一驾篷车连两匹马，天晓得（*pardieu*）！可不像一只钱袋，还能揣在马甲口袋里藏起来。诸如此类，说个没完、没完。肖维兰一路沉默，强捺着性子，走回宅子里去；身后跟着他那两位垂头丧气的同伴，还有人头馆的一班仆役，兀自哀叹着、表白着自家清白，心里却也渐渐犯起嘀咕：这桩飞来的横祸，不知会给自己招来什么后果。

===Original===
The stable-lad was then sent back to the *poste de gendarmerie*, with orders from Citizen Representative Chauvelin that the chief officer in charge present himself immediately at the Maison des Têtes. Whilst waiting for this officer, Chauvelin, sitting in the small parlour, had a few moments’ peace in which to coordinate his thoughts. The inertia which had weighed upon his spirits the first thing in the morning had been suddenly dissipated. Already his keen, imaginative brain had seized upon this catastrophe, and planned how to turn it to the furtherance of his scheme. And while his friends, no whit less voluble or more coherent than the concierge or his kind, were loudly lamenting: “What a misfortune, citizens! what bad luck!” and throwing up their arms in utter helplessness, Chauvelin broke in impatiently upon their wailings:
===Chinese===
随后那马厩小子被打发回宪兵队去，带着公民代表肖维兰的命令：主管长官即刻到人头馆来面陈。等候这位长官的当口，肖维兰坐在小客厅里，得了几分钟清净，好把心思理出个头绪。清早压在他心头的那种委顿，霎时间消散无踪。他那敏锐而富于想象的头脑，已经一把抓住这场灾祸，盘算好怎样把它转化为推进自己计策的助力。他的两位同伴呢，论饶舌不比门房之流稍逊，论条理也不见得高明多少，正高声哀叹着：「多大的不幸啊，公民们！多倒霉的运气！」绝望地甩着双手。肖维兰不耐烦地打断了他们的哀号：

===Original===
“We must make the best of it, citizens,” he said, “I shall certainly be held up here a day or two, on this stupid business, but it certainly need not detain you. The stagecoach leaves for Lyons at half-past eight if I mistake not. As soon as my calèche is recovered, which I doubt not it will be in a couple of days, I’ll follow you on. You in the meanwhile can proceed to Paris all the way by the stagecoach. It will be perhaps not quite as comfortable as my calèche, but it will serve.”
===Chinese===
「咱们只能往好里对付，公民们。」他说，「这桩蠢事，肯定要把我耽搁在这儿一两天，可绝犯不着把你们也绊住。要是我没记错，八点半有一班公共马车上里昂去。等我的篷车找回来——不出两天准能找回来，这个我不怀疑——我就随后赶来。你们呢，趁这个功夫可以先乘公共马车，一路径直上巴黎。坐起来或许不如我的篷车舒坦，但也尽够用了。」

===Original===
They demurred a little. The stagecoach would certainly not be as comfortable as Citizen Chauvelin’s luxurious calèche, and perhaps a day or two’s delay would not be very serious.
===Chinese===
他们微微踌躇。公共马车固然比不得肖维兰公民那驾豪华的篷车舒服，何况耽搁上一两天，想必也算不得什么大碍。

===Original===
“It would be fatal,” Chauvelin said emphatically. Orders from Paris such as they had received must be obeyed in the least possible delay, a couple of days idling in Valence, when a stagecoach was available, would certainly be put down to pusillanimity and want of zeal.
===Chinese===
「那是性命攸关的。」肖维兰加重语气说。他们接到的这样一道巴黎来令，必须以最快的速度遵办；明明有公共马车可乘，却在瓦朗斯闲晃两天，必定要被记成怯懦畏缩、热忱有亏。

===Original===
He could be eloquent when he liked, could Citizen Chauvelin, and on this occasion he was determined to gain his point﻿—to send these two packing, posthaste, off to Paris, and leave himself free to return to Orange immediately. As to what would happen presently, when those two arrived in Paris and found that they had been hoaxed, that they had not been sent for, and would have to return biting the dust and chewing the cud of their wrath, as to that in truth, Chauvelin had not given a thought. To save his Fleurette, to get her away out of the country, at the cost of his own life if need be, was all he thought about, and while the business of trying and condemning prisoners was at a standstill through the absence of these two men, there was a hundred to one chance that he could accomplish his purpose.
===Chinese===
他肖维兰公民想能言善辩的时候，原也能言善辩；这一回，他是铁了心要达到目的——把这两位即刻打发上路，快快去巴黎，好让自己脱开身，立刻回奥朗日。至于往后会怎样——等那两位到了巴黎，发觉自己叫人耍了，根本没人传召他们，只得灰头土脸地回来，把一肚子怒气翻来覆去地咀嚼——说实话，这个，肖维兰连想也没想过。救他的弗勒蕾特，把她带出这个国家，必要时赔上自己的性命也在所不惜——他所想的只有这一件；而趁这两个人不在、审讯定罪的公事停滞不前的当口，他有一百比一的胜算，能把自己的目的办成。

===Original===
Therefore he put forth all his powers of persuasion﻿—and they were great. He drew lurid pictures of what happened to those who were thought to be guilty of dilatoriness or want of zeal. So much so that he reduced President Legrange and Public Prosecutor Isnard﻿—at no time very valiant heroes﻿—to a state of abject fear, and half an hour later had the satisfaction of bidding them au revoir, in the yard of the posting-inn, they having found seats in the stagecoach to Lyons.
===Chinese===
于是他使出了全部说服的本事——那本事可不小。他把那些被认定办事拖沓、热忱有亏之人的下场，描绘得一幅比一幅骇人，以致勒格朗热庭长和公诉人伊斯纳尔——两位从来也算不得多么英勇的好汉——被吓得卑怯无地、战战兢兢；半小时后，他便称心如意地在驿站客栈的院子里同两人道了再会——他们已在开往里昂的公共马车里觅得了座位。

===Original===
As soon as he had seen the last of them, he made haste to requisition a chaise and the only horses to be had in Valence, to take him forthwith to Orange.
===Chinese===
一望不见他们的人影儿，他便急忙去征调一辆轻便马车（chaise），外加全瓦朗斯仅能弄到手的那几匹马，即刻载他去奥朗日。

===Original===
As for his own calèche, he wished the footpads joy in its possession and cared less than nothing what became of his driver or his postilion.
===Chinese===
至于他自己那驾篷车，他但愿那伙拦路贼握着它快活尽兴；至于他的车夫和驿童落个什么下场，他就更不放在心上了——比满不在乎还要淡漠三分。

## XXIV / 第二十四章

===Original===
Could Citizen Chauvelin have seen his calèche and horses a couple of hours later on the road, he would perhaps not have been quite so complacent as to its fate. After rattling over the cobblestones of Valence and tearing down the high road at maddening speed, it slackened a little for the hill, and worked its way slowly up through the small township of Livron. A quarter of a league or so further, it turned off at the crossroads in the direction of Cest and after another half-hour came to a halt at that small cottage which still nestles to this day, with its tumbledown roof and vine-covered harbour, beside the celebrated Roman ruins at the foot of the hill, not far from the banks of the Drôme.
===Chinese===
倘若肖维兰公民能看见两小时后他的篷车和马匹在路上的一番光景，对它们的下场，他或许就不会那么心安理得了。篷车一路辚辚碾过瓦朗斯的石子路面，又以疯狂的速度冲下大路，到上坡时才略略放缓，慢慢腾腾地爬过利夫龙（Livron）小镇。再往前约四分之一里格，它在岔路口拐上通往塞斯特（Cest）的方向，又走了约莫半个钟头，便停在一座小村舍前——那村舍带着坍颓的屋顶和爬满藤蔓的荫棚，至今仍依偎在山脚下著名的罗马遗迹旁，离德龙河（Drôme）的河岸不远。

===Original===
Three ruffians, grimy from the roots of their hair to their down-at-heel shoes, jumped out of the calèche, dragging after them in the open the driver and postilion lately in the employ of Citizen Chauvelin, Representative of the National Convention on special mission. Whilst thus journeying between Valence and Livron these two poor wretches had been securely pinioned with ropes, but they were not gagged, and they used the freedom left to their tongues, by uttering oaths and protests which appeared vastly to amuse their captors.
===Chinese===
三个恶棍——从发根到磨塌了后跟的鞋子，上上下下一身邋遢——跳下篷车，把不久前还在国民公会特派代表肖维兰公民手下当差的车夫和驿童，拖到了露天里。从瓦朗斯到利夫龙这一路上，这两个可怜虫已经被绳子捆得结结实实，只是没有堵嘴，他们便利用舌头剩下的那点自由，赌咒发誓、叫嚷抗议，看那样子，捉他们的人简直乐不可支。

===Original===
The fourth ruffian﻿—for ruffian he was﻿—despite the fact that he had donned a bourgeois’ dress, the better to carry out his coup and pass unnoticed on the road, had in the meanwhile scrambled down from the box.
===Chinese===
第四个恶棍——而且确实是个恶棍——虽然为了把这一票办得干净利落、在路上又不惹眼，特意换上了一身市民装束，这会儿也已从车夫座上爬了下来。

===Original===
“Quite successful so far,” he remarked lightly, speaking in English, and rubbing his hands, which were slender and long and firm, contentedly together.
===Chinese===
「到目前为止，一切顺利。」他用英语轻快地说，一面心满意足地搓着那双细长而有力的手。

===Original===
“What shall we do with these?” one of his companions asked, laughing and pointing to the two woebegone prisoners, who had ceased to curse and to protest, chiefly owing to want of breath, but also through astonishment at finding themselves the victims of some kind of foreign brigands whose language they did not understand.
===Chinese===
「这两位怎么处置？」他的一个同伴笑着问，手指那两个哭丧着脸的俘虏。他们已经不再咒骂，也不再抗议，一则是气都喘不上了，二则是惊愕不已——发现自己竟落在一伙听不懂他们话的外国强盗手里。

===Original===
“Poor beggars!” the other said lightly. “We’ll place them in front of an excellent breakfast and I’ll warrant we need not as much as tie their legs to their chairs. Get them inside, Ffoulkes, will you, and I’ll talk to them as soon as Tony and I have seen to the horses.”
===Chinese===
「可怜的家伙们！」另一位轻快地说。「咱们要请他们坐下一顿上好的早餐，我担保，连他们的腿都不必绑到椅子上。把他们弄进屋里去，福尔克斯，劳驾；等托尼和我照应好马，我马上就同他们谈谈。」

===Original===
“You don’t think the gendarmerie from Valence will be after us, Blakeney, do you?”
===Chinese===
「你总不至于觉得，瓦朗斯的宪兵队会追咱们来吧，布莱克尼？」

===Original===
“Not they,” Sir Percy replied. “They are very short of horses in these parts, and the best will, I doubt not, be requisitioned by my friend M. Chambertin for his own use. I wonder now,” he added musing, “what he is after, taking those two ruffians with him to Paris; and whether his errand is sufficiently urgent to cause him to travel in the stagecoach, now that we have borrowed his calèche.﻿ ﻿…”
===Chinese===
「才不会呢。」珀西爵士答道。「这一带马匹奇缺，最好那几匹，我毫不怀疑，都会被我那位朋友尚贝尔坦先生征去自用了。我倒是纳闷，」他若有所思地补充道，「他带上那两个恶棍去巴黎，究竟打的是什么主意；如今咱们把他的篷车借走了，他那趟差事，是不是急得他非去改乘公共马车不可。……」

===Original===
He paused, slightly frowning, evidently a little puzzled.
===Chinese===
他顿住了，微微皱起眉，显然有点困惑。

===Original===
“I wonder,” he added, “if our friend in there can throw some light upon the matter.”
===Chinese===
「不知道，」他又说，「咱们里边那位朋友，能不能对这件事透出几分光亮。」

===Original===
After which Sir Percy Blakeney and Lord Anthony Dewhurst took the streaming horses out of the shafts, relieved them of their harness and gave them a good rub down, a drink and a feed, while Sir Andrew Ffoulkes and Lord Hastings went into the cottage and busied themselves with their prisoners.
===Chinese===
说罢，珀西·布莱克尼爵士和安东尼·杜赫斯特勋爵（Lord Anthony Dewhurst）把汗淋淋的马匹从车辕里牵出来，卸下挽具，给它们好好擦了身、饮了水、喂了料；安德鲁·福尔克斯爵士（Sir Andrew Ffoulkes）和黑斯廷斯勋爵（Lord Hastings）则走进村舍，去张罗那两位俘虏。

===Original===
My Lord Stowmaries was for the moment in charge of this untenanted cottage, which was a stronghold as well as a rallying place of the League of the Scarlet Pimpernel, as it lay perdu, off both the main and the secondary roads. He it was who had prepared food for his chief and his comrades with the assistance of one Amédé Colombe. The cottage consisted of four rooms; unsecurely sheltered against the weather by a cracked roof, and against damp by broken floors. There were a few very rare pieces of furniture in the place, abandoned there by the late owner and his family, worthy farmers whom the League of the Scarlet Pimpernel had conveyed safely out of France when their loyal adherence to their exiled seigneurs had brought them under the ban of the Revolutionary Government.
===Chinese===
眼下这座无人居住的村舍，暂由斯托玛丽斯勋爵（Lord Stowmaries）照管；它既是猩红繁笺花同盟的一处堡垒，也是一个集合之地，因为它深藏不露，既不傍大路，也不挨支路。正是他，在阿梅代·科隆布的帮手下，为首领和众位弟兄备好了饭食。村舍共四间屋：屋顶开裂，挡风雨挡不严实；地板残破，隔潮气隔不住。屋里只剩寥寥几件家具，是先前的主人一家撂下的——那是一户本分的庄稼人，只因忠心眷念他们流亡在外的旧主，触了革命政府的禁网，是猩红繁笺花同盟把他们平平安安送出了法兰西。

===Original===
In one of the rooms the two prisoners were busy for the moment pinching one another to see if they were really awake. After thinking that they were within sight of death at the hands of a band of malefactors, they found themselves sitting at a table in front of an excellent plate of soup, some bread and cheese and a very large mug of excellent wine, while the cords round their bodies had been removed. Anyway, a very pleasant dream. Leaving conjecture to take care of itself, they fell to on this welcome repast with a healthy appetite. The door which gave on the larger room had been left open, and through it the two men could see the band of malefactors falling to, just like themselves, in front of an excellent meal, laughing and talking in that same gibberish language which they did not understand.
===Chinese===
在其中一间屋里，两个俘虏这会儿正忙着互相掐，看自己是不是真的醒着。方才还以为眼看要死在一伙恶徒手里，此刻却发现自己坐在一张桌子跟前，面前是一盘上好的浓汤、几块面包和干酪，还有满满一大杯上好的葡萄酒，身上捆的绳子也都解掉了。不管怎么说，这个梦做得够美的。猜想的事由它自去，两人便冲着这顿受欢迎的饭菜大吃起来，胃口好得很。通向大房间的那扇门敞着，打门里望进去，两位可以瞧见那伙恶徒也在痛吃痛喝，跟他们一个样，一边吃一边笑着谈着那种他们听不懂的叽里咕噜的话。

===Original===
“They don’t look to me much like brigands,” the driver remarked presently, speaking with his mouth full, “in spite of their dirty clothes.”
===Chinese===
「依我看，他们不大像强盗，」过了片刻，车夫嘴里塞得满满的，开口道，「别看他们衣裳脏。」

===Original===
“And that tall one,” the postilion added thoughtfully, “he seems to be their captain. If you ask me I think he is an aristo.”
===Chinese===
「还有那个高个儿的，」驿童若有所思地补上一句，「他像是他们的头儿。要叫我说，我看他是个贵族佬。」

===Original===
“Or an English spy.”
===Chinese===
「要不就是个英国奸细。」

===Original===
The other shook his head.
===Chinese===
另一个摇了摇头。

===Original===
“Not he. English spies would have murdered us.”
===Chinese===
「他才不是。要是英国奸细，早把咱们宰了。」

===Original===
“Then what in the name of hell﻿—”
===Chinese===
「那到底见了什么鬼——」

===Original===
He got no further, the postilion had gripped him by the arm.
===Chinese===
他话没说完，驿童一把攥住了他的胳膊。

===Original===
“*Nom de nom!*” the postilion exclaimed; and expressed further amazement by a prolonged whistle. “If that is not Amédé Colombe.”
===Chinese===
「见鬼（*Nom de nom*）！」驿童失声嚷道；又拖长了一声呼哨，把进一步的惊异表达了出来。「那不是阿梅代·科隆布是谁！」

===Original===
“*Qui ça Amédé Colombe?*” the other asked.
===Chinese===
「哪个阿梅代·科隆布（*Qui ça Amédé Colombe*）？」另一个问。

===Original===
“The son of the grocer over at Laragne. I know, I come from those parts. But what the hell is he doing here?”
===Chinese===
「拉拉涅那头那位杂货商的儿子呗。我认得，我就是那一带的人。可他究竟见鬼在这儿干什么？」

===Original===
Amédé Colombe sitting at the table with his wonderful new friends, caught the sound of his name, and gave an anxious start.
===Chinese===
阿梅代·科隆布正同他那几位神奇的新朋友坐在桌边，听见了自己的名字，不安地一怔。

===Original===
“Do not worry about them, my young friend,” Sir Percy Blakeney said reassuringly. “Before they could do you any harm we shall be many leagues out of the way.”
===Chinese===
「别为他们操心，我的年轻朋友。」珀西·布莱克尼爵士宽慰道。「不等他们伤到你分毫，我们早已远在多少里格之外了。」

===Original===
At which postilion and driver gazed at one another, more puzzled than ever before. Were they really dreaming, or had they actually heard that foreigner speaking their own language?﻿—and perfectly. The driver was inclined to think that the wine which they had been drinking was potent enough to be the cause of an hallucination. Not that this deterred him from pouring himself out another mugful, and drinking it down with much smacking of the lips and sighs of contentment. It was such very excellent wine. Didn’t his friend the postilion agree with him? Why of course, and the filling and refilling of the two mugs continued apace and at a great rate.
===Chinese===
这一来，驿童和车夫面面相觑，比先前更摸不着头脑了。他们当真是在做梦呢，还是方才实实在在听见了那个外国人讲他们自己的语言？——而且讲得地道极了。车夫倾向于认为，是他们喝下去的酒劲儿太足，才致出了幻觉。不过这并不妨碍他给自己又斟满一大杯，咂着嘴、心满意足地叹着气灌了下去。这酒可真是好极了。他的朋友驿童难道不这么想么？那当然啦，于是两只大杯斟了又斟、满了又满，一刻不停，快得很。

===Original===
“They’ll be blind in a few moments,” Lord Anthony Dewhurst remarked, glancing over his shoulder at the two men.
===Chinese===
「再过一会儿，他们就要醉得不省人事了。」安东尼·杜赫斯特勋爵说着，回头瞥了那两位一眼。

===Original===
And he was right in this surmise. In less than a quarter of an hour driver and postilion were blind to the world with arms stretched out across the table, their heads buried in the bend of their elbows, breathing stertorously.
===Chinese===
他这预料分毫不差。不到一刻钟，车夫和驿童已经烂醉如泥：胳膊横摊在桌上，脑袋埋进臂弯里，呼噜呼噜地打着鼾。

===Original===
“You are not eating, my friend,” my Lord Stowmaries remarked to Amédé Colombe, who in truth had been sitting, silent, self-absorbed, neither eating nor drinking.
===Chinese===
「您没吃东西呀，我的朋友。」斯托玛丽斯勋爵对阿梅代·科隆布说。说实在的，这小伙子一直坐在那儿，闷声不响，心事重重，不吃也不喝。

===Original===
“Friend Amédé does not appreciate your cooking, old man,” Blakeney put in lightly. “It is fairly bad, I confess. Is it not, Monsieur Amédé?”
===Chinese===
「阿梅代朋友不赏识您的手艺呢，老兄。」布莱克尼轻巧地插进一句。「我得承认，这手艺是够糟的。是不是，阿梅代先生？」

===Original===
“It is excellent, milor’,” the young man sighed, “but I ask you, how can I eat or drink when I am in such terrible anxiety?”
===Chinese===
「好极了，爵爷（milor’），」年轻人叹道，「可是我求您了，我心里焦得这般厉害，哪里还吃得下、喝得下呀？」

===Original===
“We were just going to discuss the best way﻿—and the quickest﻿—of alleviating your anxiety, *mon ami*,” Sir Percy rejoined, “all we were waiting for was for those two amiable gentlemen over there to become deaf temporarily as well as blind.”
===Chinese===
「我们正要商量一个最好的法子——也最快的法子——来解一解你的焦虑呢，我的朋友（*mon ami*）。」珀西爵士接话道，「我们方才等的，就是那边两位可亲的先生，在变瞎之外，再暂时变一变聋。」

===Original===
“It is not for myself that I am anxious, milor’,” the young man said timidly. He was over shy of these wonderful men, who had led him from adventure to adventure, in a manner that had almost addled his poor brain. His unsophisticated mind was still vibrating with the excitement of the unforgettable hour, when throwing disguise aside these strangers had revealed themselves not as revolutionary soldiers at all, but as mysterious beings, whose actions had appeared to him to savour of the supernatural. It took him a long time to understand the situation. It seems that his being in possession of Madame de Frontenac’s valuables was known to the girl Adèle who was nothing but a spy in the pay of the Committee of Public Safety. She had that night spied upon him and the girl he loved, seen the girl hand over the valuables to him, and revealed the fact to the Committee. Had these mysterious strangers not played the part of revolutionary soldiers and got him, Amédé, safely out of the way, before the real soldiers appeared upon the scene, he would at this moment be languishing in a prison at Sisteron or Orange preparatory to being sent either to the guillotine or for cannon-fodder on the frontier.
===Chinese===
「我心里焦的不是我自己，爵爷。」年轻人怯生生地说。他在这些了不起的人物跟前拘谨得很——他们领着他一桩奇险接着一桩奇险地闯，那阵仗几乎把他可怜的脑袋搅成了一团浆糊。他那不谙世故的心灵，还在那个难忘时辰的激动里颤动不息：就在那时，这些陌生人抛开伪装，露出了真面目——他们压根儿不是什么革命军士兵，而是一班神秘莫测的人物，他们的所作所为，在他看来简直带着几分超自然的意味。原来，他手中握着弗朗特纳克夫人的值钱物件这件事，早已叫阿黛尔那丫头知道了——她不过是公安委员会出钱豢养的密探。那天夜里，她窥探了他和他心爱的姑娘，看见姑娘把值钱物件交到他手上，便把这桩事捅给了委员会。若不是这几位神秘的陌生人扮作革命军的士兵，赶在真兵露面之前，把他阿梅代稳稳当当转移到安全地带，他这会儿早就在锡斯特龙或奥朗日的监狱里熬着了，只等着不是送上断头台，就是发配边境去当炮灰。

===Original===
All this Amédé understood well enough, he cursed Adèle a thousand times in his heart for being such a snake in the grass. What he could not understand was why these strangers should take an interest in him and in his fate. When to his timid query on that subject their leader laughingly replied: “Sport! *mon ami*, the fun, the excitement! nothing more philanthropic, I assure you, than just sport!” he understood still less.
===Chinese===
这一切，阿梅代心里都明明白白，他在心里把阿黛尔咒了一千遍——好一条草里藏着的毒蛇！他不明白的是，这些陌生人为什么偏偏对他、对他的命运发生兴趣。他就此怯生生地探问时，他们的头领笑着答道：「图个乐子！我的朋友，好玩儿，刺激！我向你担保，绝没有比这更乐善好施的用心，纯粹就是图个乐子！」这一来，他越发不明白了。

===Original===
No wonder that to him, Amédé Colombe, the whole adventure had come as a manifestation of something supernatural. As for M. de Frontenac, his fellow-sufferer, on the other hand, he had apparently been prepared for that manifestation. It appeared that Madame and Mademoiselle had already been rescued from peril and taken to a place of safety, where presently M. de Frontenac would be able to join them, always through the instrumentality of these wonder-working strangers. The last thing M. de Frontenac had said to him, Amédé, when he took leave of him a couple of days ago, somewhere in the lonely mountain paths where the party had called a halt, was: “Trust these Englishmen, Amédé, trust them with everything you hold dear. Look at me, had I not trusted them with my wife and daughter, I should have seen my dear ones first, and myself afterwards, facing the guillotine at this very hour!”
===Chinese===
无怪乎对他阿梅代·科隆布来说，这场从头到尾的奇遇，就像一场超自然的显圣。至于德·弗朗特纳克先生——与他同患难的那一位——却显然早对这场显圣心里有数。看样子，夫人和小姐已经脱险获救，被送到一处安全的地方；德·弗朗特纳克先生不久也能去同她们团聚，自然还是靠这几位神通广大的陌生人从中出力。两天前，一行人在荒僻的山径间歇脚，德·弗朗特纳克先生向他阿梅代告辞时说的最后一番话是：「信这些英国人吧，阿梅代，凡是你珍视的东西，都放心托付给他们。看看我：要不是我信了他们，把妻女都托付了出去，这会儿先瞧着亲人、后瞧着我自己一步步走向断头台的，就是我了！」

===Original===
It was with these words ringing in his ears, that Amédé, sitting now amongst these men to whom he owed his life, had mustered up sufficient courage to reiterate more firmly: “It is not for myself I am anxious, milor’.”
===Chinese===
正是带着这几句话在耳畔轰响，阿梅代此刻坐在这些对他有救命之恩的人们中间，才鼓足了勇气，更坚定地重申：「我心里焦的不是我自己，爵爷。」

===Original===
“I know that, *mon ami*,” Sir Percy replied, “you are thinking of that brave little girl﻿—Fleurette. Isn’t that her name?”
===Chinese===
「这我知道，我的朋友。」珀西爵士答道，「你想的是那个勇敢的小姑娘——弗勒蕾特。她是叫这个名字，对吧？」

===Original===
“Yes, milor’,” Amédé whispered timidly.
===Chinese===
「是的，爵爷。」阿梅代怯怯地低声道。

===Original===
“Some of my friends and I are going straightway back to look after her now.”
===Chinese===
「我和几位朋友这就动身回去，这就去照应她。」

===Original===
“And you will hurry, milor’, you will hurry, will you not? Every day may be fatal for her.”
===Chinese===
「那你们要快，爵爷，要快，好不好？多耽搁一天，都可能要了她的命。」

===Original===
“I think not,” Blakeney said in that decisive way of his, which carried so much conviction. “You told me she was the daughter of a man high up in the councils of the revolutionary government.”
===Chinese===
「我看不会。」布莱克尼用他那斩钉截铁的口吻说道——那口吻自有一种叫人深信不疑的力量。「你跟我说过，她是革命政府枢机里一位身居高位之人的女儿。」

===Original===
“One Armand, milor’,” Amédé continued. “Little is known of him in the neighbourhood, save that he is a widower and apparently has influence with the government.”
===Chinese===
「一个叫阿尔芒的，爵爷。」阿梅代接着说。「街坊近邻对他所知甚少，只知道他是个鳏夫，同政府那边显然说得上话。」

===Original===
“Fleurette is an only child?”
===Chinese===
「弗勒蕾特是独生女？」

===Original===
“Yes. She has lived at Lou Mas all her life.”
===Chinese===
「是。她生在庐庄（Lou Mas），长在庐庄，一辈子没离开过。」

===Original===
“If her father has influence he can protect her for a time.”
===Chinese===
「她父亲既然说得上话，就能护她一阵子。」

===Original===
“For a time﻿—yes! But﻿—oh milor’!” the poor young man suddenly burst out with passionate vehemence, “if anything were to happen to Fleurette, I would curse you for having saved my life.”
===Chinese===
「护一阵子——是的！可是——唉，爵爷！」可怜的年轻人突然激愤难抑地迸出声来，「弗勒蕾特要是出了什么事，我会咒你们救了我这条命的。」

===Original===
Blakeney smiled at the young man’s eagerness.
===Chinese===
布莱克尼对着年轻人的热切微微一笑。

===Original===
“Listen, friend Amédé,” he said lightly, “are you going to trust me and my friends?”
===Chinese===
「听着，阿梅代朋友，」他轻快地说，「你信得过我和我的朋友们么？」

===Original===
And Amédé, who remembered those last solemn words spoken by M. de Frontenac, looked into those lazy grey eyes, meeting that half earnest, half-humorous glance beneath the heavy lids, replied simply: “Yes, milor’!”
===Chinese===
阿梅代记着德·弗朗特纳克先生最后那番郑重其事的话，凝望着那双慵懒的灰眼睛，迎着沉沉眼睑下那半是认真、半是谐谑的目光，简单地答道：「信，爵爷！」

===Original===
“And you will accord me what my friends accord so ungrudgingly, bless them, implicit obedience?”
===Chinese===
「那么，你肯不肯也给我一样我的朋友们毫不吝惜地给我的东西——愿老天保佑他们——那就是：绝对的服从？」

===Original===
Again Amédé replied simply: “Yes, milor’!” And then he added: “What am I to do?”
===Chinese===
阿梅代又简单地答道：「肯，爵爷！」接着补上一句：「我该做什么呢？」

===Original===
“For the moment nothing,” Sir Percy replied, “but remain here quietly and alone until you hear from me again. Can you do it?”
===Chinese===
「眼下什么也不用做。」珀西爵士答道，「只管安安静静一个人待在这儿，等我的音信。你办得到么？」

===Original===
“If you command.”
===Chinese===
「只要您吩咐。」

===Original===
“You won’t mind the loneliness?”
===Chinese===
「一个人冷清，你不怕么？」

===Original===
“I shall be thinking of Fleurette and trusting you.”
===Chinese===
「我会想着弗勒蕾特，信着你们。」

===Original===
“Come, that’s brave!” Sir Percy concluded lightly. “You will find some provisions in the armoire in this room: but apart from that you will find your way every day down to the river, and turning to your right, you will walk along its bank till you come to a derelict shed hidden from view by two old walnut trees. In a corner of the shed, beneath a pile of leaves, you will find something to comfort you, either a loaf of bread, or a piece of cheese, sometimes a jug of milk or a bottle of wine. Scanty fare probably, but it will suffice to keep the wolf from the door. Those who supply it are poor and risk much to do it. They owe my friends and me a debt which they pay in this fashion. Now are you prepared to live this life of a lonely anchorite while my friends and I return to Laragne and gather news of your Fleurette?”
===Chinese===
「这就对了，好样的！」珀西爵士轻快地收了话头。「这屋里的碗柜（armoire）里有些吃食；除此之外，你每天自己寻到河边，向右手一拐，沿着河岸走，走到一座破废的棚屋跟前——有两棵老胡桃树遮着，瞧不见它。棚屋的角落里、一堆树叶底下，你会找到一点慰藉你的东西：或是一只面包，或是一块干酪，有时是一罐牛奶，有时是一瓶酒。吃食多半寒素，却也尽够拦住门口的饿狼。送这些来的人自己也是穷人，干这事要冒大大的风险。他们欠下我和我朋友们一份恩情，便用这个法子来偿还。怎么样——趁我和朋友们回拉拉涅去打探你那位弗勒蕾特的消息，你预备好过这种孤独隐士的日子了么？」

===Original===
“If I could only come with you, milor’!” Amédé sighed.
===Chinese===
「要是我能跟你们一起去就好了，爵爷！」阿梅代叹道。

===Original===
“Tush, man, what were the good of that?” Sir Percy retorted with a slight note of impatience in his pleasant voice. “You would only lead us all﻿—and your Fleurette﻿—into trouble.”
===Chinese===
「嗐，伙计，那有什么用处？」珀西爵士反诘道，那悦耳的嗓音里带了一丝不耐。「你只会把大伙儿——还有你的弗勒蕾特——统统拖进麻烦里去。」

===Original===
“But you will bring me news of her soon?” Amédé entreated with tears in his kind, innocent-looking eyes.
===Chinese===
「可你们很快就会给我捎来她的消息吧？」阿梅代恳求道，那双和善的、看上去一脸无辜的眼睛里噙着泪。

===Original===
“Either news of her﻿—or Fleurette herself.”
===Chinese===
「要么是她的消息——要么，是弗勒蕾特本人。」

===Original===
Amédé shook his head. “She would not leave her father,” he said dolefully.
===Chinese===
阿梅代摇了摇头。「她不会丢下她父亲的。」他悲切切地说。

===Original===
“Then she will be safe with him, until better times come along, which will be very soon, friend Amédé, you may take that from me. Another few months﻿—very few﻿—and the dragon’s own teeth will be turned against itself. This anarchy cannot endure forever, because all evil, friend Amédé, is by the grace of God finite.”
===Chinese===
「那么，有他在，她就是安全的，直等到好日子来临——好日子很快就到了，阿梅代朋友，这话你只管信我。再过几个月——用不了几个月——那恶龙自己的毒牙，就要调转头来对付它自己了。这样的乱局不可能永世长存，因为一切邪恶，阿梅代朋友，全凭天主的恩典，终有穷期。」

===Original===
He spoke these last words with unwonted earnestness, and simple Amédé Colombe looked up to him with awe as to a prophet standing there, magnificent in energy and strength, head thrown back, the lazy eyes beneath their heavy lids flashing with unquenchable inner fire. And suddenly he checked himself, laughter chased away earnestness, the eyes twinkled with merriment like those of a carefree schoolboy, rather than a seer.
===Chinese===
最后这几句话，他说得异乎寻常地恳切；单纯的阿梅代·科隆布满心敬畏地仰望他，仿佛仰望一位先知——他站在那里，精力与气魄焕发光华，头颅后仰，沉沉眼睑下那双慵懒的眼睛里，闪着扑不灭的心火。忽然他收住了话头，笑意驱散了郑重，那双眼像无忧无虑的学童一般闪出顽皮的光，倒不像一位先知了。

===Original===
“La!” he said lightly, “I verily believe we were waxing serious. No cause for that, eh, friend Amédé? My friends and I are off on a gay adventure. To take a message of love from you to a brave little girl who loves you, a shade better methinks, than she loves that mysterious father of hers. Write your love letter, my friend, but be sure and make it brief, and I’ll deliver it myself in her own little hands. I saw her, that sweet wench of yours, no woman ever showed more pluck than she did when she went to seek Madame de Frontenac’s valuables.”
===Chinese===
「嗐！」他轻快地说，「我真觉得咱们方才有点儿一本正经起来了。犯不着这样，嗯，阿梅代朋友？我和我朋友们这是去赴一场痛快的冒险哩。替你捎一封情话给一个勇敢的小姑娘——她爱你，我琢磨着，比爱她那位神秘父亲还要多上一分。写你的情书吧，我的朋友，千万记得写短些，我要亲手把它交到她自己那双小手里。我见过她，你那位可爱的小妮子——女人家里，再没有谁，比她去寻取弗朗特纳克夫人的值钱物件时显出过更大的胆量。」

===Original===
“You saw her, milor’?” Amédé exclaimed wide-eyed. “*Mon Dieu!* is there anything that you do not see?”
===Chinese===
「您见过她，爵爷？」阿梅代瞪圆了眼睛叫起来。「我的天主（*Mon Dieu*）！还有什么是您看不见的么？」

===Original===
“There is, *mon ami*,” Sir Percy replied gaily. “I have never seen your pretty Fleurette’s mysterious father. He must be a fine man to keep the love of so sweet a daughter. So write your letter, my friend,” he went on, and pointing to an oaken desk at the further end of the room on which were quill-pen, inkpot and sand, “and I promise you that I will deliver it, if only for the pleasure of having a squint at the mysterious owner of Lou Mas. Heigh-ho!” he added with a contented sigh, “but this promises to be fine sport. What say you, Ffoulkes, or you, Tony? We are going to put our heads into the wolf’s jaws again, eh? Stowmaries, you too, and Hastings. But we’ll do it, and I promise you that the sight of pretty Fleurette will be a fitting compensation for some very unpleasant half hours we may have to go through. Now then, friend Amédé! your love missive, and two of you put the horses to, we’ll have to make Montélimar by nightfall! there we’ll either abandon the calèche, steal a couple of horses and cut across the hills to Sisteron, or keep to the calèche and the road as far as the neighbourhood of Orange, where much information can always be gleaned about the district. We’ll make no plans now and trust to luck and chance. What?”
===Chinese===
「有的，我的朋友。」珀西爵士欢快地答道。「我就从没见过你那漂亮弗勒蕾特的神秘父亲。能把这么一位甜美女儿的爱留住的，准是位了不起的人物。所以写你的信吧，我的朋友。」他接着说，指了指屋子那一头的一张橡木写字台，台上搁着鹅毛笔、墨水瓶和吸墨沙。「我答应你一定送到——就冲能瞅一眼庐庄那位神秘的主人，也值当了。嗬——！」他心满意足地叹了口气，又添上一句，「这一趟，瞧着准是场好戏。你们说呢，福尔克斯？还有你，托尼？咱们这是要把脑袋再往狼嘴里伸一回了，嗯？斯托玛丽斯，你也来，还有黑斯廷斯。可咱们干得成；而且我向你们担保，同漂亮弗勒蕾特的那一面，足够补偿咱们兴许要熬过的几个顶不痛快的半个钟头。那么来吧，阿梅代朋友！你的情书；你们哪两个去套车——天黑前咱们得赶到蒙泰利马尔（Montélimar）！到了那儿，咱们要么撇下篷车，偷它两匹马，翻山抄近路直奔锡斯特龙；要么就守着篷车走大路，一直走到奥朗日左近——那一带的动静，向来能打听到不少。眼下咱们不定计划，全凭运气和机缘。怎么样？」

===Original===
Lord Tony then pointed, smiling, to the driver and postilion still fast asleep in the adjoining room.
===Chinese===
托尼勋爵随即笑吟吟地指了指隔壁屋里兀自酣睡不醒的车夫和驿童。

===Original===
“What is to happen to those mudlarks?” he asked.
===Chinese===
「那两个泥猴儿怎么处置？」他问。

===Original===
“We’ll take them along, of course,” Blakeney replied. “So thrust them into the bottom of the calèche, under the seat for choice, and those who sit inside can use them as footstools. Where we leave the calèche, there we leave them too, to find their way back to the bosom of their families in due course.”
===Chinese===
「当然是捎上他们。」布莱克尼答道。「把他们塞进篷车底下去，最好塞在座位底下，坐在车里的人还能拿他们当脚凳使。我们在哪儿撇下篷车，就在哪儿把他们也撇下，到时候他们自会寻路，回到各自家人的怀抱里去。」

===Original===
He looked so gay and so full of life and strength, so sure of himself, such pure joy in this new adventure radiated from his entire person, that some of that divine spark in him set Amédé Colombe’s blood tingling through his veins. Anxiety, melancholy, doubt fell away from him at a glance from those lazy eyes now twinkling with joy, at sight of that firm mouth, ever softened by a smile; of those long, slender hands, delicate as a woman’s, firm as those of a leader of men. Poor Amédé was almost happy at this moment, feeling that he was one with this band of heroes, that just by obedience and self-effacement, he could feel that he was one of them.
===Chinese===
他看上去那样欢快，那样生气勃勃、浑身是劲，那样胸有成竹；这场新冒险的纯粹喜悦，从他整个人的身上焕发出来，连他身上那一点神性的火花，也激得阿梅代·科隆布满身血脉嗡嗡发烫。焦虑、忧郁、疑虑，都在那一瞥之间离他而去——那双慵懒的眼睛此刻闪着欢快，那张坚毅的嘴永远带着一丝笑意的柔和；还有那双细长的手，娇嫩如妇人，坚定如统帅。可怜的阿梅代这一刻几乎是幸福的：他觉得自己同这一班英雄融为一体；觉得单凭服从和不显山露水，便能觉出自己也是他们中的一员。

===Original===
In cramped schoolboy hand, he wrote a brief, very brief little line to Fleurette, and told her how he adored her and longed for her nearness. He also told her that whatever else happened he implored her to trust the bearer of this note, who would be the means of bringing her back one day to the shelter of her Amédé’s arms.
===Chinese===
他用学童般歪歪扭扭的小字，给弗勒蕾特写下一封短短的、短得不能再短的小笺，告诉她自己多么爱慕她，多么渴望她来到身边。他还告诉她，无论旁的生出什么变故，他恳求她信赖捎信的人——正是这位捎信人，将有朝一日设法把她带回来，重新躲进她的阿梅代那怀抱的庇护。

===Original===
Less than an hour later he was all alone in the tumbledown cottage that nestled against the ruins of a former, long-since-dead civilization. The late afternoon was soothing and balmy, the sky of a pale turquoise, clear and translucent, and as Amédé, standing somewhat forlorn at the cottage door, watching the narrow road over which the calèche had lumbered awhile ago, bearing away his mysterious new friends, the pale crescent of the moon appeared above the snow-capped crest of La Lance, and Amédé, remembering the old superstition, bowed solemnly nine times to the moon.
===Chinese===
不到一个钟头，他已独自一人守在那座塌檐破壁的村舍里；村舍依偎着一处久已死灭的往古文明的遗迹。傍晚温煦宜人，天空是淡淡的绿松石色，清澈透明。阿梅代有些落寞地站在村舍门口，望着篷车不久前吱吱嘎嘎碾过的那条窄路——它载走了他那些神秘的新朋友。这时，一弯淡淡的新月升上拉朗斯峰（La Lance）积雪皑皑的峰顶；阿梅代记起古老的迷信，对着月亮，恭恭敬敬鞠了九个躬。

## XXV / 第二十五章

===Original===
What irked Fleurette most in her prison life was the monotony of it: the want of something to do. After she had cleaned out the room which she shared with ten others, and put herself and everything tidy, the day appeared interminably long. She did her crochet work while her supply of thread lasted; old Louise had been allowed to make up a bundle of some clothes for her, and in it she had also put the crochet work and a few hanks of thread, but a few days saw the end of this supply, after which there was nothing with which Fleurette could occupy her fingers. Some of her fellow-prisoners had needles, cotton and thimbles, and presently Fleurette, always willing and always smiling, was asked to darn and mend their clothes. She was glad enough to do it, as a means of killing time.
===Chinese===
狱中生活最让弗勒蕾特烦恼的，是那份单调：无事可做。她同另外十个人合住一间屋子，打扫完屋子，把自己和一切收拾停当之后，一天便显得长得没有尽头。线还够用的时候，她就做钩针活儿；老路易丝获准给她打了一个小包袱，装了几件衣裳，里头还搁了钩针活计和几绞线；可没过几天，这点存项便见了底，从此弗勒蕾特的手指再没有东西可打发。几个同狱的女犯有针、有线、有顶针，不久，总是那么热心、总是笑盈盈的弗勒蕾特便被央求着替人缝缝补补。她巴不得揽下这活儿，正好用来消磨时光。

===Original===
They were a heterogeneous crowd these fellow-prisoners of hers, culled from every social grade from the great lady to the troll out of the street. Misfortune and the precariousness of existence had brought these usually warring elements closely together: friendships sprang up where in the past even a nod of recognition would have been grudged. The Comtesse de Mornas, who belonged to the highest aristocracy of Provence, would take her morning exercise with her arm round the waist of Eugénie Blanc, daughter of a secondhand clothes dealer of Orange. Hélène de Mornas’s husband had been guillotined three months ago on some trumped-up charge or other, and Eugénie Blanc’s father, accused of traffic with the enemy﻿—whoever that enemy might be no one knew﻿—had perished in that awful wholesale massacre perpetrated in Orange last month. Sorrow brought these two women together, as it did many others, and when Claire de Châtelard, obviously a woman of evil reputation, sought Fleurette’s compassion with a tale of hunger, misery and arrest, that compassion was freely given, and the girl who had led such a sheltered life at Lou Mas, knowing nothing of temptation or of evil, had for daily companion after that, one Claire de Châtelard, the most notorious jade of Orange.
===Chinese===
她这些同监的难友，是一群形形色色的人，上自尊贵的夫人，下至街头拉客的暗娼，各个社会等级里都挑得出来。厄运和朝不保夕的处境，把这些素来相互倾轧的分子紧紧凑到了一处：从前连点头相识都不屑的人之间，如今竟生出了情谊。莫尔纳伯爵夫人（Comtesse de Mornas）出身普罗旺斯顶高层的贵族，清晨活动身子时，胳膊却搂着奥朗日一个旧衣商的女儿欧仁妮·布朗（Eugénie Blanc）的腰。埃莱娜·德·莫尔纳（Hélène de Mornas）的丈夫三个月前因某个罗织的罪名上了断头台；欧仁妮·布朗的父亲，被指通敌——那敌人究竟是谁，谁也说不清——上个月在奥朗日那场骇人听闻的大开杀戒中殒了命。忧伤把这两个女人牵到了一起，也把许多别的人牵到了一起；后来，克莱尔·德·沙特拉尔（Claire de Châtelard）——一看便知是个名声不正经的女人——拿一段饥饿、困苦、被捕的身世来求弗勒蕾特的怜悯，那份怜悯也毫不吝惜地给了她。于是在庐庄（Lou Mas）那般深藏密护中长大、不识诱惑为何物、不识邪恶为何物的姑娘，从此便日日与奥朗日名声最臭的荡妇克莱尔·德·沙特拉尔做伴了。

===Original===
Thus the first few days went by. In the prison﻿—it is architect Caristie’s house with all the furniture turned out of it and the rooms left bare of everything save a few benches, a few paillasses, a table, a wash-hand basin or two﻿—in the prison great puzzlement prevails.
