Hitherto every day, just before sunset, a captain of the guard with half a dozen men would enter the courtyard and, standing there, would in a loud voice read out a list of names. That list was the Roll-call, the decrees of the Revolutionary Tribunal condemning so many to the guillotine on the morrow. And at all the windows of the houses around the courtyard, heads would appear: men and women﻿—yes! and children too﻿—clutching their prison bars, and listening. Listening if their name be upon that list. And then a sigh of relief if that name was not called: another day’s respite! another day in which to drag this miserable, precarious existence. As for the others, the ones whose names were read out in a loud voice by the captain of the guard, there was nothing for it but to clasp their loved ones, or mayhap only the newly-found friend, in their arms﻿—for the last time. That same night they were transferred to the prison of the Hôtel de Ville, and in the morning the guillotine. Sometimes not that. Just driven like a herd to the slaughter: on the bridge or the Place de la République. And there the guns. And death pell-mell. Like cattle, with ne’er a grave nor a prayer.
===Chinese===
头几天就这样过去了。监狱里——那原是建筑师卡里斯蒂（Caristie）的宅子，家具什物一概搬空，房间里光秃秃的，只剩几条长凳、几张草垫、一张桌子、一两只盥洗盆——监狱里如今是一片大惑不解。在此以前，每天日头将落的时候，总有一名卫兵上尉带着半打士兵走进院子，站在那里，朗声宣读一串名单。那份名单就是点名（Roll-call），是革命法庭的判决：把这么多的人定于明日送上断头台。于是院子四周楼房的每一扇窗口都会探出一颗颗人头：男人和女人——是啊！还有孩子——紧紧抓着窗上的铁栏，侧耳听着。听自己的名字在不在那份名单上。名字没有叫到，便松一口气：又多了一天喘息！又能把这凄惨的、朝不保夕的日子再拖上一天。至于另一些人，那些名字被卫兵上尉朗声念出的人，除了把心爱的人儿——也许不过是新结识的朋友——搂进怀里，再没有别的可做了：这是最后一次了。当天夜里，他们被移解到市政厅的监狱，第二天早晨，便是断头台。有时连这个也没有。只像赶牲口似的被赶去宰杀：赶到大桥上，或共和国广场（Place de la République）上。那边等着的是枪。于是乱纷纷地死。像牲口一样，连一座坟、一句祷文也落不着。

===Original===
That was how it had been before Fleurette’s arrival. That cinder-wench Claire de Châtelard told her how it used to be. But Fleurette never saw anything of that. The very day after her arrival was marked by the nonappearance of the captain of the guard with his list. They all wondered, put their heads together, and for an hour or two after the usual time there was whispering, conjecturing going on. Respite for everybody: that was of course what it meant. But why? Had that awful Revolution really come to an end, as everybody had prophesied it would? Had all those tigers up in Paris really devoured one another, and was there no one left to carry on the infamy? Well! that was perhaps how it was. But no one knew anything. Not the warders. Not the prisoners. Not anybody. Inside these walls wherein news was wont to penetrate with extraordinary precision and rapidity, nothing was known. Nothing. Except that there was no list and that on the morrow the guillotine was idle.
===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特入狱之前，情形一向如此。这段旧事，是那个灰渣丫头克莱尔·德·沙特拉尔讲给她听的。可弗勒蕾特自己什么也没赶上。她到后的第二天，卫兵上尉便没有带着名单露面。众人纳闷，交头接耳，往常点名过后的一两个钟头里，到处是窃窃私语和种种揣测。人人都得了缓期——意思当然是这样。可是为什么？那场可怕的革命当真到了头，像人人预言的那样了吗？巴黎那群猛虎当真互相吞吃尽了，再没有人留下来继续这桩罪恶勾当了吗？好啊！情形或许就是这样。可是谁也说不准。狱卒们不知道。囚犯们不知道。谁都不知道。在这消息素来传得又快又准的高墙之内，竟什么也不知道。什么也不知道。只知道再没有名单，明天的断头台闲着。

===Original===
This new departure from regular routine was accepted with the same stoicism as everything else. It was the stoicism of supreme helplessness, or rather of despair, and it had engendered in all these people, men and women, herded here together on the eve of death, a kind of levity which it is difficult for modern thought to understand. Death was so familiar to them, such a daily companion, that they had ceased to think of him with awe. Familiarity had bred contempt. And deriding Death, they turned him into ridicule. Made game of him, defied him to break their spirit. It was a species of madness born of intense horror and absolute despair. Fleurette at first felt sick and wretched at sight of these people-proud countesses and highborn seigneurs, as well as muckworms and jades﻿—acting the guillotine, as they called it, in the great hall of Architect Caristie’s house which was assigned to them for recreation. She, poor little soul, had never learned to envisage death as anything but awesome for which the Holy Church was at pains to prepare doomed mankind with sacraments and prayers.
===Chinese===
对这一例行常规的打破，众人像对待其他一切那样，以同样的坚忍承受了。那是极端无助、毋宁说是绝望之下的坚忍；它在这群被驱赶到一处、处在死亡前夜的人——男男女女——心里，催生出一种今人的心智难以理解的轻浮。死亡于他们太熟稔了，是朝夕相伴的伙伴，他们已不再怀着敬畏去想它。熟稔生出了轻慢。他们嘲弄死亡，把死亡变成笑柄。拿它耍笑，激它来摧折自己的精神。这是一种由极度的恐怖和彻底的绝望孕育出来的疯狂。起初，弗勒蕾特一见到这些人就觉着恶心难受——自尊的伯爵夫人和出身高贵的爵爷，连同守财奴和荡妇们——在划给他们消遣用的卡里斯蒂宅子大厅里，演他们所谓的断头台戏。她这可怜的小人儿，从来只学会把死亡看作一件可畏的事，是神圣的教会费尽苦心，用圣事和祈祷为劫数难逃的世人作预备的。

===Original===
The first time she saw them all in their gruesome mummery, she fled affrighted back to the dark, noisome room where she slept, and throwing herself on her miserable paillasse, she sobbed her little heart out with horror and grief, stuffed her fingers into her ears so as not to hear the voices and the laughter that came from the great hall. Here Claire de Châtelard found her an hour later, and I think this was the beginning of their friendship, for the wench found just the right words wherewith to console this ignorant little country mouse.
===Chinese===
头一回撞见他们全体那副骇人的假面闹戏，她吓得逃回自己睡觉的那间又黑又臭的屋子，扑在寒酸的草垫上，又惊又痛，把小心肝都哭碎了，手指死死塞住耳朵，不去听大厅里传来的说话声和笑声。一个钟头后，克莱尔·德·沙特拉尔在这里找到了她；我想这便是两人交情的开端，因为这婆娘偏偏找出了一席恰如其分的话，来宽慰这只无知的乡下小老鼠。

===Original===
“Their one recreation,” she urged. “They mean no irreverence. Just think of them face to face with death. Always. Deprived of every consolation: mocked, jeered at. This playacting is only a blind to hide their own misery, the despair which they are too proud to display.”
===Chinese===
「这是他们唯一的消遣，」她劝道，「他们没有半分不敬的意思。你想想他们吧，日日夜夜同死亡面对面。无时无刻。一切慰藉都被剥夺了：还要受人讥嘲、耻笑。这出戏不过是块遮眼的布，遮住他们自己的凄苦，遮住那份他们太高傲而不肯显露的绝望。」

===Original===
After a while Fleurette dried her tears. But she slept ill that night. Nightmares pursued her. Visions of that mock tribunal, with the mock prosecutor, and the mock culprit. And then the setting up of two chairs, and draping them with bits of crimson rags to represent the guillotine. Once or twice she sat up on her hard paillasse, hardly able to smother a scream which would have roused her roommates from their sleep. She had seen in retrospect one of the warders, who had helped in the acting of the gruesome play, dressed as Satan with horns and tail and entering the hall with a bound and a whoop. His role was to snatch the President of the Tribunal and the Prosecutor from their seats and to drag them away with him into everlasting fire, while a weird voice boomed the query: “What hour is it?” and another replied: “Eternity.”
===Chinese===
过了一会儿，弗勒蕾特擦干了眼泪。可那一夜她睡得很坏。噩梦追缠着她。幻见那座假法庭，假的公诉人，假的罪人。又幻见搬来两把椅子，搭上几条猩红的破布片，权当断头台。有一两回她从硬草垫上惊坐起来，险些憋不住一声尖叫，把同屋的人从睡梦里惊醒。她回想起来，还看见过一名狱卒也在这出骇人戏里凑过一角：扮成撒旦，头长角，身后拖尾，一蹦一跳、呼啸着闯进大厅。他的戏份，是把庭长和公诉人从座位上一把掳走，拖着他俩同去永劫之火；这时一个怪腔怪调的声音瓮声瓮气地问：「什么时辰了？」另一个声音答道：「永恒。」

===Original===
Poor little Fleurette! It was her first experience of life. And what an experience! Yet, it had only been one step from Lou Mas with its almond-trees and rippling mill-streams, with Bibi and old Louise, one step from there to this barrack of a house concerted into a prison, with all its humiliating propinquities, and all its horrors. Her companions in misfortune were very kind to her. All of them. The men as well as the women. Claire de Châtelard and the Comtesse de Mornas. They all seemed to understand her position, her helplessness, her ignorance. They were so kind! so kind! They admired her crochet-work, and talked to her of Laragne, or the snows of Pelvoux, or the almond-trees of the Dauphiné. They thanked her and kissed her when she offered to ply her needle for them: to mend their clothes or darn their stockings. Within a few days she became one of themselves. A younger sister in this family of the despairing. Within a week, or mayhap ten days, she had lost her sense of horror at their mummeries, could laugh at the antics of the mock Satan come to carry the mock judges off to hell. The only thing to which she could not get accustomed was the representation of the guillotine, the inverted chairs and the bits of red rags, the cords, the victim, the basket and the executioner. Oh! that executioner! He was terrible! Especially of late. The role, like that of Satan, had always been undertaken by one of the warders; rough fellows these, culled from the lowest scum of the city; men who delighted in all the physical and moral torture inflicted on the aristos under their charge, who would gloat over the sight of a father torn away from his children and led to the guillotine, who would regale the unfortunate prisoners with tales culled from the *Moniteur* of wholesale executions or brutal massacres. The idea of acting the part of executioner to the mock representations of the guillotine delighted a certain grim sense of humour which most Southerners possess. There was one man in particular, lately come to replace another who was sick, who threw himself into the gruesome role with zest. He would strip almost naked for the part, and then cover his face and his large body with a mixture of soot and charcoal and oil so that he looked like a huge Negro, with gleaming teeth and long, lank hair, of a pale blonde colour, speckled with dirt.
===Chinese===
可怜的小弗勒蕾特！这是她头一遭阅历人生。而这又是怎样的一遭阅历啊！其实，从庐庄那杏树夹道、磨坊溪水淙淙的家园，从有比比和老路易丝相伴的家，到这座兵营似的改作监狱的大宅，中间不过一步之遥；这里有的是种种辱没人的挤挨，种种的恐怖。她这些同厄难的难友待她极好。个个都好。男人们和女人们一样好。克莱尔·德·沙特拉尔好，莫尔纳伯爵夫人也好。他们仿佛都体谅她的处境，她的无依无靠，她的少不更事。他们真好啊！真好！他们夸她的钩针活儿，同她谈拉拉涅，谈佩尔武（Pelvoux）的雪，谈多菲内的杏树。她提出替他们做针线——缝补衣裳、织补袜子——他们道着谢，还吻她。没过几天，她便成了他们中的一员。这个绝望之家的一位小妹妹。不出一个星期，或者十天吧，她对那班假面闹戏已不复感到恐怖，看见假撒旦来把假法官们捉下地狱的滑稽样子，也能跟着笑了。唯一让她习惯不了的，是那断头台的扮演：倒竖的椅子、红布条、绳索、受刑人、接头的筐子，还有刽子手。啊！那刽子手！真怕人！尤其是新近这一任。这个角色同撒旦一样，向来由一名狱卒承乏；都是些粗坯，从城里最下贱的渣滓里挑出来的；这些人以折磨名下贵族佬的皮肉和精神为乐，见了做父亲的被从孩子身边拖走、押上断头台，便心花怒放，还爱拿从《导报》（*Moniteur*）上拣来的成批处决、野蛮屠杀的消息，讲给不幸的囚犯们下饭。在断头台假戏里扮演刽子手这个主意，正对了大多数南方人禀有的一种阴惨惨的幽默。其中有一个，是新近来接替一个害了病的，演起这个骇人的角色来格外卖力。为着这角色，他差不多脱得赤条条的，再往脸上和魁伟的躯干上涂满煤烟、木炭和油的混合物，看上去活像一个高大的黑人，一口牙齿闪闪发亮，长长的平贴头发泛着淡金黄色，上头星星点点全是垢污。

===Original===
Poor Fleurette could not bear to look at him, nor at the mock execution when one or other of her fellow-prisoners would allow himself to be tied to the mock guillotine, amidst the well-acted laughter and jeers of men and women who impersonated the awful rabble that was always to be found around the real guillotine. It was horrible, and Fleurette would run out into the corridor, or back to her miserable paillasse, anywhere where she could shut her ears to that gruesome mockery.
===Chinese===
可怜的弗勒蕾特不忍看他，也不忍看那假处决——她的难友中总有一个人自愿让自个儿捆上假断头台，四下的男男女女扮演真断头台边永远围着的那群可怕的暴民，笑得逼真，骂得逼真。那太可怕了，弗勒蕾特便奔出去躲进走廊，或者奔回自己寒酸的草垫上——随便哪儿，只要能捂住耳朵，不听那骇人的闹剧。

===Original===
Unfortunately there came a day when the warder declared that an order had come through, that prisoners must remain together in the hall during the hour of recreation. He said it was so, and there was no one to contradict him. Of all the tyrants that had been set over their fellow-men, these days, none were more dreaded because more autocratic, than prison-warders. As far as prisoners knew, these tyrants’ power over them was absolute. In any case they could, if contradicted or thwarted, make it ten thousand times worse than before for those who did not cringe. This order then had to be obeyed and Fleurette, cowering alone in a corner of the hall, kept her eyes tightly shut while the impish scene was being enacted.
===Chinese===
不幸的是，有一天狱卒宣称上头下来了一道命令：消遣时间里，囚犯必须都聚在大厅里。他说有这道命令，就没人敢说半个不字。时下骑在同胞头上的形形色色的暴君当中，最专横、因而也最叫人惧怕的，莫过于监狱的狱卒。在囚犯们看来，这些暴君的权力是绝对的。反正，谁要是顶撞了他们、碍了他们的事，他们自有本事叫不肯屈膝的人日子比先前难上一万倍。这道命令只好服从。于是弗勒蕾特独自缩在大厅一角，那出鬼戏上演的当口，眼睛闭得紧紧的。

===Original===
Madame de Mornas, aristocratic, dignified, with her arm round Eugénie Blanc’s waist, spoke to her very kindly.
===Chinese===
莫尔纳夫人雍容华贵，气度端凝，一只胳膊搂着欧仁妮·布朗的腰，跟她说话，语气极是和蔼。

===Original===
“My dear,” she said in her gentle, well-bred voice, “if we did not make a mockery of all these horrors we should brood over them, and some of us would go mad.”
===Chinese===
「亲爱的，」她用那温柔而有教养的嗓音说，「要是不拿这些恐怖事儿打哈哈，我们就只好翻来覆去地琢磨，有些人是要发疯的。」

===Original===
And Eugénie Blanc, the “old clo” dealer’s daughter, added with a shrug: “You dear innocent! You have seen nothing of life as it is. You don’t know what it is when memory sets to work and you see things﻿—you see﻿—” She gave a shudder and then a harsh laugh. “This at any rate takes one’s mind off memory for a time.”
===Chinese===
欧仁妮·布朗——那个做「旧衣买卖」的商人的女儿——耸耸肩，又添了一句：「你这可爱的天真人儿！生活的本相你一点儿也没见过。你不知道记忆动起工来是什么滋味——你就看见了——看见了——」她打了个寒噤，随即一阵刺耳的笑。「好歹这个能叫人把心思从记忆上挪开一会儿。」

===Original===
Claire de Châtelard’s sympathy too was sincere, though rather more grim: “We’ve all got to go through the real thing presently; the mockery of it now will make the reality tomorrow more endurable.”
===Chinese===
克莱尔·德·沙特拉尔的同情也是真心，只是更冷峻些：「真刀真枪的那一场，我们人人眼下都躲不掉；如今先拿它耍笑，明天真来的时候，也就好受一点。」

===Original===
“We must practise today,” M. de St. Luce, the great scientist said lightly, “our attitude of tomorrow.”
===Chinese===
「我们今天，」大科学家德·圣吕克先生（M. de St. Luce）轻描淡写地说，「就得把明天临刑的做派先操演起来。」

===Original===
That was the general tenor of everyone’s feelings upon the subject. Fleurette, touched by so much sympathy, tried to smile through her tears, and promised to school herself to the same philosophy. But as soon as all these kindly creatures had left her, in order to join, laughing, in the grim spectacle, she once more closed her eyes and sat in the dark corner, quite still, hoping that no one would notice her. But the laughter at one time was so loud, everyone’s mood so hilarious, that involuntarily she opened her eyes and looked. The mock executioner had just completed his task. It seems he was complaining that Madame la Guillotine was still unsatisfied: she was putting out her arms, ready to embrace another lover. M. de Bollène﻿—a minor poet well known in Provence﻿—was declaiming some verses of his own composition, in praise of that promised embrace. The executioner’s coal-black face shone like polished ebony in the flickering light of the tallow candles that guttered in their sconces. Madame de Mornas, almost unrecognizable in ragged kirtle and with a crimson scarf tied round her head, was flourishing her knitting and humming the tune of the Carmagnole as an accompaniment to M. de Bollène’s verses, whilst Claire de Châtelard sprawled at the foot of the mock guillotine with a red streak across her throat.
===Chinese===
对这个题目，人人的心情大抵都是这个调子。弗勒蕾特受了这么多温存的体恤，含着泪强作笑颜，也应承要学会这套处世的哲学。可是等这些好心人一个个丢下她、笑着去赶那阴惨的场面时，她又阖上眼睛，坐在暗角落里一动不动，巴望谁也别留意她。可是有一阵笑声实在太高，人人兴头十足，她不由自主地睁开了眼。假刽子手刚做完他的活计。只听得他仿佛在抱怨，说断头夫人（Madame la Guillotine）还不餍足：她正张开双臂，准备再拥抱一个情人。德·博莱讷先生（M. de Bollène）——普罗旺斯一位颇有名气的末流诗人——正朗朗诵一首自作的诗，赞美那许诺中的拥抱。刽子手那张煤炭也似的黑脸，在烛座里淌泪将尽的牛油烛摇曳的光里，亮得像打磨过的乌木。莫尔纳夫人穿着褴褛的裙子，头上扎一条猩红头巾，几乎叫人认不出来；她挥动着毛线活计，哼着《卡马尼奥拉》（Carmagnole）的调子，给德·博莱讷先生的诗伴奏；克莱尔·德·沙特拉尔则摊手摊脚躺在假断头台脚下，喉咙上横着一道红印子。

===Original===
And suddenly, to her horror, Fleurette saw the executioner stride towards her corner.
===Chinese===
冷不防地，弗勒蕾特惊恐地看见那刽子手大步朝她的角落走来。

===Original===
“What?” he cried aloud, “tears? Tears are for aristos. To the guillotine with her!” or words to that effect. Fleurette did not rightly understand what he did say, all she knew was that this hideous, horrible man came striding towards her with hands outstretched, and that everyone was laughing or singing or clapping their hands. The next moment she felt that horrible hand upon her shoulder, on her kerchief, her breast. She gave a loud scream and cowered further into the corner thinking that she would faint with terror, until she heard a peremptory voice calling out loudly: “Leave the child alone, man, can’t you see she is frightened?”
===Chinese===
「什么？」他大声嚷道，「眼泪？眼泪是贵族佬的。把她送上断头台去！」——或是诸如此类的话。他究竟说了什么，弗勒蕾特没有听真切；她只知道这个奇丑可怕的汉子张开双手朝她大步走来，四下里人人都在笑、在唱、在拍手。跟着她便觉出那只可怕的手落在了自己肩上、头巾上、胸口上。她尖叫一声，往角落里缩得更深，心想自己要吓昏过去了，直到听见一个威严的声音高声喝道：「放开这孩子，伙计，你没看见她吓坏了吗？」

===Original===
“Frightened? Of course she is frightened,” the loathsome creature retorted with a laugh. “Did I not say that she was an aristo? Let me just call the warder and﻿—”
===Chinese===
「吓坏了？她当然是吓坏了，」那可憎的家伙笑着回嘴，「我不是说了吗，她是个贵族佬。我这就去叫狱卒来，然后——」

===Original===
A woman’s voice was raised in protest:
===Chinese===
一个女人扬声抗议道：

===Original===
“No, no, don’t call the warder. She’s done nothing wrong﻿—and he might﻿—”
===Chinese===
「别，别，别去叫狱卒。她没做错什么——他没准会——」

===Original===
And Madame de Mornas it was who added:
===Chinese===
接着开口的正是莫尔纳夫人：

===Original===
“You coveted this ring this morning, man, it is yours if you leave the child alone and say nothing to the warder.”
===Chinese===
「你今天早上就眼馋这枚戒指了，伙计。你放过这孩子，别跟狱卒提一个字，它就是你的。」

===Original===
How kind people were! How kind! As nothing further seemed to happen, Fleurette ventured to open her eyes: Claire de Châtelard was sitting beside her, trying to comfort her. The gruesome play had apparently come to an end; the prisoners in groups of three or more stood about talking and laughing, preparatory to be driven back to the sleeping-rooms for the night. The black executioner was no longer there.
===Chinese===
人心多好啊！多好！看没有什么动静了，弗勒蕾特才敢睁开眼睛：克莱尔·德·沙特拉尔坐在她身边，正竭力安慰她。那出骇人的戏似乎已经收场；囚犯们三个一堆、五个一群地站着谈笑，等着夜里被赶回卧房去。黑刽子手已经不在了。

===Original===
“He is not a bad man really,” Claire de Châtelard said to Fleurette, fondling her hand and smoothing the golden curls that clung to her moist forehead! “only very rough and coarse. Bah! these men!” she went on with a shudder. “The warder is a veritable fiend: a genius in inventing means to punish you if you do not bribe him or give in to him. All my little treasures which I was able to bring here with me, have gone into his rapacious hands. This man is not so bad, he is new to his work, he came a day or two ago to replace one who was ill. But he is only a scavenger. When the warder is dead drunk he takes his place, the rest of the time he does all the dirtiest work in the house. A loathsome creature, what? If he were not so big, we should not be so frightened of him. But he is better than the warder.”
===Chinese===
「他其实不是个坏人，」克莱尔·德·沙特拉尔对弗勒蕾特说，一面爱抚着她的手，一面抚平贴在她汗湿额头上的金色鬈发，「只是粗鲁、下作罢了。呸！这些男人！」她打了个寒噤，接着往下说。「那狱卒才是个十足的恶鬼：你要不贿赂他、不顺从他，他自有的是整治人的聪明才智。我随身带得进来的那些小宝贝，全落进了他贪得无厌的手心。这一个倒不这么坏，他干这差事还生，前一两天才来顶一个害了病的。可他到底只是个扫粪倒秽的杂役。狱卒烂醉如泥的时候，由他顶班；其余的时候，宅子里最脏的活计全归他。一个叫人恶心的东西，是吧？他要不是生得这么高大，我们才不会这么怕他呢。不过他总比狱卒强些。」

===Original===
Fleurette only listened with half an ear. She still felt bruised and ill after the fright she had had. That horrible black hand touching her breast. It was worse than any nightmare.
===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特只有一只耳朵在听。受了那场惊吓，她至今还觉着身心发疼。那只可怕的黑手，碰在她胸口上。比什么噩梦都可怕。

===Original===
She was glad when the bell clanged and the warder accompanied by his new aide﻿—only partially relieved of the soot and the grime of his role﻿—drove the prisoners like a herd of cattle back into their pens. So many women in one room, so many men in another. He had his list, and with a stout stick in his hand which he flourished as he read out the names, he drove them all in, into their respective night quarters and locked the doors upon them.
===Chinese===
铃铛铛敲响时，她真有几分高兴：狱卒由他那新来的帮手陪着——脸上身上的煤烟油垢只擦去了一半——把囚犯们像牛群一般赶回各自的栏圈。这么多女人一间屋，那么多男人另一间屋。他手里拿着名单，攥着一根粗手杖，一边念名字一边挥舞，把众人统统赶进各自的过夜去处，锁上门了事。

===Original===
Fleurette shared her wretched paillasse with Claire de Châtelard. There was no dressing or undressing in this overcrowded room. No privacy. One just lay down in one’s clothes and snatched what rest one could. Oh! the horror of it all to these women, most of them accustomed to dainty homes. Fleurette never knew which moment she dreaded most, that of opening her eyes to another awful day, or trying to close them in intermittent sleep.
===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特同克莱尔·德·沙特拉尔合睡一张破草垫。在这间挤得满满的屋子里，谈不上脱衣穿衣。没有一丝隐秘。人只能和衣躺下，能捞着多少觉就睡多少觉。唉！对这些多半过惯了精致家常日子的女人来说，这一切何其可怕。弗勒蕾特始终说不清，哪个时刻更叫她胆寒：是睁开眼睛迎来又一个可怕的白昼，还是在断断续续的睡眠里竭力想合上眼睛。

===Original===
Claire de Châtelard, less impressionable, was already asleep. Fleurette slipped out of her kirtle which she laid tidily across the foot of the paillasse; then she took off her muslin kerchief. As she did so something fluttered to the ground. A piece of paper neatly folded. Smothering an involuntary cry of surprise, she stooped to pick it up. Yet she hardly dared to touch the thing at first. How had it got between the folds of her kerchief? Who could possibly have put it there unbeknown to her? This was the second time within a very little while that Fleurette had come in contact with something that savoured of the supernatural. Still timorous, and with a trembling hand, she picked the paper up. Claire was asleep and most of the others had already stretched out their limbs upon their hard paillasses. No one paid any heed to Fleurette.
===Chinese===
克莱尔·德·沙特拉尔感受没那么敏锐，早已睡着了。弗勒蕾特褪下裙子，整整齐齐搭在草垫脚头；然后摘下薄纱头巾。摘的时候，有个东西飘然落地。是一张折得整整齐齐的纸。她压下一声不由自主的惊呼，俯身去拾。可起初她几乎不敢碰那东西。它怎么会夹在头巾的褶子里？谁又能在她不知不觉中把它放了进去？这是短短时日里，弗勒蕾特第二回撞上这种带着超凡气息的事了。她仍旧心怀怯意，伸出发抖的手，把纸拾了起来。克莱尔睡着了，其余的人也大多已在各自的硬草垫上躺平了手脚。没有谁理会弗勒蕾特。

===Original===
There was no direct light in the room itself, but an oil lamp which hung from the ceiling in the corridor threw a feeble ray of light through the fanlight over the door. Fleurette unfolded the paper and smoothed out its creases against her knee. She made her way to the centre of the room where she could just contrive, by that dim light from above, to decipher the handwriting upon the paper. But the first word that caught her eye, nearly caused her to utter a cry of joy; it was the signature: Amédé.
===Chinese===
屋子本身没有灯，只有走廊天花板上吊着的一盏油灯，透过门上的气窗投进一缕微光。弗勒蕾特展开纸，在膝盖上抚平折痕。她挪到屋子当中，借着头顶那点昏光，才勉强辨认出纸上的笔迹。可头一个映入眼帘的字，险些叫她欢呼出声：那是署名——阿梅代（Amédé）。

===Original===
Amédé! At once her eyes grew dim with tears. Amédé! Those five letters in the clumsy, school-boyish handwriting meant happiness and home. Amédé! Before trying to read further she pressed the paper against her cheek, fondled it, laid it against her lips.
===Chinese===
阿梅代！她的眼睛顿时让泪水模糊了。阿梅代！那五个字母，用笨拙的学生体写着，意味着幸福和家园。阿梅代！还没往下读，她先把那张纸贴在脸颊上，摩挲着，又把它贴上嘴唇。

===Original===
Amédé! He had written to her. Where from? How? She did not care to think. What did it matter after all? He was thinking of her. Had written to her. And some divine messenger had conveyed his missive to Fleurette. Though he was safe and well﻿—Bibi had assured her that he was﻿—he had thought of her and sent her this letter through one of God’s own angels.
===Chinese===
阿梅代！他给她写了信。从哪儿写来的？怎么送到的？她不愿多想。横竖又有什么要紧？他在想着她。给她写了信。某位神圣的信使，把他的书信送到了弗勒蕾特手里。虽然他安然无恙——比比亲口对她担保过——他仍旧惦记着她，托上帝亲遣的一位天使，给她送来了这封信。

===Original===
And then Fleurette dried her eyes, for she remembered that presently the bell would clang again, when all the lights would be put out and she might have to wait until tomorrow to read Amédé’s letter.
===Chinese===
于是弗勒蕾特擦干眼睛，因为她记起，铃声转眼又要敲响，那时灯火全灭，她也许就得等到明天，才能读阿梅代的信了。

===Original===
It was short, very, very short. Amédé had never been a scholar, but in it he told her how he adored his Fleurette and longed for her nearness. He also told her that whatever else happened, he implored her to trust the bearer of this note who would be the means of bringing her back one day to the shelter of his arms.
===Chinese===
信很短，非常非常短。阿梅代从来算不得一个读书人，可他在信里告诉她，他多么爱慕他的弗勒蕾特，多么渴望她在身边。他还告诉她，无论再出什么事，他恳求她信赖送这封信的人——正是这个人，终有一天会把她带回他怀抱的荫庇之下。

===Original===
The bearer of this note? Who was he? Surely, surely, one of God’s angels! and so of course she trusted him. And it was only *le bon Dieu* who would so guide Bibi that all this trouble would come to an end and she, Fleurette, would of a certainty find a shelter once more in her Amédé’s arms.
===Chinese===
送信的人？他是谁呢？千真万确，是上帝的一位天使！那么她自然信赖他。也只有好天主（*le bon Dieu*），才能这样指引比比，叫这一切灾殃有个尽头，叫她弗勒蕾特稳稳当当地、重新在阿梅代的怀抱里觅得庇护。

===Original===
She read and reread the few brief lines over and over again, and presently when the bell clanged, and she was forced to make her way hurriedly to her paillasse before the room was plunged into utter darkness, she laid down on the hard straw with a little sigh of contentment and of peace. Her evening prayer was one entirely of gratitude to *le bon Dieu* for His gift of Amédé’s love and Bibi’s protection. And that night Fleurette slept quite soundly, with her cheek resting against the letter from Amédé.
===Chinese===
那短短几行字，她读了又读，一遍一遍读个不了；不多时铃声铛铛响起，她只得匆匆奔回自己的草垫，赶在屋子陷入一片漆黑之前躺下；她躺在硬邦邦的干草上，轻轻舒了一口气，心里又满足又安宁。她的晚祷，从头到尾只是感谢好天主赏赐的恩典：阿梅代的爱，比比的护佑。那一夜，弗勒蕾特睡得十分香甜，脸颊贴着阿梅代的信。

## XXVI / 第二十六章

===Original===
For two whole days Citizens Pochart and Danou of the 137th Section of the Committee of Public Safety had been sorely puzzled. They had received a curt note from Representative Chauvelin telling them that he would be absent from Orange for a brief while, and bidding them suspend all business until his return. Suspend all business? In very truth all business was perforce at a standstill, not because of the absence of the representative on special mission, but because of that of two high officers of State; the President of the Tribunal and the Public Prosecutor.
===Chinese===
整整两天，公安委员会第137区的公民波夏尔（Pochart）和公民达努（Danou）一直大惑不解。他们收到肖维兰代表一张简慢的便条，说他要离开奥朗日一段短短的时日，吩咐他们在他回来之前，一切公务暂且搁置。一切公务暂且搁置？说实话，一切公务本来就无可如何地陷于停顿，倒不是因为这位特派代表不在，而是因为两位国家重臣不在——革命法庭的庭长和公诉人。

===Original===
Representative Chauvelin in his note had also alluded to this absence, stating that by direct orders from the Central Committee of Public Safety, President Legrange and Prosecutor Isnard had been obliged to proceed to Paris.
===Chinese===
肖维兰代表在便条里也顺带提到了这桩缺席，声称勒格朗热庭长和公诉人伊斯纳尔是奉中央公安委员会（Central Committee of Public Safety）的直接命令，不得不动身前去巴黎的。

===Original===
It was all very puzzling, not to say suspicious. Pochart and Danou put their heads together and came to the conclusion that here undoubtedly were some machinations at work on the part of Representative Chauvelin with a view to getting his daughter out of harm’s way. The question was how to make use of these machinations. Of their knowledge that they were machinations. How in fact to turn them against the man who hitherto had carried himself with such consummate arrogance, lording it over every officer of State in Orange, with thinly veiled threats that had roused ire, malice and hatred in these men, whose rule of life was “strike ere you yourself be struck.”
===Chinese===
这一切实在叫人纳闷，简直可以说形迹可疑。波夏尔和达努凑在一处合计，得出一个结论：这里头毫无疑问是肖维兰代表的阴谋在暗地里运作，为的是把他的女儿弄出险地。问题在于，怎样利用这些阴谋——利用他们心里明知那是阴谋的把柄。实际上，又怎样把阴谋掉转头来，去对付那个人——此人向来倨傲到了十足十，凌驾于奥朗日的每一位国家官员之上作威作福，那些半遮半掩的威吓，早已在这班人心头撩起了怒火、歹意与仇恨，而这班人立身处世的信条，原本就是「先下手为强，莫等自己先挨打」。

===Original===
One thing, however, was crystal-clear. Representative Chauvelin was hard hit. He put on an air of lofty indifference; he continued to bluster and to threaten, but he was hard hit by the arrest of his daughter, as indeed any family man would be. Pochart and Danou did not care one worthless assignat what became of the daughter, but they did feel that the pleasure of threatening and terrorizing the representative on special mission, perhaps even of dragging him down from his exalted position and sending him in his turn to the guillotine, was not one to be missed. Up to the hour when Lieutenant Godet had arrested the wench Fleurette on suspicion, Representative Chauvelin had been a living threat to every patriot in Orange. He seemed, as it were, to be always walking hand in hand with the guillotine, or else in its shadow; sheltered himself, yet a menace to others. But now the tables were reversed, and Pochart and Danou had in one hour learned to substitute threats for soft words, arrogance for servility. And they vastly enjoyed the substitution.
===Chinese===
不过，有一件事却是水晶般透亮：肖维兰代表挨了重重的一击。他摆出一副高高在上、无动于衷的架子；他照旧咆哮恫吓，可他确确实实因女儿被捕而挨了重重一击，正如天下任何一个有家室的人都免不了那样。至于那女儿往后是个什么下场，波夏尔和达努连一枚不值一文的指券（assignat）也不屑挂心；可他们倒的确觉得，能对这位特派代表恐吓威慑一番的快意——说不定还能把他从那高高的位子上拽下来，轮到把他送上断头台——这番快意，万不容错过。直到戈岱中尉以嫌疑罪名拿下那丫头弗勒蕾特的那一刻为止，肖维兰代表一直是奥朗日每一个爱国志士头顶活生生的威胁。他仿佛总在同断头台手挽着手走路，再不就走在断头台的影子里；他自己有遮有护，于旁人却是祸胎。可如今攻守易势，波夏尔和达努不出一个钟头，就学会了拿威吓换下软话、拿倨傲换下卑躬——这一换，他们受用极了。

===Original===
But the trouble was that they were void of imagination. Representative Chauvelin could be brought down, they knew that. But how? Judging other men by themselves, they quite envisaged the possibility of a father sacrificing his own daughter in order to save himself. And there was also the possibility that a representative on special mission was powerful enough to save both his daughter and himself. Strong forces would have to be marshalled against him. Pochart and Danou with heads together passed these forces in review.
===Chinese===
麻烦在于，他们两个毫无想象力。肖维兰代表是可以扳倒的——这个他们知道。可是怎么个扳法？以己度人，他们完全设想过一种可能：做父亲的为保全自己，牺牲亲生女儿。而且也还有另一种可能：一位特派代表权势够大，足以把女儿和自己一并保全下来。那就必须调集强大的力量来对付他。波夏尔和达努头凑着头，把这些力量逐一检阅了一遍。

===Original===
There was Lieutenant Godet who hated Representative Chauvelin with a hatred born of fear﻿—the deadliest hatred of all. There was that rat-faced little spy, Adèle, a mixture of petty spite and malice. She would be useful. Others might be found, for Representative Chauvelin had many enemies who had not until this hour dared to come out into the open, but who would readily show themselves once the powerful representative was attacked.
===Chinese===
先有戈岱中尉，他恨肖维兰代表，那恨意由恐惧而生——天底下最要命的恨。又有那个老鼠脸的小密探阿黛尔，一肚皮的鸡零狗碎外带歹毒。她会派得上用场。旁的也还找得到，因为肖维兰代表树敌众多，那班仇家直到此时此刻还不敢公然露头，可只要这位权势赫赫的代表一旦遭到攻击，他们立刻就会现身。

===Original===
And in the meanwhile the business of purging the countryside of aristos, suspects and traitors was at a standstill. With no Public Prosecutor to frame indictments and no President to try the accused, the order: “*Que la Terreur soit à l’ordre du jour*”: “Let Terror be the order of the day,” had become a dead letter. This could not go on, of course. Pochart and Danou, quite apart from their schemes against Representative Chauvelin, felt that a solution must be found﻿—and that quickly﻿—for this impossible situation. If allowed to continue they stood in very great risk of a reprimand from Paris for allowing the business of the State to be at a standstill. They might be accused of want of zeal. Those great patriots up in Paris were so unreasonable, one never knew what they might do. Having sent for President Legrange and Public Prosecutor Isnard, they probably expected “the order of the day” to go on just the same. But how, *nom de nom*? How?
===Chinese===
与此同时，肃清乡间贵族佬、嫌疑犯与叛徒的公事，也停摆了下来。没有公诉人起草诉状，没有庭长审判被告，那道「让恐怖成为当日的议程」（*Que la Terreur soit à l’ordre du jour*）的命令，早已成了一纸空文。这自然拖不得。波夏尔和达努且撇开他们对付肖维兰代表的种种图谋不谈，也觉得这个没奈何的局面必须寻个出路——而且要快。再拖下去，他们极可能招来巴黎的一顿申斥，罪名是听任国家公务陷于停顿；他们还可能被指为热忱有亏。巴黎那班大爱国志士如此蛮不讲理，谁也说不准他们会干出什么来。他们既已传召了勒格朗热庭长和公诉人伊斯纳尔，多半还指望「当日的议程」照旧运转不误。可是怎么运转呢，见鬼（*nom de nom*）？怎么运转？

===Original===
They were still seeking a solution, these two, Pochart and Danou, on the third morning, when to their surprise Representative Chauvelin walked in, as calm and indifferent as you please.
===Chinese===
到了第三个早晨，这两位——波夏尔和达努——还在寻摸出路，不料肖维兰代表竟自走了进来，要多镇定有多镇定，要多漠然有多漠然。

===Original===
He had completed his business, he explained to them, sooner than he had anticipated. President Legrange and Public Prosecutor Isnard on the other hand had continued their journey to Paris.
===Chinese===
他向两人解释道，自己那件事办得比预想的快，已经了结了。至于勒格朗热庭长和公诉人伊斯纳尔，倒是已继续赶往巴黎去了。

===Original===
Danou, suave as ever, expressed satisfaction at the return of the citizen representative. It was indeed a matter of congratulation, he added, for them all, seeing that the business of the State was so completely at a standstill.
===Chinese===
达努素来圆滑甜腻，这会儿照旧对公民代表的归来表示欣慰。他又添上一句：国事既然停摆得如此彻底，这对他们大家说来，实在是一件可贺之事。

===Original===
Pochart was somewhat more emphatic.
===Chinese===
波夏尔的口气可就直露得多了。

===Original===
“There are at least one hundred and sixty traitors,” he said, “who should have been dealt with days ago. Your absence, citizen, and that of two other public servants should not have occurred at this critical hour﻿—”
===Chinese===
「少说也有一百六十名叛徒，」他说，「几天以前就该发落了。您这一走，公民，外带另外两位公职人员这一走，实在不该赶在这个要命的关口上——」

===Original===
“It was inevitable, Citizen Pochart,” Chauvelin broke in dryly. “Orders from Paris, you know﻿—”
===Chinese===
「那是没法子的事，波夏尔公民。」肖维兰干巴巴地打断他，「巴黎来的命令，你是知道的——」

===Original===
“I was just proposing to Citizen Pochart,” Danou put in mildly, “that we send a message to Paris by this new aerial telegraph to ask for further orders. There is one installed at Avignon, and a courier﻿—”
===Chinese===
「我方才正跟波夏尔公民提议，」达努温温和和地插言，「咱们用新式的空中电报（aerial telegraph）给巴黎去个信儿，讨一道新的指示。阿维尼翁就装着一座，再派一名信使——」

===Original===
“The aerial telegraph is required for more important business than yours, Citizen Danou,” Chauvelin once more broke in, and this time with some impatience.
===Chinese===
「空中电报自有比你们的事更要紧的用途，达努公民。」肖维兰再次打断，这一回带着几分不耐烦。

===Original===
“What can be more important than the suppression of traitors?” Pochart argued with an obvious sneer. “I marvel at you, citizen representative, that you should think otherwise.”
===Chinese===
「还有什么事比镇压叛徒更要紧？」波夏尔争辩道，明摆着带着讥诮，「公民代表，您竟另有一副想法，我真要对您称奇了。」

===Original===
“The very latest decree of the National Convention,” Danou added, “was that Terror be the order of the day. I too marvel at you, Citizen Chauvelin.”
===Chinese===
「国民公会最新颁布的法令，」达努补充道，「就是恐怖成为当日的议程。我也对您称奇，肖维兰公民。」

===Original===
“There is no cause for marvel,” Chauvelin rejoined with well assumed indifference. “I have not been in Orange more than a few hours. I have not had time to devise for this new situation.”
===Chinese===
「没有什么可称奇的。」肖维兰答道，装出一副恰到好处的淡漠，「我回到奥朗日统共不过几个钟头，还来不及为这个新局面筹划出办法来。」

===Original===
“Well then, tomorrow, citizen,” Danou suggested, “will you be ready to consult with us on the best means of meeting this impossible situation? Otherwise, I am still of the opinion that the aerial telegraph, or perhaps a courier to Paris﻿—”
===Chinese===
「那么，公民，就明天，」达努提议道，「您能否同我们磋商一番，议出一个应付这没法收拾的局面的最好法子？要不然，我仍旧主张动用空中电报，再不然派个信使去巴黎——」

===Original===
He went on mumbling for a few seconds. His tone had been quite suave, not to say deferential; but Chauvelin’s keen ear had not failed to detect the threat that lurked behind those smooth, velvety tones.
===Chinese===
他又咕哝了几秒钟。他那口气温润之至，简直称得上恭谨；可藏在那一派光滑如丝绒的腔调背后的威吓，到底没能逃过肖维兰那只敏锐的耳朵。

===Original===
“Tomorrow, as you say,” he concluded dryly.
===Chinese===
「就依你说，明天。」他干巴巴地收了话。

===Original===
All through the wearisome journey back from Valence he had been busy scheming and planning; alternately adopting and rejecting one plan after another. He knew well enough that Pochart and Danou were stalking him like wild beasts, ready to pounce on him, to come to grips with him in a life and death struggle in which his darling Fleurette would also be involved.
===Chinese===
从瓦朗斯回来的那一程劳顿旅途中，他一直在忙着运筹谋划；一个方案接着一个方案，取来又弃，弃了又取。他心里再清楚不过：波夏尔和达努正像野兽一般潜步跟踪着他，只等扑将上来，同他做一场生死相搏——而在那场搏斗里，他心爱的弗勒蕾特也要一并被卷进去。

===Original===
Now after his interview with the two men, he knew that already they scented victory, that they too were scheming and planning, planning his overthrow, and using Fleurette as the deadliest weapon against him. These last three years of titanic struggle of man against man, of the strong against the weak, of the weak against the strong, had taught him that he could expect nothing, neither mercy nor consideration, from enemies whom he himself would never have hesitated to sacrifice to his own whim or his own tyranny. His only hope lay in his avowedly superior brain power. He no longer could dominate these snarling wild beasts, now that they were showing their fangs, but he could outwit them, before they sprang and devoured him. Brainpower as against blind lust. And Chauvelin thought that he could win.
===Chinese===
如今同这两个人打过这一场交道，他知道他们已然嗅到了胜利的气味，知道他们也在运筹谋划，谋划着把他扳倒，把弗勒蕾特当作对付他的最致命的武器。这三年来的巨人般的搏斗——人对人，强者对弱者，弱者对强者——教会了他：对这班仇敌什么也休想指望，既没有怜悯，也没有体恤；换了他自己，也从不曾犹豫过把这样的人牺牲在他的任性妄为与暴虐之下。他唯一的指望，就在他那自认高人一筹的脑力上。如今这班咆哮的野兽既然亮出了獠牙，他已再难镇服它们，可他还能斗智取胜——趁它们还没扑上来把他吞吃掉。脑力对盲目的兽欲。而肖维兰相信，这一仗自己能赢。

## XXVII / 第二十七章

===Original===
Representative Chauvelin was quite calm, businesslike, armed with sheaves of papers and documents, when he met his colleagues the following morning in the bureau of the Committee.
===Chinese===
第二天早晨，肖维兰代表在委员会的办公处里同他的同僚们会面，镇定自若，一副公事公办的做派，手边备着一叠叠的文件文书。

===Original===
“I have found,” he announced as soon as they were seated, “a solution to our difficulty.”
===Chinese===
「我找到了，」三人刚一落座，他便宣布，「一条解决咱们难题的出路。」

===Original===
“Ah?” Danou ejaculated simply. And Pochart also said “Ah,” but in a different tone.
===Chinese===
「啊？」达努简简单单地迸出一声。波夏尔也道了声『啊』，腔调却另是一副。

===Original===
“I have here,” Chauvelin continued, and selected an official document from the pile which he had deposited upon the table. “I have here a decree which exactly meets our case. It was promulgated by the National Convention on the motion of Citizen Cabot on the 6th of Brumaire last.”
===Chinese===
「我这儿有，」肖维兰接着往下说，一面从自己先头搁在桌上的那一摞文书里，拣出一件正式公文。「我这儿有一道法令，正对得上咱们眼下的局面。它是国民公会应卡博公民（Citizen Cabot）的动议、于上一个雾月（Brumaire）六日颁布的。」

===Original===
Leaning back in his chair, he began to read from the official document in his hand. The others, elbows on table, chin cupped in hand, listened with what we might call mixed feelings.
===Chinese===
他往椅背上一靠，念起手中那件正式公文来。其余两位手肘支在桌上，手掌托着下巴，怀着一种我们不妨称之为五味杂陈的心情听着。

===Original===
> “Should it occur that through any cause whatsoever, one of the chief officers of State be absent from duty for a period exceeding seven days, the Representative on special mission shall then assume his functions and continue to discharge them for as long as seems expedient. And in the event of more than one important officer of State being so absent, the Representative on special mission shall himself appoint a substitute who will also discharge such duties as the Representative on special mission shall have assigned to him for the time being.”
===Chinese===
> 「倘若因任何缘故、无论何种缘故，一位国家重臣离岗旷职超过七日，特派代表即应接掌其职务，并在其认为相宜的期间内继续履行。倘离岗旷职的国家重要官员不止一位，特派代表应亲自委派一名代理，该代理亦应履行特派代表暂时指派给他的各项职责。」

===Original===
Having finished reading, Chauvelin put the document down, and with a gesture of finality let his thin, claw-like hand rest upon it.
===Chinese===
念完之后，肖维兰把公文放下，做了一个一锤定音的手势，将那只瘦削的、爪子似的手按在上面。

===Original===
“The decree is clear enough, methinks,” he said coldly.
===Chinese===
「依我看，这法令写得够清楚了。」他冷冷地说。

===Original===
There was a pause. A silence lasting perhaps thirty seconds; then Danou said mildly:
===Chinese===
出现了一阵停顿。一段约莫持续三十秒钟的沉默；随后达努温和地说道：

===Original===
“I have never heard of this decree.”
===Chinese===
「这道法令，我从来没有听人说起过。」

===Original===
“Nor I,” Pochart echoed.
===Chinese===
「我也没听说过。」波夏尔随声附和。

===Original===
“The Central Committee in Paris,” Chauvelin put in drily, “has often remarked on the strange ignorance displayed by avowed patriots, of the decrees promulgated for the welfare of the State. The Committee deems that such ignorance almost amounts to treason.”
===Chinese===
「巴黎的中央公安委员会，」肖维兰干巴巴地插话，「已经不止一次地议论过：那些自诩的爱国志士，对为国家福祉而颁布的种种法令，竟表现出一种奇怪的无知。委员会认为，这样的无知几乎等同于叛国。」

===Original===
“May I look at the document?” Danou rejoined simply, choosing to ignore the reprimand﻿—and the thinly-veiled threat.
===Chinese===
「我能看看这份公文么？」达努简简单单地接话，只当没有听见那顿申斥——还有那半遮半掩的威吓。

===Original===
“Certainly,” Chauvelin replied, and handed the document over to his colleague.
===Chinese===
「当然可以。」肖维兰答道，把公文递给了他的同僚。

===Original===
“Is it a copy?” Pochart asked, looking over his friend’s shoulder.
===Chinese===
「这是份抄件么？」波夏尔从朋友的肩后探过头来看着，问道。

===Original===
“An attested copy, as you can see,” Chauvelin replied. “It is countersigned by Citizens Robespierre, Billaud, Couthon and Saint Just. You are not thinking of disputing the order, Citizen Danou?”
===Chinese===
「一份经核验无误的抄件，你是看得见的。」肖维兰答道。「上面有罗伯斯庇尔、比约（Billaud）、库东（Couthon）和圣茹斯特（Saint Just）诸位公民的副署。你该不是想抗辩这道命令吧，达努公民？」

===Original===
Once more and still that arrogance, those veiled threats. The situation being entirely different from what it was yesterday, Danou and Pochart dared not persist in their mood of defiance. Not before they had consulted one another, marshalled those forces﻿—Godet, Adèle, the proofs against the wench Fleurette﻿—and decided on the mode of attack. Representative Chauvelin must have something up his sleeve, some hidden power, or he would not be so arrogant, so threatening.
===Chinese===
又是那副倨傲，又是那番半遮半掩的威吓，而且丝毫不减。如今局面已同昨天全然两样，达努和波夏尔不敢再把那副抗衡的架势摆下去。总得先彼此商量妥当，把那些力量集结起来——戈岱、阿黛尔、不利于那丫头弗勒蕾特的种种罪证——再定下进攻的路数才行。肖维兰代表的袖子里必定藏着什么，藏着某种不为人知的权势，否则他不会这般倨傲、这般咄咄逼人。

===Original===
Danou wiped the sweat from his bald cranium and handed the document back to Chauvelin. Pochart shaking himself like a wet dog, returned to his seat.
===Chinese===
达努抹去秃脑门上的汗珠，把公文交还给肖维兰。波夏尔像只落水狗似的抖了抖身子，回到自己的座位上。

===Original===
“I’ll take over the office of President Legrange,” Chauvelin said calmly, “and preside over the Tribunal until his return.”
===Chinese===
「我将接手勒格朗热庭长的职务，」肖维兰平静地说，「在他回来之前主持革命法庭。」

===Original===
“Then I,” Danou put in boldly, “had best take over the work of the Public Prosecutor.”
===Chinese===
「那么我，」达努斗胆插话，「最好就由我来接手公诉人的差事。」

===Original===
“Impossible, citizen,” Chauvelin rejoined firmly; “I must have a lawyer for that office.”
===Chinese===
「办不到，公民。」肖维兰斩钉截铁地答道，「那个职位，我非得用一位律师不可。」

===Original===
“But﻿—”
===Chinese===
「可是——」

===Original===
“You do not seem to have listened very carefully, Citizen Danou,” Chauvelin broke in quietly, “to my reading of the decree, or you would remember that it is for the representative on special mission to appoint a substitute, in case of absence on the part of a second important officer of State.”
===Chinese===
「你方才似乎没有好好听我念这道法令，达努公民，」肖维兰不动声色地打断他，「要不然你该记得：万一有第二位国家重要官员离岗旷职，代理之人须由特派代表来委派。」

===Original===
“And whom do you propose to appoint, citizen representative?” Pochart inquired with a sneer.
===Chinese===
「那么您打算委派谁呢，公民代表？」波夏尔带着讥诮探问。

===Original===
“I will let you know my decision as to that tomorrow.”
===Chinese===
「这件事，明天我自会把我的决定告知诸位。」

===Original===
“The sooner the better, citizen representative,” Danou concluded unctuously. “Remember that it is my colleague and I of the 137th section of the Committee of Public Safety who will have to collect the evidence against the accused and place it before the Public Prosecutor whom you will appoint. That is a duty from which only the Central Committee can relieve us. There are one hundred and sixty prisoners,” he went on slowly, “arrested under the Law of the Suspect. Some of them gravely accused, and by witnesses too.”
===Chinese===
「越快越好，公民代表。」达努油滑甜腻地收了话。「请您记着：将来去搜罗被告的罪证、再呈到您所要委派的那位公诉人面前的，是我和我的同僚，公安委员会第137区的人。这项职责，唯有中央公安委员会才能把我们豁免。有一百六十名囚犯，」他慢吞吞地接着说，「是依嫌疑犯法令拿下的。其中一些罪名重得很，而且还有人证哩。」

===Original===
“I am well aware of that, Citizen Danou,” Chauvelin replied calmly. Not by the quiver of an eyelid did he betray the fact that the shaft had gone home. With a perfectly steady hand he collected his papers and placed a weight upon them. After which he dismissed the others with a curt nod.
===Chinese===
「这个我心里一清二楚，达努公民。」肖维兰平静地答道。那一箭分明已射中要害，可他连眼皮也不曾颤动一下，没有露出半点形迹。他一手稳稳当当收拾起文件，又在上头压了一件镇纸。说罢，他简慢地点了点头，把两位打发出去。

===Original===
“Your pardon, citizens,” he said, “I have still work to do. You too doubtless. I shall require your attendance here tomorrow at this same hour.”
===Chinese===
「恕我失陪，二位公民。」他说，「我还有公事要办。二位想必也有。明天还是这个时辰，我要两位再到这儿来。」

===Original===
When the door had finally closed behind the two men, the mask fell from Chauvelin’s face. Leaning his elbow on the table, he buried his burning head in his hands; a heartrending groan broke from his parched lips, his eyes felt as if seared with glowing charcoal. Ah! if he had not only forgotten these years past how to pray, what fervent orisons would he not have sent heavenwards at this hour. Help! where could he find help out of this web which his enemies had woven round him? How he hated them! longed to smite them before they had time to accomplish their fell purpose. They had determined on striking at Fleurette. Out of revenge or hatred, or was it fear? they had determined on striking at him, Chauvelin, through this being whom he loved beyond everything in the world. And he who had been one of the first protagonists of hatred and revenge and mutual distrust, he who had the will and the power, seemed so inextricably enmeshed that he could do nothing to save her. Fight? he would fight, inch by inch, step by step. Fight to save his Fleurette. Fight while he had breath in his body; fight until he fell vanquished by her side. For if he failed he would not let her die alone. He could not think of her being dragged through the streets in that awful tumbril which he himself had so often helped to fill; could not﻿—heavens above no﻿—could not think of her mounting the steps of the guillotine, which so many innocent feet had mounted at his bidding. Retribution! It had come nearer, more inexorable now! Death by his Fleurette’s side seemed the only possible issue. And even as he sat there alone, in that room wherein the hatred of his fellow-men seemed still to linger like noisome ghosts, a pale ray of sunlight found its way through the closed window and played upon the myriads and myriads of dust atoms that hovered in the air. Chauvelin’s hands dropped down upon the table. His weary eyes rested vacantly upon that shaft of dust-laden light. And inside its very heart he saw a face, smiling and debonair, with lazy eyes and smiling lips mocking him in his grief. It was a vision, gone as soon as seen, but vivid enough during that one brief second to bring a savage curse upon the lonely man’s lips. His claw-like hand clenched so tightly that the knuckles shone like polished ivory.
===Chinese===
等那两个人终于在门后消失，面具便从肖维兰的脸上落了下来。他把手肘支在桌上，将滚烫的脑袋埋进双手之中；一声撕心的呻吟从他干裂的唇间迸出，两眼只觉得像被烧红的炭火烙过一般。啊！这些年来，他若不是把怎样祈祷都忘了个干净，此刻该要向上天发出多么热切的祷告啊。救一救他！在这张仇人们在他周身织就的罗网里，他还能到哪里寻出一线援手？他多么恨他们！恨不得趁他们还来不及成就那狠毒的图谋，就先把他们击倒。他们已经打定主意要向弗勒蕾特下手。是出于报复，是仇恨，还是恐惧？他们打定了主意，要借这个他爱之胜过世间一切的人，来击中他肖维兰本人。而他——这个当年率先登台的仇恨、报复与彼此猜忌的主角之一，这个有意志也有权柄的人——如今竟被缠得这般脱身无路，连救她一救都无能为力。搏斗么？他要搏斗，一寸一寸地，一步一步地。为救他的弗勒蕾特而搏斗。一息尚存便搏斗下去；直到力屈战败、倒在她身旁为止。因为一旦失败，他也决不肯让她独自去死。他不忍想见她被拖着走过一条条街道，塞进那辆他亲手帮着装满过多少回的可怕的死囚车；不忍——皇天在上，不忍啊——不忍想见她一步步登上断头台的台阶；那台阶，多少双无辜的脚曾奉他之命登上去过。报应！它如今逼得更近了，愈发无可通融了！死在他弗勒蕾特的身旁，似乎成了仅剩的一条出路。就在他独自枯坐的当口，在那间世人的仇恨仿佛还似恶鬼一般阴魂不散的屋子里，一线惨淡的日光从紧闭的窗子里透进来，照在悬浮半空的千千万万粒尘埃上。肖维兰的双手颓然垂落在桌上。他一双倦眼怔怔地停在那道浮尘飞舞的光柱上。而在那光柱的正中心，他看见一张脸，笑吟吟、乐陶陶，一双慵懒的眼睛、两片含笑的嘴唇，正在他的悲苦里讥诮他。那是一个幻象，一见即逝；可就在那短短一瞬里，它鲜活得分明，逼得这个孤独的人唇边迸出一声狠毒的咒骂。他那只爪子似的手攥得死紧，指节都泛出了打磨过的象牙一般的光。

===Original===
“My evil genius!” he muttered through his teeth. “Had I succeeded in bringing you down, had I seen that mocking head fall under the guillotine, this devastating misery would never have come upon me. If only I could be even with you. I would die happier﻿—even now.”
===Chinese===
「我的魔星！」他从牙缝里挤出这句话。「当初我若真把你扳倒了，若亲眼见你那颗讥诮人的脑袋滚落在断头台下，这场灭顶的苦楚就永远不会落到我的头上。但愿我能同你算清这笔账。那样我便是死，也死得痛快些——哪怕就在此刻。」

## XXVIII / 第二十八章

===Original===
Ever afterwards to Chauvelin, it seemed as if the Scarlet Pimpernel had heard his challenge, and come in response to his thoughts: for hardly had a couple of days gone before the first rumour reached him of the nearness of his arch-enemy. Twenty-four hours later the hue and cry was all over Orange after a gang of English spies who, it was averred, made it their business to cheat Madame la Guillotine out of her dues.

===Chinese===
在此后的岁月里，在肖维兰看来，事情始终仿佛是猩红繁笺花听见了他的挑战，应着他的心念赶了来：因为前后不出两天，他的宿敌已然近在咫尺的头一阵风声，便传到了他的耳中。二十四小时之后，缉拿的喧嚣已闹遍整个奥朗日——追捕一伙英国奸细，据称这伙人干的营生，就是专叫断头夫人（Madame la Guillotine）收不着她应得的那一份。

===Original===
Citizen Pochart brought Representative Chauvelin the news which already was all over the town, namely that Architect Caristie and his family, consisting of his wife and the small son now aged ten, who was destined one day to become one of Orange’s most distinguished citizens, had unaccountably disappeared from their tumbledown lodgings in the Rue de la République, where they had taken refuge after their house had been requisitioned by the State and turned into a prison-house.

===Chinese===
公民波夏尔（Pochart）给肖维兰代表带来了那桩早已传遍全城的新闻：建筑师卡里斯蒂（Caristie）携家眷——太太，外加那个如今已十岁、注定有朝一日要成为奥朗日最杰出公民之一的小儿子——已经从共和国街（Rue de la République）的破旧寓所里莫名其妙地不见了踪影；自打自家的宅子被国家征用、改成拘押所之后，他们便一直躲在那处寓所里安身。

===Original===
For some time the Sectional Members of the Committee of Public Safety, Citizens Pochart and Danou, ardent patriots, had had their eyes on the Caristie family. Aristos, what? Architect Caristie had designed and built houses in the past for tyrants and ci-devants. The arrest of the entire family had been decided on. It was to have taken place that very evening. Orders to that effect were out, their place of incarceration fixed in the very house where they had once sat in luxury, whilst patriots had starved outside their gates.

===Chinese===
有好一阵子，公安委员会的区委员们——公民波夏尔和公民达努（Danou），两位热忱的爱国志士——早就把眼睛盯在了卡里斯蒂一家身上。贵族佬，还用说吗？建筑师卡里斯蒂从前专为暴君和旧贵族（ci-devant）设计建造宅第。满门逮捕的事已经定了案，原定就在当天晚上动手。相应的命令已经发出，关押的处所也已定妥，恰恰便在那一座宅子里——想当年他们在里头锦衣玉食，爱国志士们却在大门外挨饿。

===Original===
And suddenly the news had spread like wildfire through the town that Architect Caristie, his wife and son had disappeared. Disappeared? Where? asked every patriot. But no one knew. One evening they had still been seen, as usual, taking walking exercise on the riverbank, and the next day when the soldiers of the revolutionary army presented themselves at the door of their lodgings in the Rue de la République and demanded admittance, lo! they received no answer: the lodgings were deserted, the birds had flown from their nests. Nor could the guard at any of the gates of the city throw light upon this mysterious occurrence. No one had passed the gates without duly authenticated passes. Pochart was at his wits’ end and asked counsel of Representative Chauvelin. What was to be done in face of this mystery? Exercise strict supervision at the gates, Chauvelin advised. All passes in future to be signed by himself as well as by the Sectional Members of the Committee of Public Safety.

===Chinese===
突然之间，建筑师卡里斯蒂携妻儿失踪的消息像野火一般传遍了全城。失踪？失到哪儿去了？每一个爱国志士都这样问。可是谁也不知道。头一天傍晚，还有人照旧瞧见他们在河岸边散步消食；第二天，革命军的士兵们来到他们共和国街寓所门前要求进屋，瞧！无人应门：寓所里人去楼空，鸟儿早已飞离了巢穴。城里各处城门的卫兵，也没一个能对这桩蹊跷事说出个所以然来。凡出城的，没有一个不是凭着一应查验妥当的通行证过去的。波夏尔一筹莫展，只得向肖维兰代表讨个主意。面对这个谜团，该怎么办？肖维兰出的主意是：在各城门厉行督察。今后凡通行证，一律除公安委员会区委员签署外，还须经他本人一并签署。

===Original===
The news of the presence of the Scarlet Pimpernel in Orange had acted upon his nerves like a whiplash. Fate, it seemed, was hitting at him from every side: and he felt like a fighter who has been downed once, twice, and then suddenly feels the strength of giants in his blood; the agility of a cat spurring him to a new and stupendous effort. In a vague, fatalistic kind of way the safety of Fleurette and the destruction of the Scarlet Pimpernel appeared to him as inextricably involved. If he allowed his arch-enemy to baffle him now and here, in this city, then Fleurette was doomed and he himself must perish.

===Chinese===
猩红繁笺花现身奥朗日的消息，像一记鞭子抽在他的神经上。命运仿佛正从四面八方朝他打来：他觉得自己像个拳手，被击倒过一回、两回，忽然间浑身血液里涌起巨人的膂力，猫一般的敏捷催逼着他再做一番惊天动地的努力。弗勒蕾特的平安和猩红繁笺花的毁灭，在他心里以一种模糊而宿命般的方式纠缠在一起，解也解不开。倘若他听任宿敌此时此地、就在这座城里把他挫败，那么弗勒蕾特便注定没命，他自己也非灭亡不可。

===Original===
This was the immediate state of mind into which the news of the nearness of the Scarlet Pimpernel had thrown him. A wild desire to link the destruction of his enemy with the safety of his child, to deserve so well of the State, in fact, that the life of Fleurette would be ceded to him as a reward. A drowning man will catch at a straw, and so did Chauvelin catch at this hope, cling to it, turn the thought over and over in his mind. With feverish activity then he spurred those about him into additional vigilance, combated that superstitious terror with which every official these days regarded the gang of English spies and their mysterious chief. He brought to every man’s notice the handsome reward offered by the Revolutionary Government for the capture of the Scarlet Pimpernel, described the Englishman’s appearance, his methods, his motives, worked up every man in Orange, aye, and every woman too, into a state of enthusiasm for the possible capture of this inveterate and daring enemy of France.

===Chinese===
猩红繁笺花逼近的消息，当即将他抛入的，便是这样一副心境：一种狂热的欲望——把仇敌的毁灭同孩子的平安拴在一处；说得再实一点，就是为国家立下大大的功劳，好叫弗勒蕾特的性命当作犒赏发还给他。溺水的人连一根稻草也要抓；肖维兰也正是这样抓住了这个希望，牢牢攥住，把这个念头在心里翻来覆去地掂量。于是他以一股热病似的劲头，鞭策左右的人加倍警戒，全力抗击时下每位官员看待英国奸细团伙及其神秘头目的那种迷信般的恐惧。他把革命政府为缉拿猩红繁笺花而悬出的那笔可观赏金，摆到每个人的眼前，又把那英国人的相貌、手段、动机一一描绘，把奥朗日的每一个男人——可不，连每一个女人也在内——都鼓动得群情亢奋，一心巴望着能捉住这个积仇既久、胆大包天的法兰西之敌。

===Original===
But this particular frame of mind was not destined to endure. Soon memory got to work, recalled unpleasant moments in Calais, Boulogne, in Paris, in Nantes. What if here too, in Orange, the Scarlet Pimpernel should triumph and he Chauvelin once more be forced to eat the bread of humiliation? What if baffled once more, he should lose, at one terrible swoop, both his revenge and his last hope of saving Fleurette? And then it was that first the insidious, the stupendous thought penetrated his brain. Was it Satan himself who had whispered it into his ear? or some army of mocking imps intent upon torturing him to madness? But heavens above, what a thought! The Scarlet Pimpernel and Fleurette! Was that going to be the solution of this terrible impasse? The thought feverishly driven back at first, returned more insistent. Why not? And then again, why not? A young girl, sweet, pretty, innocent, was she not one to arouse those instincts of chivalry which Chauvelin had hitherto affected to despise?

===Chinese===
可是这一种心境注定持久不了。记忆很快就开了工，唤回了加来、布洛涅（Boulogne）、巴黎、南特（Nantes）那些不痛快的旧事。倘若在这里，在奥朗日，猩红繁笺花又一次得胜，而他肖维兰又得再吞一回屈辱的面包，那可怎么好？倘若再一次遭挫，他在可怕的一击之下就要同时输个精光——既赔上他的复仇，又赔上他营救弗勒蕾特的最后一线指望，那可怎么好？就在这时，头一个钻进他脑子里的，便是那个阴险的、骇人的念头。是撒旦亲自把它凑在他耳边说的？还是一小队存心把他折磨到发疯的讥诮小鬼？老天爷在上，这算个什么念头！猩红繁笺花和弗勒蕾特！难道这竟要成为这个可怕僵局的解法么？这个念头起初被他狠命驱开，卷土重来时却愈发咄咄逼人。为什么不呢？接着又问一遍，为什么不呢？一个年轻姑娘，甜美、秀丽、天真无邪，难道不正是能唤起骑士侠义本能的人物么——而这侠义本能，肖维兰向来只是装作鄙夷罢了。

===Original===
What a possibility! Heavens above, what a possibility! His very senses reeled now at the thought. But he allowed his mind to dwell upon it, to weigh its possibilities: to familiarize itself more and more with it. At first it had seemed like madness, but no longer now! His Fleurette! Already Amédé Colombe was far away, under the protection of the Scarlet Pimpernel, what more likely than that﻿—No! no! it could not be! His daughter! His, Chauvelin’s! And in a swift vision he saw himself luring Marguerite Blakeney, the beloved and beautiful wife of the Scarlet Pimpernel to her death, holding her as hostage, threatening her, torturing her. His enemy’s wife! What agonies she had endured at his hands! And now Fleurette! Would not the Scarlet Pimpernel, triumphant and revengeful, gloat over her death, rather than raise a finger to save her life? Would he not gaze with joy on the misery endured by his bitterest foe?

===Chinese===
多么大的一个可能啊！老天爷在上，多么大的一个可能！如今光是想上一想，便叫他神魂摇荡。可他偏偏放任心思在这上头流连，掂量它的种种可能，同它一点一点地厮熟起来。起初它还像是一句疯话，可如今再也不像了！他的弗勒蕾特！阿梅代·科隆布早已走远，正在猩红繁笺花的庇护之下，还有什么比这更顺理成章的呢——不！不！断乎不能！他的女儿！他的，肖维兰亲生的女儿！一霎眼的幻象里，他看见自己正把玛格丽特·布莱克尼（Marguerite Blakeney）——猩红繁笺花那位受尽爱慕的美丽妻子——诱往死地，扣作人质，威吓她，折磨她。他仇敌的妻子！她在他手里忍受过怎样的苦刑！而如今，是弗勒蕾特！得胜而挟着私怨的猩红繁笺花，眼看她死去难道不会幸灾乐祸，倒肯抬一抬手指救她的性命？他难道不会欢欢喜喜地观赏他不共戴天的仇敌所忍受的煎熬？

===Original===
And then once more torturing thoughts would assail him: torturing fears and torturing hopes, hopes? Yes, hopes! “Why should you not hope, man?” whispered an insidious demon in his ear: “the Scarlet Pimpernel does not know, cannot know that Fleurette is your daughter; the daughter of his enemy Armand Chauvelin. To him she is just the sweet, pretty, innocent victim of a system of government which he hates and which he combats. Then why not hope?” And the floating, racking visions of Juliette Marny, and Yvonne de Kernogan, of the Abbé Foucquet and Madeleine Lanoy, would once more haunt the daydreams of this man already steeped in misery, and hope insidious, ever-living hope, would whisper in its turn: “To that long list of innocents snatched from prison and from death by the insolent adventurer whom you hate, why should not the name of Fleurette be added? Fleurette of unknown parentage, just a sweet girl dwelling at Lou Mas, with old Louise and a father known as Armand? Why not?”

===Chinese===
接着，煎熬人的念头便又来袭击他：煎熬人的恐惧，还有煎熬人的希望。希望？不错，正是希望！「我说你呀，为什么就不该指望呢？」一个阴险的魔鬼凑在他耳边低语，「猩红繁笺花并不知道，也不可能知道弗勒蕾特是你的女儿，是他仇人阿尔芒·肖维兰的女儿。在他看来，她不过是个甜美、秀丽、天真无邪的牺牲品，牺牲于那套他既憎恨又抗击的统治之下。那么为什么不该指望呢？」于是，朱丽叶特·马尼（Juliette Marny）、伊冯娜·德·克尔诺甘（Yvonne de Kernogan）、富凯神父（Abbé Foucquet）和玛德琳·拉努瓦（Madeleine Lanoy）那些飘忽萦回的身影，便又来出没于这个早已浸透苦水的人的白日梦；而希望——阴险的、永远不死的希望——也轮到它凑上前低语：「你所恨的那个目中无人的冒险家，从牢狱和死亡手里抢出了一长串无辜者，在这份名单上，为什么就不该添上弗勒蕾特的名字？出身不明的弗勒蕾特，不过是个住在庐庄（Lou Mas）的甜美姑娘，身边伴着老路易丝和一位人称阿尔芒的父亲。为什么不呢？」

===Original===
And day after day, whilst presiding, self-appointed over a tribunal of infamy, Chauvelin’s mind became more and more familiarized with the vision of his Fleurette snatched out of the jaws of death by the man with the lazy eyes and the mocking lips, the demmed, elusive Pimpernel of his daydreams and his sleepless nights.

===Chinese===
就这样过了一天又一天：他自封为主，坐在一座恶名昭彰的法庭上主持审判，与此同时，他心里那个幻象也愈来愈厮熟——他的弗勒蕾特，被那个眼睛慵懒、嘴角含讥的男人从死亡的巨口中夺了回来，正是他白日梦里、无眠长夜中那个「该死的」神出鬼没的繁笺花。

## XXIX / 第二十九章

===Original===
Meanwhile in Architect Caristie’s house, transformed for the necessities of the State into a prison, the old routine is now restored. Daily, once more, an hour before sunset, the captain of the guard with his half-dozen men, enters the courtyard, and in a loud voice reads the names that appear upon his roll-call. They are the names of those who on the morrow are summoned before the Revolutionary Tribunal, there to answer the charges that are trumped up against them by the venal spies, who make their living out of the blood of innocent men and women and children.

===Chinese===
与此同时，在建筑师卡里斯蒂（Caristie）的宅子里——这宅子已因国家的需要改成了监狱——旧日的规程如今又恢复了。每天，又是日落前一个钟头，卫兵上尉带着他那六名部下走进院子，高声宣读名册上的名字。那是明天就要被传到革命法庭上去的人的名字，他们要在那里应对强加在自己头上的罪状——那些罪状由贪财的密探们凭空罗织，而这班密探，正是拿无辜男女和孩童的血来养活自己的。

===Original===
Impossible to refute those charges, since the law has decreed that it is a crime to be merely suspected of treason against the State. Foucquier-Tinville, the great Public Prosecutor in Paris, no longer troubles, it seems, to prepare fresh indictments against every accused in turn. He has a printed formula of accusation, with just the name left in blank, presently to be filled in as convenience arises. Therefore in other greater and lesser cities of France, patriots desirous of showing their zeal, can do no better than emulate the example set by so great a man. Local sections of the Committee of Public Safety prepare the indictments﻿—set formulae with the names left in blank. These they pass on to the Public Prosecutor who mumbles as he reads them before the Tribunal with the President sitting up on the dais, and the accused﻿—names left in blank﻿—brought up to the bar, not allowed to say a word in their own justification, nor to question the witnesses brought up to testify against them.

===Chinese===
这些罪状无从驳斥，因为法律已然明定：单是被怀疑叛国，本身便已是罪。巴黎那位大公诉人富基耶-坦维尔（Foucquier-Tinville），看来已经犯不上再为一个个被告逐一起草新诉状了。他手头有一种印好的控罪格式，单单空着姓名一项，到时候瞧着方便填上便是。于是，在法国其他大大小小的城市里，急于表露热忱的爱国志士们，最有样学样，效法这位大人物立下的榜样。公安委员会下属各区包办诉状——一式印好的格式，空着姓名。诉状转到公诉人手里，由他在法庭上含混不清地念一通：庭长高踞台上，被告——空着姓名的被告——被押到栏前，不许为自己辩白一个字，也不许盘问被带上来指证他们的证人。

===Original===
Abandon all hope then, ye whose names are upon that Roll-call! tomorrow the Tribunal, the next day the guillotine! And once again now, day after day, the captain of the guard comes to the house, late of Architect Caristie, and reads; and at all the windows that overlook the courtyard heads appear, men, women and little children﻿—clutching the bars and listening. Listening for their own name or that of one who is dear. Sighing with relief if neither has been called, or with resignation if tomorrow is destined to bring this miserable existence to an end.

===Chinese===
那么，名字上了那点名册的人，就此抛开一切希望吧！明天是法庭，后天便是断头台！而如今又再一次，日复一日，卫兵上尉来到这座原属建筑师卡里斯蒂的宅子里宣读；俯临院子的每一扇窗口都探出人头，男人、女人和小孩子——攥着栏杆，听着。听自己的名字，或某个亲爱之人的名字。两样都没叫到，便宽慰地舒一口气；倘若明天注定要了结这凄惨的一生，便认命地叹一口气。

===Original===
And day after day Chauvelin presides over this tribunal of infamy. Self-appointed, he sits upon the dais and sees before him pass a daily file of doomed and dying. Sometimes ten, sometimes as many as twenty in a day, and still the prisons are full﻿—fresh arrests make up for those whom the guillotine has claimed. Acquittals are rare, for moderation now has become a crime. Danton﻿—aye! even Danton, the lion, has perished, he who ordered the September massacres, he who thundered forth from the tribune, “Liberty! Fraternity! Equality! or Death!” he has perished because he became guilty of this crime of moderation. The glorious revolution has no use for two such of its products as Danton and Robespierre﻿—for the reality of the one and the canting hypocrisy of the other: so Danton it was who perished. “It is right,” he had dared to say once, “to repress the Royalists: but we should not confound the innocent with the guilty!”

===Chinese===
日复一日，肖维兰主持着这丧尽廉耻的法庭。他自封其位，高踞台上，眼看一队队定了死罪、行将就戮的人从面前鱼贯而过。有时一天十个，有时一天多至二十个，监狱却依旧人满——新抓的人补上被断头台索去性命的那些。开释是稀罕事，因为如今宽和本身已成了一种罪。丹东——是啊！连丹东，那头雄狮，也覆灭了；正是他下令制造了九月大屠杀，正是他在讲坛上雷鸣般吼出「自由！博爱！平等！否则死！」——他覆灭了，因为他犯下了宽和这条罪。光荣的革命用不着同时留着丹东和罗伯斯庇尔这样两件造物——一件货真价实，一件假仁假义——于是覆灭的便轮到了丹东。「镇压保王党人是对的，」他有一回竟敢说，「可咱们不该把无辜者同有罪者搅在一处！」

===Original===
“And who told thee,” Robespierre retorted, sea-green with hatred, “that one single innocent has perished by our hand?”

===Chinese===
「那么又是谁告诉你，」罗伯斯庇尔反唇相讥，恨得脸色铁青，「说有一个无辜者死在了咱们手里？」

===Original===
And because Danton had dared to raise his voice in the cause of the innocent, Danton had perished.

===Chinese===
而正因为丹东胆敢为无辜者仗义执言，丹东覆灭了。

===Original===
What chance then has Chauvelin to defend his Fleurette? His power is great. He can make or unmake your Pocharts and your Danous, your President Legrange or Public Prosecutor Isnard, but he cannot accord special privileges in prison for his own daughter. He cannot see her in private, comfort her, warn her if need be, tell her not to be afraid for Bibi chéri is there, on the watch, ready to protect her with his body, to stand by her in the last hour. He cannot. Pochart and Danou are on the watch. “We must not confound the innocent with the guilty:” Danton had dared to say. And for this he had perished: and though he perished, could not save one single innocent.

===Chinese===
那么，肖维兰还有什么机会替他的弗勒蕾特辩护呢？他的权势固然大。你们那些个波夏尔、达努之流，你们的勒格朗热庭长、公诉人伊斯纳尔，他都能扶起来，也能掀下去；可他没法子给自己的亲生女儿在狱里行半点特殊方便。他不能单独见她，不能宽慰她，不能在必要时提醒她，告诉她不必害怕，因为亲爱的比比（Bibi chéri）就在那儿守着，随时准备拿身体护她，在最后时辰陪着她。他不能。波夏尔和达努正盯梢呢。「咱们不该把无辜者同有罪者搅在一处」——丹东当年敢说这句话；他为这句话覆灭了；而他纵然覆灭，也没能救下一个无辜者。

===Original===
And all evening, after the sittings of the Tribunal are over, and ten﻿—or mayhap fifteen or twenty﻿—condemned to the guillotine, Chauvelin like a pale, thin ghost haunts the purlieus of Architect Caristie’s house. On pretext of his office he enters the courtyard with the captain of the guard and looks up at the windows to see if *she* is there. Once he saw her: just her little face peeping behind the opulent shoulders of one Claire de Châtelard, the best noted strumpet in Orange. The woman had one arm round Fleurette’s waist and when the captain of the guard read out the name of Claire known as Châtelard upon his list, Fleurette threw her arms round her, and laid her head upon the trollop’s breast.

===Chinese===
每到傍晚，法庭散了庭，十个——兴许十五个、二十个——人被判上断头台之后，肖维兰便像个苍白瘦削的鬼魂，在建筑师卡里斯蒂宅子的左近出没。他假借职务之名，跟着卫兵上尉走进院子，抬头仰望那些窗口，看*她*在不在。有一回他看见了她：只看见那张小脸，从克莱尔·德·沙特拉尔（Claire de Châtelard）丰腴的肩膀后面探出来——奥朗日最出名的荡妇。那女人一条胳膊搂着弗勒蕾特的腰；当卫兵上尉在名单上念到「人称沙特拉尔的克莱尔」这个名字时，弗勒蕾特张开双臂搂住她，把头伏在那荡妇的胸口。

===Original===
Chauvelin turned away from the spectacle with a groan, and all night he lay awake thinking of his sweet flower laying her head upon the breast of a Claire de Châtelard.

===Chinese===
肖维兰对着这幅光景呻吟一声，掉过头去；整夜整夜，他睁眼躺着，想着他的小花儿把头靠在克莱尔·德·沙特拉尔的胸口上。

===Original===
Yet Claire de Châtelard bore herself bravely before him the next day, and when, on the day after that, he watched her from the window of the Hôtel de Ville mounting the steps of the guillotine, saw her standing there, superb and defiant with a coarse jest upon her sensual lips, he gloated over the thought that his Fleurette would no longer pillow her innocent head upon that breast. He tried to picture her, grieving for this friend, the propinquity, the squalor of that house of detention, from which there was but one egress, that egress the gate of Death. Claire de Châtelard today﻿—Fleurette when? Every day the indictments are sent up to him for examination, the printed forms of accusation with the names left in blank, to be filled in as convenience demands: and every day a list of ten, perhaps fifteen names are sent along with these printed forms, and it is his business to direct the Public Prosecutor, a man of his own choosing, which of these names are to be inserted in the blank spaces, on the forms of accusation. Up to now he has been able to keep Fleurette’s name out, but it has been sent up to him on two consecutive days. The fight then was getting at close quarters. Pochart and Danou were pressing him, showing their teeth like snarling dogs ready to spring. And time was hurrying on. Time would presently bring back President Legrange and Prosecutor Isnard from Paris, time would inevitably bring to light his machinations for keeping those two men out of the way. Aye! time was hurrying on, and Fleurette’s name had twice appeared upon the list.

===Chinese===
然而克莱尔·德·沙特拉尔第二天在他面前表现得十分勇敢；再过一天，他从市政厅（Hôtel de Ville）的窗口望见她登上断头台的台阶，站在那儿，气概轩昂，桀骜不驯，两片丰润的唇上挂着一记粗鄙的笑谑，他不禁对着这个念头暗自得意：他的弗勒蕾特再也不会把那颗无辜的头颅枕在那副胸口上了。他试着去想象她的模样：为这位朋友哀伤，困在拘押所那人挨人的污秽里——那地方只有一个出口，出口便是死亡之门。今天是克莱尔·德·沙特拉尔——弗勒蕾特是哪一天？每天都有诉状呈送给他核阅，那些印好的控罪格式空着姓名，视需要填上：每天还有一份十个、或许十五个名字的单子，随这些印好的格式一道送来，而他的职分，便是指示公诉人——一个由他亲自拣选的人——哪些名字该填进控罪格式的空栏里。直到如今，他一直能把弗勒蕾特的名字压在外面，可连着两天，这名字都被呈送到他面前。这一仗，于是短兵相接了。波夏尔和达努正在逼他，像两条龇牙咧嘴、蓄势欲扑的恶狗。而时间在飞跑。时间转眼就要把勒格朗热庭长和公诉人伊斯纳尔从巴黎带回来，时间也必然会把他为支开这两个人而使的种种手腕掀在光天化日之下。是啊！时间在飞跑，而弗勒蕾特的名字已经两回上了名单。

===Original===
And for the past three days not a word in the town about the English spies. After Architect Caristie and his family, it had been the widow Colmars and her daughter, and then General Paulieu and his family. Disappeared as if the earth had swallowed them up. Always traitors and aristos whose arrest was imminent, whose subsequent condemnation certain. But after that, three days’ respite: the Scarlet Pimpernel and his gang seemed to have disappeared in their turn. The hopes which insidious demons had whispered in Chauvelin’s ears were once more merged in a sea of despair. He derided himself for these hopes, lashed himself into a state of fury against himself for having allowed his mind to dwell upon them.

===Chinese===
而这三天来，城里关于英国奸细，连一个字也没有。继建筑师卡里斯蒂和他全家之后，先后是科尔马尔寡妇（Colmars）和她的女儿，再是波利厄将军（Paulieu）和他的全家。消失得仿佛大地把他们吞了进去。清一色全是那种抓捕在即、定罪无疑的叛徒和贵族佬。可那之后，歇了三天：猩红繁笺花和他的一伙，似乎轮到他们销声匿迹了。那班阴险的魔鬼在肖维兰耳边嗫嗫私语过的种种希望，重又沉没在绝望的大海里。他为自己的这些希望自嘲，为自己竟容心思在这些希望上流连不去，把自己气恼得五内如焚。

===Original===
One scheme after another now did he devise and then reject. He would defy his enemies, the jury, the populace: loudly denounce the witnesses against Fleurette as liars and perjurers, pronounce her acquittal in the face of all opposition. Had he not made a point day after day of pronouncing acquittal on one or the other of the accused? just to test his power﻿—to see how his enemies would behave? And he saw them lying low. Sneering. Whispering. Ogling him and laughing. They knew! They saw behind his schemes and his hopes. They reserved their counterattack. They could afford to wait, whilst he could not.

===Chinese===
如今他一个方案接着一个方案地设想，又一一推翻。他要向他的仇敌、向陪审团、向民众挑战：高声斥责指证弗勒蕾特的证人全是一伙撒谎的伪证犯，顶着一切反对宣判她无罪。他不是日复一日存心对这样或那样的被告宣判无罪吗？——就为试试自己的分量——看看仇敌们会有什么举动？他看见他们按兵不动。冷笑。咬耳朵。冲他挤眉弄眼，嗤嗤地笑。他们知道！他们看穿了他的计谋和指望。他们把反扑留着后手。他们等得起，他可等不起。

===Original===
If only Fleurette bore herself well: did not allow herself to be carried away with admissions or inconsidered words, out of sentiment for that fool Amédé Colombe. Chauvelin longed to see her, if only to impress this one thing upon her; to say nothing. To admit nothing. To hold her tongue and to trust chéri Bibi. If only she did that, he felt that he might save her yet. And obsessed by this idea, devoured with the desire to convey this message to her, without compromising her or giving yet another advantage to his enemies, Chauvelin at evening would wander like a restless ghost through the city.

===Chinese===
但愿弗勒蕾特自己举止得体：莫因对那个傻子阿梅代·科隆布的情分，就被套出什么招供或有欠斟酌的话来。肖维兰渴望见她一面，哪怕只为把这一件事叮嘱进她心里：什么也别说。什么也别认。守住舌头，信赖亲爱的比比。只要她做到这一步，他觉得兴许还救得了她。他被这个念头缠住了，又被给它捎话给她的欲望煎熬着——既要不连累她，又不再给仇敌添一分便宜——于是每到晚间，肖维兰便像个不得安生的鬼魂，在城里游荡。

===Original===
That afternoon after he watched Claire de Châtelard mount the steps of the guillotine, a joke upon her lips, this restlessness became exquisite torture, and racked with tumultuous thoughts, wrapped in a black mantle, he sallied forth into the streets. It was now early in June: nearly three weeks since that last carefree day, Fleurette’s eighteenth birthday, spent with her over at Lou Mas, when the scent of almond blossom had been in the air and the nightingale had sung in the old walnut-tree. The day had been sunless and chilly, after sunset the rain began to fall. But rain and weather held no terrors for Chauvelin in his present mood. Holding his mantle tightly round his shoulders and pulling his hat down over his eyes, he wandered aimlessly through the streets, over the river and back again, down unpaved streets and lonely lanes, now and then sitting down to rest in some obscure little outlying café, where no one knew or heeded him, and then starting off again on his restless course. But always drifting back instinctively to the purlieus of Architect Caristie’s house.

===Chinese===
那天下午，他眼看着克莱尔·德·沙特拉尔唇边挂着一记玩笑，登上了断头台的台阶；这之后，那份坐立不安便成了难熬的酷刑。他被纷乱的心思折磨着，裹一件黑斗篷，出门上了街。这时节是六月初：距那最后一个无忧无虑的日子——弗勒蕾特的十八岁生日，他陪她在庐庄（Lou Mas）度过——差不多已经过去三个星期；那一天，空气里浮动着杏花的芳香，老胡桃树上夜莺啼唱。这一天没有太阳，天气冷峭，日落之后落起雨来。可在眼下这心境里，风雨吓不倒肖维兰。他把斗篷在肩头裹紧，把帽子往眼睛上一压，漫无目的地在街上游荡，过了河又折回来，走进没有铺石的路和冷清的巷子，不时在城郊哪家不起眼的小咖啡馆里坐下歇脚——那儿没有人认得他，也没有人理会他——然后又踏上他那不得安生的路。可他总是身不由己，又飘回建筑师卡里斯蒂宅子的左近。

===Original===
Almost opposite to it there was a small café: no one sitting outside because of the rain, but the interior lighted up, and sounds of merriment proceeding from within. Chauvelin thought of going inside, feeling that if he sat down there close to the window, he could watch the walls behind which lived and suffered his little Fleurette. He did not dare go in for fear of being recognized. He was just debating within himself whether he would go or stay, when he saw a man come out of the house of Architect Caristie, cross over to the café, then disappear behind its creaking door. A scavenger, no doubt, ragged and dirty﻿—not a warder, he was too ill-clad for that﻿—just a scavenger﻿—but perhaps he had seen Fleurette. The thought fascinated Chauvelin. His mind clung to it: turned it over and over. The thought that here was a man who perhaps had seen Fleurette within the last few minutes, had swept corridor or staircase when she was passing by. And with that thought there was still the burning desire to send her a message, to tell her to be brave and trust in Bibi, but above all, oh! above all, not to be led into making any admission about those valuables belonging to Madame de Frontenac, or about her association with Amédé Colombe.

===Chinese===
宅子差不多正对着有一家小咖啡馆：因为下雨，外面没人坐，里头却亮着灯，传出阵阵欢声。肖维兰想到里头去，觉得若是挨着窗子坐下，便能望见那几堵墙——他的小弗勒蕾特就在墙后活着、受着苦。他不敢进去，怕被人认出来。他正自个儿掂量着去还是不去，忽见一个男人从建筑师卡里斯蒂的宅子里出来，穿过马路进了咖啡馆，消失在那扇吱呀作响的门后。十有八九是个打扫夫，衣衫褴褛，一身泥污——不是看守，穿得太破，当不成看守——不过是个打扫夫——可他兴许见过弗勒蕾特。这个念头把肖维兰迷住了。他的心思黏在它上面，翻来覆去。这可是个说不定就在刚才几分钟里还见过弗勒蕾特的人；说不定她打走廊或楼梯旁边经过的时候，他正在那儿扫地来着。伴着这个念头，那股灼热的欲望仍在心里烧：给她捎个信，告诉她要勇敢，要信赖比比；可顶要紧的，哦！顶要紧的，是千万别被人引得招认任何有关弗朗特纳克夫人那些值钱物件的事，或任何有关她与阿梅代·科隆布交往的事。

===Original===
Chauvelin, leaning against the wall which faced the little café, dwelt on his thoughts and his desire. He allowed the rain to drip upon his hat and upon his shoulders from the roof above him. He no longer felt restless. He just wanted to stand there and watch for the return of the man, who perhaps would be seeing Fleurette again within the next few minutes. He wondered if he dare approach him, always with the idea of possibly conveying a message to Fleurette. But the fear that the man might know who he was, deterred him from entering the café himself. He had been a fairly conspicuous figure in the courtyard of Caristie’s house, standing by the side of the captain of the guard; if that scavenger was at work in the corridor, he might have looked out of the window and seen him, learned who he was. All through he had been at pains to show an indifferent attitude before his enemies: if this man happened to be a spy, would the knowledge that he, Chauvelin, was trying to establish communication with Fleurette compromise him hopelessly and do no good to her? As he stood there pondering and debating what he had better do, he saw the scavenger come out of the café. For a minute or two the man stood at the door, his hands buried in the pockets of his ragged breeches, contemplating the rain. The next moment another, equally dirty and bedraggled ruffian came down the street, paused at the entrance of the café and passed the time of day with the scavenger. The two mudlarks remained talking for a few moments, after which they parted; each going his own way. The scavenger recrossed the road and entered the Caristie House. The other passed on in the opposite direction and Chauvelin, after an instant’s hesitation, followed him. He came up with the man at the angle of the Rue Longue: and putting out his arm, touched him on the shoulder. With a cry of terror the man fell on his knees.

===Chinese===
肖维兰倚着正对那家小咖啡馆的墙，浸在自己的心思和欲望里。屋顶上的雨水滴在他的帽子上、肩膀上，他由它滴去。他不再觉得躁动。他只想站在那儿，守着那人回来——说不定再过几分钟，那人又要见着弗勒蕾特了。他盘算着敢不敢上前搭话，心里转的还是那个念头：说不定能借他给弗勒蕾特捎个信。可又怕那人认得他是谁，不敢自己进咖啡馆。他在卡里斯蒂宅子的院子里露过相当惹眼的面，就站在卫兵上尉身边；那打扫夫若正在走廊里干活，说不定从窗口望见过他，知道了他是谁。他从头到尾都在仇敌面前刻意摆出一副漠然的样子：这人万一是个奸细，倘若被他知道了肖维兰正设法同弗勒蕾特通气，岂不把他坑得无可挽回，而对她也没有半点好处？他正站在那儿思量着、盘算着怎样才好，看见那打扫夫从咖啡馆里出来了。那人在门口站了一两分钟，双手插在破马裤的裤兜里，望着雨出神。紧跟着又有一个同样肮脏、同样邋遢的流氓从街那头走来，在咖啡馆门口停下，同打扫夫寒暄了几句。两个泥猴儿站着聊了一阵，随后分手，各走各的路。打扫夫折过马路，进了卡里斯蒂宅子。另一个朝相反方向去了，肖维兰略一迟疑，跟了上去。他在长街（Rue Longue）的拐角赶上那人，伸出胳膊，在他肩头一碰。那人一声惊叫，双膝跪倒。

===Original===
“Mercy! I’ve done nothing!” he babbled almost incoherently.

===Chinese===
「饶命！我什么也没干过！」他几乎语无伦次地咕哝着。

===Original===
“I dare say not,” Chauvelin said drily, “but it will be to thine advantage if thou’lt come along quietly with me.”

===Chinese===
「想来是没有，」肖维兰干巴巴地说，「不过你要是肯安安静静跟我走一趟，于你有好处。」

===Original===
He seized the man by the arm and dragged him up from his knees. The poor wretch tried to wriggle himself free, but Chauvelin held him tightly, and without another word drew him within the shelter of the nearest doorway. Fortunately, though the man kept up a ceaseless litany of lamentations and cries for mercy, he did so under his breath, thus creating no disturbance nor exciting the attention of the few passersby who were hurrying homewards through the rain-swept streets.

===Chinese===
肖维兰一把攥住那人的胳膊，把他从跪着的地方拽起来。这可怜虫还想扭动挣脱，肖维兰却把他攥得死紧，一言不发，把他拖到最近一处门洞的遮蔽底下。幸而那人虽然哀号讨饶、絮叨个没完，却都是压着嗓子说的，既没闹出动静，也没招来那几个冒雨匆匆赶路回家去的行人的注意。

===Original===
“Are you willing, citizen,” Chauvelin began abruptly, as soon as he had assured himself that the doorway was deserted and no eavesdropper nigh, “are you willing to earn fifty livres tournoi?”

===Chinese===
「你愿不愿意，公民，」肖维兰等确信门洞里没有旁人、左近也没有偷听的，才劈头开口，「你愿不愿意挣五十图尔里弗（livres tournois）？」

===Original===
The man gave no immediate reply, it seemed as if he was shaking himself free from his first terror and pondering over this extraordinary proposal, so different to what he had anticipated. Then he cleared his throat, expectorated, slowly repeated the magic words: “Fifty livres tournoi!” and finally added in an awed whisper:

===Chinese===
那人没有立刻答话，看样子是在从起初的惊恐里缓过神来，掂量着这个非同小可的提议——同他方才料想的实在天差地别。随后他清了清嗓子，吐了口唾沫，慢吞吞地念叨那几个有魔力的字眼：「五十图尔里弗！」末了又压低嗓子，带着几分敬畏添了一句：

===Original===
“I have not seen five livres tournoi for months.”

===Chinese===
「我好几个月没见过五个图尔里弗了。」

===Original===
“Fifty are yours, citizen, if you’ll render me a service.”

===Chinese===
「五十个归你，公民，只要你替我办一件事。」

===Original===
“What is it?”

===Chinese===
「什么事？」

===Original===
“That friend of yours, to whom you spoke just now﻿—outside the café de la Lune﻿—”

===Chinese===
「你那个朋友，方才你同他说话的那个——在月亮咖啡馆（café de la Lune）门外——」

===Original===
“Citizen Rémi?”

===Chinese===
「雷米（Rémi）公民？」

===Original===
“He works in the Caristie House?”

===Chinese===
「他在卡里斯蒂宅子里当差？」

===Original===
“Yes.”

===Chinese===
「是。」

===Original===
“In what capacity?”

===Chinese===
「当的什么差？」

===Original===
“Cleaner,” the man replied laconically. “Rémi hung about for days trying to earn a bit of money. He hasn’t a sou, you understand? Same as me. A few days ago one of the inside men fell sick. Rémi presented himself and got the work. I know him well.”

===Chinese===
「打扫的。」那人回答得简洁。「雷米在这儿转悠了好些天，想挣几个钱。他一个苏也没有，你懂吗？跟我一样。几天前，里头一个杂役病倒了。雷米自个儿找上门去，把差事谋到了。我跟他熟得很。」

===Original===
“He has access to the prisoners?” Chauvelin asked.

===Chinese===
「他见得着囚犯？」肖维兰问。

===Original===
“I suppose so.”

===Chinese===
「我想是见得着的。」

===Original===
“Then tell him that there will be fifty livres for him too if he will convey a written message to number 142 in room 12.”

===Chinese===
「那就告诉他，只要他肯把一封字条捎给 12 号房里的 142 号，他也照样有五十里弗。」

===Original===
Again the man seemed to ponder: weighing the risks probably, and also the gain. Fifty livres tournoi! Immense. He had forgotten that there was such a sum of money left in the world: and then for him to have the handling of it! This led him once more to expectorate, which action apparently had the effect of stimulating his brainpower.

===Chinese===
那人似乎又掂量起来：大约是在权衡风险，也在权衡进项。五十图尔里弗！好大一笔。他都忘了这世上还剩得有这么一笔钱：况且还要经他的手！这又引得他吐了口唾沫——这个动作看来对他的脑力颇有激发之效。

===Original===
“It could be done,” he murmured at last.

===Chinese===
「这事办得成。」他终于喃喃道。

===Original===
“It can be done,” Chauvelin asserted emphatically, “but must be done quickly, or﻿—”

===Chinese===
「是办得成，」肖维兰加重语气，斩钉截铁，「可必须快办，不然——」

===Original===
“Rémi will be back at the Café de la Lune soon after eight o’clock. He always goes there for a sip of something after supper.”

===Chinese===
「八点刚过，雷米就会回到月亮咖啡馆。他每天吃过晚饭，总要上那儿去呷上一口。」

===Original===
“Good! Then you can meet him at that hour and tell him to wait for you, then come at once and find me here, under this doorway. I’ll have the letter ready﻿—”

===Chinese===
「好！那你就赶那个钟点去见他，叫他等着你，然后立刻回来，到这个门洞底下找我。我把信备好——」

===Original===
“The whole thing is very risky, citizen,” the man demurred.

===Chinese===
「这整桩事，风险大得很呐，公民。」那人迟疑道。

===Original===
“If it were not,” Chauvelin rejoined drily, “I would not spend one hundred livres tournoi in the attempt.”

===Chinese===
「要不是有风险，」肖维兰干巴巴地答道，「我也犯不着为这一遭花一百图尔里弗。」

===Original===
“Fifty livres is not over much, when one risks one’s neck.”

===Chinese===
「拿脖子去冒险，五十里弗可不算多。」

===Original===
“You are not risking your neck,” Chauvelin retorted, “as you well know. And you’ll not get more from me than fifty livres each. Take it or leave it.”

===Chinese===
「你那脖子冒不着险，」肖维兰反诘道，「这你自己清楚。你们每人五十里弗，休想再从我这儿多拿一个子儿。要么干，要么拉倒。」

===Original===
He knew how to deal with these mudlarks, apparently, for the man after he had spat once more once or twice, seemed satisfied.

===Chinese===
看来他很懂得怎么对付这班泥猴儿：那家伙又吐了一两口唾沫，似乎是满意了。

===Original===
“I’ll be back here,” he said laconically, “after I have seen Rémi again.”

===Chinese===
「我再见着雷米，」他简洁地说，「就回这儿来。」

===Original===
Then Chauvelin let him go. The darkness and the rain soon swallowed him up: but Chauvelin himself remained for quite a while standing motionless under the doorway. He had not yet burnt his boats, was still free, if he thought the risk too great, to fail in his appointment. The man did not know who he was, had not seen him in the darkness and under the wide brim of his hat: but there was the risk that this Rémi might be a spy, who would take the letter intended for Fleurette straightway to Pochart or Danou. The letter might thus betray him and so minimize his power of saving Fleurette. He had to safeguard himself against the merest breath of suspicion in order to keep his power. The more irreproachable, detached, incorruptible he appeared before the populace, the more Spartan in his attitude towards his own child until the day of her trial, the greater his chance of saving her at the last. But his desire to warn her against unconsidered words or any kind of admissions outweighed for the moment every other consideration. He hurried back to his lodgings through the rain, and at once sat down to pen his letter to the child.

===Chinese===
于是肖维兰放他走了。黑暗和雨水很快把他吞没：肖维兰自己却在门洞底下纹丝不动地站了好一阵。他还没有破釜沉舟，倘若觉得风险太大，尽可以失约不去，仍是自由之身。那人不知道他是谁，在黑暗里、在那顶宽檐帽子底下，没有看清他：可是还有一层风险——这雷米说不定是个奸细，会把那封写给弗勒蕾特的信径直送到波夏尔或达努手里。那封信便可能出卖他，从而削减他搭救弗勒蕾特的力量。为保住手中的权柄，他必须护住自己，连最细微的一丝怀疑的气息也不容沾身。他在民众眼前显得越是无可指摘、越是超然物外、越是油盐不进，在女儿开审之前对亲生孩子的姿态越是斯巴达式的冷硬，到最后一刻救下她的胜算便越大。可是，他渴望警告她提防有欠思量的言语、提防任何形式的招认，这份渴望此刻压倒了一切别的考虑。他冒着雨匆匆赶回寓所，立刻坐下来给这孩子写信。

===Original===
