> “My beloved one,” he began, “at last I am able to send a word to you, which I hope and trust will reach your darling little hands. Child of my heart, this is to entreat you to continue in your trust of me, for I swear to you by the memory of your dead mother, that while you trust me I can save you. I can save the man you love. Moreover, I entreat you, beloved child of my soul, do not make any admission when brought before the tribunal, as you must be shortly, alas! If witnesses testify against you, just hold your peace; if others question you, deny everything. This I entreat you to do for the sake of the love I bear you, for the sake of the tears I have shed these past weeks, ever since your folly hath brought you to this pass.”

===Chinese===
> 「我亲爱的人儿，」他这样起头，「我终于能给你捎上一句话了，我盼望也笃信，它会送到你那双可爱的小手里。我心头的孩儿，写这几行字，是要恳求你继续信任我；我凭你亡母的在天之灵向你起誓：只要你信我，我就能救你。我能救你所爱的那个人。还有，我恳求你，我灵魂钟爱的孩子，当你被押到法庭上去的时候——唉，那一天已为期不远！——什么也不要承认。若有证人出面指证你，你只管缄默；若有人盘问你，一概否认。我求你为我怀着你的这份爱这样做，为我这几星期来洒下的眼泪这样做——自从你的痴愚把你带到这般田地。」

===Original===
He signed the letter “Bibi.” Thus he had mentioned no names and in addition taken the precaution of disguising his writing as far as he was able. After which he sealed the letter and slipped it in the inner pocket of his coat. Time was now hanging heavily. Like a beast in its cage, Chauvelin paced up and down the narrow room, his hands clenched behind his back, a world of soul agony expressed upon his wax-like face.

===Chinese===
他在信末署名「比比」。如此一来，信里一个名字也没有提，此外他还谨慎从事，尽力把笔迹做了改扮。随后他封好信，把它塞进外衣的内袋。这时辰沉得难熬。肖维兰像笼中困兽，在狭窄的屋子里来回踱步，双手紧攥在背后，蜡似的脸上写满一腔灵魂的煎熬。

===Original===
As soon as he heard the tower-clock of Notre-Dame strike eight, he picked up his hat and cloak and once more sallied forth into the streets.

===Chinese===
他一听圣母院（Notre-Dame）的钟楼敲响八下，便拿起帽子和斗篷，再次出门上街。

## XXX / 第三十章

===Original===
A quarter of an hour later two out-at-elbows ragamuffins met inside the Café de la Lune. Outside the rain had not abated, both the men, who were clad in what were little more than rags, appeared soaked through to the skin. At this hour the little café was almost deserted. Citizen Sabot, the proprietor, was sitting at one table with a couple of friends; at another a couple of road-menders were sipping their absinthe, when the scavenger from the prison house came slouching in. He sat down on the bench against the wall in the darkest corner of the room and ordered a bottle of wine for himself and a friend. Presently the latter came and joined him, and for a while the two men sat drinking in silence. Soon an animated discussion arose between the proprietor and his friends on the respective merits of Vouvray and Beaujolais as a table wine.

===Chinese===
一刻钟之后，两个肘头磨穿、破衣烂衫的流浪汉在月亮咖啡馆（café de la Lune）里碰了头。外头的雨没有小下去；这两个人身上穿的那一身同破布也差不了多少，看上去都淋得透湿，湿到了皮肉。这个钟点，小咖啡馆里几乎是空的。老板萨博公民（Citizen Sabot）同两个朋友坐在一张桌边；另一张桌上，两个修路工正小口呷着苦艾酒；就在这时，拘押所的打扫夫拖着步子晃了进来。他挨着墙，在店堂最暗的角落里那条长凳上坐下，给自己和一个朋友要了一瓶酒。不多时那朋友来了，坐到他身边；两个汉子有一阵子只是闷头喝酒。不多一会儿，老板同他的朋友们便热闹地争执起来，争论武弗雷（Vouvray）和博若莱（Beaujolais）两样酒拿来做佐餐酒，各自有几分高下。

===Original===
This entailed much shouting and copious gesticulations. Sabot had a deep-booming voice which reverberated from end to end of the room and caused the windowpanes to rattle in their frames.

===Chinese===
这一场争执，少不了高声嚷叫和大开大合的比划。萨博生就一副低沉洪亮的大嗓门，声音从店堂这头回荡到那头，震得窗玻璃在窗框里格格作响。

===Original===
The scavenger from the prison house had apparently drunk more during the day than was good for him. His head leaned heavily on his hand, his elbow resting upon the table, his eyes had become bleary, his speech uncertain. His friend sat opposite to him, with his back to the rest of the company, and when Sabot’s voice roused the echoes in the small stuffy room, he leaned forward and whispered in the other’s ear:

===Chinese===
拘押所的打扫夫白天里显然喝过了量，那酒于他并无好处。他的头沉沉地靠在手上，胳膊肘支在桌面，两眼已经喝得发黏，话也说不利索。他的朋友坐在对面，背朝着店堂里的旁人；萨博的嗓门在这又小又闷的屋子里激起回响时，他便把身子前倾，凑在对方耳边低声说：

===Original===
“I had an adventure after I left you this afternoon.”

===Chinese===
「今儿下午同你分手以后，我遇上一桩奇事。」

===Original===
“Eh?” the scavenger murmured incoherently.

===Chinese===
「嗯？」打扫夫语无伦次地咕哝了一声。

===Original===
“Where?”

===Chinese===
「在哪儿？」

===Original===
“At the angle of the Rue Longue I was pounced upon in the darkness and dragged under the shelter of a doorway. A man had me by the shoulder. He had seen me talking with you. He offered me fifty livres and the same for you, if you will give a letter to a certain prisoner in there.”

===Chinese===
「在长街（Rue Longue）的拐角上，黑暗里冷不丁有人扑上来，把我拖进一处门洞的遮蔽底下。那人一把攥住我的肩膀。他看见过我同你说话。他许给我五十里弗，你也照样五十，只要肯把一封信捎给里头的某个囚犯。」

===Original===
And he nodded in the direction of the high walls of the Caristie house. His friend’s reply to this preliminary statement was a prolonged snore.

===Chinese===
说着，他朝卡里斯蒂（Caristie）宅子那几堵高墙的方向点了点头。他朋友对这番开场白的回应，是一声拖得长长的鼾。

===Original===
“The prisoner to whom you are to give the letter is number 142 in room 12,” the other went on, still speaking below his breath. “Who is that? Do you know?”

===Chinese===
「你要送信的那名囚犯，是 12 号房里的 142 号，」那人接着往下说，声音仍旧压在嗓子底下，「那是个什么人？你知道吗？」

===Original===
The scavenger from the prison house waited for a moment or two until the discussion at the next table was specially loud-tongued, then he murmured:

===Chinese===
拘押所的打扫夫等了一两分钟，捱到邻桌的争论正嚷得特别响的时候，才低声说道：

===Original===
“Yes! It is the girl Fleurette.”

===Chinese===
「知道！是那个弗勒蕾特姑娘。」

===Original===
“Ah!” remarked his friend.

===Chinese===
「啊！」他的朋友应了一声。

===Original===
“Who was the man who spoke to you?”

===Chinese===
「同你说话的是个什么人？」

===Original===
“I don’t know. It was pitch-dark. He wore a broad-brimmed hat and spoke in a hoarse whisper.”

===Chinese===
「不知道。黑得伸手不见五指。他戴一顶宽檐帽，开口是沙哑的低语。」

===Original===
“Her father, probably. The man Armand, I have marvelled why we did not hear from him before. What have you arranged?”

===Chinese===
「多半是她父亲。就是那个阿尔芒。我一直纳闷，先前怎么一直没有他的一点音信。你们是怎么商量定的？」

===Original===
“That I meet him under the same doorway, after I’ve seen you. He will then give me the letter.”

===Chinese===
「商量定的是：我见过你之后，还到原先那处门洞底下去会他。到时他再把信交给我。」

===Original===
“We’ll keep to that then. But try and see the man. I might recognize him by your description.”

===Chinese===
「那就照这么办。不过你得想法子看看那人。凭你描述的模样，我兴许认得出来。」

===Original===
He paused for a moment or two, yawned, stretched, emptied his mug of wine and then went on. “If I went myself I might scare him off. So it is best you should go. But try and see his face. I’ll wait here till you come.”

===Chinese===
他停顿了片刻，打个哈欠，伸个懒腰，把杯里的酒喝干，接着又说：「我要是亲自去，兴许会把他吓跑。所以还是你去顶妥当。不过你得想法子看清他的脸。我就在这儿等你回来。」

===Original===
After which he ordered another bottle of wine. Sabot broke away from his friends in order to serve his customer.

===Chinese===
说罢他又叫了一瓶酒。萨博从朋友们中间抽出身来，招呼这位主顾。

===Original===
“You’ve had about as much as you ought to have, citizen Rémi,” he said dryly, as he uncorked the bottle and set it on the table.

===Chinese===
「你喝得也够数了，雷米（Rémi）公民，」他一面拔开瓶塞、把酒瓶搁上桌，一面干巴巴地说。

===Original===
“That is none of your business, citizen,” Rémi retorted with a bibulous laugh, “so long as I pay for what I drink.”

===Chinese===
「这轮不着你管，公民，」雷米带着酒意的笑回敬道，「只要我喝的酒我照付钱。」

===Original===
He threw some coins on the table. Sabot picked them up with a shrug and then rejoined his friends, and resumed the discussion with them on the merits or demerits of Vouvray and Beaujolais. The other ruffian took the opportunity of shuffling out of the café, and the scavenger, sprawling over the table, composed himself to sleep.

===Chinese===
他往桌上抛了几枚钱币。萨博耸耸肩，把钱拾起来，回到朋友们中间，接着同他们把武弗雷和博若莱孰优孰劣的争论续了下去。那另一个流氓趁这个当口趿拉着脚溜出了咖啡馆；打扫夫则摊开手脚伏在桌上，自顾自安顿下来睡了。

===Original===
Hugging the walls, the other slunk through the street till he came to the doorway, where effectively he had appointed to meet Chauvelin.

===Chinese===
那一个贴着墙根，鬼鬼祟祟地一路溜过街道，来到那处门洞底下——他同肖维兰约好的，果然正是这里。

===Original===
“Well!” the latter queried impatiently as soon as the other came in sight. “Have you seen your friend?”

===Chinese===
「怎么样！」那汉子刚一露面，肖维兰便不耐烦地问道，「见着你那位朋友了？」

===Original===
“Yes.”

===Chinese===
「见着了。」

===Original===
“Does he agree?”

===Chinese===
「他肯吗？」

===Original===
“Yes.”

===Chinese===
「肯。」

===Original===
With a sigh of relief Chauvelin drew the sealed letter from his breast pocket.

===Chinese===
肖维兰宽慰地吁出一口气，从胸前内袋里取出那封封好的信。

===Original===
“Fifty livres, remember,” he said slowly, “for each of you, when you bring me back the answer.”

===Chinese===
「五十里弗，记住，」他慢吞吞地说，「你们每人五十，等你们把回话给我带回来的时候。」

===Original===
“Oh!” the man exclaimed, visibly disappointed. “There’s an answer then?”

===Chinese===
「哦！」那人叫出声来，一脸的失望，「这么说，还得有回话？」

===Original===
“Yes! An answer. Your friend will see to it that you bring me back either an answer or some token which will satisfy me that the letter is in the right hands.”

===Chinese===
「对！要回话。你那位朋友自会留意，叫你给我带回来的，要么是一句回话，要么是一件信物，好叫我放心，信确确实实到了该到的人手里。」

===Original===
The man gave a short laugh.

===Chinese===
那人短促地笑了一声。

===Original===
“You do not trust me, citizen,” he said.

===Chinese===
「你信不过我，公民。」他说。

===Original===
“No,” Chauvelin replied laconically. “I do not.”

===Chinese===
「信不过，」肖维兰回答得干脆，「就是信不过。」

===Original===
“I do not blame you,” the other retorted. “I do not trust you altogether either. How do I know, when Rémi and I have risked our lives in your service, that the money will be forthcoming?”

===Chinese===
「这也怪不得你，」那人反唇相讥，「我对你也不是全然信得过。我和雷米替你办事，把性命都豁出去了；我凭什么知道，到时候那笔钱就一定拿得出来？」

===Original===
“You do know that, citizen,” Chauvelin rejoined dryly, “and anyway you are bound to take that risk.”

===Chinese===
「这一点你明明知道，公民，」肖维兰干巴巴地答道，「再说，这个险你反正也是非冒不可的。」

===Original===
“Why should I?” the man retorted.

===Chinese===
「凭什么我就非冒不可？」那人顶了回来。

===Original===
“Because you are more sorely in need of money than I of your services.”

===Chinese===
「因为你缺钱缺得厉害，远比我缺你这份差事来得急。」

===Original===
This argument appeared unanswerable. At any rate the ruffian now said with a light laugh:

===Chinese===
这番道理看来无从反驳。不管怎样，那流氓这会儿呵呵一笑，说道：

===Original===
“Have it your own way. Give me the letter. Number 142 in room 12 shall have it, you can wager your shirt on that.”

===Chinese===
「就依你的便吧。把信给我。12 号房里的 142 号保险收得着信——这个，你把衬衫押上打赌都行。」

===Original===
Without another word Chauvelin handed him the letter. It was so dark under the doorway that it was only by groping that the other was able to get hold of it. He drew so near to Chauvelin that the latter, fearing that the man was trying to have a close look at him, pulled his hat lower down over his eyes. The other resorted to his habitual expression of indifference by spitting upon the floor; then he slipped the letter underneath his ragged blouse.

===Chinese===
肖维兰二话不说，把信递了过去。门洞底下黑得很，那人全靠摸索才算把信抓到了手。他凑得离肖维兰极近，肖维兰生怕他是想凑近端详自己的脸，便把帽子往眼睛上又压低了些。那人却使出他那副惯常的漠然神气，朝地上啐了一口；随后把信掖进了他那件破罩衫底下。

===Original===
“Where do I find you,” he asked, “after Rémi has done your errand?”

===Chinese===
「雷米替你办完了差事之后，」他问，「我上哪儿找你去？」

===Original===
“You will go into the Rue Longue,” Chauvelin replied, “to the house of Citizen Amouret, the chandler. Up the first flight of stairs, on the right-hand side, you will come to a door which is painted a slate-grey. Knock at that door and you will find me within.”

===Chinese===
「你到长街去，」肖维兰答道，「找蜡烛匠阿穆雷公民（Citizen Amouret）的家。上了头一段楼梯，右手边就会见到一扇漆成石板灰色的门。敲那扇门，我便在里头。」

===Original===
“At what hour?”

===Chinese===
「什么钟点？」

===Original===
“At any time tomorrow after the executions in the Place de la République,” Chauvelin replied.

===Chinese===
「明天共和国广场（Place de la République）上行刑过后，随便什么时辰来都行。」肖维兰答道。

## XXXI / 第三十一章

===Original===
To say that Fleurette had in the past few days become familiarized with the grim mummeries that went on in the common room, would be putting it rather strongly. But she certainly had no longer the same horror of them as she had had at first. The presentment of the mock guillotine still harrowed her, it is true, but she could not help laughing at the antics of the mock Satan and his satellites when they seized the President of the Tribunal and the Public Prosecutor by the feet and dragged them off to an imaginary hell. There was that one man in particular whom she had sometimes noticed before and who was aide to one of the warders, he was very diverting. She used to watch him turning and wriggling his huge body, which he had painted all over with soot and draped in bits of red rags. He made an ideal Satan with tail and horns complete, and sometimes it seemed to Fleurette as if he went through all his antics for the sole purpose of bringing a smile upon her lips. Moreover, in a vague kind of way, she associated him with that lovely letter from Amédé, which she had found inside the folds of her kerchief one evening.

===Chinese===
要说弗勒蕾特在这几天里已经看惯了那间公共大厅里上演的一出出阴森的假面闹戏，未免说得过重了些。可她对这些闹戏，确实已不复起初那般的恐惧了。诚然，演断头台戏的那一幕仍旧剜着她的心，可是看到假撒旦和他那群小鬼揪住庭长和公诉人的双脚、把他们一路拖往一座凭空想象的地狱时的那副滑稽样子，她又禁不住要发笑。其中尤其有一个男人，她先前就不时留意过——他是给一名看守当帮手的——这人非常逗趣。她常常望着他扭动、摆撼那具庞大的身躯：那身子他从头到脚涂满了煤烟，又披挂着一条条红布条。尾巴犄角一应俱全，他扮起撒旦来真是再理想不过；有时弗勒蕾特简直觉得，他把那套把戏从头耍到尾，就只为引她唇边浮起一丝笑意。再说，朦朦胧胧地，她总把他同阿梅代那封可爱的信联想到一处——那封信，是一天傍晚她从头巾的褶缝里发现的。

===Original===
The death of so many who had been her prison-companions at first, especially that of Claire de Châtelard, had deeply affected her. The want of fresh air, of exercise, and above all of love and joy, had begun to affect her health: her cheeks had lost their freshness, her eyes their lustre, her lips their smile.

===Chinese===
起初与她同监的那些人，死了那么多，尤其是克莱尔·德·沙特拉尔（Claire de Châtelard）的死，深深刺痛了她。缺少新鲜空气，缺少活动，更缺少爱与欢乐，她的身子开始吃了亏：脸颊失去了红润，眼睛失去了光彩，嘴唇失去了笑意。

===Original===
It was only in the recreation hour that she would smile sometimes. Always when that big, clumsy, hideous-looking fellow who was some kind of aide to one of the warders, set himself the task of fooling for her benefit. She came to look upon him as a friend, and remembering how mysteriously that letter from Amédé had come inside her kerchief, she would look up whenever he came near her, wondering if he had another such welcome message for her. And one evening﻿—she really had not the least idea how it happened﻿—she found a sealed letter inside her workbasket. And the letter was from chéri Bibi. Oh! the joy of it! The joy! She read, and reread it, and kissed the paper whereon his dear hand had rested. How she had missed Bibi all these days! How she longed to reassure him that she was well and that she trusted and believed in him! As to obeying him in all things, of course she would do it. To begin with, she was not afraid, not the least bit in the world. He was watching over her, and he was so great and powerful that no danger could possibly assail her while he cared for her. She would indeed obey him in all things, hold her peace while that wicked Adèle tried to do her harm; she would hold her peace before the Tribunal just as le bon Jésus had done when he was questioned by his judges.

===Chinese===
只有到了消遣的钟点，她才偶尔会笑一笑。而她的笑，总是落在那个大块头、笨手笨脚、长相凶丑的家伙——就是那个给哪名看守当什么帮手的——存心插科打诨、专为她逗乐的当口。她渐渐把他看作一位朋友；想起阿梅代那封信当初是怎样神不知鬼不觉地进了她的头巾，每逢他走到近旁，她总要抬起眼来，心里琢磨着，他是不是又给她捎来了这样一封叫人欢喜的信。一天傍晚——她实在半点也想不明白这是怎么一回事——她在针线篮（workbasket）里发现了一封封了口的信。信是亲爱的比比（chéri Bibi）写来的。哦！多么欢喜！多么欢喜啊！她读了又读，又亲吻那张纸——他那亲爱的手曾在上面停歇。这些天来，她多么想念比比啊！她多么盼着让他放心：她好好的，她信赖他、笃信他。至于凡事都听他的话，那自然是不消说的。头一条，她不怕，一点儿也不怕，天底下一点儿也不怕。他正守护着她呢；他是那样伟大、那样有力量，只要他惦着她、护着她，无论什么危险都休想近她的身。她真的要凡事都听他的：那歹毒的阿黛尔存心害她，她就缄默不语；到了法庭上，她也照样缄默，就像好耶稣（le bon Jésus）当年受审判官们盘问时那样。

===Original===
Oh! it was a dear, a comforting, an infinitely precious letter. And beside it Fleurette had found a tiny little slip of paper on which were scribbled the words: “Let me have something to take back to the writer, to let him know that you are well. Leave it in your work basket, and I will see to it that he gets it.” And so Fleurette had written a few lines to chéri Bibi; told him that she was well, and assured him that she was not afraid and would obey his commands in all things. She would hold her peace and trust in him. This little note she had hidden that evening in her workbasket and by noon on the following day it had gone.

===Chinese===
哦！这是一封可爱的信，一封暖人的信，一封无限珍贵的信。信的旁边，弗勒蕾特还发现了一小片纸条，上面潦潦草草地涂着几句话：「给我点东西带回去交给写信的人，好叫他知道你平安。把东西留在你的针线篮里，我自有法子送到他手上。」于是弗勒蕾特便给亲爱的比比写了几行字；告诉他她很好，还向他担保：她不怕，凡事都听他的吩咐。她会缄默，会信赖他。这张小字条，她当晚就藏进了针线篮；到第二天晌午，它已经不见了。

## XXXII / 第三十二章

===Original===
“But me no buts, my dear Tony, I am sick of all these filthy rags. And if I am to see pretty Fleurette’s papa then must I see him decently clad and in my right mind.”

===Chinese===
「别跟我『但是但是』的，亲爱的托尼，这一身龌龊的破衣烂衫我算受够了。既然要去见美丽的弗勒蕾特的爸爸，我就得穿得体体面面、头脑清清楚楚地去见他。」

===Original===
So spake Sir Percy Blakeney to his friend, late the following evening, it was in an attic under the roof of a half-derelict house in the Rue du Pont close to the riverbank. The owners of the house had long since disappeared, fled into the mountains or perished on the guillotine; no one knew or cared. Blakeney, and those members of his league who were with him, had hit upon it on their arrival in Orange, had made the attic their headquarters, whilst most of the vagabonds of the city used the rest of the house as their lair. They too were outwardly vagabonds, dressed in rags, appeared unkempt, unshaven, and unwashed, when they sallied forth in the early mornings each on an errand of mercy to succour those in need of help or those who were in danger or distress.

===Chinese===
这是第二天深夜，珀西·布莱克尼爵士对朋友说的话；地点在桥街（Rue du Pont）近河岸处一栋半废弃宅子的屋顶阁楼里。宅子的主人家早已没了踪影，或是逃进了山里，或是上了断头台；没有人知道，也没有人在意。布莱克尼，还有随他同来的几名同盟成员，一到奥朗日便相中了这地方，把阁楼当作大本营，而城里大多数流浪汉则把宅子的其余部分当作巢穴。这班英国绅士自己外出时也扮作流浪汉模样：衣衫褴褛，形容邋遢，胡子拉碴，一身尘垢，每天一早出门，各去干一桩行善的差事，救助那些需要援手的人，或是身陷险境、困顿无告的人。

===Original===
It was only o’ nights, sometimes, that an overwhelming desire for cleanliness and nice clothes caused these English gentlemen to cast aside their rags and to venture out into the open dressed in clothes that would have caused the ragamuffins of Orange to snarl at their heels like so many hungry curs.

===Chinese===
只是有时到了夜里，一股对清洁和体面衣裳的压不住的渴望，才会叫这班英国绅士抛开破衣烂衫，换上另一身行头冒险走到露天底下去——那样的衣裳，若教奥朗日的小叫花子们瞧见了，准会像一群饿狗似的追着他们的脚跟狂吠。

===Original===
They had been eight days in Orange now, and already Architect Caristie, with his wife and small son, the widow Colmars and her daughter, and poor old General Paulieu with his family owed their safety to this gallant League of the Scarlet Pimpernel. But there was still more to do.

===Chinese===
他们到奥朗日已经八天了。建筑师卡里斯蒂（Caristie）连同他的妻子和小儿子、科尔马尔寡妇（Colmars）和她的女儿、还有可怜的老波利厄将军（Paulieu）和他的全家，至今都已托这支英勇的猩红繁笺花同盟的福，保全了性命。可要做的事还有。

===Original===
“We must get that child Fleurette out of that hell,” the chief had said, and since then brain and heart had been at work to find the means to that end.

===Chinese===
「咱们得把弗勒蕾特那孩子从那座地狱里弄出来。」首领这样说过。打那以后，脑和心便一齐开动起来，寻思达到这个目的的门路。

===Original===
Later on Lord Tony had remarked: “I wish we could find out about that father of hers; this man Armand. He seems to hold some kind of position under this government of assassins, but I for one have tried in vain to learn something more definite about him.”

===Chinese===
后来托尼勋爵议论道：「咱们要是能摸清楚她那位父亲就好了——就是这个阿尔芒。他在这帮杀人凶手当道的政府里似乎担任着什么职位，可单说我吧，白费了不少力气，也没能探出一点更确实的消息来。」

===Original===
“I think,” Sir Andrew Ffoulkes added, “that his position must be a high one, or the girl would have been brought to trial before now.”

===Chinese===
「依我看，」安德鲁·福尔克斯爵士（Sir Andrew Ffoulkes）补充道，「他的职位必定不低，不然那姑娘早就被提堂受审了。」

===Original===
“Unless our amiable friend, M. Chauvelin, has got this Armand under lock and key somewhere else,” was my Lord Stowmaries’ comment upon the situation.

===Chinese===
「除非是咱们那位和气的朋友肖维兰先生，已经把这个阿尔芒关到旁的什么地方去了。」这便是斯托玛丽斯勋爵（Lord Stowmaries）对时局的评语。

===Original===
Sir Percy was silent. Frankly the position puzzled him. He would have liked to get into touch with the man Armand, but for once he and his friends were baffled by this anonymity which appeared so closely guarded. Great then had been the rejoicing in the attic of the derelict house in the Rue du Pont, when Lord Anthony Dewhurst﻿—a most perfect type of ruffian in rags and a thick coating of grime﻿—related his adventure with the mysterious individual who, under cover of darkness and rain, had offered him and his friend Rémi fifty livres each for delivering a message to a prisoner, who was none other than little Fleurette.

===Chinese===
珀西爵士沉默着。老实说，这局面把他难住了。他本想同这个阿尔芒取得联络，可是这一回，他和他的朋友们竟被这份守得严严实实的匿名弄得一筹莫展。于是不难想见，当安东尼·杜赫斯特勋爵（Lord Anthony Dewhurst）——配上那身破衣烂衫和厚厚一层油泥，他把流氓这个行当扮演得尽善尽美——讲起自己同那个神秘人物的奇遇时，桥街破宅的阁楼里该是怎样一片欢腾：那家伙趁着黑夜和大雨作掩护，许给他和他的朋友雷米（Rémi）每人五十里弗，只求送一封信给一名囚犯，而那名囚犯不是别人，正是小弗勒蕾特。

===Original===
“At last we’ll get in touch with the mysterious Armand,” they all declared eagerly. It was arranged that the chief would himself take Fleurette’s reply to the house in the Rue Longue. But go on this errand in the filthy rags of a scavenger he would not.

===Chinese===
「咱们到底能同那位神秘的阿尔芒接上头了。」众人急切地齐声说道。大家议定，由首领亲自把弗勒蕾特的回信送到长街（Rue Longue）那所宅子里去。可是要穿着打扫夫那身龌龊破烂去办这桩差事，他可不干。

===Original===
“The night is pretty dark,” he declared, “and I would rather the mysterious Armand saw me as I am. I may also have a chance,” he added with his merriest laugh, “of coming across my good friend M. Chambertin. It is some weeks since last we met, and not to have had a pleasant chat with him all these days, while we were within a stone’s throw of one another, has been a sore trial to me. I caught a glimpse of him a day or two ago, in the courtyard of the Caristie house. He looked to be sick and out of sorts. A sight of me might cheer him up.”

===Chinese===
「夜色够黑的，」他宣称，「我倒情愿让那位神秘的阿尔芒瞧瞧我的本来面目。说不定我还有机会，」他以最欢快的笑声补充道，「撞见我的好朋友尚贝尔坦先生（M. Chambertin）。咱们上次见面已是几个星期之前，这些日子明明近在咫尺，却没能同他愉快地聊聊，真叫我苦不堪言。一两天前，我在卡里斯蒂宅子的院子里瞥见过他一眼。他看上去病恹恹的，没精打采。见我一面，兴许能叫他精神起来。」

===Original===
“You won’t take any risks, Blakeney,” Sir Andrew Ffoulkes remarked.

===Chinese===
「你可别去冒什么险，布莱克尼。」安德鲁·福尔克斯爵士说道。

===Original===
“Any number, my dear fellow,” Sir Percy replied laughing. “And you know you envy me, you dog. But I feel thoroughly selfish tonight. I mean to take the note to Armand myself, and I mean to take the privilege of having a little chat with my friend Chambertin. And both these things I am going to do as an English gentleman and not as a mudlark in stinking, filthy rags.”

===Chinese===
「有多少险就冒多少险，亲爱的伙计。」珀西爵士笑着回答。「你分明是眼红我呢，你这个坏东西。不过今晚我自私透了。我打定主意亲自把字条送给阿尔芒，也要独享同我的朋友尚贝尔坦聊上一聊的特权。这两件事，我都要以一个英国绅士的身份去做，而不是扮成一个浑身发臭、衣衫龌龊的泥猴儿。」

===Original===
He had completed his toilet now, looked magnificent in clothes cut by the leading London tailor, which set off his splendid figure to perfection, with snow-white stock and speckless boots.

===Chinese===
这时他已梳洗穿戴停当，一身出自伦敦头号裁缝之手的衣裳，把堂堂的仪表衬托得恰到好处，颈间一条雪白的领巾，脚下一双纤尘不染的靴子，望上去气派非凡。

===Original===
“If a single pair of eyes should see you,” Sir Andrew insisted, with an anxious sigh.

===Chinese===
「万一叫哪一双眼睛瞧见了呢。」安德鲁爵士还不放心，焦灼地叹了一声。

===Original===
“I should have a whole pack of wolves at my heels,” Blakeney admitted. “But that wouldn’t be the first time any of us have had to run for our lives, eh? nor the first time we gave an entire pack of them the slip.”

===Chinese===
「那我就得让整整一群狼跟在脚后了。」布莱克尼承认道。「可咱们谁没为逃命撒过腿呀，这又不是头一遭，嗯？整整一群狼叫咱们甩了个干净，也不是头一遭。」

===Original===
He picked up his hat and took a last look at Fleurette’s little note which he had to deliver at the House in the Rue Longue.

===Chinese===
他拿起帽子，又把弗勒蕾特那张小字条最后看了一眼——这字条他得送到长街那所宅子里去。

===Original===
“This man Armand must be a very decent fellow,” he mused, “his letter to the child was really fine in spirit as well as in affection. Yes! he must be a decent fellow and we must get the girl for his sake as much as for that of our friend Colombe. What?”

===Chinese===
「这个阿尔芒准是个十分正派的人。」他沉吟道，「他写给那孩子的信，论骨气、论慈爱，都实在见得真情。是的！他必定是个正派人，为了他的缘故，也同为了咱们的朋友科隆布一样，咱们非把那姑娘救出来不可。可不是么？」

===Original===
On that, of course, they were all agreed. The activities of the League, since the rescue of General Paulieu and his family, were centred now on Fleurette. There were still one or two minor points to discuss, arrangements of detail, to complete, but the main project for the girl’s rescue could not be determined until it was definitely known whether her father, Armand, was going to be a help or a hindrance.

===Chinese===
在这一点上，众人自然都无异议。自打救出波利厄将军和他全家之后，同盟的行动便集中到了弗勒蕾特身上。还有一两个细枝末节要商议、几样具体的安排要敲定，但营救那姑娘的主方案，非等确切弄清她的父亲阿尔芒到底是帮手还是掣肘，才能定下来。

===Original===
“Anyway I shall know more,” Blakeney said finally, as he made for the door, “when I have sampled this man.”

===Chinese===
「反正，等我掂过了这个人的斤两，」布莱克尼朝门口走去，临了说道，「我自会知道得更多。」

===Original===
It was then nine o’clock in the evening. The night was dark and stormy. Gusts of wind alternated with sharp showers of rain﻿—an altogether unusual state of weather for the time of year in these parts. The few passersby of respectable appearance on their way home from business or work did no more than throw a cursory glance on the tall figure that passed hurriedly by. A few vagabonds clinging to their rags which the wind threatened to tear off their meagre bodies, did perhaps pause, cowering against a dark wall, murmuring a threat or a curse against the aristo, but an unexpected coin slipped into their grimy hands, quickly silenced both curse and threat.

===Chinese===
这时是晚上九点钟。夜黑风狂，一阵阵狂风夹着急骤的阵雨——这样的天气，在这个时节、在这个地带实属少有。几个相貌体面、办完事或收了工正往家走的路人，至多朝那个匆匆而过的高大身影草草瞥上一眼。有几个流浪汉，紧紧攥着身上的破布——狂风险些把那点破布从他们瘦削的身上撕了去——或许还真停下来过，缩在一堵黑墙根下，对着这个贵族佬咕哝一句恫吓或是咒骂；可一枚意想不到的钱币悄悄塞进他们脏兮兮的手心，咒骂和恫吓登时都噤了声。

===Original===
Blakeney knew his way well through the streets of Orange. Having kept along the river bank till he came to the bridge, he turned up the Rue de la République. Glancing up at a house on his right, a smile of pure joy lit up his anxious face. Three nights ago on this spot, he had carried Architect Caristie’s small son in his arms, while Caristie and his wife followed him down the street to the market cart which awaited them at the top of the bridge. Three hours later an officer of the revolutionary army was hammering at the door of Caristie’s lodgings, only to find that the birds had flown. It had been a merry night, and merrier morning, while he, Blakeney, drove the market cart out of the city with Caristie and his wife concealed amidst the sacks of haricots and peas, and the boy thrust into an empty oil-jar.

===Chinese===
奥朗日的街道，布莱克尼熟得很。他沿着河岸一直走到桥头，拐上了共和国街（Rue de la République）。朝右手边一所宅子瞥了一眼，一缕纯粹的欢愉点亮了他焦虑的脸。三夜之前，就在这个地方，他怀里抱着建筑师卡里斯蒂的小儿子，卡里斯蒂和他的妻子跟在他身后，沿街走向那辆在桥顶候着他们的集市大车。三个钟头之后，一名革命军的军官擂响了卡里斯蒂寓所的大门，却发现人去楼空，鸟儿早已飞走。那是一个快活的夜晚，更是一个快活的清晨——他，布莱克尼，赶着集市大车出了城：卡里斯蒂夫妇藏在菜豆和豌豆的口袋中间，小男孩则被塞进了一只空油坛里。

===Original===
Well! something equally daring would have to be devised for the girl Fleurette, and perhaps for her father, the mysterious Armand. Blakeney, throwing back his head in the teeth of rain and wind, drew a deep breath of delight. This was life in very truth. To plan, to scheme, to accomplish. Alternately hare and hound, to revel in this chase with human lives as the goal. And if at times the thought of beautiful Marguerite, lonely and anxious in far-off England, caused a pang like a knife-thrust to his heart, her soothing voice, her reassuring smile came to him as a swift vision from the spirit-land to encourage and console. In suffering and anxiety, as well as in the joy of reunion, Marguerite always understood.

===Chinese===
好哇！为弗勒蕾特那姑娘，说不定还要为她的父亲——神秘的阿尔芒——也得筹划出一场同样胆大包天的行动来。布莱克尼迎着风雨仰起头来，深深吸了一口畅快的气。这才是千真万确的人生。运筹，设谋，功成。时而做兔，时而做犬，以人命为标的，在这场追逐里陶然忘我。而每当想起美丽的玛格丽特正在遥远的英国孤寂悬望，一阵利刃剜心似的痛楚便扎上他的心头，她那抚慰的声音、她那宽人心怀的笑靥，便如灵界倏然飞来的一幅幻象，来给他打气，给他宽慰。无论在忧患焦虑之中，还是在重逢的欢喜里，玛格丽特总是懂得的。

===Original===
Now he turned from the Place de la République into the Rue Longue, and the next couple of hundred yards brought him to the house of Lucien Amouret, corn-chandler. The outside door was on the latch. Pushing it open he found himself in a narrow hall, with an inner door leading into the shop on his left and a staircase in front of him. A lamp hung from the ceiling and shed a dim light on stair and hall. From the shop came the sound of voices in conversation, but though the stairs creaked under his tread, no one came out to see whose the step might be.

===Chinese===
这时他从共和国广场（Place de la République）拐进长街，再走两百来码，便到了粮商吕西安·阿穆雷（Lucien Amouret）的宅子。临街的外门只搭着闩。他推门进去，置身于一条窄窄的门厅：左手一道内门通往店堂，面前一道楼梯。天花板下挂着一盏灯，给楼梯和门厅洒下一层昏光。店堂里传来人语交谈之声，可是楼梯被他踏得吱呀作响，也没有人出来看看这是谁的脚步。

===Original===
Sir Percy ran lightly up the stairs, and on the first landing came to the door, painted a slate grey. This part of the house appeared silent and deserted; the upper floors wrapped in dead gloom. A rusty bellpull hung beside the door. Sir Percy gave it a pull, and a discordant clang roused the sleeping echoes of the chandler’s house. A moment or two later he caught the sound of shuffling footsteps, the door was opened, an old woman in cap and shawl mutely inquired what the visitor desired.

===Chinese===
珀西爵士轻快地奔上楼梯，在第一个楼梯平台来到一扇漆成石板灰色的门前。宅子的这一部分悄无声息，像是无人居住；上面几层楼裹在死寂的昏暗里。门边垂着一条生锈的铃绳。珀西爵士一拉，一阵刺耳的铿锵惊起了这座粮商家宅里沉睡的回声。片刻之后，他听见了趿拉趿拉的脚步声，门开了，一个戴便帽、披披巾的老妇人默然探问来客的来意。

===Original===
“Is Citizen Armand within?” Blakeney asked.

===Chinese===
「公民阿尔芒在家么？」布莱克尼问。

===Original===
The woman, he thought, looked at him rather curiously for a second or two, then shrugged her shoulders. Without wasting words she shuffled off down a dimly lighted passage, leaving him to enter or not, as he pleased. The next moment he heard a woman’s voice﻿—the same woman probably﻿—say: “An aristo is asking to see Citizen Armand.” Again a moment’s silence, then the woman came shuffling back, signed to him to enter and closed the door behind him.

===Chinese===
他觉得，那老妇人颇为好奇地打量了他一两秒钟，随即耸了耸肩。她一个字也不多费，趿拉着脚步顺一条灯光昏暗的走廊走远，任凭他进或不进，悉听尊便。紧接着，他听见一个女人的声音——多半便是同一个女人——说道：「有个贵族佬求见公民阿尔芒。」又静了片刻，那老妇人趿拉着脚步回来了，示意他进屋，随手在他身后关上了门。

===Original===
“In there,” she said laconically, and nodded towards the end of the passage where a half open door revealed a shaft of more brilliant light. Then she shuffled off again, presumably to her kitchen, leaving the visitor to his own devices.

===Chinese===
「在里头。」她言简意赅地说，朝走廊尽头点了点头，那里一扇半开的门里透出一道更亮的光。说完她又趿拉着脚步走开了，想必是回她的厨房去，把来客撂在一旁任他自便。

===Original===
Sir Percy took off his hat and coat and laid them down on a chair close by; he then walked the length of the passage to the half-open door, pushed it open and found himself in a small room, comfortably furnished, lighted by a lamp which stood upon a centre table. The table was littered with papers. Behind it sat a man writing. At sound of Sir Percy’s footsteps he looked up. The eyes of the two men met, and it almost seemed to one of them at least that time for a few seconds stood still.

===Chinese===
珀西爵士脱下帽子和外衣，搁在近旁的一把椅子上；随后他顺着走廊走到那扇半开的门前，推门进去，置身于一间小小的屋子：陈设舒适，一张居中的桌子上立着一盏灯，照得满室通明。桌上摊满了文件。文件后面坐着一个人，正在写东西。听得珀西爵士的脚步声，他抬起头来。两双眼睛相遇了，至少对其中一人来说，有那么几秒钟，时间仿佛凝住了。

===Original===
And then a pleasant laugh broke the silence, and a gentle lazy voice said slowly:

===Chinese===
随后，一阵悦耳的笑声打破了寂静，一个温和而慵懒的嗓音慢悠悠地说道：

===Original===
“Egad! if it is not my engaging friend M. Chambertin! The gods do indeed favour me, sir, for there’s no man in the world I would sooner have seen at this hour than your amiable self.”

===Chinese===
「天哪！这不是我那位迷人的朋友尚贝尔坦先生么！众神当真眷顾我，先生——这个钟点，天底下我最乐意见到的，便是您这位和蔼可亲的人了。」

===Original===
After the first paralysing second, Chauvelin had jumped to his feet. He had thought that once again his feverish fancy was playing his senses a mocking trick, that the face which ever haunted his daydreams and his sleepless nights had only come to him on the wings of imagination. But the merry laugh, the lazy voice were all too real. His enemy was truly there, not a vision, but a cruel, mocking reality. Swiftly his claw-like hand shot out, fastened on an object that lay amidst a litter of papers, and would have lifted it, had not another slender and firm hand shot out likewise and fastened itself upon his wrist with a grasp like a vice of steel.

===Chinese===
最初那叫人瘫软的一秒钟过去，肖维兰一跃而起。他原以为又是自己燥热的幻觉在捉弄他的感官，以为那张无时无刻不纠缠着他的白日梦和不眠之夜的脸，不过是乘着想象的翅膀飞来。可那欢快的笑声、那慵懒的嗓音，都真实得不能再真实。他的仇敌当真就在这里，不是幻影，而是一个残忍的、嘲弄人的现实。他那只爪子似的手倏地探出，攥住了乱纸堆里的一件东西，眼看就要举起来——若不是另一只修长而坚定的手同样倏地探出，像钢钳一样扣住了他的手腕。

===Original===
Chauvelin had the greatest difficulty in the world to smother a cry of pain. His fingers opened, spread out fan-wise, the pistol which he had seized fell back upon the litter of papers. With a soft laugh Sir Percy sat down on the edge of the table, picked up the pistol, withdrew the charge and swept it into the sandbox close to his hand, the while Chauvelin watched him greedily, hungrily, as a caged feline might watch a prey that was beyond its reach.

===Chinese===
肖维兰好不容易才把一声痛呼硬生生咽了回去。他的手指松开，扇面似的张了出去，他攥住的那支手枪跌回乱纸堆上。珀西爵士轻轻一笑，在桌沿上坐下来，拈起手枪，退掉弹药，把火药扫进手边的沙盒里；这工夫，肖维兰贪婪地、饥渴地盯着他，活像笼中的猛兽盯着一份够不着的猎物。

===Original===
A white-faced clock on the wall struck the half-hour. Sir Percy laid the pistol down upon the table, and flicked his fine, well-shaped hands one against the other.

===Chinese===
墙上一只白面的挂钟敲了半点。珀西爵士把手枪搁在桌上，将那双纤秀漂亮的手轻轻合起来掸了一掸。

===Original===
“There now, my dear M. Chambertin,” he said gaily, “we can converse more comfortably together. Do you think it would have been wise to put a charge of powder through your humble servant? We should both of us have missed much of the zest of life.”

===Chinese===
「这就对了，亲爱的尚贝尔坦先生，」他欢快地说，「咱们如今可以舒舒服服地叙谈了。您以为，往在下身上轰一枪火药，是明智之举么？那样一来，你我两位可都要错过人生的好些滋味啦。」

===Original===
“It is always your pleasure to mock, Sir Percy,” Chauvelin said with an effort. “There are various popular sayings which I might recall to your mind, such as that the pitcher went once too often to the well.”

===Chinese===
「珀西爵士，讥嘲永远是您的乐事。」肖维兰吃力地说。「世上有各式各样的俗谚，我大可以提醒您一句，譬如说：瓦罐不离井上破。」

===Original===
“And Sir Percy once too often to visit his friend M. Chambertin, eh?”

===Chinese===
「还有珀西爵士登门太勤，也终有在朋友尚贝尔坦先生这里碰破的一遭，嗯？」

===Original===
“I think you will find that this is so,” Chauvelin rejoined trying, none too successfully, to ape his enemy’s easy familiarity. “Orange is not a healthy place for English spies these days.”

===Chinese===
「我想您会发觉，事情正是这样。」肖维兰答道，竭力想学他仇敌那副从容亲热的口吻，却学得不太成功。「如今这年头，奥朗日可不是个叫英国奸细养身保命的地方。」

===Original===
“Possibly not,” Blakeney retorted lightly. “Nor for some unfortunate children of France, I am thinking.”

===Chinese===
「兴许不是，」布莱克尼轻巧地回敬，「照我想，对法兰西某些不幸的孩子们，也不是。」

===Original===
“Traitors and spies, you are right there, Sir Percy. We have no use for them in Orange﻿—or elsewhere.”

===Chinese===
「叛徒和奸细——这一点您说对了，珀西爵士。无论在奥朗日——还是别处，我们都用不着他们。」

===Original===
“Or for honest men, eh, my friend? for chaste women and innocent children. That is why your humble servant and the league of which methinks you know a thing or two, propose to remove these from this polluted soil.”

===Chinese===
「也用不着正直的人，嗯，我的朋友？也用不着贞洁的女子和无辜的孩童。所以在下，还有那个同盟——我想您对它的底细也略知一二——才打算把这些人从这片污浊的土地上迁走。」

===Original===
Chauvelin had rested his elbow on the table. His hand shading his face against the glare of the lamp, effectually concealed its varying expressions from the keen eyes of his enemy.

===Chinese===
肖维兰把一只胳膊肘支在了桌上。那只手遮着灯光的强光，恰好把他脸上变幻不定的神情整个掩住，不让他仇敌那双锐利的眼睛瞧见。

===Original===
“You have not told me yet, Sir Percy,” he said after a few seconds’ silence, “what procures me the honour of your visit at this hour.”

===Chinese===
「您还没有告诉我呢，珀西爵士，」沉默了几秒钟之后，他说，「是什么缘故，叫我在这个时辰有幸恭迎大驾。」

===Original===
“Pure chance, my dear sir,” Blakeney replied, “though the honour is entirely mine. As a matter of fact I came to find one Armand.”

===Chinese===
「纯属碰巧，亲爱的先生，」布莱克尼答道，「不过这份荣幸全然是在下的。不瞒您说，我是来找一位阿尔芒的。」

===Original===
Twice did the pendulum of the white-faced clock tick the seconds before Chauvelin said quietly:

===Chinese===
白面挂钟的钟摆走过了两秒钟，肖维兰才悄声说：

===Original===
“My colleague? Have you business with him?”

===Chinese===
「我的同僚？您找他有事？」

===Original===
“Yes,” Blakeney replied slowly. “I have a message for him.”

===Chinese===
「是。」布莱克尼缓缓答道，「我有一封信给他。」

===Original===
“I can deliver it.”

===Chinese===
「我可以转交。」

===Original===
“Why not I? since I came on purpose.”

===Chinese===
「为什么不由我来交？我可是专程来的。」

===Original===
“My colleague is absent.”

===Chinese===
「我的同僚外出了。」

===Original===
“I can wait.”

===Chinese===
「我可以等。」

===Original===
“From whom then is the message?”

===Chinese===
「那么，这信是打哪儿来的？」

===Original===
“From his daughter.”

===Chinese===
「他女儿捎的。」

===Original===
“Ah!”

===Chinese===
「啊！」

===Original===
Once more there was a pause. The white-faced clock ticked on but the two men were silent. Chauvelin’s face was shaded by his hand, and it needed all the energy, all the strength of his will to keep that hand absolutely steady, not to allow a finger to tremble. In the other hand he held a long quill pen and with it he traced a geometrical pattern upon a blank sheet of paper. Sir Percy Blakeney, still sitting on the edge of the table watched him, motionless.

===Chinese===
又是一阵停顿。白面挂钟照旧嗒嗒地走，两个人却都不作声。肖维兰的脸掩在手的阴影里，他须得拿出全部的劲力、全部的意志，才能叫那只手绝对平稳，才能不容一根指头打颤。另一只手里，他握着一支长长的鹅毛笔，用它在一页白纸上描画着一幅几何图形。珀西·布莱克尼爵士依旧坐在桌沿上，一动不动地望着他。

===Original===
“Pretty drawing that,” he said abruptly. And with slender finger pointed to the design that grew in intricate lines under Chauvelin’s aimless pen.

===Chinese===
「这图画得漂亮。」他没头没脑地冒出一句，还伸出修长的手指，点着那幅图形——肖维兰那管漫无目的的笔底下，它的线条正越长越繁复。

===Original===
The other gave a start, the pen spluttered, scattering the ink in spots all over the paper.

===Chinese===
对方猛地一惊，笔尖一颤，墨水溅出来，在纸上洒了一片斑点。

===Original===
“There now, you have spoilt it,” Sir Percy continued lightly. “I had no idea you were such a master draughtsman.”

===Chinese===
「瞧，您把它弄坏了。」珀西爵士继续轻快地说，「我还不知道您竟是这样一位制图大师呢。」

===Original===
Chauvelin threw down his pen. He had his nerves under control at last, was able to drop his hand, to lean back in his chair, and with both hands buried in the pockets of his breeches, to throw back his head and look his enemy squarely in the face.

===Chinese===
肖维兰把笔摔了下去。他总算把心神管束住了，能够放下那只手，向后靠进椅背，双手插进马裤口袋，仰起头来，正眼直视他仇敌的脸。

===Original===
“About that message, Sir Percy,” he said with well-feigned indifference.

===Chinese===
「说说那封信吧，珀西爵士。」他说道，装出一副满不在乎的口气。

===Original===
“What about it, my dear M. Chambertin?” Blakeney rejoined lightly.

===Chinese===
「它怎么了，亲爱的尚贝尔坦先生？」布莱克尼轻快地应道。

===Original===
“My colleague, Citizen Armand, has been called away﻿—to Lyons on State business.”

===Chinese===
「我的同僚公民阿尔芒被调走了——到里昂去办国务。」

===Original===
“But how unfortunate!” Sir Percy exclaimed.

===Chinese===
「哎呀，多不凑巧！」珀西爵士惊呼道。

===Original===
“I am sending a courier to Lyons this very night.”

===Chinese===
「就在今天夜里，我要差一名信使去里昂。」

===Original===
“Too late, my dear M. Chambertin! Too late, I fear!”

===Chinese===
「太迟了，亲爱的尚贝尔坦先生！恐怕太迟了！」

===Original===
Chauvelin frowned. “What mean you by too late, Sir Percy?” he asked slowly.

===Chinese===
肖维兰皱起眉头。「您说太迟了，是什么意思，珀西爵士？」他慢吞吞地问。

===Original===
“Armand’s daughter is sick, my dear M. Chambertin,” Blakeney rejoined, speaking very slowly, as if to weigh his every word. “Before your courier can possibly reach Lyons, she will be dead.”

===Chinese===
「阿尔芒的女儿病了，亲爱的尚贝尔坦先生。」布莱克尼答道，说得极慢，仿佛在掂量每一个字。「不等您的信使赶得到里昂，她就要死了。」

===Original===
“My God!﻿—”

===Chinese===
「我的天主！——」

===Original===
It was the most heartrending cry that had ever come from a man’s throat. Chauvelin had jumped to his feet; his two hands, claw-like, as if carved in marble, gripped the arms of his chair; his knees were shaking, his pale eyes stared like those of a maniac, his cheeks were the colour of lead.

===Chinese===
那是从一个人的喉咙里发出过的最摧心裂肝的一声喊。肖维兰跳了起来；他的两只手，爪子似的，仿佛大理石雕成，死死扣住椅子的扶手；他的膝盖直打颤，淡色的眼睛瞪得像疯人的眼睛，两颊是铅一样的颜色。

===Original===
For the space of ten seconds he stood thus, with his whole body quivering, his senses reeling, his eyes fixed on those finely moulded lips that had dealt this appalling blow. Then slowly consciousness returned, a veil seemed to be lifted from before his eyes, knowledge had entered his brain. He knew that he had fallen into the trap set for him by this astute adventurer. He realized that he had betrayed the secret which he would have guarded with his life.

===Chinese===
他就这样站了足足十秒钟，浑身发抖，神智晕眩，双眼死死盯住那两片轮廓秀美的嘴唇——就是它们，发出了这记骇人的打击。随后，知觉慢慢地回来了，仿佛一道幕布从他眼前揭起，认识进了他的头脑。他知道，自己落进了这个狡狯的冒险家为他设下的圈套。他明白了，自己泄露了那个他甘愿以性命去守护的秘密。

===Original===
“So,” Sir Percy said at last very slowly, “ ’tis you are Citizen Armand, and the sweetest flower that ever bloomed in this putrid atmosphere has its roots in polluted soil?”

===Chinese===
「这么说，」珀西爵士终于开了口，说得极慢，「您就是公民阿尔芒，而在这腥臭的空气里开放过的最甜美的那朵花，根子原来扎在污浊的泥土里？」

===Original===
Still quite slowly and deliberately he drew Fleurette’s note out of the breast-pocket of his coat; for a second or two he held it lightly between slender finger and thumb, then laid it on the table in front of Chauvelin.

===Chinese===
他依旧慢慢地、不慌不忙地从外衣的胸前口袋里取出弗勒蕾特的字条，用修长的手指和拇指轻轻拈了一两秒钟，然后把它放在桌上，摆在肖维兰面前。

===Original===
“She is not sick,” he said quietly, “nor yet dying. If you have not forgotten how to pray, man, pray to God now, pray with all your might, that the same power which enabled you to torture my wife and well-nigh to break her brave spirit, will aid you to save your daughter from those tigers whom you have called your friends.”

===Chinese===
「她没有病，」他平静地说，「也没有垂危。要是您还没忘记怎样祈祷，伙计，那就现在向天主祈祷吧，拿出您全部的力气祈祷：愿当年那股让您得以折磨我的妻子、险些压垮她那勇敢灵魂的力量，如今也能帮您把您的女儿从那些猛虎口中救出来——那些老虎，您可是管他们叫朋友的。」

===Original===
Chauvelin had sunk back in the chair. His head was buried in his hands. Tumultuous thoughts rushed through his brain until he felt that his reason must be tottering. A haze was before his eyes. Perhaps it was caused by tears. Who knows? Only the recording angel mayhap. Even wild beasts cry in agony when deprived of their young.

===Chinese===
肖维兰跌坐回椅子里，头埋进了双手。纷乱的心思从他脑海里横冲直撞，直冲得他觉得自己的理性怕是要摇晃了。他眼前起了一层迷雾。兴许是泪水造成的。谁知道呢？多半只有记录天使知道。就连野兽，被夺走幼崽的时候，也要痛得哀嚎。

===Original===
Only after a few minutes did he become aware of the note penned by his little Fleurette and laid in front of him by his bitterest foe. The Scarlet Pimpernel! The only man in all the world who might perhaps have saved Fleurette, who would have saved Fleurette, if he, Chauvelin, had not betrayed the secret of his heart.

===Chinese===
过了好几分钟，他才意识到那张字条——他的小弗勒蕾特亲笔写的、被他最切齿的仇敌摆到他面前的字条。猩红繁笺花！这普天之下唯一或许救得了弗勒蕾特的人，唯一本来一定救得了弗勒蕾特的人——倘若他肖维兰没有泄露自己心底的秘密。

===Original===
Like one waking from a dream, Chauvelin picked up the note, and looked fearfully about him, dreading to meet those mocking lazy eyes, which, no doubt, at this hour gleamed with malicious triumph.

===Chinese===
肖维兰像大梦初醒一般，拾起那张字条，惶惶地环顾四周，生怕撞上那双嘲弄而慵懒的眼睛——此刻，那双眼睛里无疑正闪着恶毒的得意。

===Original===
But Sir Blakeney was no longer there.

===Chinese===
可是布莱克尼爵士已经不在了。

## XXXIII / 第三十三章

===Original===
The stage was now set for the last act of the tragedy, which the chief actor himself knew could only end one way. He had schemed and planned until he felt that his reason would give way, until he feared that he would lose the nerve and the power of which he had such sore need. He had thought of everything, weighed every possibility from the bribing of prison warders, to the suppression﻿—by murder if need be﻿—of the two witnesses Godet and Adèle. He had thought of turning the tables on Pochart and Danou, by launching accusations against them. But all these plans had to be rejected one by one. Fleurette liberated today through the success of one or the other of these schemes would only be re-arrested on the morrow. The suppression of the witnesses, the arrest of his more powerful enemies, would only rouse more bitter antagonism against himself and, failing in the end to save his Fleurette, would end in precipitating her doom.

===Chinese===
悲剧的最后一幕如今已经搭好了台，而这出戏只能有一种收场，主角本人对此心知肚明。他运筹，设谋，直谋得觉得自己的理性快要撑不住了，直怕自己会失掉那万分急需的胆魄与力量。他什么都想到了，把各种可能一一掂量过：从贿赂监狱看守，到除掉戈岱（Godet）和阿黛尔这两名证人——必要时不妨诉诸杀人。他还想过反咬波夏尔（Pochart）和达努（Danou）一口，对他们发动控告，来个反客为主。然而所有这些计划，只得一个接一个地否弃。靠其中哪一条计谋得手，弗勒蕾特今天获了释，明天也只会再度被捕。除掉证人也好，逮捕权势更大的仇敌也罢，都只会激起对他本人更刻毒的敌意，到头来救不得他的弗勒蕾特，只会加速她的毁灭。

===Original===
Driven by despair, he had at one time pinned his hopes of salvation for the child on the possible interference of the Scarlet Pimpernel, but even that fond and foolish hope had been shattered by his betrayal of his jealously guarded secret. What was there left to hope for? That his power was great enough at the Tribunal to force an acquittal in spite of the witnesses, in spite of Pochart and Danou and all the mob whom they had already gathered round them. The Public Prosecutor, a man of his own making, would not dare to side against him. But there was the populace, the rabble, the swinish multitude, who, now that even the worst type of venal and corrupt jury had been abolished, were judges and jury, advocate and prosecutor all in one. The last word always rested with them, and Pochart and Danou, egged on by envy and revenge, would know how to sway the rabble.

===Chinese===
绝望驱使之下，他一度把搭救这孩子的指望，寄托在猩红繁笺花可能出手干预上头；可就连这点又痴又傻的希望，也随着他泄露了自己严密守护的秘密而化为了泡影。还有什么可指望的呢？无非是指望他在革命法庭上的权势足够大，大到任凭那两名证人、任凭波夏尔和达努以及他们已然纠集在身边的那伙暴民作梗，也能强行断出一个无罪开释。公诉人是他一手扶植起来的人，不敢与他作对。可是还有平民，有贱民，有那帮猪猡一般的乌合之众——如今连最贪赃、最腐败的那一路陪审团都已废除，他们便一身兼任法官与陪审员、辩护人与公诉人了。最后的一锤定音，永远操在他们手里；而波夏尔和达努受着嫉妒与复仇心的撺掇，自有一套法子左右这帮贱民。

===Original===
Chauvelin was not the man to indulge in illusions. He knew well enough﻿—none better﻿—that the passions of hatred and of spite which he himself had engendered and fostered in the hearts of his fellow-men, were turned against him, as they had been turned on all the makers of this bloody revolution, on your Brissots and your Carriers, your Philippe d’Orléans, and your great Danton. They would destroy his exquisite Fleurette as effectually as they had destroyed thousands of others, equally innocent.

===Chinese===
肖维兰不是那种耽于幻象的人。他再清楚不过——天底下没有谁比他更清楚——那股他自己亲手在同胞心里播下又培育起来的仇恨与怨毒的激情，如今调转头来对准了他，正如它们当年也曾对准这场血腥革命的一切缔造者，对准你们那些布里索（Brissot）呀、卡里埃（Carrier）呀，对准你们的菲利普·德·奥尔良（Philippe d’Orléans）、你们那位了不起的丹东。他们会把他的娇美的弗勒蕾特毁掉，毁得同他们毁掉成千上万同样清白无辜的人一样彻底。

===Original===
And now the end had come. No longer could the day be put off. President Legrange and Public Prosecutor Isnard might be arriving in Paris any hour when the new aerial telegraph might be set in motion, or a courier sent down to Orange posthaste and burst the bubble of Chauvelin’s machinations.

===Chinese===
而如今，结局到了。这一天再也无法往后拖了。勒格朗热（Legrange）庭长和公诉人伊斯纳尔（Isnard）说不定哪个钟点就会抵达巴黎——一到那时，新式的空中电报（aerial telegraph）可能随之开动，或是急差十万火急地打发下来，直奔奥朗日，把肖维兰机关算尽织成的那只肥皂泡一捅而破。

===Original===
And then on that afternoon of the 15th of June two things occurred. To begin with when the Public Prosecutor placed before him the printed forms of accusation with the names left in blank, and with them a list of the names of those awaiting trial, Chauvelin with a hand that appeared quite steady, wrote in one of the blank spaces the name of Fleur Chauvelin, *nommée* Armand. Secondly when, an hour later, the captain of the guard stood in the courtyard of the Caristie house reading out the names of those who were to stand their trial on the morrow, Fleurette heard the sound of her own name.

===Chinese===
于是，六月十五日下午，发生了两件事。头一件：公诉人把姓名留白的印制控告书摆到他面前，随同还附了一份候审者的名单，肖维兰用一只看上去相当平稳的手，在其中一处空白里写下了一个名字：弗勒尔·肖维兰（Fleur Chauvelin），*nommée*（又名）阿尔芒。第二件：一个钟头之后，卫兵上尉站在卡里斯蒂（Caristie）宅的院子里，宣读次日即将出庭受审者的名单，弗勒蕾特听见了自己的名字。

===Original===
She was not frightened, nor did she weep. Tears were a thing of the past for her. Twenty days had gone by since she had been happy, more than a fortnight since she had been brought into this house and deprived of air and sunlight and joy. One by one those who had been kind to her in this prison house had gone: Claire de Châtelard, Madame de Mornas, poor Eugénie Blanc, and kind M. de Bollène. Their names had been on the roll-call. The next day they were gone, and Fleurette never saw them again. Lately she had been lonely too. No one had taken the place in her unsophisticated heart of Claire de Châtelard. The only friend she had left was the warder’s aide, the rough scavenger who had brought her the two welcome letters, Amédé’s and Bibi’s. He still continued his antics, joined in the gruesome mummeries which still went on in the common room, and Fleurette somehow had a sense of reassurance when he was nigh. But this night of all nights, after she had heard the captain of the guard read her name upon the roll-call, her grimy friend was not there. Fleurette missed him, and disappointment over his absence was the only sorrowful feeling of which she felt conscious, when she realized that her fate would be decided on the morrow.

===Chinese===
她没有害怕，也没有哭。眼泪于她已是过去的东西了。从她上一次快活算起，二十天已经过去了；从她被带进这座宅子、被夺去空气、阳光和欢乐算起，也有两个多星期了。在这座牢狱般的宅子里，待她好过的人一个接一个都走了：克莱尔·德·沙特拉尔（Claire de Châtelard）、莫尔纳夫人（Madame de Mornas）、可怜的欧仁妮·布朗（Eugénie Blanc），还有和善的德·博莱讷先生（M. de Bollène）。他们的名字上了点名（Roll-call）的名单。第二天人就不在了，弗勒蕾特从此再没能见到他们。近来她还添了一层孤单：在她那颗质朴未凿的心里，谁也没能填补克莱尔·德·沙特拉尔的空位。她仅剩的朋友，便是那个看守的帮手——粗手粗脚的打扫夫，正是他给她送来过那两封受欢迎的信，一封是阿梅代的，一封是比比的。他照旧耍他的把戏，照旧加入公共大厅里还在上演的那一出出阴森森的假面闹戏；不知怎么的，只要他在近旁，弗勒蕾特心里便觉得踏实。可是偏偏在今天这个夜里，在她听过卫兵上尉在点名名单上念出自己的名字之后，她那位满身油泥的朋友却不在。弗勒蕾特惦记他；待她明白自己的命运明天就要见分晓时，因他不在而生出的那点失望，竟是她唯一觉察得到的愁绪。

===Original===
She was not afraid. Had not Bibi enjoined her, begged her to trust him and not to be afraid? She wondered when she would be allowed to see Bibi, whether he would be there tomorrow, at her trial encouraging her with his presence and with his glance when she was made to stand before the judge. She knew that in a sense she had done wrong. She had taken Madame’s valuables and handed them over to Amédé. This she had no right to do, and since Adèle had seen her with M’sieu’ Amédé that evening, and spoken ill of her because of that, she supposed that she would be punished. It was only vaguely that she marvelled what the punishment would be. But she was not afraid because she trusted Bibi. Nor did she regret her actions. If it had all to be done over again, she would act in precisely the same way. The mysterious voice often rang in her ear even now. She had obeyed the commands of *le bon Dieu*, and it was *le bon Dieu* who had chosen a still more mysterious way for saving M’sieu’ Amédé from the consequences of her actions.

===Chinese===
她不害怕。比比不是叮嘱过她、恳求过她，叫她信他、不要害怕么？她盘算着什么时候能获准见上比比一面，盘算着他明天会不会到场——在她的庭审上，当她被带到法官跟前站着的时候，用他的身影、用他的目光给她打气。她知道，从某种意义上讲，她是做下了错事。她拿了夫人的值钱物件，交到了阿梅代手里。这事她原是无权做的；而既然那天傍晚阿黛尔撞见她同阿梅代先生在一起，还为了这个说她的坏话，她料想自己是免不了要受罚的。至于那惩罚会是个什么样子，她也只是朦朦胧胧地有点儿好奇罢了。可她不害怕，因为她信比比。她也不懊悔自己的所作所为。倘若这一切能重来一遍，她还是会分毫不差地照原样再做一回。即使到如今，那神秘的声音仍时常在她耳畔回响。她遵从了好天主（*le bon Dieu*）的诫命，而恰恰是好天主，择定了一条更加玄妙的路子，把阿梅代先生从她那些行为招来的后果里搭救了出去。

===Original===
Thus did Fleurette envisage the day that was to come, with love and trust in her heart for Bibi, and the certainty after all these trials and tabulations of a happy reunion with him and old Louise at Lou Mas.

===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特便是这样，心里怀着对比比的爱与信任，怀着历尽这一切磨难与波折之后、定能同他和老路易丝在庐庄（Lou Mas）欢然团圆的笃定，展望着即将到来的那一天。

===Original===
Not to mention the reunion with M’sieu’ Amédé.

===Chinese===
更不必说同阿梅代先生的团圆了。

## XXXIV / 第三十四章

===Original===
The first thing that struck Fleurette’s perceptions when she entered that huge room, was that up at the further end of it﻿—upon a raised platform and behind a tall desk, sat Bibi chéri himself. Two other men sat there with him, but Fleurette hardly saw them. It was on Bibi that she looked. She had slept very little during the night. Excitement had kept her awake, as well as the tears and lamentations of two of her roommates who were to appear with her this day before the tribunal.

===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特走进那间大厅，头一件触动她知觉的事便是：大厅顶里头的高台上，一张高高的案桌后面，端坐着亲爱的比比（Bibi chéri）本人。另有两个人同他坐在那里，弗勒蕾特却几乎没有看见。她一双眼睛只顾望着比比。夜里她睡得极少。兴奋使她睡不着，同室两位室友的眼泪和哀叹也叫她睡不着——这一天，她们也要同她一道上革命法庭（Revolutionary Tribunal）受审。

===Original===
And it was Bibi who was to be her judge. Well then obviously she had nothing to fear. One of some fifteen of her fellow-prisoners, she was bustled with them across the room to a wooden bench where they were roughly ordered to sit down. As they crossed the room boos and hisses, and one or two louder cries of execration, greeted them. A few remarks, all of them malevolent, rose above the murmurs.

===Chinese===
而将要给她当法官的，正是比比。那么，明摆着她没有什么可怕的了。她是大致十五名难友中的一个，同他们一道被推推搡搡地拥过大厅，来到一条木长凳前，被粗声粗气地喝令坐下。穿过大厅时，迎接他们的是一片嘘声倒彩，还有一两嗓子更响的咒骂。几句议论从嗡嗡的人语上方冒出来，句句恶毒。

===Original===
“That old man there, I knew him once. Old tyrant. He’s getting his deserts at last.”

===Chinese===
「那边那个老头子，我从前认得他。老恶霸。他到底遭上报应来了。」

===Original===
“Do you see the woman next to him? Five freeborn Frenchwomen she had at a time once, to wait on her and do her hair. Aristo, *va*! It won’t take long to do thy hair tomorrow. One snick with the scissors, what?”

===Chinese===
「你瞧见他旁边那个女人了吗？她从前一度同时使唤着五个自由出身的法国女人，伺候她，替她梳头。贵族佬，哼！明儿再替她梳头，可就用不了多大工夫啦。剪刀咔嚓一剪，是不是？」

===Original===
“That young wench too. Not much more than eighteen, I warrant.”

===Chinese===
「还有那个小丫头。我敢保，至多不过十八岁出头。」

===Original===
“I hear she is a thief as well as a traitor.”

===Chinese===
「我听说她又偷东西，又当叛贼。」

===Original===
“Pity they should have abolished the whipping-post. That would have done the young traitors a world of good.”

===Chinese===
「可惜他们竟把鞭笞柱（whipping-post）给废了。那玩意儿本可以叫这些年轻的叛贼们受益不浅呢。」

===Original===
“Me, I prefer the guillotine; quickest work, eh?”

===Chinese===
「我嘛，宁可要断头台；干起活来最麻利，是不是？」

===Original===
Fleurette had blushed with shame to the roots of her hair. She tried not to look in the direction whence these voices, harsh and coarse, had come. She tried to think of all the prayers which M. le Curé had taught her long ago. She tried to think of M’sieu’ Amédé and of the joy she would have when she saw him again. But she could not shut the gates of her consciousness against all these people who had gathered here for the sole purpose of seeing their fellow-creatures suffer. Men and women and even little children. The women for the most part had brought their knitting, for everyone was knitting socks these days for the brave soldiers who were fighting against the enemies of France, and through the murmur of voices, the monotonous click-click of the needles acted as an irritant upon the nerves.

===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特羞得连头发根都红了。她竭力不去看这些又刺耳又粗鄙的声音传来的方向。她竭力去想本堂神父很久以前教过她的那些祷告。她竭力去想阿梅代先生，想她再见到他时将有多么欢喜。可是她关不上自己意识的大门，挡不住这一群人——他们聚集到这里来，唯一的目的就是要看自己的同类受苦。男人、女人，甚至连小孩子都有。女人们多半带来了毛线活计，因为这些日子，人人都在为同法兰西的敌人作战的勇敢士兵们织袜子；而在一片嗡嗡的人语底下，织针那单调的咔嗒咔嗒声不住地磨着人的神经。

===Original===
All around there appeared to be a sea of faces. And eyes. Innumerable eyes that glared, and mouths that grinned and derided. And above the faces, a sea of red caps with tricolour cockades. Fleurette tried hard not to look. She closed her eyes and tried to murmur the prayers she and M’sieu’ Amédé used to say together when M. le Curé prepared them for their first communion.

===Chinese===
四下里望去，只见一片脸的海洋。还有眼睛。数不清怒瞪着的眼睛，还有一张张咧着讪笑嘲弄的嘴。脸海之上，又是一片缀着三色帽徽的红帽子的海洋。弗勒蕾特竭力不去看。她闭上眼睛，试着低声念诵那些祷告——当年本堂神父为他们俩预备初领圣体时，她同阿梅代先生常在一块儿念的。

===Original===
Bibi wore a hat with feathers. He had a bell in front of him, and this he often tinkled, when the noise from the crowd all around became too great. Once or twice he was addressed as “Citizen President.” Fleurette had never seen him look so stern. The words which he spoke to the accused were not only bitter but terribly cruel. He seemed so unlike her real chéri Bibi, that she caught herself marvelling whether her fancy was not playing her aching eyes some strange and horrible trick.

===Chinese===
比比戴着一顶插羽毛的帽子。他面前摆着一只铃，四周人群的喧声闹得太大时，他便常常把铃摇得叮当响。有一两次，人们称呼他『公民庭长』。弗勒蕾特从来没有见过他神色这样严厉。他对被告们说的话，不但刻毒，而且狠得可怕。他同她真正的、亲爱的比比如此不相像，弄得她不由暗自诧异：莫不是她的幻觉，正对着她酸痛的眼睛耍什么奇怪而可怕的花招？

===Original===
One after the other the names of her fellow-prisoners were called, and one by one they were made to stand up and then walk to the centre of the room and up a couple of shallow steps to a small raised platform round which there was a wooden railing. In every instance as soon as the prisoner mounted this platform, and became as it were the centre of attraction for all these innumerable eyes, he or she would be greeted with groans and hisses and cat’s calls, until Bibi tinkled his bell and loudly demanded silence.

===Chinese===
难友们的名字一个接一个地被点到，他们便一个个被迫站起身，走到大厅中央，再登上两级浅浅的台阶，上一座小小的高台——台子四周围着木栏杆。每逢有囚犯登上这座高台，俨然成了这无数只眼睛齐齐注视的中心，迎接他或她的，便是一片哼声、嘘声和倒彩，直到比比摇响铃铛，大声喝令肃静。

===Original===
A man in a red cap who sat just below Bibi’s desk then stood up and read something out aloud, which Fleurette never understood, but which the crowd apparently did, for the reader was frequently interrupted by more boos and hisses and often cries of execration. After this reading Bibi, or one of the two men who sat beside him, asked the prisoner questions. These were sometimes replied to, but not always. The crowd invariably threw in loud comments on both questions and answers, and Bibi was then forced to tinkle his bell in order to demand silence. And through the noise, the cries and the hisses, the questions and answers, the one sound that was never drowned, and never was still, was the click-click of hundreds of knitting needles.

===Chinese===
随后，一个坐在比比案桌正下方的红帽子男人站起来，高声宣读一件什么东西。弗勒蕾特从来听不懂那是什么，可人群显然听得懂，因为宣读的人不时被打断——又是一片嘘声倒彩，还常常夹着咒骂。读完之后，比比，或者坐在他身旁那两个人中的一个，便向囚犯发问。这些问话有时有人回答，有时没有。人群照例要对问话和答话都高声插上一番议论，于是比比又不得不摇响铃铛，喝令肃静。而在这一切喧闹、呼喊与嘘声、问话与答话之中，唯有一种声音从不曾被淹没，也从来不曾停歇——那便是几百根织针咔嗒咔嗒的声响。

===Original===
The first batch of prisoners to face the Tribunal, were men and women almost unknown to Fleurette. They had not long been brought into the Caristie house, had replaced others who had been Fleurette’s early companions in prison. She had seen them in the common room, acting in the grim farces that were the fashion there, but she had not made friends with them as she had done with Claire de Châtelard or Madame de Mornas. But when came the turn of a woman who had actually been her roommate, who had sat next to her on the bench of the accused, and squeezed her hand ere she was led up to the raised platform with the wooden railing, then, Fleurette felt all her resolution of bravery and trust in Bibi, giving way.

===Chinese===
头一批被提上革命法庭的囚犯，是些弗勒蕾特几乎不认识的男男女女。他们进卡里斯蒂（Caristie）宅子不久，顶替了另一些人——那些人原是弗勒蕾特入狱之初的同伴。她在公共大厅里见过他们，在那些那里时兴的阴惨闹戏里扮演角色；可她不曾同他们结下交情，不像同克莱尔·德·沙特拉尔（Claire de Châtelard）或莫尔纳夫人（Madame de Mornas）那样。可是，当轮到一个真正同她合住一室的女人时——那女人方才还同她并排坐在被告席上，在被带上那座围木栏杆的高台之前，还紧紧握过她的手——弗勒蕾特便觉得，自己那一腔勇敢的决心，还有对比比的信任，全都垮了下去。

===Original===
The heat in the room had become unbearable. The stench of dank and grimy clothes, of perspiring humanity, of hot breaths charged with hate, acted as a pungent soporific. Fleurette’s head fell forward once or twice, her eyes involuntarily closed. For a time she lost consciousness. It was her own name spoken in a stentorian voice that brought her back to reality.

===Chinese===
大厅里的热气已变得叫人受不了。那股恶臭——阴潮垢腻的衣裳、汗涔涔的人群、饱含仇恨的火热呼吸——像一味刺鼻的催眠药，熏得人昏昏欲睡。弗勒蕾特的头有一两次垂了下去，眼睛不由自主地合上了。有一阵子，她失去了知觉。把她拉回现实中来的，是一个洪钟般的嗓音喊出了她自己的名字。

===Original===
“Fleur Chauvelin, *nommée* Armand.”

===Chinese===
「弗勒尔·肖维兰（Fleur Chauvelin），*nommée*（又名）阿尔芒。」

===Original===
Someone nudged her elbow. An impatient voice rasped out a sharp: “*Allons! allons!*” and she found herself dragged to her feet and led by the arm to the raised platform, amidst a din which fortunately was too great to allow her ears to catch individual sounds.

===Chinese===
有人用胳膊肘捅了捅她。一个不耐烦的嗓音厉声甩出一句尖利的「得啦！得啦！（*Allons! allons!*）」，她便觉着自己已被拽起身来，被人抓着胳膊带上那座高台，耳边一片鼎沸——幸而那喧闹大得使她的耳朵捕捉不到任何单个的声音。

===Original===
She looked straight across to Bibi, who was as pale as a waxen image.

===Chinese===
她直直地望向比比，比比的脸色白得像一尊蜡像。

===Original===
“Fleur Chauvelin, *nommée* Armand.”

===Chinese===
「弗勒尔·肖维兰，*nommée*（又名）阿尔芒。」

## XXXV / 第三十五章

===Original===
There is no doubt that everything would have gone well, had it not been for Fleurette herself. Perhaps “well” is the wrong word: “differently” would be better. Nothing could have gone “well,” because even though Chauvelin had succeeded in obtaining an acquittal, his enemies would have returned immediately to the charge, and forced on the girl’s re-arrest even before she had left the Tribunal. There had been cases during the past few weeks, in Paris, in Lyons and so on, when prisoners were acquitted and re-arrested, retried, acquitted again, and again re-arrested. A regular cat-and-mouse game, at which Chauvelin himself was an adept. Nevertheless with a first acquittal there might have been some hope. And he practically had obtained that acquittal, when Fleurette herself ruined her chance, and caused her own condemnation. Chauvelin could have struck her for her folly. His love for her always pertained to that of a wild beast for its young; the instinct to devour in moments of peril. If she was destined to perish, then it should be by his own hand, not as a spectacle for the rabble to gloat on.

===Chinese===
毫无疑问，倘若不是弗勒蕾特本人从中作梗，一切本来都会顺顺当当地过去。也许「顺顺当当」这个词用得不妥，换作「换个样子」更贴切些。本来就没有什么事能「顺顺当当」，因为即便肖维兰果真争取到了一纸开释，他的仇敌们也会立刻卷土重来，在那姑娘还没走出法庭大门之前，就逼着把她重新逮捕。过去几个星期里，巴黎、里昂等地就有过这样的先例：囚犯被开释了又被捕，再审再放，放了再捕。十足一场猫捉老鼠的把戏，而肖维兰本人正是此道的老手。话虽如此，头一回开释到底还存着几分指望。而他也实实在在就要把那纸开释弄到手了，偏偏是弗勒蕾特自己毁了自己的生路，亲手促成了对自己的判决。为了她的愚蠢，肖维兰简直恨不得掴她一巴掌。他对她的爱，始终脱不出野兽疼崽子的路数：一到危难关头，便生出把它吞吃下肚的本能。倘若她注定要死，那也该死在他自己手里，而不是充当一出让暴民们看了称快的戏。

===Original===
The *Moniteur* of the 22nd Messidor gives one or two interesting details concerning the trial of a country girl named Fleur Chauvelin, daughter of a Citizen Armand Chauvelin of the Central Committee of Public Safety, and member of the National Convention, and relates at full length the extraordinary incidents which marked its close. Looking back upon that memorable day, and on the solemn hour which saw the girl Fleur Chauvelin *nommée* Armand called to the bar of the accused, we visualize Chauvelin the father, presiding over that Tribunal of infamy, and having sent within the last half-hour half a dozen fellow-creatures callously to death, now seeing his own daughter, the only being in all the world whom he had ever loved, standing there before him, accused, condemned already in the eyes of the canaille.

===Chinese===
穗月（Messidor）二十二日的《导报》（*Moniteur*）记载了一两处有趣的细节，是关于一个名叫弗勒尔·肖维兰（Fleur Chauvelin）的乡下姑娘的审判——她是中央公安委员会（Central Committee of Public Safety）的公民阿尔芒·肖维兰、国民公会成员的女儿——并绘声绘色地记述了审判收尾时那几桩离奇的变故。今日回顾那个难忘的日子、那个庄严的时辰——姑娘弗勒尔·肖维兰，官册上唤作「阿尔芒家的」（*nommée* Armand），被传到被告栏前——我们眼前便浮现出那位身为人父的肖维兰：他主持着这座恶名昭彰的法庭，前半个钟头里刚麻木不仁地把六条人命送上死路，此刻却眼睁睁看着自己的亲生女儿、这普天之下他唯一爱过的人，站在他面前受审，在那帮暴民眼里早已被判了死罪。

===Original===
There was no time wasted during the proceedings, wherein the accused was allowed neither jury nor advocate. The State as represented by its three nominees who sat as judges, was judge and jury and prosecutor all in one. It was men like Chauvelin who had invented this travesty of justice and eliminated all procedure devised by civilization for the protection of the accused.

===Chinese===
审判过程没有任何耽搁：被告既不许有陪审团，也不许有辩护人。国家由它指派的三名坐堂法官作代表，一身兼任法官、陪审团和公诉人。正是肖维兰这号人发明了这套对正义的滑稽模仿，把文明社会为保护被告而创设的一切程序扫荡得干干净净。

===Original===
The Public Prosecutor opened the proceedings by reading the indictment in mechanical monotone; it was identically the same as that framed against hundreds of others﻿—guilty or innocent alike﻿—the printed formula invented by the odious Foucquier-Tinville in which the words “Traitor,” and “Enemy of the Republic” were alone intelligible. All else was a jumble of words. The crowd was not listening. Their attention was fixed on the accused whose modest bearing and spotless attire seemed to arouse their spite and their derision, more than the rags and filth displayed by a previous prisoner had done.

===Chinese===
公诉人以机械的单调腔调宣读起诉书，开了庭。这份起诉书同安到成百上千人头上的一模一样——有罪的和无罪的统统一样——是那个可憎的富基耶-坦维尔（Foucquier-Tinville）发明的印好的套话，其中只有「叛徒」和「共和国的敌人」几个字还听得明白。其余全是乱七八糟的文字堆砌。人群并没有在听。他们的注意力全钉在被告身上：她那端庄的举止、一尘不染的衣着，似乎比先前一名囚犯显出的破烂和肮脏更加激起他们的怨毒和讪笑。

===Original===
When the reading of the indictment came to an end, Pochart sitting beside the Presiding Judge asked the usual question:

===Chinese===
起诉书念完之后，坐在庭长旁边的波夏尔（Pochart）照例发问：

===Original===
“Is the prisoner accused publicly or in secret?”

===Chinese===
「被告是被公开告发，还是秘密告发？」

===Original===
And the Public Prosecutor replied: “Publicly.”

===Chinese===
公诉人答道：「公开告发。」

===Original===
Danou, the third judge then asked: “By whom?”

===Chinese===
第三名法官达努（Danou）接着问：「告发人是哪几位？」

===Original===
And again the Public Prosecutor gave reply:

===Chinese===
公诉人又答道：

===Original===
“By one Adèle,” he said, “of unknown parentage, and by Citizen Lieutenant Godet of the revolutionary army.”

===Chinese===
「一个是来历不明的阿黛尔（Adèle），」他说，「一个是革命军的公民中尉戈岱（Godet）。」

===Original===
“And to what will these persons testify?”

===Chinese===
「这两个人要证明什么呢？」

===Original===
“To the treason committed against the State by the accused and to her connection with the enemies of the Republic.”

===Chinese===
「证明被告对国家犯了叛国罪，证明她勾结共和国的敌人。」

===Original===
After which Adèle was called. Her small ratlike face looked wan and pinched; her hands trembled visibly, and she wiped them continually against the ragged apron which she wore. She was obviously very nervous and never looked once in the direction of the accused, but she spoke clearly enough in a shrill, high-pitched voice. Questioned at first by the Public Prosecutor, she presently embarked more glibly upon her story, relating the events which were intended to condemn Fleurette. Chauvelin already knew the tale by heart. The soldiers on the bridge. The raid on the château. Fleurette’s halt that evening in the cottage of the widow Tronchet. Her assignation, through Adèle, with Amédé Colombe. The casket and wallet underneath her shawl, then transferred into young Colombe’s keeping.

===Chinese===
随后便传唤阿黛尔。她那张老鼠似的小脸看上去灰败而干瘪；两只手抖得肉眼可见，一个劲儿地在身上那条破围裙上擦来擦去。她显然紧张极了，一次也没有朝被告的方向望过，不过说起话来倒还清楚，嗓音又尖又高。起初由公诉人发问，她很快便顺溜地讲开了她那套故事，把那桩桩件件足以给弗勒蕾特定罪的事情一一抖落出来。这个故事肖维兰早已烂熟于心。桥上的士兵。庄园里的搜捕。弗勒蕾特那天傍晚在特龙谢寡妇（the widow Tronchet）小屋里的歇脚。她通过阿黛尔的传话，同阿梅代·科隆布（Amédé Colombe）的幽会。藏在她披巾底下的匣子和钱夹，后来又转到小科隆布手里保管。

===Original===
Ofttimes Chauvelin tried to break into the girl’s narrative; he put stern questions to her, tried to intimidate her, to trip her into misstatements of obvious contradictions. But Adèle held her ground. Informer, ingrate, wanton though she was, she was speaking the truth and was not to be shaken. Hisses and boos from the crowd oft greeted the President’s cross-questionings, cries of approbation greeted Adèle’s spirited rejoinders. In the wordy warfare between herself and Chauvelin, she scored nearly every time. Encouraged by the sympathy of the rabble, she lost her nervousness, whilst he gradually lost his self-control. He had so much at stake, and she nothing but the satisfaction of vanity and of spite.

===Chinese===
肖维兰几次三番想打断姑娘的叙述；他向她提出严厉的质问，想吓唬她，想套她出错、说出明显的破绽来。可阿黛尔稳稳站住了脚。告密的、忘恩负义的、放荡的——她尽管是这样一个人，此刻说的却是实话，撼动不得。庭长每一次盘问，人群里常报以嘘声和倒彩；阿黛尔针锋相对的回嘴，倒赢得阵阵喝彩。在她同肖维兰这场唇枪舌剑里，十次倒有九次是她占上风。有暴民的同情撑腰，她的紧张一扫而空，他却渐渐沉不住气了。他押上的东西太多太多，而她除了虚荣心和小怨毒得到满足之外，一无所有。

===Original===
“Be not intimidated, citizeness,” Pochart put in forcefully at one moment, “let not powerful influences sway you from your duty.”

===Chinese===
「不要害怕，女公民，」波夏尔有一刻厉声插话道，「不要让权势的影响使你背离自己的职责。」

===Original===
“*Vas-y* Adèle of unknown parentage!” one of the women shouted from above. “ ’Twas some aristo doubtless who betrayed thy mother. Let this aristo at least pay for her kind.”

===Chinese===
「*Vas-y*（说下去呀），来历不明的阿黛尔！」一个女人从人群上头喊道。「当年坑害你娘的，八成是哪个贵族佬。如今就叫这个贵族佬，好歹也替她那一类人还还债吧。」

===Original===
Amidst thunderous applause Adèle stepped down from the bar. Chauvelin tried in vain to command silence, he was shouted down by the crowd.

===Chinese===
阿黛尔在雷鸣般的掌声中走下证人席。肖维兰想喝令肃静，却是枉然，他被人群的吼声压了下去。

===Original===
“Thou’rt a true patriot, Citizen Chauvelin,” one woman called out lustily. “To have a traitor for a daughter is a curse. Her death will not be for thee a sacrifice.”

===Chinese===
「你才是真正的爱国志士呢，肖维兰公民，」一个女人扯着嗓子喊道。「养个叛徒女儿是造孽。她的死对你算不得什么牺牲。」

===Original===
He waited in seeming patience, white to the lips, until the tumult had subsided, then calling all his reserves of strength, moral and mental, to his aid, he said in a calm firm voice:

===Chinese===
他表面上耐着性子等待，嘴唇都发了白，直到骚动平息下去，才把道德上和精神上的全部后备力气都调集起来，用平静而坚定的声音说道：

===Original===
“The witness has lied. The events which she has described could not have taken place in her presence seeing that on that day and at that hour she was in my house, at Lou Mas, half a league away.”

===Chinese===
「证人撒了谎。她所描述的那些事情，她根本不可能在场目睹，因为那一天、那一刻，她正在我家里——在半里格之外的庐庄（Lou Mas）。」

===Original===
This pronouncement was greeted with mighty uproar. Derisive laughter, cat’s calls, whistling, strident shouts made riotous confusion. Only two persons in the crowded room appeared serene. One was the accused, the other her judge. The *Moniteur* says that throughout the whole proceedings the attitude of the accused was astonishingly calm: “*d’une sérénité étonnante.*” She looked straight before her, sometimes at the President, but more often her eyes appeared to be fixed on the tricolour flag draped over the wall above his head, and ornamented with a red cap and the words writ largely: “*Liberté, Egalité, Fraternité ou la Mort.*”

===Chinese===
这句话一出，顿时掀起轩然大波。嘲弄的大笑、怪叫、口哨、尖利的呼喊，乱成一团。济济一堂的人里，只有两个看上去泰然自若：一个是被告，一个是她的审判官。《导报》说，整个审判过程中，被告的态度镇定得出奇：「*d’une sérénité étonnante*」（镇定得令人惊奇）。她直视前方，偶尔看看庭长，更多时候，她的目光似乎牢牢钉在他头顶上方悬着的那面三色旗上——旗上缀着一顶红帽，写着几个大字：「*Liberté, Egalité, Fraternité ou la Mort*」（自由、平等、博爱，否则死亡）。

===Original===
And so too was the President equally serene. Outwardly. He stood upright whilst the turmoil continued, with head erect and hands held behind his back. Insults and jeers flew at him from every side. But he never winced. The rabble called him, “Traitor, Liar, Tyrant!” and various other names impossible to record. But he waited in seeming patience, until the crowd, eager to hear more, fell to comparative stillness once more. Then Pochart’s rasping voice cut through the silence, like the sound of a file against metal.

===Chinese===
庭长也同样镇定。至少表面上是。骚乱持续期间，他一直笔直地站着，昂着头，双手背在身后。侮辱和讥诮从四面八方向他飞来，可他纹丝不动。暴民们骂他「叛徒！骗子！暴君！」还骂了许多没法记录的名字。但他表面上仍耐着性子等，直到人群急于听下文，重新相对安静下来。这时波夏尔那刺耳的嗓音划破寂静，好似锉刀锉在金属上。

===Original===
“You’ll have to substantiate that statement, Citizen President,” he said.

===Chinese===
「你这番话可得拿出实据来，公民庭长。」他说。

===Original===
“My statements need no substantiation,” Chauvelin retorted coolly. “The word of a representative of the people is sufficient against any witness.”

===Chinese===
「我的话不需要实据，」肖维兰冷冷地回敬道，「人民代表的一句话，压倒任何证人绰绰有余。」

===Original===
And while Pochart was considering a suitable repartee, Danou put in smoothly:

===Chinese===
波夏尔还在斟酌一句得体的反唇相讥，达努已温吞吞地插了进来：

===Original===
“Should we not hear the next witness, Citizen Lieutenant Godet, before we discuss the matter?”

===Chinese===
「咱们何不先听听下一位证人，公民中尉戈岱的证词，再来讨论这件事呢？」

===Original===
“Yes, yes!” the crowd yelled in response.

===Chinese===
「对，对！」人群狂喊着应和。

===Original===
Scenting the unusual, the crowd was more excited than was its wont. Of late these hasty trials, six to the hour, with condemnation as a foregone conclusion, had become monotonous. One condemnation had been very much like another. But here was something novel. The rumour had already spread like wildfire that the accused was no less than the daughter of the President, Citizen Chauvelin, who was well known in the councils of State, a prominent member of many committees, and, some said, a personal friend of the great Robespierre. Here in truth was a test of supreme patriotism; a judge called upon to condemn his own daughter if she be guilty. And of course she was guilty, or she would not be here. There was no sympathy for either of them, only interest in the issue of this amazing trial. The crowd did not like the prisoner’s attitude, what they called her aristocratic airs and disdainful ways; even the children pointed grimy little fingers at her and hurled the poisonous darts of loathsome epithets at the aristo.

===Chinese===
嗅出了一点不寻常的味道，人群比平日兴奋得多。近来这种草草的审判，一个钟头六场，定罪早成定局，已经变得单调乏味。一场定罪同另一场没什么两样。可眼下出了新鲜事。谣言早已野火般传开：被告不是别人，正是庭长肖维兰公民的女儿——此人在国务枢机里赫赫有名，身兼好几个委员会的要职，有人说，还是伟大的罗伯斯庇尔（Robespierre）的私交。这才真真是对爱国至诚的考验：一个法官，倘使女儿有罪，就得判自己亲生女儿的罪。她当然有罪，不然也不会站在这儿了。对父女两个，人群都没有半点怜悯，只有对这场惊人审判结局的兴味。人群不喜欢这囚犯的做派，不喜欢他们所谓的她那贵族派头和傲慢样子；连孩子们也伸出污黑的小指头指着她，朝那贵族佬掷去一支支恶毒咒骂的毒箭。

===Original===
Thus was the scene prepared for the entrance of Lieutenant Godet, who stepped up to the witness’ platform with a display of self-assurance and a swagger that charmed the women. He was a man after their own heart, a real sans culotte in grimy rags, unkempt, unshaved, unwashed, the type of which the martyr Jean Paul Marat had been the most perfect exponent.

===Chinese===
于是，场景便这样铺排停当，单等戈岱中尉登场了。他踏上证人台，那副自信满满、大摇大摆的架势很讨女人们的欢心。他是正合她们心意的那种人，一个真正的无套裤汉（sans culotte）：破衣烂衫满是油垢，蓬头垢面，胡子不刮，脸也不洗——殉道者让-保罗·马拉（Jean Paul Marat）正是这一路人物最完美的代表。

===Original===
Conversations, objurgations, murmurs even were stilled; the click-click of knitting needles alone made a soft accompaniment to Citizen Godet’s replies to the Public Prosecutor’s preliminary questions. It was indeed a remarkable, an amazing, an almost unbelievable tale, which he had to tell. And gradually as he unfolded the various details of this extraordinary adventure a hush fell over the crowded room, very like the calm which nature assumes ere she sends forth the thunders of her wrath.

===Chinese===
谈话声、呵斥声、连窃窃私语都静了下来；只有毛线针咔嗒咔嗒的轻响，给公民戈岱回答公诉人初步讯问的声音作伴。他要讲的故事，实在离奇、惊人、几乎叫人不敢相信。而随着他一点点展开这段奇遇的种种细节，一片肃静渐渐罩住了满堂的人群，很像大自然在发出雷霆之怒前装出的那副平静。

===Original===
Godet, still with this air of self-assurance, related how he and the soldiers under his command, as well as the whole commune of Laragne had been tricked by a band of English spies whose actions proved them to have been in league with Amédé Colombe and with the accused. He told of the magnificently dressed soldiers. Their raid on the premises of Colombe the grocer of the Rue Haute. Their march through the village. Their captain’s swagger. His orders to himself, Godet, and to the real soldiers of the revolutionary army.

===Chinese===
戈岱仍旧带着那副自信的神气，讲述他和他麾下的士兵、连同拉拉涅（Laragne）全镇上下，如何被一伙英国奸细愚弄了，那伙人的行径证明他们同阿梅代·科隆布和被告串通一气。他讲到那群衣着华丽的士兵。讲到他们突袭上街（Rue Haute）杂货商科隆布的铺面。讲到他们穿过村子的行军。讲到他们那位上尉的趾高气扬。讲到他对戈岱本人、对革命军的真正士兵们发号施令。

===Original===
Still the crowd gave no sign of approbation, or disapprobation. Only that ominous, expectant hush which presaged a storm. The accused, always serene, smiled﻿—so the *Moniteur* avers﻿—as she encountered the President’s glance. Smiled cheerfully and trustfully. But the President’s face was inscrutable, and the colour of wax.

===Chinese===
人群仍没有表示赞成，也没有表示反对。只有那不祥的、有所期待的屏息，预示着一场暴风雨。被告始终镇定，与庭长的目光相遇时，她笑了——《导报》可以作证——笑得欢快，笑得坦然。可庭长的脸深不可测，颜色如蜡。

===Original===
And then Godet went on to relate the long, weary tramp along the mountain roads. The dust. The fatigue. The want of food. He told how ci-devant Frontenac and Amédé Colombe wrested from the hands of justice, were presently taken to some unknown place of safety, while the soldiers of the Republic were left by the wayside, to perish of fatigue or inanition.

===Chinese===
接着，戈岱又讲到山路上那漫长难挨的跋涉。尘土。劳顿。断粮。他讲到旧贵族（ci-devant）弗朗特纳克和阿梅代·科隆布如何从正义之手夺了去，转眼被送到什么不知名的安全地方，而共和国的士兵却被丢在路边，任他们累死饿死。

===Original===
He had finished speaking, and still the click-click of the knitting needles was the only sound that broke the silence. The witness, sensing this silence, feeling its menace, had lost something of his arrogance; the hand with which he stroked his shaggy moustache trembled perceptibly. The accused, overcome by the heat, wiped her forehead with the corner of her apron, then she smiled once more across at her father.

===Chinese===
他讲完了，打破寂静的仍旧只有毛线针的咔嗒声。证人觉出了这份寂静，嗅出了其中的威胁，那份神气已丢了几分；他捋着乱蓬蓬小胡子的那只手，抖得看得分明。被告被暑气熏得难受，用围裙角擦了擦额头，随后又朝她父亲笑了笑。

===Original===
And suddenly through the solemn stillness a woman’s shrill voice was raised:

===Chinese===
突然，一片庄重的死寂中，响起一个女人尖利的嗓音：

===Original===
“Those English spies did make a fool of thee, I am thinking, Citizen Godet!”

===Chinese===
「我说呀，那伙英国奸细可真把你耍了个够，戈岱公民！」

===Original===
This suddenly relieved the tension. It was like a dam let loose. In a moment every kind of call and of cry, of laughter and of groan rang from end to end of the room.

===Chinese===
这一下突然泄了绷紧的劲儿。好比闸门一下子打开。转眼间，各种叫喊、呼号、大笑、呻吟，响彻大厅的两头。

===Original===
“The English have made a fool of thee!”

===Chinese===
「英国人把你耍了！」

===Original===
Within a minute or two this became a general cry, accompanied by the stamping of feet, and loud and prolonged laughter, both malevolent and derisive. Godet, ludicrous in his bewilderment, rolled terror-filled eyes, whilst vainly trying to raise his voice above the din. The *Moniteur* says definitely that the accused put her hands to her ears. The uproar was in truth deafening.

===Chinese===
不消一两分钟，这成了一片齐声的呐喊，伴着跺脚声，伴着响亮而持久的、又恶毒又嘲弄的大笑。戈岱张皇失措，样子可笑，瞪着充满恐怖的眼睛，枉费力气想抬高嗓门压过喧腾。《导报》言之凿凿地说，被告当时用双手捂住了耳朵。那喧嚣实在震耳欲聋。

===Original===
A few moments of this confusion, and the next, Chauvelin was on his feet clanging his bell. His stentorian voice rose above the tumult, demanded silence, and in the lull that presently ensued, that same voice now subdued to a lower, though no less impressive key, rang clear and calm.

===Chinese===
这样乱了一阵，紧接着，肖维兰霍地站起，把手铃摇得叮当响。他洪亮的嗓音盖过喧嚣，喝令肃静；待随后出现间歇，还是那个嗓音，如今压低了几度，却依旧威严十足，清晰而平静地响起来。

===Original===
“Is it not an insult, citizen patriots, to ask you to listen to the words of a fool, when the life of a French girl is at stake?”

===Chinese===
「公民们，爱国志士们，当一个法国姑娘的性命悬于一线之际，还要请你们听一个蠢货的胡言，这岂不是对你们的侮辱？」

===Original===
The passionate earnestness with which he spoke, the burning indignation expressed in that calm, subdued voice, had the effect of awing the screaming rabble. They turned to gaze on him, as he stood there, facing them all, calm, proud, almost majestic, despite his small stature. Seizing this sudden advantage he began to speak. Without a gesture, hardly raising his voice, he began quietly, not choosing his words, or striving after eloquence, but only as a man speaking to his friends. And by one of those inexplicable reactions which will so often change the temper of a crowd, men, women and children ceased to curse and to deride. The innumerable eyes were fixed with more curiosity than malevolence upon him, the mouths, agape, uttered no further groan, and once more the click-click of knitting needles was momentarily stilled.

===Chinese===
他说话时那股热烈的诚恳，那平静低沉的嗓音里透出的熊熊义愤，竟把这群尖声喊叫的暴民震慑住了。众人转过脸来望着他：他站在那里，面对着他们全体，镇定、高傲、几乎称得上威严，全然不顾自己身材矮小。他抓住这突如其来的优势，开口说话了。不做手势，几乎不提高嗓门，他平平静静地开了头，不雕琢字句，不追求雄辩，只像一个汉子在同朋友们谈心。于是，凭着那种常常莫名改变人群心性的不可思议的反转，男人、女人和孩子都不再咒骂、不再嘲弄了。无数双眼睛盯着他，好奇多过了恶意；一张张张开的嘴，不再发出嘘声；毛线针的咔嗒声，也再一次暂时停歇了。

===Original===
“Citizens,” he said, “you have heard two witnesses against the accused. One of these, the wench Adèle I myself, representative of the people, have convicted of deliberate falsehood, spoken to the prejudice of a French patriot. The other your own words have condemned for a fool, and an easy tool in the hands of English spies. You called him a fool, citizens, but I call him a traitor. Lieutenant Godet was not a tool in the hands of the English spies, he was their confederate, their help. Can you bring yourselves to believe, citizens, that a loyal soldier of the Republic could be deceived by false uniforms, by French words spoken by alien lips? Can you believe this story of a forced march, of starvation by the wayside in the company of English spies whose every action, every word, every gesture almost, must have betrayed them as the foreigners they actually were. Citizens, I appeal to that reputation for clear thinking and for logic, for which French men and women are famous throughout the world. At this hour when our beloved country is threatened on every side, is this the time, I say, for allowing yourselves to be duped by traitors who would sell you and your land, your dues and your liberty for English gold﻿—?”

===Chinese===
「公民们，」他说，「你们已经听过了指控被告的两个证人。其中一个，那个阿黛尔丫头，我以人民代表的身份，已经证实她存心撒谎，诬害了一个法国爱国志士。另一个，你们自己的嘴已经给他定了罪：是个蠢货，是英国奸细手里得心应手的工具。你们叫他蠢货，公民们，我却叫他叛徒。戈岱中尉不是英国奸细手里的工具，他是他们的同伙，他们的帮凶。公民们，你们能叫自己相信吗——共和国的一个忠诚士兵，竟会被假军服骗住，被外国人的嘴唇说出的法国话骗住？你们能相信这套强行军、被丢在路边挨饿的鬼话吗——同行的可是英国奸细，他们的一举一动、一言一语、几乎每个手势，都必定要暴露他们外国佬的真面目。公民们，我诉诸你们那以思路清晰、逻辑严密闻名的声誉，法兰西的男男女女正是以此闻名全世界的。此时此刻，我们可爱的祖国四面受敌，我说，难道正当此时，反倒听任叛徒们把你们、把你们的土地、你们的权利和自由，统统出卖给英国的黄金吗——？」

===Original===
“No! no!” came a lusty shout in response. And the crowd took up the cry. “No! We’ll not sell our liberties for English gold.”

===Chinese===
「不！不！」响应他的是一声响亮的呐喊。人群随即接过了这口号。「不！我们绝不出卖自由，去换英国的黄金。」

===Original===
“Say on, citizen representative.”

===Chinese===
「说下去，公民代表。」

===Original===
Pochart had jumped to his feet; once or twice he had tried to break in on Chauvelin’s peroration, with cries of: “Thou’rt slandering a soldier of the Republic!” or: “Traitor! thou’rt in league with thy daughter!”

===Chinese===
波夏尔早已跳了起来；肖维兰这篇宏论进行当中，他一两次想插嘴，喊些什么「你诽谤共和国的士兵！」或者「叛徒！你和你女儿串通一气！」

===Original===
But he was not listened to. There was something about Chauvelin which fascinated the mob. His white, calm face, his pale, piercing eyes, his voice, dull, even monotonous, but penetrating to the most distant corners of the room. And there was also that welcome element of novelty.
