This pleased the women. Trials and condemnations in incessant routine had begun to pall. Here was something new. Witnesses summoned, then discredited, and finally accused. Such a thing had never been witnessed before in Orange.

===Chinese===
可是没有一个人听他的。肖维兰身上有种东西，把这群暴民迷住了。他那张苍白、平静的脸，那双苍白而锐利的眼睛，他的嗓音——平板、近乎单调，却能穿透到大厅最远的角落。再者，还有那讨人喜欢的新鲜劲儿。这正合女人们的心意。审判和定罪没完没了的例行公事，早就叫人腻烦了。这里有了新花样：证人传上来，随即被拆穿，最后反倒成了被告。这样的事，奥朗日还从未见过。

===Original===
And so the crowd would not listen to Pochart or Danou, they wanted to hear Chauvelin; they did not particularly wish to see Fleurette *nommée* Armand acquitted, but they did relish the prospect of the two witnesses being sent to the bench of the accused. That was novelty for them, and it was what they wanted for the moment. Moreover they did think that the citizen lieutenant with all his swagger had been such a consummate fool, if no worse, that it would be distinctly amusing to see that stupid head of his roll down into the basket of the guillotine.

===Chinese===
于是人群不肯听波夏尔和达努的，他们要听肖维兰的。弗勒蕾特——官册上唤作「阿尔芒家的」——开释不开释，他们倒不特别指望；可两位证人被送上被告席的前景，他们着实受用。对他们来说这是新鲜事，也正是他们眼下想要的。况且他们也当真认为，那位公民中尉别看神气活现，实在蠢得登峰造极，就算不是更坏，也够瞧的了——眼看着那颗蠢脑袋滚进断头台底下的筐里，必定很有趣。

===Original===
Neither Pochart nor Danou, however, were men to give up the struggle quite so easily. In the fight against the representative on special mission, who had threatened them and lorded it over them for so long, they only contemplated one issue: victory. Victory! which would mean satisfaction of pride and of revenge. They had set out to win and did not consider themselves beaten. Not yet. Already Pochart was on his feet, and his rasping voice rose booming above the tumult. As soon as a slight lull gave him an opportunity he seized it, and cried in thunderous accents:

===Chinese===
不过，波夏尔和达努都不是肯这么轻易认输的人。同这位特派代表较量——这么久以来，他威胁他们，在他们头上作威作福——他们盘算的结局只有一个：胜利。胜利！那将意味着自尊心的满足和报复的快意。他们是抱着必胜之心来的，不认为自己已经败了。还没有呢。波夏尔已经站了起来，他那刺耳的嗓音轰隆隆地压过喧闹。稍稍一阵间歇刚给他露出可乘之机，他便抓住不放，用雷霆般的声调喊道：

===Original===
“Citizens! Frenchmen! French women! All of you!” And then again: “Citizens all! Let me put the same question to you, that the President asked you just now: will you allow yourselves to be duped? Will you go like sheep whithersoever traitors may lead you?”

===Chinese===
「公民们！法兰西的男人们！法兰西的女人们！你们全体！」接着又喊：「全体公民们！让我把庭长方才问过你们的那个问题，再问你们一遍：你们甘愿受人愚弄吗？你们甘愿像绵羊一样，叛徒把你们牵到哪儿就到哪儿吗？」

===Original===
The crowd murmured and shrugged shoulders, would have shouted Pochart down only that that rasping voice of his rose above the cry of: “*A la lanterne*, all traitors and fools!”

===Chinese===
人群咕哝起来，耸着肩膀，本想把波夏尔轰下去，可他那刺耳的嗓音偏偏压过了那条呐喊：「吊上路灯去（*A la lanterne*），吊死所有的叛徒和蠢货！」

===Original===
Pointing an accusing finger at Chauvelin, Pochart took up the cry.

===Chinese===
波夏尔手指直指肖维兰，接过了这条口号。

===Original===
“So say I,” he roared in a terrific straining of his powerful lungs: “*A la lanterne* all the traitors who try to throw dust in your eyes. Have you forgotten that the citizen President is the father of the accused? And that he knows well enough that if the child be guilty, then is the parent guilty too? To save himself he is trying to shield a traitor. Do not allow yourselves to be duped by him. Look on the Citizen President, my friends, and ask him how it comes about that he lavished all the treasures of his eloquence upon this one traitor, when yesterday and the day before that, he sent to the guillotine every man, woman and child who came before the Tribunal, and on a mere suspicion of treason.”

===Chinese===
「我就是这个意思，」他鼓足强壮的肺叶，吼出雷霆般的声音：「凡往你们眼里揉沙子的叛徒，统统吊上路灯去。你们忘了没有：公民庭长是被告的父亲？他心里再明白不过：孩子有罪，老子就有罪？为了保全他自己，才拼死拼活包庇一个叛徒。别让自己受了他的愚弄。朋友们，好好看看这位公民庭长，问一问他：昨天，还有前天，凡押到这个法庭来的男人、女人和孩子，只凭一点叛国的嫌疑，他就一律送上断头台，怎么偏偏对这个叛徒，他就把满腹雄辩的看家本事都使出来了？」

===Original===
A dull murmur greeted this peroration. There had been something in Pochart’s eloquence which caused the crowd not to veer round just yet, but at any rate to look on the President of the Tribunal with rather less awe, and something approaching suspicion.

===Chinese===
这番宏论换来一阵低低的嗡嗡声。波夏尔的雄辩里有些东西，虽还没叫人群立刻倒戈，却至少使他们望向法庭庭长的目光里少了些敬畏，多了些近乎猜疑的东西。

===Original===
“That is true,” a woman said loudly. “The President showed no mercy to traitors yesterday. And it is treason now to be as much as suspected of treason, we’ve been told.”

===Chinese===
「这话不假，」一个女人大声说。「庭长昨天对叛徒可没有留情。再说，如今只要有叛国的嫌疑，本身就是叛国——人家就是这么教导我们的。」

===Original===
“It is my duty to protect the innocent,” Chauvelin retorted firmly, “as well as to punish the guilty.”

===Chinese===
「保护无辜是我的职责，」肖维兰斩钉截铁地回敬道，「惩办有罪，同样也是。」

===Original===
“Methinks,” Danou now broke in, and his slow, and suave tones came in strange contrast to the clamorous eloquence of his colleagues: “methinks that the traitor Danton made some such remark too, ere justice put her hand on him.”

===Chinese===
「我想呀，」这时达努插了进来，他慢条斯理、柔声细气的腔调，同两位同僚声嘶力竭的雄辩恰成奇怪的对照，「我想呀，叛徒丹东（Danton）当年也讲过差不多的话，直到正义之手抓住了他。」

===Original===
“Danton was a traitor, and thou too, Citizen Danou, art a traitor for speaking his name in this hall of justice.”

===Chinese===
「丹东是叛徒，你，达努公民，在这座正义之堂里敢提他的名字，也是叛徒。」

===Original===
“Justice!” Pochart cried, pallid with rage, for he had felt that the word “traitor” hurled at Danou was meant to strike him also. “Justice! hark at the traitor, who should be standing in the dock beside his brood.”

===Chinese===
「正义！」波夏尔喊了起来，气得脸色发白，因为他觉出那句掷向达努的「叛徒」，也是冲着他来的。「正义！听听这叛徒说的，他自己就该站在被告席上，同他那一窝并排。」

===Original===
“*Vas-y*, Citizen President,” the women cried excitedly. “It is thy turn now.”

===Chinese===
「来呀，公民庭长，」女人们兴奋地喊道。「这回可轮到你了。」

===Original===
They had cast aside their knitting, so palpitating had this duel become between these three men. Insensate, doltish as they were, they scented the tragedy that underlay this wordy warfare; they guessed that the man who presided over this infamous tribunal and who with a casual stroke of the pen had sent hundreds indiscriminately to death, had one soft corner in his callous heart, and that his colleagues, consumed with envy and hatred were hitting at that vulnerable spot and had already succeeded in making him writhe in agony.

===Chinese===
毛线活儿早被她们撂在了一边，这三个人之间的这场决斗，已叫人心惊肉跳到这般田地。她们尽管麻木、愚钝，也嗅出了这场唇枪舌剑底下埋着的悲剧；她们猜到，这个主持着这座臭名昭著的法庭、随手一笔便不分良莠送上几百人去死的人，他那颗冷酷的心里也有一个柔软的角落；而他的两位同僚，正被妒火和仇恨烧着，专朝那个致命处下手，而且已经打得他痛彻心扉、辗转煎熬了。

===Original===
At the same time, such is the psychology of a multitude as against that of individuals, there was still a wave of sympathy tending in the direction of this father fighting so desperately for the life of his child. Strictly speaking it was not sympathy, rather was it mere instinctive understanding of family ties. Five years of this awful revolution, during which every cruel lust in man or woman had been sedulously fostered, every softer mood repressed, had not yet succeeded in crushing altogether that feeling for family solidarity which is the most distinctive characteristic of the French nation. And this spectacle of a father sitting in judgment over his own child, and actually expected to pronounce the death-sentence over her, did undoubtedly for the time being sway the crowd in his favour. He was given a more respectful hearing than either of his colleagues or either of the witnesses, and when Godet’s name recurred on the tapis, it was greeted with derisive cries of “*Cet imbécile!*” and when Adèle was mentioned, most of the women shouted spitefully: “Liar!”

===Chinese===
与此同时——人群的心理同个人的心理，分别正在于此——还有一股同情的潮流，朝着这位为孩子性命拼死相争的父亲这边涌。严格说，那不是同情，只是对家庭骨肉的本能的体认。这场可怕的革命五年了，其间人的、女人的每一分残忍欲念都受到悉心的浇灌，每一分柔软心肠都遭到压制，却终究没能把这骨肉团结之情斩草除根——那正是法兰西民族最鲜明的特性。而一个父亲高坐堂上审判自己的孩子、当真还要亲口宣判她死刑的这幕光景，眼下确确实实把人群拉到了他这一边。他得到的倾听，比两位同僚、两位证人都更见敬意；每当戈岱的名字重新被提起，迎接它的是「这个蠢货！」（*Cet imbécile!*）的讪笑；一提到阿黛尔，大多数女人便恶狠狠地齐喊：「撒谎精！」

===Original===
Chauvelin, sensitive of course to the slightest wavering in the temper of the populace, felt his advantage and strained every nerve to press it home. The whole situation was of course terribly precarious. At any moment a look, a word, a false move on his part, might cause the crowd to veer right over against him. Even after an acquittal sometimes, the populace would suddenly demand that the accused be re-arrested: a second trial, more of a mockery and a travesty of justice than the first, would be insisted on, after which condemnation was a foregone conclusion. All this Chauvelin knew, none better, and there were moments when he felt as if madness or death were preferable to this terrible fight that in the end could have but one issue. And yet fight he must, fight for every inch of ground, fight with the last breath in his body, and with it silence the vituperations of those fiends who had raised their noisome voices against his Fleurette.

===Chinese===
肖维兰对民情最细微的摇摆自然敏感不过，此刻他觉出了自己的优势，便绷紧每一根神经，要把它用足。整个局势当然凶险已极。任何一个眼神、一句话、一步错着，都可能叫人群整个倒向他敌对的一面。有时候，甚至开释之后，暴民还会突然要求重新逮捕被告：非再演一场比头一场更像是把正义当猴耍的二审不可，过后定罪便是板上钉钉。这一切肖维兰清楚得很，谁也没有他清楚；有几次他甚至觉得，与其打这场到头来只有一种结局的恶战，倒不如疯了或死了痛快。然而他非战不可，一寸土地一寸土地地争，用他体内最后一口气去争，用这口气堵住那群恶魔的恶骂——他们正扯着污秽的嗓子，冲着他的弗勒蕾特大叫大嚷。

===Original===
Even now Pochart was on his feet again, shouting, gesticulating, banging his fist upon the table.

===Chinese===
就在此刻，波夏尔又跳了起来，又叫又比划，拳头擂得桌子咚咚响。

===Original===
“Citizens,” he reiterated for the third time, “do not let yourselves be duped by men who are ruining your country by pandering to traitors. Look at the accused! I say she is nothing but a wanton, who should be tied to the whipping-post ere she be sent to the guillotine. Look at the aristocrat, I say, with the demure airs and the folded kerchief; she, forsooth, goes forth o’ nights to meet her lover under the almond trees, there to concoct treason with her lover against the Republic. She was seen, remember, seen, I say, in spite of what interested parties may aver. You have heard the witness, a humble, simple girl, the victim of aristocratic lust and of tyranny. That witness spoke the truth. She saw the accused and her lover at dead of night whispering and embracing. I ask you, does a clean-minded, respectable woman, citizen of our glorious Republic, spend her nights in the company of her lover? Rather is it not the wanton, the traitor, who shuns the light of day and seeks the darkness, for the hatching of treasonable plots against the State? Look at the witness, citizens. Humbly and simply did she speak the truth﻿—”

===Chinese===
「公民们，」他第三次重弹老调，「千万别让自己受那些人的愚弄——他们姑息叛徒，正在断送你们的国家。看看这被告！我说，她不过是个荡妇，送上断头台之前，先该绑到鞭笞柱上抽一顿。看看这个贵族女人，我说，装出一副娴静模样，围巾叠得整整齐齐；她呀，夜里倒会出门，到杏树底下去会情郎，同她的情郎一块儿在那儿密谋反对共和国的叛国勾当。有人看见过她，记住，看见过，我说，任凭那些有利害关系的人怎么赌咒发誓也不中用。你们都听到证人说了——一个卑贱的、单纯的姑娘，贵族淫欲和暴虐的牺牲品。那证人说的是实话。她亲眼看见被告和她的情郎半夜三更又是咬耳朵又是搂抱。我问你们，一个心地干净、体体面面的女人、咱们光荣共和国的女公民，会整夜整夜陪着情郎过吗？与其这样说，倒不如说：那荡妇、那叛徒，避开白昼的光亮，专找黑暗的角落，好去孵化颠覆国家的叛国阴谋，对不对？看看这证人吧，公民们。她说的是实话，说得谦卑，说得朴素——」

===Original===
“She lied as well you know it, Citizen Pochart,” Chauvelin broke in forcefully. “Liar, forger and thief, I decree her accused and command that she stand her trial for these offences against the Republic. Look at her, my friends, citizens all,” he went on, and pointed an accusing finger at Adèle whose pinched little face had become the colour of lead, and who sat in a corner of the witness’ bench, cowering within herself, her trembling hands, now and then, lifting a handkerchief to wipe the sweat of terror that had risen to her brow. “Look at her,” Chauvelin continued, appealing to the sea of faces before him: “And now look at the accused. She is serene, because she is innocent, whilst the guilty trembles because she knows her treachery has come to light at last. Look at those two women, citizens, and yourselves pronounce which is the traitor and which is the stainless.”

===Chinese===
「她撒了谎，你心里再清楚不过，波夏尔公民。」肖维兰厉声插了进来。「撒谎精、伪造犯、小偷——我宣布她为被告，命令她为这些危害共和国的罪行受审。看看她，我的朋友们，全体公民们，」他接着说，手指直指阿黛尔——她那张干瘪的小脸已成了铅灰色，缩在证人席的一角，整个人瑟缩成一团，抖抖索索的手不时举起一方手帕，去擦额上沁出的、吓得冒出来的冷汗。「看看她，」肖维兰继续说道，向着眼前的一片人脸呼吁，「再看看那被告。她镇定，因为她清白；那有罪的直打哆嗦，因为她知道自己的背叛行径到底败露了。公民们，看看这两个女人，请你们自己来断一断：哪一个是叛徒，哪一个白璧无瑕。」

===Original===
Of a truth all would have been well after that. Chauvelin passed a quivering hand across his brow. It was streaming with moisture. The strain had been immense. Mentally he felt broken by the effort. But he also felt that for the moment at least he had won the day. The *Moniteur* states definitely that: “*il y eût tout lieu de croire qu’un acquittement eût été applaudi.*” At any rate the applause at the moment was deafening, and if Chauvelin could have obtained a hearing for another sixty seconds he would have put the acquittal to the populace vote, and, as the *Moniteur* says, it would have been carried.

===Chinese===
说实话，打那以后，一切本该顺顺当当了。肖维兰抬起一只发颤的手，抹了一把额头。额上汗涔涔的。这场煎熬实在非同小可。他自觉心力已被这一番力气使尽了。可他也觉出，至少此刻，他已经赢了这一天。《导报》言之凿凿地说：「*il y eût tout lieu de croire qu’un acquittement eût été applaudi*」（大有理由相信，一纸开释本会博得满堂喝彩）。不管怎样，那时的掌声确是震耳欲聋；倘若肖维兰能再争取到六十秒钟的倾听，他就会把开释付诸民众表决，而且正如《导报》所说，必定通得过。

===Original===
What would have happened afterwards nobody can say. The most fickle entity in the world is a multitude, and of all the multitudes, an audience watching the suffering of a fellow-creature is the most fickle and the most callous. For the next two or three minutes at any rate, Chauvelin held the sympathy of the crowd. Fleurette did not count either way. For the spectators of this heartrending pageant she was just a thing, an insentient object placed there for their entertainment, the pivot round which circled their excitement. But Chauvelin, the father pleading for his daughter’s life, had won their sympathy﻿—the sympathy of tiger-cats, satiated for the moment and licking their chops in the intervals of snarling.

===Chinese===
往后会怎样，谁也说不上。世上最反复无常的东西莫过于人群，而一切人群里头，围观同类受苦的看客又最是反复无常、最是心如铁石。至少在随后的两三分钟里，肖维兰还攥着人群的同情。弗勒蕾特则怎样都无足轻重。在这幕摧人心肝的活剧的看客眼里，她只是一件东西，一个没有知觉的物件，摆在那儿供他们取乐，是他们的兴奋围着打转的轴心。可肖维兰，这位为女儿性命哀恳的父亲，却赢得了他们的同情——那是一群虎猫的同情，一时餍足了，便在龇牙咆哮的间隙里舔着嘴巴。

===Original===
All then would have been well but for the action of one of the sympathizers who stood leaning up against the wall in the crowd; a giant he was, coated with grime﻿—coal heaver or scavenger probably, only half clad in ragged shirt and torn breeches, with dirty feet thrust stockingless into sabots, a red worsted cap over his unkempt hair, his face streaked with sweat and coal-dust. In one hand he held a large raw carrot which he was munching with loud snapping of the jaws and smacking of the lips. He was one of the noisiest in his approval of the President’s peroration.

===Chinese===
本来，到这时一切也都可以顺顺当当了，坏就坏在人群里一个同声喝彩的汉子的事上。那人斜倚着墙站在人堆里，是个大块头，浑身裹着油垢——多半是个搬煤工，要不就是打扫夫——身上只胡乱披着破衬衫和烂裤子，一双脏脚没穿袜子，趿拉在木鞋（sabots）里，一头乱发扣着顶红绒线帽，脸上横一道竖一道全是汗水和煤灰。他一只手攥着根大生胡萝卜，正大嚼特嚼，牙床咬得脆响，嘴巴咂得啧啧有声。方才庭长那篇宏论博得喝彩，喊得最响的数他一个。

===Original===
“*Vas-y*, Président,” he shouted. “*A la lanterne*, the fools and traitors. Where is that trollop? Let her stand up. We want to look at her, eh, citizens?”

===Chinese===
「说呀，庭长先生，」他嚷道。「吊上路灯去，吊死那些蠢货和叛徒。那个小荡妇在哪儿？叫她站起来。咱们要瞧瞧她，嗯，公民们？」

===Original===
“Yes! Yes! we want to see her! Stand up, Adèle of unknown parentage! Let’s look at you.”

===Chinese===
「对！对！我们要看看她！站起来，来历不明的阿黛尔！让咱们瞧瞧你。」

===Original===
The women, of course, were the loudest in their demand for the unfortunate Adèle. Bred by misery, often out of degradation, trained by five years of an execrable revolution, the women of France were not *féministes* these days. The spectacle of one of their own sex on the guillotine gave them more satisfaction than that of a man. Now they wanted to see Adèle of the pinched, ratlike face, Adèle with the trembling hands and the shrinking shoulders, they wanted to see her squirm before their wrath, they wanted to see her wriggle like a worm prodded with a pin. Incidentally they had almost forgotten Fleurette.

===Chinese===
要瞧倒霉的阿黛尔，喊得最响的自然又是女人们。苦难养大的，常常还是从堕落里泡大的，再经了这场天杀的革命五年的调教，法国的女人如今算不上什么女权主义者（*féministes*）。自家同性上断头台的光景，比男人的更叫她们称心。眼下她们就要看看这张干瘪老鼠脸的阿黛尔，看看这双手发抖、肩膀缩紧的阿黛尔，要看她在她们的雷霆之怒跟前扭曲变形，要看她像一条被针戳着的蛆虫那样扭来扭去。顺带说一句，弗勒蕾特她们几乎已经忘到脑后了。

===Original===
Louder and even louder they clamoured for Adèle, and at an order from the President, two soldiers of the National guard did presently drag Adèle from the corner of the witness’ bench where she was cowering like a frightened rodent, and dragged her﻿—or rather carried her﻿—to the bar of the accused. The crowd seeing that its dictates were being obeyed, restrained its frenzy for an instant and, through the comparative stillness that ensued, a piercing shriek rang out from the unfortunate Adèle.

===Chinese===
她们喊着要阿黛尔，一浪高过一浪；庭长一声令下，两名国民自卫军士兵便当即把阿黛尔从证人席的角落里拖了出来——她在那儿缩成了一团，活像一只吓坏的老鼠——把她拖去，不如说是抬去，抬到被告栏前。人群见自己的号令得了遵行，狂热收敛了一瞬；趁着随之而来的相对的寂静，倒霉的阿黛尔发出一声刺耳的尖叫。

===Original===
“Mercy! Mercy!” she cried, and struggled fiercely to free herself from the men’s grasp. “I am innocent! I spoke the truth.”

===Chinese===
「饶命呀！饶命呀！」她哭喊着，拼命挣扎，想从那两个士兵手里挣脱。「我是清白的！我说的是实话呀。」

===Original===
A thunderous shout of derisive laughter greeted her cry. The women, with their hands on their knees, were literally rocking with laughter. They thought that Adèle with a face like a rat, wisps of lank hair poking out from underneath her cap which sat all awry, with mouth wide open uttering shrieks which no one could hear through the deafening tumult, was supremely funny.

===Chinese===
回答她这声哭喊的，是一阵雷鸣般嘲弄的大笑。女人们双手按着膝盖，笑得前仰后合。她们只觉眼前这阿黛尔实在滑稽透顶：一张老鼠脸，歪到一边的便帽底下钻出几绺稀疏的直头发，嘴巴大张，发出一声声尖叫——可那震耳的喧嚣里，谁也听不见。

===Original===
The President made no attempt to quell the disturbance. It was all to the good. The greater the hatred against Adèle, the greater his chance, not only of forcing an acquittal now for Fleurette, but also of keeping the wave of sympathy for himself at full-tide, until he had the opportunity of getting Fleurette out of Orange. He was striving with all his might to catch his darling’s eye. But Fleurette’s glance was fixed on Adèle. She seemed to him to be fascinated with horror, mute and paralysed. She was looking on Adèle, and her dear little hand was fidgeting the corner of her kerchief.

===Chinese===
庭长没有设法平息这场骚乱。这于他只有好处。对阿黛尔的仇恨越大，他的机会就越大——不但眼下可以为弗勒蕾特硬争到一纸开释，还能把冲着他来的那股同情浪潮保持在满潮，直到他寻着机会把弗勒蕾特弄出奥朗日。他竭尽全力想接住爱女的目光。可弗勒蕾特的目光死死钉在阿黛尔身上。在他看来，她是吓呆了，说不出话，动弹不得。她望着阿黛尔，那只亲爱的小手正绞弄着围巾的一角。

===Original===
Through the earsplitting uproar led by the women, Pochart and Danou, their sympathizers, men of their own choosing, vainly tried to get a hearing. As well try to shout down a tempestuous sea as these hundreds of women gloating over the spectacle of one of their own sex writhing in an agony of terror.

===Chinese===
女人们领头掀起这场穿云裂石的喧嚣，波夏尔和达努——他们的党羽，他们自己挑的人——白费力气想让人听见。对着这成百上千个女人喊话，好比对着怒海狂涛喊话——她们正津津有味地观赏一个同性在恐怖的煎熬里挣扎打挺呢。

===Original===
“*Hein!*” came in a stentorian shout from the grimy giant in the rear of the crowd; “thou wouldst slander the innocent girl with lies. Take that for thy pains.”

===Chinese===
「嗬！（*Hein*！）」人群后头那个满身油垢的大块头扯开洪亮的嗓子喊了一声；「你竟敢用谎话糟蹋清白的姑娘。赏你这个，算你辛苦一场！」

===Original===
And he hurled the remnant of his raw carrot over the head of the intervening crowd at the unfortunate Adèle. It missed her by a hairbreadth, but the action delighted the crowd. They took up the cry: “Take that for thy pains!” and sent various missiles flying at the girl, who, crouching down on her knees, lay there like a bundle of goods just below the bar of the accused where Fleurette stood, gazing down at her, fascinated with horror.

===Chinese===
说罢，他把啃剩的半截生胡萝卜从隔在中间的人群头顶上，朝倒霉的阿黛尔猛掷过去。胡萝卜擦着她毫发之差飞了过去，可这一下把人群乐坏了。他们接过了那条口号：「赏你这个，算你辛苦一场！」于是各式各样的飞弹一齐朝那姑娘身上招呼。她双膝跪倒，蜷在那儿，活像一捆货包，正倒在被告栏底下——弗勒蕾特站在栏前，低头望着她，吓得魂不附体一般。

===Original===
Looking back later on that terrible moment, Chauvelin felt that it was the action of the grimy coal-heaver﻿—or scavenger, whatever he was﻿—that precipitated the catastrophe. He it was who egged on the rabble to virulent hatred against Adèle. It was he who by hurling that first missile at the girl brought in a further, more immense element of cruelty and horror into the situation. Certain it is that up to that moment Fleurette had appeared more dazed than horrified. She must even in her own gentle heart have felt a burning indignation against Adèle for the treacherous part which she had played, and if the girl’s arrest had been effected outside the Tribunal, she would perhaps never have actually realized what had brought it about. But with that shout of “Thou wouldst slander the innocent girl with thy lies,” full consciousness returned to her, and with it the recollection of everything that had gone before. Chauvelin, who watched her with the devouring gaze of his love, saw as in a flash, through the quick glance which swept from Adèle to himself and thence over the sea of perspiring faces, the full workings of her mind.

===Chinese===
后来回想那可怕的一刻，肖维兰认定：正是那个油垢满身的大个子——搬煤工也罢，打扫夫也罢，管他是什么——这一掷，促成了这场大祸。是他，鼓动起暴民对阿黛尔毒汁四溅的仇恨；是他，朝那姑娘掷出第一件飞弹，给这局面又添进了一层更大、更骇人的残忍与恐怖。可以断言，直到那一刻为止，弗勒蕾特看上去与其说是惊骇，不如说是发懵。就连她那颗温柔的心里，对阿黛尔使出的那手阴毒的背叛，想必也曾燃起一股灼热的愤慨；倘若这姑娘的逮捕是在法庭之外执行的，她兴许永远不会真正明白这场逮捕由何而来。可是随着那声「你竟敢用谎话糟蹋清白的姑娘」，她的神志完全清醒了，随之涌回的，是先前一切种种的记忆。肖维兰用他那吞噬般的爱的目光凝视着她，在一瞬之间，透过她那道飞快的、从阿黛尔扫到自己、又扫过那一片汗涔涔人脸的目光，把她心里的全部活动看了个通透。

===Original===
He tried to keep the tumult going; he hoped that Fleurette would faint, so that she might be carried out of court. He prayed that the roof of the gigantic building would come crashing down and bury him and Fleurette and all that swinish multitude in its ruins ere she spoke the words which he saw hovering on her lips.

===Chinese===
他竭力想让这场喧闹继续下去；他指望弗勒蕾特晕过去，好叫人把她抬出法庭。他祈求这座巨大建筑的屋顶当场坍塌下来，把他、把弗勒蕾特、把那一整群猪猡似的暴民统统埋在瓦砾底下——只求赶在她开口之前；那些话，他已看见在她唇边打转了。

===Original===
But none of these things happened. Rather by that perversity which is peculiar to Chance, a sudden lull broke in on the mighty uproar, a lull through which Fleurette’s calm voice rang clear as water poured into a crystal glass.

===Chinese===
可是这些一样也没有发生。倒像是造化偏爱作弄，一片突如其来的间歇，竟插进了那滔天的喧嚣；就在这片间歇里，弗勒蕾特平静的嗓音清亮地响起，好似清水注入水晶杯。

===Original===
“Adéle was not lying, nor did she slander me, I did give some valuable articles into the keeping of my beloved M’sieu’ Amédé Colombe, at the hour spoken of by her, and I have no doubt that she did see me, as she says.”

===Chinese===
「阿黛尔没有撒谎，她也没有诬蔑我。我确实在她所说的那个时辰，把几件值钱物件托付给了我亲爱的阿梅代·科隆布先生保管；而且毫无疑问，正像她说的，她确实看见了我。」

## XXXVI / 第三十六章

===Original===
One must of necessity turn once more to the *Moniteur* of the 22nd Messidor year II of the Republic One and Indivisible. There in the *Choix des Rapports* XXV 516﻿–﻿17, despite its sobriety of language and paucity of detail, there is ample proof that throughout the proceedings it was the action of one unknown that precipitated the final catastrophe. “*Un géant*,” we are told, “*fût le premier à lancer l’accusation fausse contre le Président du Tribunal, et un tumulte irrépressible s’ensuivit*.”

===Chinese===
我们势必还得再一次去翻统一不可分割的共和国二年穗月（Messidor）二十二日那期的《导报》（*Moniteur*）。在《报告选辑》（*Choix des Rapports*）XXV 卷 516–17 页上，尽管措辞节制、细节寥寥，却不乏充分的证据，证明整场审判的进程中，正是那一个无名之人的行动，促成了最后这场大祸。据载：「一个巨人第一个抛出对法庭庭长的虚假指控，随之而起的是一场无法遏止的骚乱」（*Un géant fût le premier à lancer l’accusation fausse contre le Président du Tribunal, et un tumulte irrépressible s’ensuivit*）。

===Original===
“False,” you observe. But on that 16th day of June 1794, Chauvelin of the National Convention, member of committees and confidant of Robespierre, did, we know, stand in danger of being dragged out into the open and hung on the nearest lamp post. The crowd was in no mood even to wait for the paraphernalia of the guillotine. They wanted to see the arch-traitor, the perjurer, who had sworn false oaths and lied in order to save himself and his brood, hang then and there. The giant spoken of in the *Choix des Rapports* had, it seems, hardly waited till the words were out of Fleurette’s mouth, before he pushed his way to the forefront of the crowd, with vigorous play of his powerful elbows. Down he was now, in the body of the court. In the struggle, his ragged shirt had been half torn off his shoulders, and his broad chest and sinewy arms could be seen, nude and immense, and coated with grime. Out of one of the pockets of his tattered breeches he had produced another uncooked carrot, and into this he bit lustily, then with a wide sweep of the arm he launched one by one against the President of the Tribunal the damning invectives which the *Moniteur* has characterized as false. “Traitor!” he cried. “Liar and perjurer! Citizens all, have you in all your lives ever witnessed such infamy?”

===Chinese===
「不实之词，」你会这样说。可是我们知道，在 1794 年 6 月 16 日那一天，国民公会的肖维兰——好几个委员会的委员、罗伯斯庇尔（Robespierre）的心腹——当真处在被拖到露天、吊上最近一根灯柱的危险之中。人群连等断头台那一套行头架起来的耐性都没有。他们要亲眼看着那个罪大恶极的叛徒、那个赌假咒发假誓的伪誓者——为了保全自己和他那一窝子，赌的是假咒，撒的是谎——当场吊死。《报告选辑》里说的那个大个子，似乎压根儿没等弗勒蕾特的话音落地，就抡开两条有力的胳膊肘，一路拱到了人群的最前排。此刻他已经下到了法庭场子的当中。一路挤撞里，他身上那件破衬衫已被扯掉半边，露出宽阔的胸膛和虬结的臂膀，赤裸裸、巍巍然，覆着一层油垢。他从破裤子的一只口袋里又掏出一根生胡萝卜，对着它狠狠咬上一大口，随后手臂大开大阖地一挥，一句接一句，朝法庭庭长掷去那足以定罪的痛骂——《导报》倒已把那些痛骂定性为不实之词。「叛徒！」他喊道。「骗子！发假誓的！公民们，大伙儿这辈子可曾见过这样的无耻勾当？」

===Original===
The *Choix des Rapports* describes the tumult as irrepressible. Indeed at that moment it would have been easier to dam a raging torrent with one pair of hands, than to suppress the riotous confusion that ensued. Fleurette of a truth stood there forgotten, so did Adèle and Godet. All eyes were fixed on the President, every menacing gesture tended in his direction, all the strident cries, the insults, the varied and foul epithets were hurled against him. There were but few sober tempers in that crowded room at the moment. A dozen perhaps: no more. Older men, one or two women who watched rather than yelled. And what they saw interested and puzzled them, so much that, when the time came, when everybody else was shouting themselves hoarse to the verge of mania, they still kept cool and silent.

===Chinese===
《报告选辑》把那场骚乱形容为无法遏止。的确，在那一刻，凭一双赤手去堵住一条咆哮的激流，也比压下随之而起的那阵乱局容易些。说实话，弗勒蕾特站在那儿，早被忘了个干净，阿黛尔（Adèle）和戈岱（Godet）也一样。所有的眼睛都钉在庭长身上，每一个威吓的手势都朝他比划，一切尖利的呼喊、侮辱、五花八门的污言秽语，全冲他掷去。那一刻，满堂拥挤的人里，头脑还清醒的没有几个。兴许十来个：绝不会再多。几个上了年纪的男人，一两个只顾观看、不肯叫喊的女人。而他们的所见所闻又使他们既纳闷又着迷，着迷到这般地步：到了后来，旁人个个喊哑了嗓子、喊到发狂的边缘，他们却还保持着冷静和沉默。

===Original===
Like everybody else these few were gazing on the President. They saw him standing there on the bench like a figure carved in stone, and, like a stone, his face was of a grey, ashen colour. His eyes looked dim and colourless as if a hand had drawn a film over them; his lips were parted, his nostrils distended. The breath seemed to come with difficulty out of his lungs. A figure, in truth of terror and despair. But calm and still. Motionless as a stone. The giant munching his carrot had waved his huge arms about and yelled himself hoarse until he had lashed all the spectators into a state of frenzy. Finally he strode across the room, and came to a halt close to the judges’ bench facing the President.

===Chinese===
同旁人一样，这几位也凝望着庭长。他们看见他站在法官席上，像一尊石雕的人像，而那张脸也同石头一般，是一种灰败的死灰之色。一双眼睛黯淡无光，仿佛有一只手在上面蒙了一层翳；嘴唇微张，鼻孔翕张。气息仿佛很艰难才从他肺里透出来。说实话，那是一副恐怖与绝望铸成的形象。却又平静，却安然。纹丝不动，宛如石头。那嚼着胡萝卜的大个子挥舞两条巨臂，直着嗓子喊到哑，终于把满场看客抽打得癫狂起来。最后他大步横穿大厅，在紧挨法官席、正对庭长的地方收住了脚。

===Original===
The three judges had been watching him all along: Pochart and Danou with undisguised glee, and President Chauvelin with that stony stare out of his colourless eyes. But even as the giant approached, Chauvelin, though apparently motionless, seemed inwardly to sink within himself, to crouch as a hunted beast in face of the menacing enemy. And suddenly like that of an automaton, up went his arm. With finger outstretched he pointed at the giant and one word escaped his trembling, rigid lips.

===Chinese===
三位法官自始至终都望着他：波夏尔（Pochart）和达努（Danou）毫不掩饰地幸灾乐祸，庭长肖维兰则用那双无色的眼睛，石雕一般死盯着。可就在大个子逼将过来时，肖维兰虽然外表纹丝不动，内里却仿佛整个人往下沉缩，像一头被追猎的野兽对着逼上来的仇敌那般蜷伏。突然间，仿佛自动木偶一般，他的手臂霍地抬起。手指笔直地指向那大个子，一个词从他颤抖而僵直的唇间逸了出来。

===Original===
“You!”

===Chinese===
「你！」

===Original===
Those who were watching him could not understand the word, for it was spoken in an alien tongue. Nor could they understand what happened afterwards. But what actually did happen was that the grimy giant threw back his head and gave a quaint and altogether pleasant laugh.

===Chinese===
望着他的人听不懂这个词，因为它说的是一种外国语。随后发生的事，他们同样听不懂。而实际发生的是：那满身油垢的大块头仰起头，发出一阵古怪而十足愉快的笑。

===Original===
“Why yes!” he said in the same alien tongue, which no one present understood. “At your service, my dear M. Chambertin.”

===Chinese===
「可不是嘛！」他用同一种外国语说道——在场没有一个人听得懂。「听候吩咐，我亲爱的尚贝尔坦先生（M. Chambertin）。」

===Original===
And Chauvelin murmured almost under his breath:

===Chinese===
肖维兰几乎压在嗓子底下，喃喃道：

===Original===
“You have your revenge at last, Sir Percy.”

===Chinese===
「你到底还是报了仇，珀西爵士。」

===Original===
“Hitting back as you see, my friend.”

===Chinese===
「你瞧，这不就叫反击嘛，我的朋友。」

===Original===
It all passed unperceived in the midst of the irrepressible tumult, save by those few who sober-tempered chose to watch rather than to yell. It is doubtful whether even Pochart and Danou, who sat close by, saw anything of this brief, this mysterious scene.

===Chinese===
这一切都在那场无法遏止的喧腾中央，悄没声儿地过去了，只有那几个头脑清醒、宁可观看而不肯叫喊的人瞧在了眼里。就连坐在近旁的波夏尔和达努，怕是也没有看见这转瞬即逝、这神秘的一幕。

===Original===
The very next moment the grimy giant, this time with a hoarse and not at all pleasant laugh, had hurled his half-munched carrot straight into the President’s face. Then facing the crowd once more he threw up his great arms high above his head.

===Chinese===
紧接着的下一刻，那满身油垢的大块头——这一回是一声沙哑而毫不愉快的笑——把他啃剩的半截胡萝卜，直直掷到了庭长脸上。随后他再次转向人群，把两条巨臂高高举过头顶。

===Original===
“Why should we wait, citizens,” he shouted louder than the rest of the yelling crowd. “*A la lanterne*, I say, the traitor and his brood. The guillotine is ready outside the Place. The executioner is to hand. Why wait?”

===Chinese===
「干吗还要等呢，公民们，」他喊道，嗓门压过周遭喊叫的人群。「我说，吊上路灯去（*A la lanterne*），吊死那叛徒和他那一窝子。断头台在广场外头现成架着。刽子手就在手边。还等什么？」

===Original===
Nothing could have pleased the crowd better. They were all like tigers scenting blood, demanding it, licking their jaws in anticipation.

===Chinese===
再没有比这更叫人群痛快的了。他们活像一群嗅着血腥的老虎，嚷着要血，馋涎欲滴地舔着牙床。

===Original===
“Who is for a front place for the spectacle?” a man shouted from the rear of the crowd.

===Chinese===
「这场好戏的前排位子，谁要？」人群后头一个男人喊道。

===Original===
“*A moi!* the front place,” a woman cried in response.

===Chinese===
「*A moi*（归我）！前排的位子，」一个女人应声喊道。

===Original===
“*A moi! A moi!*” came from every side.

===Chinese===
「*A moi! A moi!*（归我！归我！）」四下里齐声喊起。

===Original===
Then the general scramble began. A stampede down the gradients. The clatter of wooden sabots against the floor. The screams of women and children pushed and squeezed by the crowd. The grounding of arms, the click of bayonets, the words of command from the officer in charge of the guard, who were there to maintain order and who were quite powerless. They did of a truth try to stem the mob, to prevent the mad rush, the trampling, the stampede. But there were in reality too few of them for the task. All available fighting men being required for the army abroad, these were for the most part too inexperienced and too incompetent; raw recruits, half-trained for a wholly inadequate corps of gendarmerie. The officers did what they could, but the men themselves were soon caught in the vortex. Having no idea of discipline or duty, they soon became just a part of the mob, allowed themselves to be carried along by the crowd. They were just as excited, just as eager to see the President of a revolutionary tribunal sent summarily to the guillotine, as anyone else. Their lust for the spectacle was as keen as that of any ragamuffin in the place. They were but half-trained ragamuffins themselves, and as every man these days was at least as good as his officer and owed him neither obedience nor respect, it was small wonder that in emergencies like these, the soldiers got out of hand, whilst the officers, shrugging their shoulders, viewed the scene with indifference.

===Chinese===
于是，一场全场大争抢开始了。顺着坡道的一路狂奔。木鞋（sabots）磕在地板上的咔嗒乱响。被人群推搡挤压的女人孩子们的尖叫。枪托顿地，刺刀咔哒，带队的军官发出口令——这队警卫本是为维持秩序而来，此刻却全然无能为力。说实话，他们倒真想拦住暴民，拦住那疯狂的冲撞、踩踏和奔逃。可他们的人数实在少得办不成这桩差事。能打仗的壮丁全数征去补充对外作战的军队了，眼下这些人多半既无经验又不管用：净是些生手新兵，为一支全然不成气候的宪兵队伍受过半吊子的操练。军官们尽了力，可士兵们自己转眼便被卷进了漩涡。他们脑瓜里全没有纪律和职责的概念，很快便成了暴民的一分子，任凭人潮裹挟着走。要看一位革命法庭庭长被从速送上断头台，他们同旁人一样兴奋、一样眼热。对这场好戏的馋劲，他们不输场子里任何一个破衣烂衫的小混混。他们自己本就是一群半吊子操练出来的小混混；何况如今这年月，当小兵的至少同长官平起平坐，既不欠他服从、也不欠他敬意，所以碰上这样的乱子，士兵撒了手，军官耸耸肩膀冷眼旁观，也就不足为怪了。

===Original===
In the meanwhile the grimy giant had effectually fought his way along the floor of the house as far as the bar of the accused, where Fleurette, wide-eyed, deathly pale, half-crazy now with terror, had just fallen forward unconscious across the railing, drooping like a lily that is battered by the storm.

===Chinese===
与此同时，那满身油垢的大块头已经着着实实地杀开一条路，沿着大厅的地面一路挤到了被告栏跟前——弗勒蕾特正在那儿，瞪着眼，面如死灰，这会儿已吓得半疯，刚刚昏死过去，扑倒在栏杆上，像一株被暴雨摧折的百合，蔫蔫地垂着。

===Original===
“And *à moi* the traitors,” the giant shouted, and it was marvellous how his booming voice rang above the uproar and the confusion.

===Chinese===
「还有那俩叛徒，*à moi*（归我）啦，」大块头喊道；他那洪钟般的嗓音竟响亮地盖过满场的喧嚣纷乱，真是不可思议。

===Original===
He dragged Fleurette’s inanimate body from the bar and flung it over his shoulder, as if it were a bundle of goods. Then with two huge strides he was right in front of the judges’ bench, and there turned back to face the crowd again.

===Chinese===
他把弗勒蕾特失了知觉的身子从被告栏里拖出来，往肩上一甩，活像甩一捆货物。随后两大步，他已站到法官席正前方，在那儿又转过身来，面对人群。

===Original===
“Take your places for the spectacle, citizens,” he cried. “I’ll bring the actors along.”

===Chinese===
「各位占好看戏的位子吧，公民们，」他喊道。「戏子我来带。」

===Original===
He looked almost unreal as he stood there, dominating the crowd, grimy, unkempt, immense, with blackened face and huge bare chest, and the inanimate body of the girl across his massive shoulders. He seemed a being from another world, a Titan, a monster, a fiend-like fury, the embodiment of all the hates and the furies that animated the rest of the crowd. They glanced at him and trembled; some of them who had not wholly forgotten their age and innocence surreptitiously crossed themselves.

===Chinese===
他站在那儿，威临全场，几乎显得不像个真人：满身油垢，蓬头垢面，躯体巍然，脸膛熏黑，胸膛宽阔地袒露着，姑娘失了知觉的身子横扛在他厚实的双肩上。他仿佛是来自另一个世界的生灵，一位泰坦（Titan），一头怪物，一尊恶魔般的狂怒，是点燃全场其余众人的一切仇恨与暴怒的化身。众人瞥他一眼，直打哆嗦；有几个还不曾把旧日岁月和清白无邪忘个干净的人，偷偷画了个十字。

===Original===
“Take your places for the spectacle, citizens,” he went on lustily. “One actor I have ready for you. Who will bring the other?”

===Chinese===
「各位占好看戏的位子吧，公民们，」他兴致勃勃地继续喊。「一个戏子我给你们备现成了。另一个哪个去请？」

===Original===
Three men in the forefront of the crowd were at that moment standing quite close to the judges’ bench, where the President lay back in his chair, dead to everything about him, alive only through the intensity of his agony. In response to the Titan’s suggestion, which was greeted with loud applause by the crowd, the three men scrambled over the desk, seized the inert person of the President between them. One of them flung a sack over his head. Thus adorned they hoisted him upon their shoulders while the crowd stamped and shouted with glee.

===Chinese===
就在那一刻，人群前排有三个男人正站在离法官席很近的地方——庭长瘫在椅子里，对周遭一切浑然如死，只凭那阵剧痛的剧烈才透出活气。应着这泰坦的提议——它博得人群响亮的喝彩——三个汉子翻过案桌，把庭长那瘫软的身子七手八脚架住。一个往他头上罩了条麻袋。这般装扮停当，他们把他扛上肩头，人群欢喜得直跺脚、直呐喊。

===Original===
“*Un tumulte irrépressible s’ensuivit*,” says the *Choix des Rapports* in the *Moniteur* of the 22nd Messidor. Tumult is but a poor word to express the actions of that multitude. Men and women and children had become blind, insentient with lust, mad with hatred and excitement.

===Chinese===
《报告选辑》在穗月二十二日的《导报》上写道：「随之而起的是一场无法遏止的骚乱」（*Un tumulte irrépressible s’ensuivit*）。「骚乱」这个词，实在描不尽那群人的行状。男人、女人和孩子，全都瞎了眼，欲念烧得没了知觉，仇恨和亢奋逼得发了狂。

===Original===
“Take your places for the spectacle,” the Titan shouted, “and I’ll bring along the actors for you.”

===Chinese===
「各位占好看戏的位子吧，」泰坦吼道，「戏子我这就给你们带来。」

===Original===
And so they rushed out in a compact, struggling mass, hurrying, scurrying, fighting and pushing and struggling. Out in the open, in the Place de la République, into the sunshine and under the blue vault of heaven they rushed. The guillotine was set up there ready for its afternoon work, but, as the grimy giant had said, “Why wait?” Why indeed! No one was in a mood for waiting. The blackest traitor this town had ever seen had tried to save himself and his brood by slandering worthy citizens of the République. By the by, where were they? Adèle of unknown parentage and the swaggering Lieutenant Godet? Ah bah! they were forgotten. Lost in the crowd. Who cared? Time enough to cheer them when the traitors and slanderers were punished. Who cared indeed? For the moment the most important thing in the world was to secure a place of vantage for witnessing the wonderful spectacle. The President of a revolutionary tribunal, a representative of the people in the National Convention, was not often to be seen in Orange mounting the steps of the guillotine. That spectacle was reserved for the Parisians﻿—lucky people!﻿—who saw the heads of ci-devant kings and queens, of generals and dukes and duchesses and of countless other aristos roll into the basket. Therefore everyone scrambled for a good seat. The houses all round the Place were invaded by the mob; windows and balconies were soon filled with eager faces; boys and men swarmed on the roofs, clung to the rain-pipes, the gargoyles on the Hôtel de Ville, the lampposts and lamp-brackets. Many were injured in the struggle. But that made no matter so long as one got a good seat. Fortunately the weather was glorious. The sun shone gaily on this scene which suggested a coming pageant.

===Chinese===
于是他们蜂拥而出，挤作一团推推搡搡的人流，奔着，窜着，打着，推着，搡着。一出露天，他们便冲进了共和国广场（Place de la République），冲进阳光里，冲到蓝天的穹顶底下。断头台就在那儿架着，备好了午后的活计；可是，正像那满身油垢的大块头说的：「还等什么？」可不是嘛！谁也没心思等。本城见所未见的最黑心的叛徒，竟想靠诽谤共和国的忠实良民，来保全自己和他那一窝子。哦对了，他们俩呢？来历不明的阿黛尔和大摇大摆的戈岱中尉？嗐，早忘啦。淹在人堆里了。谁管呢？等叛徒和诽谤者受了惩办，再给他们喝彩也不迟。当真谁管呢？眼下天底下顶顶要紧的事，是抢一个亲睹这场好戏的好位置。革命法庭的庭长、国民公会的人民代表，在奥朗日踏着断头台的台阶上去，可不是常有的眼福。那种场面是专留给巴黎人——好福气的人们！——的：他们看惯了旧日（ci-devant）的国王王后、将军、公爵公爵夫人，还有数不清的其他贵族佬，脑袋一颗颗滚进筐里。于是人人争抢好座位。广场四周的房屋全叫暴民占了；窗户和阳台转眼挤满了热切的脸；半大小子和汉子们蜂拥上屋顶，攀着雨水管、市政厅（Hôtel de Ville）的滴水石兽、灯柱和灯架。争抢里伤了不少人。可那算得了什么，只要占着一个好座位。幸而天气好极了。太阳喜气洋洋地照着这一幕光景，恍如一场即将开演的盛典。

===Original===
In the centre of the place, facing the steps of the Hôtel de Ville, the guillotine reared its gaunt arms, painted a vivid red. The officers of the gendarmerie had succeeded by dint of threats, in restoring some semblance of order in the *tenue* of their men. They now stood at attention round the guillotine on the platform of which the executioner was busy with his grim task.

===Chinese===
广场正中，正对着市政厅的台阶，断头台竖起它嶙峋的架臂，漆得一片猩红。宪兵队的军官们连吓带喝，总算在部下的军容（*tenue*）上挽回了几分体统。此刻他们围绕断头台肃然立正；台上，刽子手正忙着那桩阴森的活计。

===Original===
The crowd around was very still. Something oppressive, unconnected with the heat of midday sun, seemed to hang in the air. People were still pouring out of the Hôtel de Ville, though not in such compact numbers. Gradually these numbers too were thinned. Those that came out last appeared more sober, less excited than the mob that had spread itself all over the Place shrieking and gesticulating in the manner habitual to these natives of the South.

===Chinese===
四周围的人群非常安静。有种沉甸甸的东西——同正午骄阳的暑热不相干——仿佛悬在半空。市政厅里还在往外涌人，只是不再成团成簇。渐渐地，这股人流也稀落了下来。最后出来的那些人，看上去比那摊满整个广场、尖着嗓子喊叫、抡着胳膊比划的暴民清醒些，也没那么亢奋——喊叫和比划，本是这些南方老乡土生土长的习惯。

===Original===
Some of the last to come out were a group of men well known in Orange, one was the butcher from the Rue Longue, another the innkeeper of Les Trois Abeilles, a third kept the haberdashery shop over the bridge. Citizen Pochart and Danou were with them. They were all talking eagerly together as they came down the steps. A group of women were standing close by.

===Chinese===
最后出来的，有一伙在奥朗日城里颇有名头的人物：一个是长街（Rue Longue）的肉铺老板，一个是「三蜜蜂」客栈（Les Trois Abeilles）的掌柜，第三个开着桥那边的一家针线杂货铺。波夏尔公民和达努公民也在其中。他们一面下台阶，一面起劲地交谈着。一群女人站在近旁。

===Original===
“Are they bringing the traitors?” they asked.

===Chinese===
「他们把叛徒们押来了吗？」她们问。

===Original===
“Yes, Citizen Tartine,” the butcher replied, “that fine patriot Rémi, one of the scavengers at the Caristie house is close behind us, with some of his mates. They’ve got the traitors between them. We are to give the sign by firing this pistol when the executioner is ready.”

===Chinese===
「押来了，塔尔蒂纳公民（Citizen Tartine），」肉铺老板答道，「那位顶刮刮的爱国志士雷米（Rémi）——卡里斯蒂（Caristie）宅的打扫夫之一——带着几个伙计，就跟在我们后头。叛徒们就夹在他们当中。等刽子手一切备妥，就由我们放这把手枪为号。」

===Original===
He showed the women the pistol which he said Rémi himself had given him.

===Chinese===
他给那几个女人看了那把手枪，说它就是雷米亲手交给他的。

===Original===
“The executioner is ready now,” the women said, three of them speaking at once.

===Chinese===
「刽子手这就备妥了，」女人们说，三个人异口同声。

===Original===
Citizens Pochart and Danou and the others then walked across the Place to the foot of the guillotine, one of them spoke a few words with the executioner. The crowd of spectators watched with feverish excitement. And presently Citizen Tartine, the butcher, raised his arm and fired a pistol in the air. A number of women shrieked. The excitement was so tense that the loud report sent the others into hysterics. Soon, however, the rumour went round that the pistol-shot was the signal that everything was ready for the spectacle and for the entrance of the chief actors in the play. After which every noise subsided. The multitude held its breath; a thousand pairs of eyes were fixed on the wide-open portals of the Hôtel de Ville waiting for the grandiose appearance of Rémi the scavenger and his mates bearing the traitors upon their shoulders.

===Chinese===
随后，波夏尔公民、达努公民同其余几位穿过广场，走到断头台脚下，其中一个还同刽子手搭了几句话。围观的人群热狂地盯着看。不多时，肉铺老板塔尔蒂纳公民抬起胳膊，朝天放了一枪。好几个女人尖叫起来。气氛绷得死紧，这一声脆响把其余的人都送进了歇斯底里。然而不多时，风声便四下传开：这一枪是信号，表明好戏的一切均已齐备，只等戏里的主角登场。此后，种种声响俱归平息。人群屏住了呼吸；上千双眼睛钉在市政厅洞开的大门上，等着打扫夫雷米和他的伙计们肩扛叛徒、堂而皇之地登场。

===Original===
Up, on the platform of the guillotine, the executioner was giving a last look to the pulleys. The soldiers stood at attention.

===Chinese===
高处，断头台的台子上，刽子手正给滑轮做最后一遍检视。士兵们肃然立正。

===Original===
The huge crowd waited.

===Chinese===
黑压压的一大群人，等待着。

## XXXVII / 第三十七章

===Original===
The *Moniteur* does not say much about what happened afterwards. “*La foule attendit avec assez de patience*,” is all it says, “*mais personne ne vint*.”

===Chinese===
对于后来的事，《导报》（*Moniteur*）着墨不多。统共只道了这么两句：「*La foule attendit avec assez de patience*」（人群等得相当耐心），「*mais personne ne vint*」（可是谁也没有来）。

===Original===
The portals of the Hôtel de Ville which should have been a frame for the entrance of the principal actors in the last act of the drama, showed nothing but the yawning black emptiness beyond. The crowd waited, says the *Moniteur*, with sufficient patience. They did wait quite happily for ten minutes, agitatedly for twenty. But nobody came. Citizens Pochart and Danou, also citizen Tartine, the butcher, and three or four others, were seen to make their way back across the Place, to run quickly up the steps of the Hôtel de Ville and subsequently disappear inside its portals. Still the crowd waited, very much as a crowd will wait in a theatre when the entr’acte is too long; some of them hilariously, others with impatient yawns, others again with tapping of feet and presently with murmurs of, “*La Lan-terne! La Lan-terne!*”

===Chinese===
市政厅（Hôtel de Ville）的大门，原本该给这出戏最后一幕的主角们登场作框景，此刻门里显露的，唯有一片张着大口的、黑洞洞的空荡。据《导报》说，人群等得相当耐心。头十分钟，他们确也等得颇为快活；再二十分钟，便等得坐立不安了。可是谁也没有来。只见公民波夏尔（Pochart）、公民达努（Danou），还有当肉铺老板的塔尔蒂纳（Tartine）公民，以及三四个旁人，折回来穿过广场，快步奔上市政厅的台阶，随即消失在大门里面。人群仍在等，那样子十足像剧院里幕间休息拖得太久时观众的等法：有的兴致勃勃，有的不耐烦地打呵欠，有的直跺脚，不多时便有人低声哄起「吊上路灯去！吊上路灯去！（*La Lan-terne! La Lan-terne!*）」

===Original===
The next thing that happened was the reverberating clang of the portals of the Hôtel de Ville being suddenly closed. Then only did the crowd realize that they were being cheated of the spectacle. Murmurs were loud, and there were some hisses and boos and cat’s calls. But on the whole they took the event with extraordinary calm. There was no rioting as indeed might have been expected. A few hotheads tried to create a disturbance by loudly demanding that the executioner be given something to do. Madame la Guillotine should not be cheated of her dinner.

===Chinese===
接着发生的一件事，是市政厅的大门訇然一声突然关上，回响四震。直到这时，人群才醒悟过来：这场好戏要跟他们失约了。怨声大作，还起了些嘘声、倒彩和怪叫。但总的说来，他们把这桩事接受得出奇地平静。并没有像原本可以料到的那样闹起乱子。几个头脑发热的家伙想生事，大声嚷着该给刽子手找点事做。断头夫人（Madame la Guillotine）的晚饭可不能赖掉。

===Original===
“She’s hungry, give her something to eat,” was the catchword these hotheads used in order to excite the rest of the crowd. Somehow it did not work. There certainly were a few bouts of fisticuffs, one or two broken heads, the soldiers round the guillotine and those on guard at the street corners did use their bayonets with some effect, but on the whole the crowd was strangely subdued, more inclined to whisper than to shout.

===Chinese===
「她饿啦，给她点吃的吧」——这是那几个头脑发热的家伙为煽动其余人群用的口号。不知怎么，竟没奏效。拳脚固然是动了几场，脑袋也打破了一两个，断头台四周和把守各街口的士兵也确实动了刺刀、见了成效，但总的说来，人群出奇地驯顺，与其说想喊叫，不如说更爱窃窃私语。

===Original===
For quite a little while after the portals of the Hôtel de Ville had been closed, they still waited, thinking that perhaps something more was being devised for their entertainment. But as time went on and nothing happened, they thought they might as well get home. It was dinner time. The children were hungry, and though there was little enough in the larders these days, one had to get home and give them what there was. The whole thing had been strange. Very strange. As men and women wended their way homeward, their thoughts reverted to that titanic figure with the grimy face and huge bare chest, one sinewy arm encircling the body of the wench Fleurette *nommée* Armand, which hung limp across his massive shoulder. He was no mere mortal, that was certain. And though the Government up in Paris had abolished *le bon Dieu*, and declared that it was Citizen Robespierre who was the “*Etre Suprême*,” something of the old superstitions imbibed at their mothers’ knees, still lingered in these untutored, undisciplined minds. That the Titan with the flashing eyes and grimy face should have vanished with the traitors whom he and his satellites had seized, was but the fitting ending to his meteoric appearance. The Government might forbid belief in God and the Devil, in heaven and in hell, but here was proof positive that the Devil did exist. He was black and he was of abnormal stature, he had a great bare chest and strong muscular arms, and﻿—clearest proof of all﻿—he had before the very eyes of the citizens of Orange seized upon two traitors and carried them away with him to limbo.

===Chinese===
市政厅的大门关上之后好一阵子，他们还在等，想着也许又有什么新节目正为他们张罗。可时间一点点过去，什么动静也没有，他们便寻思不如回家去。正是吃晚饭的时辰。孩子们饿了，虽说这些日子粮橱里的存粮少得可怜，总得回去把仅有的那点儿给他们垫垫肚子。这桩事从头到尾透着古怪。太古怪了。男男女女往家走的路上，心思又回到那个满面污垢、袒着宽阔胸膛的泰坦般的身影上：一条筋腱虬结的胳膊，搂着那丫头弗勒蕾特——官册上唤作「阿尔芒家的」（*nommée* Armand）——软塌塌垂挂在他厚实的肩头。他绝非凡人，这一点可以断定。巴黎的政府虽然把好天主（*le bon Dieu*）废了，宣布公民罗伯斯庇尔才是「最高主宰」（*Etre Suprême*），可这些没受过教化、不受管束的头脑里，打小在娘膝下吸进的那点旧迷信仍有一星半点没散尽。那目光灼灼、满面污垢的泰坦，连同他和他的党羽抓走的叛徒们一道消失无踪，这不过是他流星般现身的一个相称收梢。政府尽可以禁止人信天主信魔鬼、信天堂信地狱，可眼前便是铁证：魔鬼实实在在是有的。他浑身乌黑，身量高得反常，袒着宽阔的胸膛，生着筋腱虬结的臂膀，而且——最确凿的铁证是——他当着奥朗日全体公民的眼睛，一把攫住两个叛徒，带着他们遁入冥府去了。

===Original===
Nothing would take that idea out of the people’s mind, and long after these horrible days of the revolution had passed away and men and women had returned to sanity, those who were present on that day in June at the trial of one Fleur Chauvelin *nommée* Armand, would recount the marvellous story of how the devil had entered the courthouse and spirited the accused away. Only a few knew the true facts of the case, and even so a great deal was left to surmise. Among those who knew was Citizen Tartine, the butcher. And this is what he told his friends when they pressed him with questions. It seems that when the crowd stampeded out of the Hôtel de Ville, he, Tartine, together with Citizens Pochart and Danou who had stepped down from the judges’ bench, and three or four other notabilities of the city among whom was Motus, the chief warder of the Caristie house, put their heads together for a moment or two, wondering if something could not be done towards sending the wench Fleurette and her father by a back way to one or other of the prison houses, with a view to bringing them up for formal trial on the morrow. They did feel, however, that given the present temper of the populace, such a move might prove dangerous to themselves. “The people will demand a victim, two victims, perhaps more,” Danou said with a doubtful nod of the head. “They might vent their wrath on us.”

===Chinese===
什么也休想把这念头从百姓脑子里赶出去。直到革命的那些可怕日子逝去许久、男男女女重又清醒过来之后，六月里那一天在场旁听弗勒尔·肖维兰——*nommée*（又名）阿尔芒——受审的人们，还会讲起那个奇妙的故事：魔鬼如何走进法院，把被告凭空摄了去。实情只有少数人知情，即便这少数人，也有许多地方只能靠猜。知情者当中便有肉铺老板塔尔蒂纳公民。朋友们追问起来，他讲的是这么一回事：据说，人群蜂拥冲出市政厅之后，他塔尔蒂纳，连同从法官席上走下来的公民波夏尔、公民达努，再加城里三四位头面人物——其中有卡里斯蒂拘押所的看守长莫蒂斯（Motus）——凑在一道合计了一两分钟，琢磨能不能想法子把弗勒蕾特丫头和她父亲走小路送往哪一处监狱，好等明天提出来正式过堂。不过他们也着实觉得，按眼下百姓的火气，这么办恐怕自身难保。「老百姓会讨一个祭品，两个，说不定更多，」达努疑虑重重地点点头，说道。「他们说不定会把怒火撒到咱们头上。」

===Original===
That was sound logic, and the project was abandoned almost as soon as it was formulated.

===Chinese===
这话在理，那计划几乎刚一议出便作罢了。

===Original===
Motus, it seems, then turned familiarly to the giant and said:

===Chinese===
据说莫蒂斯随即熟络地转向那大个子，说道：

===Original===
“*Tiens*, Rémi, is it thou?”

===Chinese===
「哟（*Tiens*），雷米（Rémi），是你呀？」

===Original===
“Myself, citizen,” the giant replied.

===Chinese===
「正是在下，公民，」大个子答道。

===Original===
In response to inquiries from the others, Chief Warder Motus then explained that Rémi was a scavenger whom he himself had taken on in the Caristie house for extra work when the regular man fell sick. A splendid patriot, Motus averred. There was, therefore, not the faintest cause for suspicion.

===Chinese===
经旁人打听，看守长莫蒂斯随即解释说：雷米是个打扫夫，原先那人害了病，是他本人把雷米添雇进卡里斯蒂拘押所干额外活计的。莫蒂斯力言，那是个顶呱呱的爱国志士。因此，丝毫没有起疑的由头。

===Original===
“Come along, all of you,” Pochart now said addressing Rémi and his mates. “Bring along the prisoners. The people are waiting.”

===Chinese===
「你们几位，都来吧，」这时波夏尔冲着雷米和他的伙伴们说。「把囚犯们带上。老百姓等着呢。」

===Original===
“Give them time to settle down,” Rémi replied with a shrug and laugh. “We are the chief comedians in this play. Do you all go and prepare everything for our entrance.”

===Chinese===
「让他们先安顿安顿，」雷米耸耸肩，笑着答道。「我们是这出戏里的头牌角儿。诸位都先过去，把我们登台的各项准备拾掇妥当。」

===Original===
“You won’t tarry?” Danou admonished.

===Chinese===
「你们可别磨蹭？」达努叮嘱道。

===Original===
“Not we,” Rémi replied. “We’re as eager as you for the spectacle, eh, citizens?” he added, turning to his mates who had the President of the Tribunal still between them.

===Chinese===
「不会的，」雷米答道。「这场热闹，咱们跟诸位一样眼馋呢，对不对，公民们？」他转向自己的伙伴补了一句，那几个人中间还夹着法庭庭长。

===Original===
Rémi then took a pistol out of his ragged breeches and handed it to Citizen Tartine.

===Chinese===
雷米随即从破裤子里掏出一把手枪，递给塔尔蒂纳公民。

===Original===
“When the executioner is ready,” he said, “and everything prepared for our entrance, just give us the signal by firing the pistol. We’ll be with you a few minutes after that. We’ve yet another surprise for the spectators,” he added with another laugh, “which will delight them and you.”

===Chinese===
「等刽子手准备停当，」他说，「我们登场的一切也布置齐了，就放一枪为号。不出几分钟，我们就到诸位跟前。我们还给看客们另备了一桩意外之喜，」他又笑道，「保管叫他们和诸位都乐开怀。」

===Original===
Tartine vowed that not the slightest suspicion entered his head or that of his companions. How could one suspect a patriot vouched for by no less a person than Motus the chief warder? In the end, however, Pochart decided that two men of the gendarmerie, one of whom was a sergeant, who were still standing at attention below the judges’ bench, should remain with Rémi and his mates and escort them when the time came, on to the Place.

===Chinese===
塔尔蒂纳赌咒发誓地说，无论是他还是同伴们，当时脑子里都没掠过半点疑心。有看守长莫蒂斯这样的大人物具保的爱国志士，谁会起疑呢？不过到最后，波夏尔还是定夺：让仍在法官席下笔直立正的两名宪兵——其中一个是个中士——留下来守着雷米一伙，到时候押他们上广场。

===Original===
After which the group of notabilities followed the rest of the crowd out into the open. When looking back upon what followed, they all agreed that some fifteen minutes must have gone from the time when they finally left the courthouse and took their last look on Rémi and his mates, to that when they returned and found the place empty. They all said that even then, at first glance, no suspicion entered their minds and they stood about for a few minutes talking together, thinking that Rémi was preparing the surprise spectacle which he had promised them. Thinking too that every moment would bring the scavenger back with his mates and the prisoners. Tartine, the butcher, was the first to suspect that there might be something wrong. He crossed the floor of the room, and made his way to the private door which was at the back of the judges’ bench and led to some corridors and private rooms, and also to the back of the premises of the Hôtel de Ville, and to a back door which gave on a narrow street that ran parallel with the façade.

===Chinese===
这一行头面人物随即随着其余的人群走到外头露天里。事后回想接下来的情形，他们一致认定：从他们终于离开法院、对雷米一伙投下最后一瞥，到他们回头发现那屋子已空空如也，中间约莫过去了十五分钟。他们都说，即便到了那时，乍一看也谁都没起疑，还站着聊了好几分钟，只当雷米正在张罗他许下的那场意外好戏，只当打扫夫随时会带着伙伴们和囚犯们回来。肉铺老板塔尔蒂纳头一个起了疑。他穿过大厅地面，走向法官席后部的一扇内门——那门通着几条走廊和几间内室，也通到市政厅楼的后部，还通到一扇后门，门外便是一条与正立面平行的小街。

===Original===
The private door was locked, with no key to be seen. But even then, so remote was suspicion from their minds, that Tartine and the others hammered away on the door and called loudly to Rémi. The door was made of solid oak, but Pochart and Tartine were both of them powerful men. Receiving no answer to their call, they searched amidst the litter left pell-mell by the crowd upon the gradients, and found an axe and a leaded stick. Thus armed they attacked the panels of the door, whilst Danou and one of the others wisely thought of closing the portals of the Hôtel de Ville. The oak panels yielded after awhile. The door battered in, fell under the heavy blows dealt by Tartine the butcher with the axe. He and Pochart and two or three of the others striding over the debris, found themselves in a dark corridor. Some twenty paces down the corridor on the right, they came to another door. It was locked, but behind it came a vigorous sound of banging and the door shook now and again as if under heavy blows. Once more the axe was brought into play, the door was smashed in and as it fell in with a crash, it revealed the two men of the gendarmerie, with arms and legs securely pinioned, and their crimson caps stuffed into their mouths. One of them had succeeded in rolling along the floor, near enough to the door to kick against it with his otherwise helpless feet.

===Chinese===
那扇内门锁着，钥匙不见踪影。可即便到了那时，疑心离他们的脑子仍旧那么远，塔尔蒂纳和众人只顾捶门，大声喊雷米。那门是整料橡木做的，好在波夏尔和塔尔蒂纳都是膀大腰圆的壮汉。喊了几声没人应，他们便在人群溃散时七零八落丢在阶坡上的杂物堆里翻拣，寻出一把斧头和一根包铅木棍。这样武装起来，他们便朝门板砍砸过去，达努和另一个人则明智地想起该去关市政厅的大门。橡木门板撑了一阵，便吃不消了。门被砸开了口，在肉铺老板塔尔蒂纳斧头的重重捶击下轰然倒下。他同波夏尔和另外两三个人跨过断木碎屑，发觉自己置身于一条黑黢黢的走廊。沿走廊走约莫二十步，靠右手，他们又遇上一扇门。门也锁着，门后却传来一阵用力的撞击声，门板不时震颤，仿佛正挨着沉重的捶打。斧头再度派上用场，门被砸开，轰隆一声倒下时，露出了那两名宪兵：他们手脚都被捆得结结实实，深红色的帽子塞在嘴里。其中一个总算在地板上滚到了离门够近的地方，用他那双再干不了别的的脚，一下一下踹着门板。

===Original===
There could no longer be any doubt. The public had been hoaxed either by an impudent impostor, or by a traitor, bribed to aid the prisoners to escape. The words: “English spies,” soon cropped up as did those of Amédé Colombe and Architect Caristie and a host of others. This too, no doubt, was their work. At least this was the opinion of some, whilst others, headed by Danou, shook their heads dubiously. Citizen Chauvelin was known to be the sworn enemy of those English spies﻿—weren’t they called the League of the Scarlet Pimpernel?﻿—and it was Citizen Chauvelin and his daughter Fleur who had been so insolently spirited away.

===Chinese===
再没有怀疑的余地了。公众要么是被一个厚颜无耻的冒牌货愚弄了，要么是被一个受贿帮囚犯逃跑的叛徒愚弄了。「英国间谍」几个字很快冒了出来，阿梅代·科隆布、建筑师卡里斯蒂以及另外一长串名字也随之被提起。毫无疑问，这也是他们干的。至少有些人是这么看的；而以达努为首的另一些人，则将信将疑地直摇头。谁都知道，肖维兰公民正是那些英国间谍——他们不是被唤作猩红繁笺花同盟么？——誓不两立的死敌，而这回被如此放肆地摄走的，恰恰是肖维兰公民和他的女儿弗勒尔。

===Original===
Having hastily released the men of the gendarmerie, they all ran down the length of the corridor as swiftly as they could, chiefly because one of the soldiers said that this corridor led ultimately to a back entrance of the Hôtel de Ville. But the building itself was something of a maze, the passages were dark and narrow. It took them all some time to find that back door, and when at last they came upon it, they found it locked.

===Chinese===
他们慌忙给两名宪兵松了绑，随即撒开腿，顺着走廊能跑多快跑多快——主要因为其中一个士兵说，这条走廊到底通着市政厅的一处后门。可这楼本身就好比一座迷宫，过道又黑又窄。他们费了好一番工夫才找到那扇后门，待终于寻到跟前，却发现门上了锁。

===Original===
Once more the axe had to come into play, and time had in the meanwhile slipped by to the tune of some twenty minutes. Nor did the narrow back street reveal any of the secrets of this amazing adventure. Impostors, traitors or English spies, Rémi the scavenger and his mates had disappeared with the two prisoners and taken their secret with them. On the other side of the road there was a row of one-storied, tumbledown houses, inhabited by some of the poorest families in the city. Inquiries at each house in succession revealed but little. Nearly all the inmates had spent their morning as usual watching the trials in the Hôtel de Ville and were not yet home; but in one of the houses a sick woman had, it seems, been standing at the window when she saw four or five men come out of the building opposite. One of them, she said, was very tall and was carrying what she thought was a large bundle on his shoulder. The others were hustling a short, thin man who wore a blue coat and had on a tricolour sash round his waist. They turned sharply to their right and she soon lost sight of them. She thought nothing about the incident, one saw so many strange things these days.

===Chinese===
斧头只得再一次出场，这时时间已经溜掉了约莫二十分钟。那条狭窄的后街，也没能泄露这场惊人历险的半点秘密。冒牌货也罢，叛徒也罢，英国间谍也罢，打扫夫雷米和他的伙伴们已带着两名囚犯消失得无影无踪，把秘密也一并带走了。马路对面是一排单层的破屋，住着城里顶穷的几户人家。挨家挨户问下来，问出的东西少得可怜。住户几乎全都照常在市政厅看了一上午审判，人还没到家；只有一户里，一个害病的女人似乎当时正站在窗前，看见四五个男人从对面那座楼里出来。其中一个，她说，个子极高，肩上扛着一件她当作大包袱的东西。其余的人正连推带搡地押着一个矮瘦男人，那人穿一件蓝外套，腰里系一条三色绶带。他们猛地朝右一拐，她很快便望不见了。这事她压根没往心里去——这些日子，稀奇事谁没见过几桩。

===Original===
In the meanwhile the crowd on the Place had begun to disperse, the first stragglers were wending their way to their homes. Pochart and Danou holding high functions in the administration of justice, did not feel that it was incumbent upon them to go hunting for spies. That was the business of the gendarmerie, and they parted presently from their friends, declaring their intention of sending immediately for the Chief Commissary of Police. The others, feeling that it was not part of their duty either to run after escaped prisoners, found that they had pressing business to see to at home.

===Chinese===
这当口，广场上的人群已开始散去，最先零零落落的几个已经往家里走了。波夏尔和达努在司法界身居高位，觉得四处缉拿间谍这等事算不得自己的分内职责。那是宪兵队的差事，于是他们不久便与朋友们作别，声称要立刻派人去请警察总监（Chief Commissary of Police）。其余的人也觉得，追赶脱逃的囚犯同样与己无干，随即发觉家里都有急事等着照应。

===Original===
As far as Citizen Tartine, the butcher, was concerned, the incident had no further interest for him, save for the pleasure of recounting his share of the adventure to his numerous friends. A couple more traitors escaped from the clutches of justice, a few more English spies when already the country swarmed with them, was nothing to worry one’s head about.

===Chinese===
至于肉铺老板塔尔蒂纳公民，这桩变故于他再没有什么趣味，唯一的好处，是往后可以向他那许多朋友吹讲自己在其中的一份。不过又从正义的魔爪底下逃掉两个叛徒，又添几个英国间谍——反正这号人全国早已多得成灾——实在犯不着为此伤脑筋。

===Original===
Pochart and Danou did, on the other hand, worry their heads considerably about it all. They had a burning desire to know just what the English spies did ultimately do with their colleague Chauvelin. They hoped﻿—oh! very ardently﻿—that as soon as the much-vaunted Scarlet Pimpernel discovered that it was his inveterate enemy whom he had rescued from the guillotine, he would either hand him back straightway to the tender mercies of justice, or simply murder him in some convenient and out-of-the-way corner of the district. Pochart and Danou would have preferred the former alternative as being more satisfactory to their wounded vanity and their baffled spite.

===Chinese===
波夏尔和达努却不然，为这桩事把脑袋都想痛了。他们火烧火燎地想知道，那些英国间谍到头来究竟把他们的同僚肖维兰怎样发落了。他们巴望——哦！热切地巴望——那被吹上天的猩红繁笺花一发现他从断头台下救出的竟是自家宿敌，要么当即把人交回去、听凭「正义」的慈悲发落，要么干脆在本地区哪个便当的僻静角落把他结果了。波夏尔和达努宁可是前一种结局，因为那更能抚慰他们受了伤的虚荣心和不得发泄的怨毒。

===Original===
Unlike Tartine, they seldom spoke of their experiences in connection with the affair. But their hopes did rise to their zenith when a week or so later President Legrange and Public Prosecutor Isnard returned from their fool’s errand to Paris; there could be no doubt that even Robespierre, friend of Chauvelin though he be, would order the punishment of such a consummate liar and traitor.

===Chinese===
与塔尔蒂纳不同，他们难得谈起自己与这桩事的干系。但约莫一个星期后，勒格朗热庭长和公诉人伊斯纳尔从巴黎那趟扑空的差事回来，他们的指望一度涨到了顶：毫无疑问，哪怕是肖维兰的朋友罗伯斯庇尔，也必定会下令惩办这样一个十足的骗子兼叛徒。

## XXXVIII / 第三十八章

===Original===
An immense lassitude had held Fleurette in a kind of semiconsciousness, a dreamless sleep from which she woke at intervals, only to open her eyes for a moment, and immediately let the lids, heavy with sleep, fall over them again. It was the reaction insisted on by health and youth against the terrible nerve-strain of that awful day.

===Chinese===
一股极度的倦怠把弗勒蕾特困在一种半昏迷的状态里，像一场无梦的酣睡，她时时从中醒转，却只把眼睛睁开片刻，随即又让沉甸甸载满睡意的眼皮阖落下来。这正是健康与青春执意讨还的一口气，为的是抵住那可怕的一日加在神经上的骇人重负。

===Original===
During the brief intervals while she had a certain consciousness of things about her, she found herself nestling against chéri Bibi’s shoulder! and when, with half-dimmed eyes she looked up at him, and tried to smile between two yawns, she always saw his pale, grave face turned away in profile, gazing straight out before him into the dark recess of the post-chaise, in which apparently they were travelling. She called softly to him once or twice, but he never turned to look at her, only his hand, which felt cold and clammy, would gently stroke her hair.

===Chinese===
在那些她还多少晓得周遭事物的短短间隙里，她发觉自己正偎在亲爱的比比的肩头；每当她睁着半昏的倦眼仰望他，想在两个哈欠之间挤出一丝笑意时，她总看见他那张苍白、凝重的脸侧向一旁，直望着自己前方驿车深处的暗影——看样子，他们正坐着这辆车赶路。她有一两次柔声唤他，可他从不回过头来看她，只有他那只摸上去又冷又黏湿的手，会轻轻抚弄她的头发。

===Original===
How long all this lasted, what happened to her in the intervals of sleep, Fleurette never knew, but there came a time when the chaise rattled unpleasantly over the cobblestones of a city, and lights darted to and fro through the darkness as the vehicle lumbered along through fitfully lighted streets. Fleurette sat up straight; all the sleep suddenly gone out of her eyes.

===Chinese===
这一切持续了多久，睡去的间隙里她又经历了什么，弗勒蕾特一概不知；只记得后来有那么一阵子，驿车喀啦喀啦碾过一座城里的鹅卵石路面，颠得人好不难受；车身顺着时明时暗的街巷隆隆前行，一团团灯火在黑暗中来去穿梭。弗勒蕾特一下子坐直了身子；睡意霎时从眼中退得干干净净。

===Original===
“Where are we going, chéri Bibi?” she asked. “Do you know?”

===Chinese===
「我们要上哪儿去呀，亲爱的比比？」她问，「你知道吗？」

===Original===
“No! I do not,” Bibi replied, and his voice sounded hoarse and hollow. “Would to God I did.”

===Chinese===
「不！我不知道。」比比答道，他的嗓音听来沙哑而空洞。「但愿天主让我知道。」

===Original===
Fleurette had never heard him invoke *le bon Dieu* before, and she tried through the gloom to peer into his face.

===Chinese===
从前弗勒蕾特从不曾听他呼求过好天主（*le bon Dieu*），她便竭力透过昏暗，想看清他的脸。

===Original===
“But we are out of danger now, chéri?” she asked wide-eyed, the old terror which had caused her to lose consciousness in that awful courthouse, once more clutching at her heart.

===Chinese===
「可我们已经脱险了，对不对，亲爱的比比？」她睁大眼睛问；那股曾叫她在一座可怕的法庭里昏死过去的老恐惧，又一次攫住了她的心。

===Original===
“I do not know,” he murmured mechanically; “would to God I did.”

===Chinese===
「我不知道。」他机械地喃喃道，「但愿天主让我知道。」

===Original===
And then as if recalled to himself by the half-drawn sigh of terror from Fleurette, he seized hold of her, and pressed her head against his breast.

===Chinese===
接着，仿佛是弗勒蕾特那半声惊惧的叹息把他唤回了神，他一把将她抓住，把她的头按在自己胸口。

===Original===
“No! No!” he said hastily, “they cannot harm you whilst I’m here to guard you.”

===Chinese===
「不！不！」他慌忙说道，「只要我在这儿护着你，他们就伤不着你。」

===Original===
Just then the coach came to a halt, and a moment later the door was thrown open and a gruff voice said:

===Chinese===
正在这时，驿车停了下来；片刻之后，车门被猛地拉开，一个粗哑的嗓音说道：

===Original===
“Will you descend, citizeness?”

===Chinese===
「您下车吗，女公民？」

===Original===
Fleurette, frightened, clung to Bibi. She made no attempt to move. Whereupon the gruff voice resumed:

===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特吓坏了，紧紧贴住比比。她一动也不动。于是那粗哑的嗓音又开了腔：

===Original===
“If you don’t come willingly, I shall have to send someone to fetch you.”

===Chinese===
「你要是不肯乖乖下来，我就只好叫人来带你下去了。」

===Original===
Fleurette buried her face against Bibi’s coat. His arms held her tightly. A minute, perhaps less, went by, and then﻿—suddenly﻿—she heard a voice﻿—a very gentle, very timid voice this time, saying:

===Chinese===
弗勒蕾特把脸深深埋进比比的外衣。他的双臂紧紧搂住她。一分钟过去了，兴许还不到一分钟，然后——蓦地——她听见一个声音——这一回，是个非常温柔、非常怯生生的声音，正说：

===Original===
“Mam’zelle Fleurette! Oh, Mam’zelle Fleurette, I pray you to turn to me. It is I﻿—Amédé.”

===Chinese===
「弗勒蕾特小姐！哦，弗勒蕾特小姐，求您转过脸来看我。是我——阿梅代（Amédé）。」

===Original===
What had happened? Was she dreaming? Or had she died of fright and gone straight up to heaven? Certain it is that she felt a timid hand upon her shoulder, whilst Bibi’s hold upon her relaxed.

===Chinese===
这是怎么了？她是在做梦？还是她已经吓死过去、魂儿径直升了天？可以肯定的是，她觉出一只怯生生的手落在了自己肩头，而比比搂着她的手臂松了下来。

===Original===
“Hold up the lantern, man,” the gruff voice now broke in upon the delicious silence that ensued, “and let her see that she is not dreaming.”

===Chinese===
「把灯举起来，伙计，」那粗哑的嗓音此刻打断了随之而来的一片妙不可言的静默，「让她瞧瞧，她可不是在做梦。」

===Original===
The light of a lantern flashed across Fleurette’s eyes, she opened them and turned her head, and found herself gazing on M’sieu’ Amédé’s pink and moist face, into his kind eyes full of anxiety and of tenderness, upon his mouth which had taught her how to kiss. Gently, slowly, she extricated herself from Bibi’s embrace. Gently, slowly she seemed to glide into Amédé’s arms.

===Chinese===
一盏提灯的光在弗勒蕾特眼前一闪。她睁开眼，转过头，发现自己正望着阿梅代先生那张红扑扑、汗津津的脸，望进他那双盛满焦灼与温柔的慈和眼睛里，望着那张曾经教会她怎样亲吻的嘴。轻轻地，缓缓地，她从比比的怀抱里挣出身来。轻轻地，缓缓地，她仿佛滑进了阿梅代的臂弯。

===Original===
He carried her whither she knew not. All she knew was that presently she found herself snuggling in a deep, cosy armchair, and that Amédé was kneeling beside her, with his eyes fixed ecstatically upon her as if she were *la sainte Vierge* herself.

===Chinese===
他抱着她去了哪儿，她并不知道。她只知道，不多时她便窝进了一把又深又暖和的扶手椅，阿梅代跪在她身旁，一双眼睛出神地凝望着她，仿佛她就是圣母（*la sainte Vierge*）本人。

===Original===
“Where am I, dear M’sieu’ Amédé?” she asked.

===Chinese===
「我这是在哪儿呀，亲爱的阿梅代先生？」她问。

===Original===
“At Ste. Césaire, Mam’zelle Fleurette,” he replied.

===Chinese===
「在圣塞泽尔（Ste. Césaire），弗勒蕾特小姐。」他答道。

===Original===
“Where is that?”

===Chinese===
「那是什么地方？」

===Original===
“Just outside Nîmes. Your chaise passed through the streets of Nîmes.”

===Chinese===
「就在尼姆（Nîmes）城外。你们的驿车方才穿过了尼姆的街道。」

===Original===
“I daresay,” she said with a tired little sigh. “I was so sleepy; I didn’t know where we were.”

===Chinese===
「多半是吧，」她带着一丝倦倦的轻叹说，「我困得厉害；都不知道咱们到了哪儿。」

===Original===
“We are under the protection of the bravest men that ever lived,” Amédé said slowly. “They saved me from death. They have saved you, Mam’zelle Fleurette.”

===Chinese===
「咱们如今在天底下最勇敢的人们的保护之下。」阿梅代缓缓说道，「他们把我从死亡里救了出来。他们也把您救出来了，弗勒蕾特小姐。」

===Original===
A shudder went through her. She closed her eyes as if to try and shut away the awful visions which his words had conjured up. But his kind, strong arms encircled her closer, and she nestled against him and once again felt comforted and safe. He told her the entire odyssey of his rescue, from the hour when the mock soldiers entered his father’s shop at Laragne, until when his brave rescuers took leave of him outside the derelict cottage by the banks of the Drôme, and he, seeing the pale crescent of the moon rise above the snow-capped crest of La Lance, had solemnly bowed nine times, praying for that joy which today was his at last.

===Chinese===
一阵战栗传遍她的全身。她合上眼睛，仿佛想把他的话唤起的种种可怕景象关在眼外。可他那双慈和而有力的臂膀把她搂得更紧了，她偎在他怀里，又一次感到宽心而安全。他把自家获救的整套历险打头至尾讲给她听：从那些假扮的士兵走进他父亲在拉拉涅（Laragne）的铺子的那一刻起，直到他那些勇敢的救命恩人在德龙河（Drôme）岸边那座荒废小屋外与他作别——他望着一弯淡淡的新月升上拉朗斯峰（La Lance）积雪盖顶的峰巅，庄严地鞠了九个躬，祈求那份欢喜，而今这欢喜终究落到了他的身上。

===Original===
He had spent a few very lonely days in the cottage after that, devoured with anxiety as to the fate of his beloved. He could not eat, he could not sleep. For hours he would watch the filmy crescent of the moon, whose pale light mayhap illumined the window behind which his own Fleurette would also be watching and praying. And three days ago he received the message which he was waiting for. It appeared mysteriously early one morning outside the cottage door. A missive, with a stone put upon it to prevent its being blown away by the wind. How it got there Amédé never knew. It came from the leader of that gallant little league of Englishmen who devoted their lives to helping those in distress. In it he, Amédé, was ordered to walk as far as Crest, to the house of Citizen Marcor the farrier, where he could hire a horse. And then to hie him straightway hither to Ste. Césaire, not sleeping in any wayside inn, but rather in the fields, under shelter of hedges or forest trees, getting food for himself and his horse as best he could. The missive further directed him, on arriving at Ste. Césaire, to seek out an empty house situated in the Rue Basse, and there to wait, for of a surety within two days he would hold his beloved Fleurette in his arms. Amédé had obeyed these commands to the letter. This very morning he had arrived at Ste. Césaire and found the house in the Rue Basse. It was neither empty nor uninhabited. There was furniture in the house, and what’s more there were two friends, two fine English heroes, who had been expecting Amédé and who made him welcome when he arrived. Oh! and didn’t Mam’zelle Fleurette think that these Englishmen were the finest and bravest men that ever lived? As for their chief who was known amongst them as the Scarlet Pimpernel (*le mouron rouge*, M’sieu’ Amédé called it), he surely was more like one of the mythological gods rather than a mere mortal.

===Chinese===
此后，他在那座小屋里独自熬过了好些天孤寂的日子，为他心爱之人的命运熬煎得五内俱焚。他吃不下，睡不着。一连几个钟头，他呆望着那轮薄纱似的弯月，心想这月儿的清辉，兴许也正照着那扇小窗——他自家的弗勒蕾特，多半也在窗后守望着、祈祷着。三天前，他盼来了他等候的消息。一天清早，它神秘地出现在小屋门外：一封信，上面压着块石头，免得叫风吹跑。这信是怎么到的，阿梅代始终无从知晓。它出自那支豪侠的英格兰人小同盟首领之手——这些英国人甘把性命搭上，专门搭救落难之人。信里命令他——阿梅代本人——徒步走到克雷斯特（Crest），到蹄铁匠公民马科尔（Citizen Marcor）家中，在那儿雇一匹马；然后即刻兼程直奔圣塞泽尔，沿途不在任何客栈投宿，宁可露宿田野，借树篱或林间树木遮身，人吃马嚼，都自己设法张罗。信里还吩咐他，一到圣塞泽尔，便去寻下街（Rue Basse）的一座空房子，在那儿候着，因为不出两日，他准能把他心爱的弗勒蕾特搂进怀里。阿梅代把这些吩咐一字不差地照办了。就在今天早晨，他抵达圣塞泽尔，找到了下街的那座房子。房子既不空，也并非无人居住。屋里有家具，更有两位朋友、两位好样儿的英国英雄正候着阿梅代，他一进门便受到热诚的欢迎。哦！弗勒蕾特小姐难道不觉得，这些英国人是开天辟地以来最出色、最勇敢的男子汉吗？至于他们的首领——在他们中间，他以猩红繁笺花之名为人所知（阿梅代先生管它叫 *le mouron rouge*，法语即「猩红繁笺花」）——他实在更像神话里的一位天神，而非区区一个凡人。

===Original===
M’sieu’ Amédé seemed very anxious to know what Mam’zelle Fleurette thought about all these marvellous adventures, but how could she tell him, how could she talk at all when every time she raised her blue eyes to him, he broke off in the midst of a most exciting narrative in order to ask her in a voice vibrating with passion: “*Tu m’aimes* Fleurette?”

===Chinese===
阿梅代先生似乎急切地想知道，弗勒蕾特小姐对这些奇妙的冒险有何想法；可她怎么告诉他呢？她又怎么开得了口呢？——每当她抬起那双蓝眼睛望他，他总要把一段正讲到最来劲处的叙述拦腰打断，用激动得发颤的声音问她：「你爱我吗（*Tu m'aimes*），弗勒蕾特？」

## XXXIX / 第三十九章

===Original===
Chauvelin, after he had seen Fleurette safely carried away in her lover’s arms, sat for awhile in the dark interior of the coach, staring into the gloom, his folded hands clasped between his knees. His thoughts were in such a whirl that it almost seemed as if he were unconscious. He certainly was insentient; he neither saw, nor heard, nor felt anything save the joy of knowing that his Fleurette was safe. It was only a few minutes﻿—fifteen perhaps﻿—later that a pleasant laugh broke in on his riotous thoughts, and that he became aware of a tall figure sitting beside him in the coach.

===Chinese===
看着弗勒蕾特已被她的情人稳稳抱走，肖维兰在驿车黑暗的车厢里又坐了一会儿，直勾勾地望着黑暗深处，交叠的双手紧夹在两膝之间。他的思绪乱作一团，人几乎像是失去了知觉。他也确凿是全然无知无觉了；看不见，听不见，什么也感觉不到，只知道他的弗勒蕾特平安了，单是这一喜便充盈了他整个心神。不过几分钟——兴许十五分钟——之后，一声愉快的轻笑打断了他纷乱的思绪，他也才觉察出车厢里有个高大的身影坐在自己身旁。

===Original===
“You see, my dear M. Chambertin,” the voice which he dreaded most in all the world said suddenly in his ear, “I would not forego the pleasure of bidding you au revoir.”

===Chinese===
「你瞧，我亲爱的尚贝尔坦先生（M. Chambertin），」这个他在普天之下最怕听见的嗓音忽然在他耳边开了口，「我可不肯放弃向你道一声『再会』的乐趣。」

===Original===
Chauvelin half turned to his enemy, the man whom he had so persistently wronged, so persistently pursued with hatred and with spite. Through the gloom he could just see the outline of the massive figure, wrapped in a dark, caped coat, and of the proud head so nobly held above the firm, somewhat stiff neck.

===Chinese===
肖维兰半转过身，面向他的仇敌——这个他曾那样百般亏待、又那样怀着仇恨与怨毒穷追不舍的人。透过昏暗，他勉强辨得出那副伟岸身躯的轮廓，裹在一件带披肩的黑色大衣里；辨得出那颗高傲的头颅，何等轩昂地挺在坚实而略显僵直的脖颈之上。

===Original===
Did all that this man stood for in the way of heroism and selflessness, strike a chord of shame in the heart of this callous, revolutionary tyrant? Who shall say? Certain it is that for the moment Chauvelin felt awed, and sat there in the gloom, silent, motionless, staring into the black vacancy. But after a second or two his lips uttered mechanically the name that was uppermost in his mind:

===Chinese===
此人所代表的那一切英勇与无私，可曾在这个冷酷的革命暴君心底，拨动过一丝羞愧的心弦？谁说得准呢？可以肯定的是，肖维兰此刻感到了一阵敬畏，他坐在暗处，一言不发，一动不动，直望着那片漆黑的空虚。可过了一两秒，他的嘴唇便机械地吐出了此刻最压在他心头的那个名字：

===Original===
“Fleurette?”

===Chinese===
「弗勒蕾特？」

===Original===
“She is under my care,” Blakeney said slowly. “Tomorrow at break of day she and her sweetheart will set sail for England with some of my friends. There she will be under the care of the noblest woman in the world, Lady Blakeney, who will take her revenge on you for all the wrong you did her, by lavishing the treasures of her sympathy upon your child.”

===Chinese===
「她由我照看着。」布莱克尼缓缓说道。「明天破晓时分，她就同她的心上人一道，随我的几位朋友扬帆启程去英国。到了那边，自有天底下最高贵的女人——布莱克尼夫人照料她。你从前怎样亏待了夫人，夫人如今向你复仇的法子，便是把她满怀怜惜的珍宝，尽数倾注在你的孩子身上。」

===Original===
“Then Fleurette will be happy?” Chauvelin murmured involuntarily.

===Chinese===
「那么弗勒蕾特会幸福的？」肖维兰不由自主地喃喃道。

===Original===
“Happy, yes! she will soon forget.”

===Chinese===
「幸福，是的！要不了多久，她就把这一切忘掉了。」

===Original===
“Then I am ready, Sir Percy.”

===Chinese===
「那么我准备好了，珀西爵士。」

===Original===
“Ready? For what?”

===Chinese===
「准备好了？准备做什么？」

===Original===
“My life is at your service. My enemies are waiting for me over in Orange. You have but to send me back thither and your own vengeance will be complete.”

===Chinese===
「我这条命听凭你差遣。我的仇人们正在奥朗日那头等着我。你只消把我送回去，你自家的复仇便十全十美了。」

===Original===
For a second or two after that there was silence in the old post-chaise; only Chauvelin’s laboured breathing broke the utter stillness of the gloom. Until suddenly a pleasant, mocking laugh struck upon his ear.

===Chinese===
此后有一两秒钟，老旧的驿车里一片沉寂；惟有肖维兰那吃力的呼吸，划破黑暗中十足的静谧。蓦地，一声愉快的、带着嘲弄的轻笑传入他的耳中。

===Original===
“Egad man, you are priceless,” quoth Sir Percy gaily. “You must indeed credit me with a total lack of the saving grace, if you think it would amuse me to hand you over to your genial friends over in Orange.”

===Chinese===
「天哪，伙计，你真是个无价之宝。」珀西爵士快活地说。「你若以为把你移交给你在奥朗日那班和和气气的朋友，会让我觉得有趣，那你实在该把我看作全无半点得救的恩典的人才对。」

===Original===
“But I am at your mercy, sir.”

===Chinese===
「可我正任凭你发落，爵士。」

===Original===
“As I and my beloved wife have been once or twice, eh? Well! I am hitting back now. That’s all.”

===Chinese===
「就像我和我亲爱的妻子从前也有过一两回那样，嗯？好啦！如今轮到我反击了。就这么回事。」

===Original===
“Hitting back?” Chauvelin exclaimed. “You have the power now. I admit it. I am in your hands. My life is at your command.”

===Chinese===
「反击？」肖维兰失声道，「如今你大权在握。我承认。我在你掌中。我这条命听你发落。」

===Original===
“La, man!” Sir Percy retorted lightly, “what should I do with your worthless life? For the moment all I want is to make that sweet child up there completely happy by telling her that you are safe and well. After that you may go to the devil for aught I care. You probably will.”

===Chinese===
「嗐，伙计！」珀西爵士轻快地应道，「你那条不值钱的命，我要它何用？眼下我一心想做的，不过是告诉上头那位可爱的小姑娘，说她父亲平安康健，好让她彻底安心、彻底欢喜。这之后，你爱找魔鬼去便找魔鬼去，与我毫不相干。多半你也真会去的。」

===Original===
“Then,” Chauvelin murmured aghast, “you grant me my life, you﻿—”

===Chinese===
「那么，」肖维兰惊骇地喃喃道，「你饶我一条性命，你——」

===Original===
“I am sending you back safely as far as Nîmes. What happens to you after that I neither know nor care. You have tried to do me such an infinity of wrong at different times, you still hate me so cordially, you﻿—”

===Chinese===
「我会平平安安把你送到尼姆（Nîmes）为止。至于那之后你会遇上什么，我既不知道，也不关心。你一而再、再而三，不知多少回处心积虑地想坑害我；你至今还这样真心实意地恨着我，你——」

===Original===
He paused for a moment with firm lips tightly pressed together and slender hand clutched upon his knee.

===Chinese===
他顿住片刻，坚毅的双唇紧紧抿拢，一只修长的手紧攥在膝上。

===Original===
“You are right there, Sir Percy,” Chauvelin murmured between his teeth. “God knows how I still hate you, even after this. You have the power to hit back. Why the devil don’t you do it?”

===Chinese===
「这话你说对了，珀西爵士，」肖维兰咬着牙喃喃道，「天知道我至今还多么恨你，哪怕经过这一遭。你有反击之力。可你到底为什么不使出来？」

===Original===
Whereupon Sir Percy threw back his head and his merry, infectious laugh woke the slumbering echoes of the sleepy little town.

===Chinese===
闻言，珀西爵士把头向后一仰，那欢快而富于感染力的笑声，惊醒了这座睡意沉沉的小镇里沉睡已久的回声。

===Original===
“La, man,” he said, “you’re astonishing. Can’t you see that this is my way of hitting back?”

===Chinese===
「嗐，伙计，」他说，「你真叫人叹为观止。你难道看不出，这正是我反击的方式吗？」
